- 2026-09-01 — **v1.43.0 — a report can be scoped to ANY payment period, by the studio or by the client.** No migration (`view_flags` is jsonb). Nitsan: *"change 'latest perios only' to 'current period', add new button 'previous period'. allow admin or client to select a payment period from the list of periods to see it in the hours table."*
  **`ReportTable`'s `periodOnly: boolean` became `periodIndex: number | null`.** One value, not a set of switches — the table is scoped to exactly one period or to none, so a pair of lit pills would describe a state that cannot exist. The editor gets **Current period** / **Previous period** / a **`Period ▾`** picker (rendered only past two periods); the client link gets **All periods** / **Current period** / **Previous period**, and every row of its existing "next billing" dropdown is now a button that scopes the table.
  ⚠️⚠️ **THE SELECTION IS PUBLISHED AS THE PERIOD'S START DATE (`view_flags.periodFrom`), NEVER AS AN INDEX, AND `sanitizeSnapshot` IS THE REASON.** Hiding a period (`p:{i}`) physically REMOVES it from the client's copy, so index 3 in the editor is a different period — or out of range — in the payload the client receives. An index would have scoped the client's table to the wrong period and shown real hours under the wrong heading, which is worse than showing none. `periodIndexFromDate` returns null when the named period is not in the array at all (the studio focused a period and then hid it) and the client opens on the whole table, which is the honest reading of that.
  ⚠️ **THE DRAFT ON THE EDITOR SIDE HOLDS THE DATE TOO, for a second and independent reason:** that page EDITS periods — dragging a divider, adding one, renaming one — and an index would slide onto a different period the moment the list changed underneath it. It resolves to an index at render, against the periods the preview is showing, which is why that resolution sits below `preview` rather than up with the rest of the view state.
  **Old links keep working, and this was verified against a real published row, not reasoned about.** Rows published before today carry `{periodOnly: true}` alone; a report URL is permanent and an archived client's link is never republished, so "no `periodFrom` field at all" still means what it meant when it was written — the current period. ⚠️ `periodOnly` is therefore NOT deleted from `ReportViewFlags`; it is still written (as "is a period selected") beside the new field.
  ⚠️ **THE GREEN "PERIOD" COLUMN FOLLOWS THE SELECTION** (`focusIndex`, was `latestIndex`). Scoping the table to July while a column headed "Period" kept totalling August would put two different periods side by side under one heading, on the screen hours are read off. With nothing selected it is the current period, exactly as before.
  **"Current" is the latest period by END date, not the one today falls inside** — carried over verbatim from the `latestIndex` it replaces, and the reason is unchanged: a published report is frozen, and a "today" rule would make the same link total a different period next month with nobody republishing it. Now in `src/lib/report-period-focus.ts` with 13 tests, because three places resolve it and only one of them renders the table; a re-derivation that drifts would light "Current period" while the table shows another.
  **MOBILE — the client link only** (Client Reports is gated below a desktop width). ⚠️ Three 44px capsules reading "All periods / Current period / Previous period" need 347px of a 359px phone line, so they wrapped MID-SET: "Previous period" landed beside "Only rows with hours", which reads as though those two belong together when one picks a period and the other filters rows. Below `sm` the pills drop the word "period" — **the width comes out of the WORDING, not the 44px tap floor or the phone's `px-5`**, both of which are untouched (v1.15.0/v1.42.0 rules). Measured at 375px: 56 + 84 + 89px, three on one line, filter beneath. Desktop keeps the full wording on one line.
  **Verified in the browser against real data, both surfaces.** Editor: Anchor's "Previous period" showed its two August columns with the green header retitled "Hours in August: 2026-08-01 → 2026-08-19", "Current period" its two September columns; Blazepod's picker (20+ periods) scoped to July's three week columns and the button then read "July" with neither pill lit. Client link on a real legacy row: opened scoped to August with "Current period" lit; picking January from the dropdown scoped to January; clicking that row again cleared back to all periods. The v1.42.3 per-client drafts carry the new selection too (Anchor "previous" while Blazepod held "July"). No console errors.

  ⚠️ **FOUND WHILE VERIFYING, NOT FIXED, AND IT IS A BILLING HOLE:** Anchor's periods are August 1/8 → **19/8** and September **21/8** → 19/9, so **20 Aug 2026 falls in no period and 11.5h of Anchor's time sits on that day** (counted by the week columns and the Total, invisible to the period breakdown). Not the rollover — `nextPeriod` always starts the day after the last one ends. `commitPeriodDate` blocks OVERLAPS (`to >= after.dateFrom`) but permits GAPS, so a hand edit that shortens a period leaves the day orphaned with nothing said. Raised with Nitsan; whether August should end 20/8 or September start 20/8 is his call, and the guard is worth revisiting either way.
- 2026-08-31 — **v1.42.3 — the client-reports view filters stop forgetting what the studio picked.** No migration. Nitsan: *"when selecting things and moving in tabs it doesnt remeber my picks: latest period only, only rows with hours, hours through."*
  ⚠️⚠️ **ONE CAUSE, THREE SYMPTOMS, AND THE THIRD IS THE ONE NOBODY WOULD HAVE CONNECTED TO A TAB.** The five view controls were plain component state re-seeded from the client's published `report_links` row by an effect keyed on `[selectedClient?.id, links]`. So (1) switching to another tab and back reverted the pills and the cut-off to whatever was last published; (2) a client never published reverted to defaults; and (3) **with the tab standing still, ANY write to `links` did the same** — dragging a period divider saves `customWeeks`, publishing, or the session's first `ensureLink` — so a pick could vanish mid-edit with nothing the studio did to explain it.
  **The fix is a map keyed by client id (`views`), not five loose scalars.** Each client is seeded ONCE, from its published link, the first time it is opened; from then until a page reload the draft belongs to the studio. ⚠️ **Seeding is therefore idempotent — `prev[id] ? prev : {…}` — and that is load-bearing, not defensive:** the effect still re-runs on every `links` change, and the guard is the only thing standing between a link write and the studio's picks.
  ⚠️ **AND IT WAITS FOR A NEW `linksLoaded` FLAG.** The links arrive after first paint, so seeding on the first run would bank the DEFAULTS against an empty map and then refuse — being a draft already — to adopt what was actually published. That failure would have looked exactly like the bug being fixed, only harder to see: correct after a tab switch, wrong on arrival.
  **`showAll` joined the draft rather than keeping its own reset.** It was being cleared by that same effect, so publishing or dragging a divider silently switched off the peek. Made per-client, the reset disappears instead of being narrowed — one mechanism, not a mechanism plus an exception. ⚠️ The suspend/restore rule is untouched: `showAll` still SUSPENDS `periodOnly`/`hideEmptyRows` rather than clearing them, and a publish still records the studio's actual setting.
  ⚠️ **DELIBERATELY NOT PERSISTED TO `localStorage`, AND `through` IS THE WHOLE REASON.** A remembered cut-off decays silently every week — the failure `cutoffIsStale` exists to warn about (Anchor sat at 22 Aug while the week ending 29 Aug was about to be published). Surviving a tab switch is worth having; surviving a reload would make that decay outlive the session that could notice it. A reload returns every client to its published values, warning included.
  **Verified in the browser, signed in, against real clients** — not by the gates alone. A sweep of Anchor → Maccabi → DualBird → No Traffic → Blazepod → Anchor → Maccabi read `aria-pressed` and the date field at each stop: every client returned its own four values, twice. Anchor seeded from its link (on/on/`2026-08-22`, stale warning shown) rather than from defaults, which is the after-first-paint race proven directly. No Traffic went 113 columns / 99 rows → 8 / 15 with both filters on, so the pills are still wired to the table. Show all on → Latest-period reads off; back off → it returns. Reload → Anchor back to on/on/`2026-08-22`.
  ⚠️ **NOT verified by hand: the links-write trigger** (divider drag, publish) — both write real client rows, and Copy link proved nothing because `ensureLink` returns early when the link is already in the map, so no `setLinks` fires. Said plainly rather than implied. What IS verified is the guard itself: the tab round trip re-runs that same effect for a client that already holds a draft and `through` stayed at `29/08` instead of reverting to the link's `22/08`. A links write re-enters the identical body through the identical guard; only the trigger differs.
  Noticed in passing, not this release: **Blazepod shows zero task rows and 0h current period with both filters on, against 262 columns of history** — either genuinely dormant or wanting the same rollover look the other clients got. Raised with Nitsan.
  Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 40 warnings (baseline), 422 tests, production build clean with `/_not-found` still `ƒ`.
- 2026-08-27 — **v1.42.2 — cleanup pass over the v1.42.1 review fixes. No behaviour change; v1.42.1 was already functionally identical in production.** No migration. A `/simplify` fan-out over the 502-line diff; five findings applied, four skipped with reasons.
  ⚠️ **`billing-rollover.ts` HAD A LOCAL `parse()` DUPLICATING `parseISO`, IN A FILE THAT ALREADY IMPORTED FROM `format.ts`.** Worth fixing rather than noting, because the shared one carries `.slice(0, 10)`: a value arriving as a full timestamp parses as its DATE instead of `Invalid Date`, which on this path would write a garbage `dateFrom` or period label. ⚠️ **And the local copy's own comment claimed it "matched `parseISO` elsewhere" — a claim that was only true by hand-maintenance, which this codebase treats as worse than no comment at all** (the v1.19.10 rule). 6 call sites re-pointed.
  ⚠️ **`parseInvoiceDay` WAS BUILDING A REGEX OUT OF THE INPUT IT HAD JUST MATCHED** (`new RegExp(\`${digits}(st|nd|rd|th)\`)`). Two costs: a pattern interpolated from matched text cannot be read in isolation — you have to reconstruct what `digits` was to know what it matches — and it would match an ordinal on **any later occurrence of the same digit run** rather than the one that was found. Now a static literal anchored at the match offset (`/^\d+(?:st|nd|rd|th)\b/i` against `trimmed.slice(m.index)`), which preserves the documented property exactly: **no leading `\b`, because in "20th" there is no word boundary between the `0` and the `t`** — the bug that made v1.41.0's regex read nothing at all from the real data. All 7 payment-term cases still pass.
  **`report-table.tsx`: the "not shared" marker was byte-identical in two headers 60 lines apart** → one `NotSharedMark`. ⚠️ Collapsed now precisely because it had not drifted yet while the `title` strings beside it already had ("their total is lower than this" vs "it holds fewer hours than this" for the same fact). Restyling one and not the other is how the **Total** silently stops being marked, and the Total is the figure most likely to be read aloud to a client.
  **`settings/page.tsx`: `read()` lost its parameter**, which both callers now filled identically. The `() => true` that opted the Refresh button out of the very guard v1.42.1 added is no longer **representable**, rather than merely discouraged.
  **The CSP route's compare-and-set states its success path ONCE**, via two named helpers (`claimRow`/`replaceRow`) returning "did our write land?". It was `logViolations(violations); break;` twice under two different success tests (`!error` vs `applied?.length`), so any future addition to that path had to be remembered in two places — and the failure mode is the quiet one: the write lands and nothing appears in Vercel's logs, which is the whole reason `logViolations` exists.
  ⚠️⚠️ **AND THE ALTITUDE QUESTION IS ANSWERED IN THE ROUTE, BECAUSE THE NEXT READER WILL WANT TO "HARMONISE" TWO THINGS THAT MUST NOT BE.** `/api/egress` does the same read-merge-write on its own `app_settings` row with a **plain upsert and correctly needs no guard**: every poll pulls a 7-day window, so a dropped sample backfills itself next time. A violation report arrives **once** and is gone. **The asymmetry is whether the data is re-derivable — not the shape of the code** — which is also why this is NOT extracted into a shared `updateJsonSetting` helper for one real caller.
  **Skipped, with reasons rather than argument.** (1) Routing the CAS through `db.ts`'s `updateWithOptional`: genuinely different contract — it exists for *optional columns that may not exist yet*, its flat `match()` cannot express `IS NULL` on a JSON path, and it never `.select()`s, so it cannot report the row count that IS the race signal; using it would silently turn a lost race into a success. (2) Moving `parseInvoiceDay` to `format.ts`: it is domain-shaped and scans prose, its 1–31 range check exists *because* of `nextInvoiceDate`'s month clamping two functions above, and `format.ts`'s parsers are anchored and format-shaped. (3) Folding "not shared" into `hiddenColumns`: those are **opposites** — `hiddenColumns` is censorship (rows stripped server-side by `sanitizeSnapshot`), this marks a column that IS in the editor's preview and ISN'T in the client's copy. v1.20.0 separated the two deliberately. (4) **Efficiency: nothing found, checked rather than assumed** — round trips are unchanged from the pre-CAS code in every uncontended case (1 for a throttled repeat, 2 for a write), `.select("key")` avoids echoing ~20KB of jsonb per report, and `notShared`/`totalNotShared` sit in the component body, not the ~58,000-cell path.
  Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 40 warnings (baseline), 422 tests, production build clean with `/_not-found` still `ƒ`. ⚠️ **The `NotSharedMark` extraction is verified by construction and the compiler — identical JSX and class strings — NOT re-checked in the browser.** Said plainly rather than implied.
- 2026-08-27 — **v1.42.1 — the six findings from the v1.42.0 review, and a CSP sink that can no longer lose a colleague's report.** No migration.
  **The three that mattered, from the first pass:**
  ⚠️ **A REMEMBERED CUT-OFF COULD SHIP A COLUMN THE CLIENT WAS NEVER MEANT TO GET, AND THE TOTAL AGREED WITH IT.** `report-table.tsx` gained a `clientCutoff` prop: any week column ending after the published cut-off is dimmed and labelled **"not shared"**, and — the half that was missing — **so is the TOTAL column**, because the preview's total spans those weeks. Verified on Maccabi with a mid-week cut-off of 2026-08-25: `23 AUG – 29 AUG` and `TOTAL` both flag, `2 AUG – 8 AUG` correctly does not.
  ⚠️ **`parseInvoiceDay` READ PAYMENT TERMS AS A DAY OF THE MONTH.** The field was a free-text note for four years before v1.42.0 gave it meaning, so **"Net 30"** and **"30 days from invoice"** both carried a number in 1–31 and would have silently re-aligned that client's billing periods with nothing in the UI saying a day had been inferred. A value is now a day only when the digits are the WHOLE value ("5", "28") or carry an ordinal suffix ("20th", "1st of the month"); everything else is null, which means a plain calendar month. ⚠️ The suffix test must NOT carry a leading `\b` — in "20th" there is no word boundary between the `0` and the `t`, which is the bug that made v1.41.0's regex read nothing at all from the real data. Seven new payment-term cases pin it.
  ⚠️ **`mergeReports` HANDED BACK A STORE THAT DESCRIBED A STATE THE CALLER DISCARDS.** When nothing earned a write it still returned an incremented `count`, and the route only persists when `changed` — so the returned object was fiction, and the unit tests, which thread the returned store forward, agreed with it while production did not. It now returns `existing` untouched. **The collapsed count is the deliberate trade; a returned store that lies is not.**
  **The three low-severity ones, cleared in the same release:**
  **(1) A false catch-up warning.** The rollover logged "stopped after N periods without reaching today" whenever `rows.length >= MAX_CATCH_UP`, i.e. also when it had reached today on the twelfth period — a warning that fires on success teaches people to ignore it. Now keyed off `rows[rows.length - 1].date_to < todayIso`, which is the actual condition.
  **(2) The CSP viewer's Refresh button bypassed its own unmount guard.** v1.42.0 factored the fetch out so the effect could pass an `alive` check; the button called the same fetch with no guard, so pressing it and navigating away set state on an unmounted component. Both callers now share one `alive` ref.
  ⚠️⚠️ **(3) THE CSP STORE IS ONE JSONB ROW, AND A BARE READ-MODIFY-WRITE LOST A CONCURRENT REPORT ENTIRELY.** Two colleagues loading a page at the same moment, each hitting a different first-time violation, both read the same base and the later write overwrote the earlier — **so a genuinely new dependency vanished while `dropped` stayed 0, i.e. the store reported itself complete.** That is the one thing `dropped` exists to prevent. Replaced with a bounded 3-attempt **compare-and-set** guarded on `value->>updatedAt`: if the guard does not match, somebody wrote in between, so it re-reads and merges onto theirs. ⚠️ `is null` rather than `eq` for a store that has never been written — `->>` on an absent key yields SQL NULL, which `eq` never matches. ⚠️ A plain `insert` for the no-row case rather than an upsert, so losing that race is an ERROR we can see and retry instead of quietly overwriting the winner.
  ⚠️⚠️ **AND THAT GUARD WAS THE RISK OF THE RELEASE, SO IT WAS EXERCISED AGAINST THE REAL DATABASE RATHER THAN ARGUED.** If the `value->>updatedAt` filter never matched, the update would silently apply to zero rows and **every CSP write would stop, with the endpoint still answering 204** — a feature dead in exactly the way it exists to prevent. Two synthetic reports were POSTed to the running app; both took the UPDATE path against the pre-existing row (the real `fonts.gstatic.com` entry) and both landed, so the filter matches. ⚠️ **A 204 proves nothing here** — the route answers 204 on every path by design, so the only honest check is reading the row back. **Both synthetic entries were then removed and the store restored byte-identical to its real single entry** (gstatic, count 2, dropped 0, `updatedAt` 14:30:42.394Z).
  Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 40 warnings (baseline), 422 tests, production build clean with `/_not-found` still `ƒ`.
- 2026-08-27 — **v1.42.0 — the invoice day finally does something, and blocked requests have somewhere to be read.** No migration.
  ⚠️⚠️ **THE INVOICE-DAY FIELD ALREADY EXISTED AND I ADDED A SECOND ONE. DON'T REPEAT THIS.** `clients.invoice_note` (migration **0010**) is edited as **"Invoice day"** on the Client Reports panel, placeholder "e.g. 15th" — it is the field Nitsan meant by "= invoice day", and three clients already use it (Anchor "20th", Baseline "1st", Visitt "20th"). v1.41.0's `invoice_day` int column is therefore **NOT READ** and is left in place with the reason recorded in 0036 rather than churning another migration. **Search for an existing field before adding one** — the giveaway was sitting in the same four-cell grid as the hour cap I had already read.
  **The rule, which is Nitsan's own wording:** blank or unreadable → the new period runs a calendar month from the day after the closed one; filled → same start, end pulled to the day before the next invoice day. `parseInvoiceDay` returns null for anything it cannot read ("end of month", a typo) and null means a calendar month — ⚠️ **never a guessed boundary**, because a period edge nobody chose is worse than no alignment.
  ⚠️ **MY FIRST REGEX READ NOTHING FROM THE REAL DATA:** `\b(\d{1,2})\b` — in "20th" there is no word boundary between the `0` and the `t`, so it failed on EVERY ordinal, which is every value in use. Now the whole digit run, range-checked, which also makes "2026" safe where a 1–2 digit match would have taken "20" out of it. **Test a parser against the values actually in the table.**
  The rollover reads `client.invoiceNote` from the store, so v1.41.0's separate tolerant `invoice_day` query is gone — one less request per visit.
  **CSP viewer** — a card on **Settings → Studio setup**: blocked ORIGIN, rule, count, last seen, plus `dropped` whenever it is non-zero. ⚠️ An empty list says "nothing has been blocked" out loud, because a blank card reads as broken and empty is the expected state.
  ⚠️⚠️ **IT FOUND SOMETHING REAL WITHIN MINUTES, AND THE APP WAS INNOCENT:** `font-src` blocking `fonts.gstatic.com` twice. `next/font/google` self-hosts Rubik and the **entire production build contains zero references to gstatic or googleapis**, so a browser EXTENSION had injected a stylesheet wanting a Google font on our page. A violation an extension causes through an ordinary https origin is indistinguishable from our own code doing it, so the scheme filter cannot catch it. **Before widening the policy for anything in this list, check the app actually asks for it** — `grep -rl <origin> .next-build/static/`. Allowing gstatic would have weakened `font-src` for a font we never load.
  ⚠️ `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` fires on `useEffect(load, [load])` when `load` flips state on entry. The fetch is factored out so the effect only awaits it (with an unmount guard) while the Refresh button keeps the synchronous flip, which is correct in an event handler.
- 2026-08-27 — **v1.41.0 — CSP violations now report themselves, the 404 stops breaking its own policy, billing periods roll over, and the editor can see the current week.** ⚠️ **NEEDS MIGRATION 0036** (`invoice_day` + the unique index) — Nitsan ran it; the rollover is INERT without the index, by design.
  **(1) `/api/csp-report`.** v1.39.0's CSP caught a live dependency nobody knew about — 17 avatars on Everhour's retired CDN — and only because someone had the console open. ⚠️ POST is UNAUTHENTICATED because browsers send reports without credentials, so it is bounded throughout: 16KB body cap before parsing, reports about pages that are not ours dropped, extension noise dropped (the bulk of real traffic), 50 distinct signatures max, repeats throttled to one write per 10 min, always 204 so it cannot be probed. Blocked URLs group by ORIGIN — those 17 avatars would have been ONE finding. ⚠️ The store carries `dropped`: a full cap that silently discarded new signatures would read as "no new violations". ⚠️ BOTH reporting directives are wired and both are needed — `report-to` for Chrome (with the `Reporting-Endpoints` header), the deprecated `report-uri` for Safari and Firefox, and Safari is most of the studio.
  **(2) `src/app/not-found.tsx`.** `/_not-found` was PRERENDERED, so its scripts carried no nonce and `'strict-dynamic'` made the browser refuse them — verified against production, which served one unnonced script tag. ⚠️ Nothing clickable was broken because Next's default 404 had no links, which is exactly why it went unnoticed for two releases. `connection()` opts it out; build now shows `ƒ`.
  **(3) Billing-period rollover.** Nothing ever created these, so once a client's last period ended the Total and week columns stayed correct **while the period breakdown silently dropped the hours**. Opening Client Reports now opens periods up to today for any NON-ARCHIVED client that is behind. ⚠️⚠️ **A DRY RUN AGAINST REAL DATA IS WHY THAT WORD MATTERS: 15 clients were due and TWELVE WERE ARCHIVED**, one dormant since May 2025. Always dry-run a bulk write against production data here.
  ⚠️⚠️ **THE IN-MEMORY GUARD WAS NOT A GUARD, and this cost real data.** The effect ran THREE TIMES against an already-open Client Reports page — saving the file hot-reloads it, which REMOUNTS the component and resets the guard ref, and each run read the same stale `billingPeriods` and inserted. **60 unwanted rows across 20 clients in one second**, backed up to `data/billing-periods-rollover-backup.json` and deleted. It now upserts on `(client_id, date_from)` with `ignoreDuplicates`. **A ref does not survive HMR; only the database can settle this.**
  ⚠️ **AND THE WRITE WAS INVISIBLE UNTIL THE STORE REFRESHED.** Because it writes straight to Supabase (`addBillingPeriod` cannot upsert), the store never learned about the rows and the page kept rendering the OLD latest period — Maccabi's periods were correctly created while its PERIOD column still read "Jan – Jul", which is indistinguishable from the feature not working, and is what Nitsan reported. It calls `refresh()` after any successful write.
  **(4) Two snapshots in the editor.** `preview` runs to TODAY so the current, incomplete week has a column; `publishable` is cut at `through`. ⚠️⚠️ **THE PUBLISH BUTTON MUST WRITE `publishable`** — publishing stores the snapshot VERBATIM and the public page renders that frozen object rather than rebuilding from `through_date`, so handing it `preview` would ship every hour up to today whatever the cut-off said. Columns the client will not get are dimmed and labelled **"not shared"**, because the preview's Total includes them. ⚠️ Extra columns only ever appear at the END (weeks are chronological), which is what keeps `hiddenColumns` indices valid across both snapshots.
  ⚠️ **A week column still requires LOGGED HOURS in that week** — that rule is unchanged. Baseline showed no current-week column because its last entry was 19 Aug, not because of a bug.
  **(5) The weekly plan's month label** is `sticky left-0`, so it stops sliding away while the date column it belongs to stays pinned. Band pre-composited opaque, since a pinned element over a translucent band is what caused that day's four rounds of "holes".
  Verified after the migration: **13 periods across 7 active clients, no archived ones**, and a second page load added nothing (164 → 164, zero duplicate `(client, date_from)` pairs).
- 2026-08-27 — **v1.40.6 — the pinned edges finish: shadow spans the whole table, and the left-edge gap closes.** No migration. Confirmed by Nitsan on localhost before publishing.
  ⚠️⚠️ **AN OVERLAY IN A SCROLLER MUST BE SIZED TO THE CONTENT, NOT THE BOX.** A plain `relative` div inside `overflow-auto` takes the CLIENT width, so the wrapper measured **814px against a 1323px table**: the header's gradient stopped partway across and the missing strip GREW as you scrolled right — Nitsan photographed it at the Freelancers column as "shadow is trimmed here". The wrapper now takes the measured table width, the same way `client-timeline.tsx` sets `width: leftW + chartW`. Verified at maximum scroll (507px): wrapper 1321 = table 1321, gradient's right edge 1px inside the scroller instead of short.
  ⚠️⚠️ **`border-collapse: collapse` RESERVES THE TABLE'S OUTER BORDER, WHICH LEFT A 2px HOLE DOWN THE LEFT EDGE.** Rows started at **x=235 while the scroller's content box starts at 233**, so a 2px strip of the scroller's white `bg-surface` showed beside every row — invisible on a white row, and an obvious gap against a mint today row or a lavender weekend row. Now **`border-separate border-spacing-0`**: rows start at 233, flush, and a hit-test finds a cell at the first pixel inside the border.
  ⚠️ **THE SWITCH IS SAFE FOR A SPECIFIC REASON — check it still holds before adding borders here.** De-duplicating shared edges is all `collapse` was buying us, and every cell in this grid declares `border-b border-r` and NEVER a top or left border, so each shared edge is drawn by exactly ONE cell under either model. **If a cell ever gains `border-t` or `border-l`, that edge will now double to 2px.** Border grid confirmed visually unchanged after the switch.
  ⚠️ Side effect worth knowing: cell `box-shadow` PAINTS again under `border-separate` (collapse suppressed it entirely, which cost four releases — see v1.40.4/.5). **Do not take that as licence to go back to per-cell shadows** — they render as a dashed column of smudges cut by every row border, which is the other half of what Nitsan called "holes". The single overlay gradients stay.
- 2026-08-27 — **v1.40.5 — one gradient per pinned edge, at the timeline's strength.** No migration. Nitsan: *"still there are holes, and im not sure its exact shadow strength like in timeline but its too strong here"*.
  ⚠️⚠️ **THE "HOLES" WERE THE SHADOW ITSELF, AND `client-timeline.tsx` HAD ALREADY WRITTEN DOWN WHY.** Its `shadow.x` layer says: *"ONE shadow for the whole pinned column, not one per row … each row's bottom border cut across it, so what should have been a single soft edge came out as a column of separate smudges with ticks between them."* My v1.40.4 pseudo-elements were per-cell, so they produced exactly that dashed column. **The lesson I should have taken four attempts earlier: when a component in this repo already solves the problem, read ITS code before inventing an approach** — Nitsan had even named the timeline as the reference.
  **Now two overlay gradients**, copied from the timeline: a full-height `sticky` strip at `left: DATE_COL_W` for the pinned column, and a full-width one offset by the measured header height. Both `pointer-events-none` — the grid hit-tests with `elementFromPoint`, and a live 10px strip at the pinned edge would swallow drops into the first person column. Strength is the timeline's own **`rgba(0,0,0,0.075)` at 10px**, replacing the 0.16 I had guessed at, which he correctly called too strong.
  ⚠️ **THE VERTICAL OVERLAY MUST COME BEFORE THE TABLE IN DOM ORDER.** A sticky element cannot rise above its own static position, so placed after the table it stayed unstuck until the very bottom of the grid — measured at **y=1659**, far below the header it was meant to sit under. Before the table it sticks immediately; verified at `relY 38` against a 37px header.
  ⚠️ `headerH` is MEASURED, not a constant — the person cells carry an avatar and a wrapping name, so the header is 37px some weeks and taller others, and a hardcoded offset floats the shadow inside the header or leaves a gap under it.
  The per-cell `.pin-edge-x/.pin-edge-y` rules from v1.40.4 are deleted; the row-opacity fix from that release stands and is the separate, real cause of the see-through pinned cells.
- 2026-08-27 — **v1.40.4 — the real two causes: box-shadow does not paint on collapsed table cells, and `opacity` on a row leaks its sticky cell.** No migration. Nitsan reported the same two symptoms FOUR times; v1.40.0/.2/.3 all missed. Both causes are things a background-colour audit and a computed-style check cannot see.
  ⚠️⚠️ **CAUSE ONE — `box-shadow` ON A CELL OF A `border-collapse: collapse` TABLE DOES NOT PAINT.** Chrome renders nothing. Every earlier "verification" checked that the class was applied and that `getComputedStyle` returned a real box-shadow — both were TRUE the whole time, and both were irrelevant. **The test that finally settled it: a hard, fully opaque `14px 0 0 0 rgba(255,0,0,1)` on 42 sticky cells produced ZERO visible pixels.** ⚠️ Use that trick — paint it opaque red — before tuning any shadow value again. The fix is a positioned pseudo-element (`.pin-edge-x::after`, `.pin-edge-y::before`, globals.css), which is an ordinary box and paints normally. Two DIFFERENT pseudo-elements so the corner cell can carry both edges, which also deleted the old `SHADOW_XY` special case. ⚠️ `pointer-events: none` is required — the grid drag-and-drops and hit-tests with `elementFromPoint`, and a 10px strip would steal drops at the pinned edge. ⚠️ The person `<th>`s needed an explicit `relative`: only the `<thead>` is sticky, so a static cell's pseudo-element would anchor to a distant ancestor.
  ⚠️⚠️ **CAUSE TWO — `opacity-55` ON THE PAST-DAY `<tr>` MADE ITS STICKY DATE CELL SEE-THROUGH.** `opacity` below 1 applies to the element and all descendants AS A GROUP, so no background-color on the cell can survive it: measured **18 of 45 rows at opacity 0.55 while their date cells reported a perfectly opaque `rgb(255,255,255)`**. That is the "holes that show whats underneath", and it is why my two "every cell is alpha 1, the grid is clean" reports were worthless — **I audited only the TODAY row, and today is never a past row.** The fade now applies to the cells' CONTENT (`.plan-row-past > td > *`); backgrounds stay opaque and the row still looks dimmed.
  ⚠️ **THE LESSON, worth more than either fix: if a pinned cell looks see-through, walk every ANCESTOR for `opacity` and `filter` before trusting a computed background — and audit a PAST, a WEEKEND and a HOLIDAY row, not just today.** The check that proves it: for each sticky cell, climb to `document.body` asserting `opacity === '1'` and `filter === 'none'`. It reported **18 leaky cells before, 0 after**.
  Row tints `bg-blue-100/60` and `bg-aqua/10` were pre-composited too (`.plan-row-holiday`, `.plan-row-today`) — a translucent row background is one more surface that cannot hide what passes behind it.
- 2026-08-27 — **v1.40.3 — the pinned shadow was self-cancelling, which is why "no shadows" survived two fixes.** No migration.
  ⚠️⚠️ **THE SHADOW VALUES WERE GEOMETRICALLY INVISIBLE, AND THE CLASS BEING APPLIED PROVED NOTHING.** `6px 0 8px -6px` — a **-6px spread against a 6px offset**. The spread shrinks the shadow rect by exactly the distance the offset moves it, so its hard edge lands ON the cell's own edge and only ~4px of blurred 0.14 alpha escapes, next to a 1px solid cell border. I confirmed the class was present and the computed `box-shadow` non-empty and called it verified TWICE while Nitsan kept reporting "no shadows at all". **He was right both times.** ⚠️ A non-empty computed `box-shadow` is NOT evidence a shadow is visible — check that `offset + spread` leaves clearance outside the edge before believing it.
  **Now `8px 0 12px -4px rgba(6,17,47,0.18)`**: spread smaller than offset leaves 4px clear plus half the blur, so ~10px of falloff. ⚠️ The spread must stay NEGATIVE — at 0 the shadow also spills from each cell's top and bottom, boxing every row instead of drawing one continuous pinned edge. Colour is the app's shadow ink (`rgba(6,17,47,…)`), not pure black, which reads as grime on the brand-tinted surfaces.
  ⚠️ **Still unresolved: the "holes that show whats underneath".** Every sticky cell in the grid was walked and measured **opaque (alpha 1)**, so nothing is see-through by measurement, and Nitsan's screenshot is byte-similar across three reports — which may mean he was never looking at the fixed build. **Do not fix this by guessing again; get the URL and a pointer to the exact pixel first.**
- 2026-08-27 — **v1.40.2 — the pinned shadow he could never have seen, and a column tint that had been dead.** No migration. Nitsan's report: *"no shadows and still some holes there that shows whats underneath"*.
  ⚠️⚠️ **THE SHADOW MECHANISM WAS FINE; THE GATE WAS WRONG.** v1.40.0 gated the pinned column's shadow on `scrollLeft > 0`, copied from `client-timeline.tsx`'s "silent until something is behind it". Measured on the live grid, **the entire horizontal overflow is 189px** — so the studio sits at scrollLeft 0 essentially always and the shadow rendered for nobody. Proved both halves: at `scrollLeft 0` it was `none`, at 189 it was `rgba(0,0,0,0.14) 6px 0 8px -6px`. **`x` now keys off `scrollWidth > clientWidth`** — a column you CAN scroll under is a pocket whether or not you have yet. `y` still keys off `scrollTop > 0`, deliberately, because the day list is hundreds of rows deep. ⚠️ Since `x` is now a MEASUREMENT it must be taken on mount and on resize — a `ResizeObserver` on both the scroller and the table, because adding a person column changes `scrollWidth` without changing the scroller's size.
  ⚠️⚠️ **THE STUDIO COLUMN'S TINT HAD NEVER BEEN APPLYING — found by accident while chasing the holes.** The `<th>` carried `bg-surface`, `bg-brand-soft/60` and `bg-aqua/20` AT ONCE. Tailwind emits those at equal specificity, so the **stylesheet** order wins, not the class-attribute order, and the live header measured plain `rgb(255,255,255)`. Reordering the string would never have fixed it. Each element now gets exactly ONE pre-composited class picked with an if/else (`.plan-head-studio`, `.plan-head-me`, `.plan-cell-studio`, `.plan-cell-me` — same `color-mix` recipe as `.pin-today`). The studio header now measures an opaque `oklab(…)`.
  ⚠️ **`bg-surface` STAYS as the base under the tint.** I first replaced it, which left the sticky header cell fully TRANSPARENT when a tint applied — rows scrolling straight through the header, a worse hole than the one I was fixing. A Tailwind base under ONE unlayered class is deterministic (unlayered wins); two Tailwind backgrounds were the ambiguity.
  ⚠️ **The corner cell needed a COMBINED shadow.** It is the only cell pinned on both axes, and `SHADOW_X SHADOW_Y` together are two equal-specificity `shadow-[…]` utilities — one edge silently lost its shadow. `box-shadow` takes a list, so `SHADOW_XY` carries both; verified the corner now computes both offsets at once.
  **Audited rather than eyeballed:** every sticky cell in the grid was walked and its computed alpha checked — **zero translucent sticky cells** remain. ⚠️ My first pass at that audit was WRONG and nearly cleared a real bug: `rgba(0,0,0,0)` has no alpha component for a `/ x)` regex to match, so a fully TRANSPARENT cell reported as opaque. Parse the channel count, never pattern-match the alpha.
  ⚠️ **Dev-server CSS staleness cost 20 minutes here and will again:** new rules appended to `globals.css` did not reach the browser at all — the served chunk kept its old hash through a reload AND a `touch`. The production build had them correctly. **If a new `globals.css` rule seems not to exist, check the built CSS in the output dir before doubting the rule, then restart the dev server** — that cleared it.
- 2026-08-27 — **v1.40.1 — the preview pane was never broken; the CSP was refusing to be framed.** No migration, and **production headers are byte-identical** — verified against a real `next start`, which still returns `X-Frame-Options: DENY` and `frame-ancestors 'none'`.
  ⚠️⚠️ **THE CAUSE: v1.39.0's security headers block iframing, AND CLAUDE CODE'S PREVIEW PANE IS AN IFRAME.** Nitsan reported "its not responding" twice. The server was fine the whole time — `curl -sI` returned **200 in 27ms** — but the browser refused to paint a frame that `frame-ancestors 'none'` and `X-Frame-Options: DENY` forbid. A blank pane over a healthy server looks identical to a hung server, which is why it survived two wrong diagnoses.
  ⚠️ **I got it wrong twice first, and both wrong answers are recorded above on purpose.** First I blamed my own `npm run build` clobbering the dev server's `.next` and built `NEXT_BUILD_DIR` around it; measuring showed dev serves from `.next/dev` while the build writes `.next/server`, and a build with the server up left `/login` at **0.03s**. Then I blamed the dev-server restart for `proxy.ts`. Also wrong. **The lesson is the order of operations: read the response headers before suspecting the process.** A healthy `curl` plus a blank pane is a browser refusal, not latency.
  **The fix exempts DEV ONLY** — `frame-ancestors` is omitted from the policy when `isDev`, and `next.config.ts` spreads `X-Frame-Options` in only outside development. ⚠️ **BOTH WERE NEEDED: either header alone still blocks framing**, so relaxing the CSP while the config kept an unconditional `DENY` would have looked like a failed fix. Dev is a local origin nobody can reach; production keeps every protection.
  **`policy()` moved out of `src/proxy.ts` into `src/lib/csp.ts`** so it could be tested at all — `proxy.ts` imports `next/server`, which the node-environment vitest setup should not load. New `src/lib/csp.test.ts` (6 tests, **363 → 369**) pins the dev/prod framing split, `'unsafe-eval'` being dev-only, `style-src` keeping `'unsafe-inline'` with no nonce, and — less obvious — that dropping a middle directive leaves **no empty `; ;`**, which browsers reject as malformed and would take the entire policy down with it.
- 2026-08-27 — **v1.40.0 — the phone gets the motion round it was owed, and the weekly plan's pinned column stops being see-through.** No migration. Two pieces: the mobile motion pass Nitsan asked for, and a bug he reported mid-session with a screenshot.
  **(1) THE WEEKLY PLAN'S PINNED CELLS — HIS REPORT: "sticky rows/columns dont have a shadow to show its a pocket also theres a collision".** ⚠️⚠️ **THE COLLISION HAD TWO INDEPENDENT CAUSES, AND FIXING EITHER ALONE LEAVES IT BROKEN.** In his screenshot the date column read "Toda", "Sunda", "Mond" — chips painting over the pinned dates.
  ⚠️ **Cause one: EQUAL z-index.** The chip wrapper is `relative z-10` and the pinned date cell was **also `z-10`** — so DOM order decided it, and the chips come later. Now `z-[15]`: above the chips, still **below** the sticky `thead` at `z-20`, so vertical pinning is unchanged. Verified by hit-test rather than by eye — `elementFromPoint` 80% into the pinned column now returns the date cell, where it previously returned a chip.
  ⚠️⚠️ **Cause two, and the one that would have been missed: THE PINNED CELL WAS TRANSLUCENT.** `bg-aqua/20` on today and `hover:bg-brand-soft/60` composite to **0.2 and 0.6 alpha** — measured `oklab(… / 0.2)` — so chips scrolling underneath showed straight THROUGH the date. Raising the z-index does not help: the cell was already on top, it was simply see-through. **This is the SECOND time this exact trap has cost something here** — v1.10.0 records it on the client table ("a translucent tint REPLACES bg-surface rather than layering over it, so the pinned column went 40% transparent on hover and the calendar showed through the task's own name"). Fixed by PRE-COMPOSITING the tint against the surface with `color-mix` (`.pin-today`, `.pin-hover` in globals.css), giving the same colour at alpha 1. **Change the mix percentage, never the alpha.**
  **The shadows**, copied from `client-timeline.tsx` along with its reasoning: `gridShadow` state gated on scroll position, so **each edge stays silent until something is actually behind it** — a shadow at rest is decoration, a shadow that appears is information. ⚠️ Only a BOUNDARY CROSSING re-renders; this grid runs to ~10 columns × 378 days, so a setState per scroll event would be a re-render per frame while dragging a chip. ⚠️ **Box-shadows, not borders** — the table is `border-collapse: collapse`, which does not paint borders on sticky cells reliably (the same reason `report-table.tsx` uses an inset shadow), and the negative spread confines each to its own edge.
  **Verified on the real grid:** at rest no shadow; scrolled 400px right the pinned column carries `rgba(0,0,0,0.14) 6px 0 8px -6px` and the header row its own; the today cell measures **alpha 1**; and "Today 27/8" reads in full with the pink chip cut cleanly at the pinned edge.
  **(2) THE MOBILE MOTION PASS.** v1.33.0 shipped motion desktop-first by the standing rule and never got its phone round.
  **What already reached a phone, checked rather than assumed:** the **task pane slides** (it renders `w-full` on a phone and is not `fullscreen` by default, so `translateX(100%)` applies); the **notice and write-error banners** carry `.notice-in`; and — the one that matters most — **`.row-flash` already works**, because `logged-day.tsx`, the phone's own entry list, reads `freshEntryId`. That is the only motion here that carries INFORMATION rather than polish (a new entry lands in a date-sorted list, so the flash answers "where did mine go?"), and it needed nothing.
  **What was missing: the two phone-only overlays had no animation at all.** The bottom sheet — the most-used phone surface, since it is how time gets logged — appeared and vanished between two frames.
  ⚠️⚠️ **THE SHEET OWNS ITS OWN EXIT, WHICH `useEnterTransition` EXPLICITLY DOES NOT — and the difference is the point.** That hook's doc warns against exactly what this looks like: the task pane could not own its exit because it derives ~15 values from a `task` that is null the instant it closes, so holding the outgoing value meant reading a ref during render (which took eslint 38 → 111 warnings). **A sheet has no such problem** — its `children` belong to the CALLER, which has not cleared anything yet because `onClose` has not fired. So the animation runs first and the caller unmounts after, with real data on screen throughout. ⚠️ A bottom sheet EARNS an exit where a 110ms popover does not: it covers half a phone screen, and half a screen disappearing between two frames reads as a crash rather than a dismissal.
  ⚠️⚠️ **THE UNMOUNT TIMER IS DELIBERATELY 40ms LONGER THAN THE CSS, AND THE GAP WAS MEASURED, NOT GUESSED.** `setLeaving` and `setTimeout` start in the same tick, but the transition cannot begin until React commits the new transform — a frame or two later — so a timer equal to `SHEET_MS` fires mid-slide. **Caught frame by frame: the sheet was at top 637 of 812 when it disappeared, i.e. it popped out of existence 80% of the way down.** With headroom it now reaches **top 811** before unmounting. Erring long is the safe direction: idle ms on an invisible sheet cost nothing, short is a visible glitch.
  ⚠️ **The drawer gets an ENTER only, and that is a judgement rather than a shortcut:** nearly every dismissal comes WITH a navigation (AppShell closes it on a pathname change), so the page underneath is being replaced at that moment — sliding a menu out over a page that is itself changing reads as two things fighting. The sheet gets a real exit because it closes onto the SAME page.
  **Verified at 375×812 with frame-by-frame sampling:** the sheet travels **13 distinct positions** — 812 → 809 → 798 → 771 → 730 → 690 → 660 → 638 → 613 → 606 → 599 — over ~220ms, backdrop 0 → 1 on the same curve. ⚠️ **The drawer's intermediate frames could NOT be captured** (`requestAnimationFrame` is starved in the preview pane — one sample arrived 4,174ms late), so it rests on its endpoints and properties instead: start `translateX(-100%)` at left −288, settled `transform: none` at left 0, `transitionProperty: transform`, `transitionDuration: 0.2s`, backdrop matching. Stated rather than implied.
  ⚠️ **The reduced-motion guard needed nothing** — it is one `*` block using `!important` specifically to reach inline `transition` values, which is what both of these use. Free by construction, which is why that block was written that way.
  ⚠️ **KNOWN LIMIT: a caller that unmounts the sheet from its OWN state bypasses the exit** and it vanishes instantly. Three call sites pass `onClose` straight through (`mobile-log-time.tsx` ×2, `task-list-row.tsx`), and the dismiss paths that matter — backdrop and Escape — go through the animation. Not worth a callback contract at three sites.
  Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 40 warnings (baseline), 363 tests, production build clean. ⚠️ **Method note: `history.pushState` does not drive Next's router** — five "navigations" reported an identical image count because the page never changed; and the console buffer accumulates across navigations, so stale violations reappear after the fix. Live DOM reads are the trustworthy signal.
- 2026-08-27 — **v1.39.0 — a real Content Security Policy with a per-request nonce, five security headers, and the last Everhour dependency removed.** No migration; one data migration, applied and reversible. Closes the final open item from the v1.32.2 security review ("No CSP or security headers — `next.config.ts` sets only `devIndicators`").
  ⚠️⚠️ **THE FILE IS `src/proxy.ts`, NOT `middleware.ts` — THIS NEXT VERSION RENAMED IT.** Read the docs first as `AGENTS.md` demands; training data is wrong here. This is the app's **first request-level hook**, and it sets headers and nothing else: **auth stays in `(app)/layout.tsx`**, because moving an access decision into a file that runs on every request is a far bigger change than the header it would ride along with.
  ⚠️⚠️ **A NONCE FORCES DYNAMIC RENDERING, AND THAT WAS CHECKED AGAINST THE REAL BUILD BEFORE COMMITTING TO IT: 35 of 39 routes were ALREADY dynamic.** Only `/login`, `/reset`, `/reset/update` and `/_not-found` were prerendered. **The first three would have shipped as dead HTML** — `'strict-dynamic'` makes browsers ignore `'self'`, so a build-time script tag with no nonce is refused, and the login form would have painted and then done nothing: no typing, no submit, **no way into the studio**. Fixed with two tiny server layouts carrying `export const dynamic = "force-dynamic"` (verified `○ → ƒ`). ⚠️ They had to be LAYOUTS because all three pages are `"use client"`, and **route segment config is silently ignored in a client module** — a `dynamic` export added to `page.tsx` would look right, change nothing, and leave the page broken. ⚠️ `/_not-found` is deliberately left static: its HTML still renders, and nothing there needs to be interactive.
  ⚠️⚠️ **STYLES GET `'unsafe-inline'` AND DELIBERATELY NO NONCE, and getting this backwards would have blanked the product.** A nonce in `style-src` does **not** cover inline `style="…"` ATTRIBUTES — those fall under `style-src-attr`, which inherits from it — and adding a nonce makes browsers **ignore `'unsafe-inline'` entirely**. This app positions every Gantt bar, timeline row and chart element that way. **Measured on Anchor's real Timeline: 2,579 inline-styled elements, all with real dimensions**, chart canvas 4228×4385. The nonce-on-styles version would have shown a client an empty plan.
  **`script-src 'self' 'nonce-…' 'strict-dynamic'`**, plus `'unsafe-eval'` **in dev only** (React uses eval to rebuild server stacks; neither React nor Next needs it in production). Safe here because the app loads **no third-party script at all** — no analytics, no tag manager, no CDN. Next stamps its own framework and chunk scripts automatically, so no layout changes were needed: **`/login`, `/reset` and `/reset/update` each serve 24 script tags and 26 nonce attributes, with zero un-nonced script tags in the served HTML.**
  ⚠️ **`img-src` and `connect-src` must name Supabase, and the reason is v1.35.0:** the PUBLIC report and Gantt pages render a client's own mark from a **server-signed `/object/sign/` URL**, which is on Supabase's origin, not ours. Verified on Anchor's real shared link — the one image loads from `hjrhfifbmxduwacjzqdt.supabase.co`. Without that entry **every client would see a broken mark on their own report**. `wss:` is included for realtime: unused today, but the client library opens the socket and a blocked one logs errors on every page.
  ⚠️ **`frame-ancestors 'none'`** because the client report is a page strangers open, and framing it is how a convincing fake gets built. No iframes exist anywhere in `src`, so nothing legitimate breaks.
  **Five headers in `next.config.ts` rather than the proxy**, deliberately: `headers()` covers **static assets and API routes too**, which the proxy's matcher skips, and costs no invocation. `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` (this is what makes the v1.0.1 upload allow-list a real defence — sniffing is how a forced-safe type gets run as HTML anyway), `X-Frame-Options: DENY` (belt to `frame-ancestors` for older browsers — **if the app ever needs embedding, BOTH change**), `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin` (⚠️ **report and intake URLs carry an unguessable token IN THE PATH** — leaking that in a `Referer` to a client's own outbound link would hand over the whole report), `Permissions-Policy` denying camera/mic/geolocation/payment/usb, and **HSTS 2 years, includeSubDomains, preload**. Plus `poweredByHeader: false`.
  ⚠️⚠️ **THE CSP IMMEDIATELY FOUND A REAL DEPENDENCY NOBODY KNEW WAS LOAD-BEARING: 17 profile avatars were still served from `d36887svjhykt4.cloudfront.net` — EVERHOUR's CDN.** Imported with the original data and never rewritten; Everhour was retired 2026-07-29 (v0.99.35) and the studio stopped paying for it, so 17 colleagues' faces lived entirely at a former vendor's discretion. **15 archived, 2 ACTIVE — `Office` and `Itay Biran`, an admin.** They still return 200, so this was a live dependency, not dead data.

Working days average **281 MB**, weekends **124 MB**, and the shape matters enormously — **Sat 22 Aug was 90 requests / 3 MB against Tue 25 Aug's 13,403 / 417 MB.**

Fri 21 Aug is the clean control: **a day the studio does not work, three releases logged, 245 MB.** So a large share of today's 236 MB/day is the building, not the studio, and routine-only steady state is plausibly 100–150 MB/day, i.e. **3–4.5 GB per cycle, which would fit.**
  **(1) THE FORECAST — `projectCycle` + `combinedLevel` in `egress.ts`.** The banner now reads *"On this rate the cycle lands near 14.0 GB by 2026-09-04 — about 280% of the allowance. 285 MB a working day, 124 MB at the weekend, from 7 days measured."*
  ⚠️ **WORKING DAYS AND WEEKENDS ARE PROJECTED SEPARATELY, because a flat mean is wrong in whichever direction the sample leans** — 3 MB against 417 MB is not noise around an average. ⚠️ **Only COMPLETE days set the rate**: today held 1,897 requests at the time of writing, and extrapolating from a partial day reads as a collapse in the morning and a crisis by evening. Today is then counted as a WHOLE day at the going rate, with its already-counted partial subtracted, so the projection neither double-counts nor stops short. ⚠️ **With no weekend sampled yet the weekday rate is used for weekend days too** — that OVER-estimates, which is the safe direction for an alert.
  ⚠️ **THE FORECAST MAY RAISE THE LEVEL BUT NEVER SOFTEN IT, AND NEVER REACHES `critical`.** A claim about days that have not happened is allowed to say "this is heading somewhere bad", not to declare the limit already gone — and `stale` still outranks both. ⚠️ It needs **5 complete days** before it may raise a banner at all (3 before it is even computed): two days projected over a month is noise, and a banner that cries on day two is one nobody reads on day twenty. **This is the half that makes the feature worth having** — the actual-usage figure can only ever report a limit already crossed, and on these numbers the cycle was always going to blow the quota with nothing saying so until it had.
  **(2) DEVELOPMENT POLLS TEN TIMES SLOWER — 60s → 10 min.** ⚠️ **An egress fix, not a convenience**, for the 72 MB/hour above.

A local stack is out too: **no Docker on this machine.** So upgrading would be a prerequisite for the change meant to help avoid upgrading, and the traffic is cut where it is generated instead. ⚠️ **If the studio does go Pro, do the real split** — a third project also stops verification sessions touching live client data, which is worth more than the bytes.

Because every poll pulls a 7-day window, **any gap shorter than a week backfills itself** the next time an admin opens the app. ⚠️ Deliberately no cron: the Vercel account is Hobby (daily only, 2 slots) and somebody opens this app every working day; a quiet fortnight shows as `stale` rather than as a wrong number. - 2026-08-27 — **v1.36.0 — a render error costs a panel instead of the whole app, and a time entry can no longer be absurd.** ⚠️ **MIGRATION `0035_time_entry_minutes_bound.sql` — pasted to Nitsan, not yet applied when this shipped.** The two robustness items the v1.32.2 security review named and left.
  ⚠️⚠️ **(1) THERE WAS NO `error.tsx` ANYWHERE IN `src/app`, AND THAT IS THE MULTIPLIER THAT TURNED A TYPO INTO AN OUTAGE.** v1.32.2's finding, restated because it is the whole justification: a designer typed `33` into a task's Figma field, React 19 refuses a `javascript:`-shaped href by **throwing**, and with no boundary the throw unmounted the tree — My Tasks and that client's page went blank **for everyone**, persistently, until somebody edited the row in SQL. One member, one field, one row.
  **Five boundaries, in two registers.** `(app)/error.tsx` for the studio, `global-error.tsx` as the last resort, and one each on the three pages strangers open (`/report/[token]`, `/gantt/[token]`, `/intake/[token]`) sharing a single `PublicError` component.
  ⚠️ **`unstable_retry`, NOT `reset` — this Next version renamed it, and training data is wrong.** Read the docs first (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/.../file-conventions/error.md`) exactly as `AGENTS.md` instructs. They are not interchangeable: `unstable_retry()` re-fetches and re-renders the segment, while `reset()` only clears the error state and re-renders the same data — which for a render-time bug throws again immediately.
  ⚠️ **`error.tsx` wraps the PAGES, not the layout above it, and here that is the good outcome:** `AppLayout` renders `AppShell`, which owns the store, the sidebar and the header, so all of it survives and the person can navigate away. **Verified by deliberately throwing** from a temporary `(app)/boom` route: the panel rendered with Try again, and the sidebar, header and search were all still there. Route deleted afterwards. A throw inside the SHELL escapes to `global-error.tsx`, which must bring its own `<html>`, `<body>` and `globals.css` because it REPLACES the root layout.
  ⚠️ **`global-error.tsx` offers only a reload, deliberately** — at that level the failure is the shell itself, so re-rendering the same children very likely throws again and traps somebody in a loop.
  ⚠️ **The client-facing fallback says NOTHING technical**, and shows the `digest` framed as something to quote. A stack trace in front of a client is worse than useless — they cannot act on it, and a server message can carry detail we would never choose to show (which is why Next replaces it with a digest in production). No retry button there either: that data is a frozen snapshot or a token-gated fetch, so a button that visibly does nothing reads as a second failure.
  **(2) `time_entries.minutes` HAD NO BOUND — not in the schema, not in RLS.** A member may write their own rows (`own time *`, 0001), so a negative or absurd figure could be posted straight to the API and flow into client reports, the KPI tiles and every per-person total. `LogTimeForm` already refuses `<= 0`, so this guards the path that does not go through the form — the one that matters.
  ⚠️⚠️ **`NOT VALID` IS LOAD-BEARING: WITHOUT IT THE MIGRATION CANNOT APPLY.** Measured before writing it — **454 existing rows violate the rule and every one legitimately**: 82 with NEGATIVE minutes (the `מפתח` hours REDUCTIONS recovered from Asana comments, v0.99.31 — real ledger lines) and 372 over 24h (recovered lumps and the v0.99.34 estimated-date spread; the largest is **333h on one 2021 date**), of which 20 are non-legacy Everhour cut-over backfills dated 2022-11-29. `NOT VALID` enforces on every INSERT/UPDATE from now on and leaves that history alone — the standard Postgres idiom for this, not a dodge.
  ⚠️ **Legacy rows are exempt for a live reason, not only a historical one:** the recovery scripts still insert legacy rows, and the roll-up **reversal SQL written yesterday re-inserts 31 of them**. A bound without that escape would block our own undo. To tighten later: clean the 20 non-legacy outliers, then `validate constraint`.
  Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 39 warnings (baseline), 326 tests, production build clean. ⚠️ **No unit tests here** — five of the six files are React error components outside the node-environment suite, and the sixth is a SQL constraint; the boundary was verified by making it fire instead.
  ⚠️ **I deleted `.next` WHILE THE DEV SERVER WAS RUNNING for the second time in two days** — the exact trap this log recorded on 2026-08-26 — and the server started 500ing. Same fix: stop the preview server, delete, start it again. **Stop the server first.**
- 2026-08-26 — **data — the roll-up double-count is measured and is ~197h, not ~14,900h. The open correctness issue from 2026-07-29 is CLOSED.** No app change, no migration, no deploy — one new read-only script and this correction.
  ⚠️⚠️ **THE OLD FIGURE WAS AN UPPER BOUND ON IGNORANCE, NOT AN ERROR, AND IT HAS BEEN READ AS AN ERROR EVER SINCE.** `report-rollup-risk.mjs` answers *"do we know this task's subtask shape?"* — and it now reports **0h at risk**, because `parents.json` has been extended from the 23 legacy projects to **2,758 tasks**, so the shape is known everywhere. But knowing the shape and having ACTED on it are different facts, and only the second one is about the numbers the studio bills from. **The risk report cannot tell them apart.** So the first move was not to fetch anything from Asana — it was to ask the database the question that actually matters.
  **New `scripts/report-rollup-applied.mjs` (READ ONLY)** answers it: which tasks (a) carry recovered hours, (b) are parents with `subs > 0`, and (c) have at least one CHILD in the database that ALSO carries recovered hours. Both halves must be true — a parent whose children were never imported is the only surviving record of those hours and must be left alone.

  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Volta | 221.8h | 38.8h | 260.5h | 200.5h | **over by 60h** |
  | Fiverr | 101.8h | 76.8h | 178.5h | 71.5h (unparsed) | **over by ~107h** |
  | Volta — `Q&A Movies - 128.25h` | 18.3h | 107.0h | 125.3h | 128.3h | **under by 3h — CORRECT** |
  | Volta | 16.8h | 13.3h | 30.0h | 16.8h | over by 13.3h |
  | Donsplus | 14.3h | 6.8h | 21.0h | 14.3h | over by 6.8h |
  | Studio | 6.0h | 9.5h | 15.5h | 6.0h | over by 9.5h |
