Under by 3h, not double-counted.
  ⚠️ **NO ASANA TOKEN WAS NEEDED, and the documented "FIX" would have been wasted work.** The old note prescribed re-running `fetch-asana-stories.mjs` over the 965 gids in `data/rollup-refetch-gids.csv` — **that CSV now contains 0 rows**, because there is nothing left whose shape is unknown. A token was the first thing I asked for and the last thing this needed.
  ⚠️ **NOT FIXED, AND DELIBERATELY LEFT WITH NITSAN — these are hours a client was billed from.** Three of the five (Website Wireframes, Donsplus, Studio) share one shape: **the parent carries EXACTLY its title figure and the children add more on top**. Applying "children win, parent zero" mechanically would zero the parent and leave only the children — which for Website Wireframes means reporting **13.3h where the title says 16.75h**, i.e. trading an overstatement for an understatement. The other two (Volta 60h, Fiverr ~107h) have a parent that exceeds its OWN title before any child is counted, so no rule derives the right answer — somebody has to decide which hours are real. Same posture as v1.27.1's summary-tile finding: a change to billed hours is his call, not a unilateral one. **All six are in the table above** — and note that `data/rollup-applied.csv` is NOT in the repo: `/data` has been gitignored since line 44 of `.gitignore`, so the 706 files tracked under it predate that rule and anything new is local-only. Re-generate it with the script whenever it is needed.
  ⚠️ **And the Fiverr row needs a parser note:** `parseLegacyName` returns no `actual` for `"Illustration animation (50h+6h+8h+2h+5h) - 71.5h - done"` — the `+`-separated list defeats it — so its 71.5h was read by eye and its "~107h over" is the one figure in the table not produced by code. Worth a test case if that shape recurs.
  ⚠️⚠️ **NITSAN'S RULING, 2026-08-26 — DECIDED, NOT OUTSTANDING. DO NOT RE-OPEN THIS.** *"donsplus include children too in addition to parent, other take parent title only. dont erase numbers anyway from anywhere."* So:
  | task | the authoritative figure | status |
  |---|---|---|
  | **Donsplus** `3 small tasks (18-20) - 14.25h` | **parent + children = 21.0h** | correct as it stands |
  | Volta `Full Website Design … 200.5h` | **the parent's title, 200.5h** | children not added on top |
  | Fiverr `Illustration animation … 71.5h` | **the parent's title, 71.5h** | ditto |
  | Volta `Website Wireframes (32-42) - 16.75h` | **16.75h** | ditto |
  | Studio `לעדכן אתר קיים - 6HRS` | **6h** | ditto |
  | Volta `Q&A Movies - 128.25h` | 128.25h | already correct (parent holds the remainder) |
  ⚠️ **AND NOTHING IS DELETED — that is the other half of his instruction, and it is the half a later session is most likely to "tidy".** No hours were removed from any task, no child row was zeroed, no `legacy_hours` was reduced. The child tasks are real records of work and they stay exactly as they are; the ruling is about which figure to READ as the total, not about rewriting history. **Both scripts involved are read-only and no write was made.**
  ⚠️ **THE CONSEQUENCE, STATED PLAINLY SO IT IS NEVER REDISCOVERED AS A BUG:** because nothing was erased, a naive sum over tasks still counts ~190h twice on four clients (Volta ~73h, Fiverr ~107h, Studio ~9.5h, Volta wireframes ~13.3h). **That is the accepted position, exactly as v1.0.2 accepted that hiding a report row does not change its totals.** If a future report shows those clients a little high, this is why — it is a decision, not a defect. Anyone tempted to "fix" it by zeroing the children is undoing an explicit instruction.
  **Scale, for context:** ~197h against **23,792h** of Asana-recovered history is **0.8%**, on hours recovered from ten-year-old task titles. The finance cross-check (2026-07-29) put the studio-wide overage at ~2,700h, so roll-up was never the bulk of it.
- 2026-08-26 — **v1.35.0 — `avatars` goes private. All three buckets are closed, and the v1.0.1 security review is finally finished.** No migration. The hard one, because this bucket renders where there is no session.
  ⚠️⚠️ **THE PROXY CANNOT SERVE THE PUBLIC PAGES, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE PROBLEM THIS RELEASE SOLVES.** `/api/file` checks `auth.getUser()` — correct for the app, fatal for `/report/[token]` and `/gantt/[token]`, whose reader is a CLIENT with no account. Adding `avatars` to the allow-list and stopping there would have replaced **every client's own mark on their own report with a broken image**. So there are now TWO mechanisms: the session-checked proxy for the app, and **`signStorageUrl` (new, `src/lib/sign-storage.ts`) signing SERVER-SIDE** on those two pages, which pass an absolute signed URL down as a prop.
  ⚠️ **WHAT LETS ONE COMPONENT SERVE BOTH: a signed URL is `/object/sign/`, not `/object/public/`, so `proxyStorageUrl` does not recognise it and hands it back untouched.** `ClientAvatar` is therefore unchanged in shape — it proxies inside the app and passes the pre-signed URL through on a public page. **There is a test pinning exactly that**, because if it ever stopped being true the failure is silent and lands on the client's own report.
  ⚠️ **1 HOUR on the server signer, against 60s on the route.** Both pages are `force-dynamic` so a reader gets a fresh URL every load — but a client may leave a report open, and an expired mark is a broken image on a page we cannot ask them to refresh. Returns null on any failure, which renders the glyph or the initial: the same fallback as a client who never uploaded a mark. ⚠️ **SERVER ONLY** — it holds the service key and must never be imported into a client component.
  ⚠️⚠️ **I PATCHED ONE `<img>` OF FOUR AND THE MEASUREMENT CAUGHT IT.** `MemberPhoto` draws the same url in FOUR branches (avatar, the two bleed layers for the head-breaking-the-circle portrait, plain); I patched the first and `/team` still served **8 raw public URLs**. Fixed at the single place `url` is COMPUTED, not at the four places it is used — patching call sites one at a time is precisely how three got missed. A local fallback asset passes through untouched.
  ⚠️ **A FOURTH RENDER SITE EXISTS AND MUST NOT BE PROXIED:** `public-gantt-view.tsx` has its own `<img>` rather than using `ClientAvatar`, and it receives the already-signed URL. Leave it alone.
  **326 tests** (was 325). The allow-list refusal test no longer names a real bucket — all three are listed now — so it guards the SHAPE instead (an unknown name is refused), which is what actually stops the route signing an arbitrary bucket for anyone with a session.
  **Verified in the browser, every surface, before and after the flip.** Public: Visitt's report and Anchor's shared Gantt both serve a **SIGNED** url that loads at 256px. In-app: `/team` **8 PROXIED, 0 raw, 0 broken**; `/clients` **15 PROXIED, 0 raw, 0 broken**; `/settings` clean. After flipping: a raw stored avatar URL with no auth returns **400** where it returned 200.
  **ALL THREE BUCKETS ARE NOW PRIVATE** — `intake` (v1.34.0), `task-files` (v1.34.1), `avatars` (here). The last open item from the v1.0.1 review, dated 2026-07-30, is closed.
  ⚠️ **Two things worth knowing for whoever comes next.** (1) The stored rows STILL hold absolute public URLs and are rewritten on the way out — nothing was migrated, so any bucket can be flipped back with one command and no restore. A backfill to bare paths is the tidier end state and is still not done. (2) The Gantt's **empty state** (`rows.length === 0`) draws only the client's initial and never the mark — pre-existing, not introduced here, and the reason Visitt's Gantt could not be used to test this.
  Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 39 warnings (baseline), 326 tests, production build clean.
- 2026-08-26 — **v1.34.1 — `task-files` goes private too. Two buckets down, one to go.** No migration. The second half of v1.34.0, same mechanism, no new machinery.
  **Task attachments are now signed like intake files.** `task-files` joins `PROXIED_BUCKETS`, and its ONE render site — the task pane's attachment list — goes through `proxyStorageUrl`. Small surface: **5 rows in `attachments`, 5 objects in the bucket**, all holding a public URL.
  ⚠️ **`attachments.file_path` IS MISNAMED — it stores an absolute PUBLIC URL, not a path** (see `/api/task-attachments`, which writes `getPublicUrl().publicUrl` into it). Worth knowing before anyone "fixes" a caller that treats it as a URL, because it is one.
  ⚠️ **THE ALLOW-LIST TEST FLIPPED, AND THAT IS THE POINT OF HAVING IT.** v1.34.0 asserted `task-files` was REFUSED; that assertion is now the opposite, and `avatars` took its place as the live case — it is still public, so it must not be signable through this route. Two tests were rewritten rather than deleted: one proving `task-files` is accepted, one proving `avatars`, an unknown bucket and an empty string are all refused. **325 tests** (was 323).
  **Deploy order again: code first, bucket second.** Signing works on a public bucket, so the pane keeps working across the whole window; flipping afterwards only kills the raw URLs.
  **NOT done: `avatars` is still public**, and it is the awkward one — `ClientAvatar` renders on the PUBLIC client report and the shared Gantt, where there is no session to check, so those have to be signed in the server component rather than proxied through `/api/file`. Doing it the lazy way (adding it to the allow-list) would break every client's report header.
- 2026-08-26 — **v1.34.0 — client brief attachments stop being permanent public URLs. `intake` is a PRIVATE bucket.** No migration. This is the last unfixed item from the v1.0.1 security review, open since 2026-07-30.
  ⚠️⚠️ **THE EXPOSURE, STATED PLAINLY: all three storage buckets were created `public = true`, so `getPublicUrl` mints a URL anybody holding it can open FOR EVER — no session, no expiry — and the app has been writing those absolute URLs into the database since the beginning.** Measured before the change: **42 rows** hold one (`clients.icon_url` 15, `links.url` 9, `profiles.photo_url` 4, `profiles.avatar_url` 3) plus **11 of 11 uploaded intake files** inside `task_requests.answers`. A client sends a document with their brief and its URL is world-readable on our own domain until the object is deleted.
  **`intake` FIRST, deliberately — the other two follow.** It is the bucket holding other people's documents rather than our own marks and staff photos, so it is the one where the exposure is a client's material rather than ours. `avatars` and `task-files` are next and are a bigger surface (avatars render on the PUBLIC client report, so that one needs signing in a server component).
  ⚠️ **A PROXY ROUTE, NOT STORED SIGNED URLS, and the app's own shape is the reason.** A signed URL expires within the hour while this app keeps a tab open all day — so a signed URL written into state at boot is a broken image by the afternoon. New **`GET /api/file?b=…&p=…`** checks the session, signs for **60 seconds**, and **307s** to it; `proxyStorageUrl` rewrites a stored public URL to that route at RENDER time, so every view asks for a fresh one and nothing time-bombed goes in the database.
  ⚠️ **It REDIRECTS rather than streaming the bytes** — streaming would put every attachment through a serverless function, paying for the bandwidth twice and capping a 30MB file on the function's own limits. The signed URL becomes visible to someone already authorised to read it, which is not a leak, and 60s keeps the window small. `Cache-Control: no-store`, or a cached 307 hands a browser a dead link.
  ⚠️ **NO DATABASE MIGRATION, AND THAT IS WHAT MAKES IT REVERSIBLE.** The rows keep their absolute public URLs and are rewritten on the way out, so a bucket flipped private and back is exactly where it started. A backfill to bare paths is the tidier end state and can come once every render site is known to go through here.
  ⚠️⚠️ **DEPLOY ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING: the code ships FIRST, the bucket flips SECOND.** Signing works on a public bucket too, so once deployed every intake link already goes through the proxy and keeps working; flipping the bucket then only breaks the RAW urls. In the other order every attachment 400s until the deploy lands. `scripts/set-bucket-private.mjs` (dry-run by default) carries that warning and refuses nothing else.
  ⚠️ **`proxyStorageUrl` returns anything it does not recognise UNCHANGED**, which is what makes it safe at a render site that mixes storage objects with arbitrary client-supplied links — `links.url` and the intake queue both do exactly that. A bucket not in `PROXIED_BUCKETS` is left alone, so the two still-public buckets are untouched by this release. **The allow-list is checked again in the route**: the query string is user input, and without that second check the route would sign an object in ANY bucket for anyone with a session.
  **323 tests** (was 311). Twelve new, and the two that matter most are the negatives: a client's own typed link, a `data:`/`mailto:` and a URL for a still-public bucket all come back byte-identical, and `parseProxyRequest` refuses a non-allow-listed bucket, `..` traversal and an absolute key. Also pinned: the stored percent-encoding is decoded exactly once, because signing a still-encoded key looks up an object that does not exist and renders as a broken image with a 404 that explains nothing.
  **Verified end to end in the browser, signed in, on a real client brief** (No Traffic, "Partner Event 2026 | Big Banner", 3 attachments): all three hrefs are `/api/file?b=intake&p=…`, **zero raw `/object/public/` links left on the page**, and following one gives **307 → a `token=` signed URL → 200 `image/png`, 1086px wide**. The refusals were exercised too: **401 with no session**, and 404 for `b=avatars` (not allow-listed), for `p=../avatars/x.png`, and for an object that does not exist — one message for every refusal, so the endpoint cannot be used to test whether a file exists.
  ⚠️ **The client's own intake FORM was checked and needs nothing:** uploads go through signed upload URLs (v1.19.3) and the form never reads a file back — the only URLs it renders are the links the client typed. So no client-facing surface breaks.
  Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 39 warnings (baseline), 323 tests, production build clean.
  **NOT done, and named so it is not mistaken for finished:** `avatars` and `task-files` are STILL PUBLIC. `task-files` is the smaller job (task attachments render only for signed-in members); `avatars` needs care because `ClientAvatar` renders on the public client report, so those must be signed server-side in the page rather than proxied through a session-checked route.
- 2026-08-26 — **v1.33.1 — "days left" stops being the one live number on a frozen report.** No migration (0034 was applied by Nitsan between releases). Caught by him reading the scoped Anchor report: *"days left and active tasks needs to be for end of last week - when the report is scoped to - its not a live updated numbers."*
  ⚠️⚠️ **HE IS RIGHT, AND IT WAS A BUG I INTRODUCED BY HALVES IN v1.33.0.** `daysLeftIn` measured from `new Date()`, so on a report where every other figure comes from a frozen snapshot this one kept counting down after publishing: Anchor, scoped through 22 Aug against an August period, read **9** when published and would have read **5** by today — moving, against hours that had not changed. It now counts from the report's own as-of day: `report_links.through_date`, falling back to the publish DAY for links that predate that column (the honest as-of for them, and still never the clock).
  ⚠️ **`active tasks` needed NO change, and the reason is worth recording so nobody re-checks it:** it comes from `periodActiveTasks`, computed server-side over the snapshot, whose entries are already cut off at the same day. It was never live. Only the countdown was.
  **Renamed to "days left to period"**, his wording.
  ⚠️ **Extracted to `daysLeftInPeriod` in `period-math.ts` rather than fixed in place**, because a pure date function inside a 460-line component is outside the vitest suite — which is exactly how it shipped measuring from the clock. **311 tests** (was 306); five new, including the real Anchor case, "0 on the last day", "0 rather than a negative for a period already past", and one that crosses Israel's 25 Oct clocks change (it is built on `daysBetween`, never ms arithmetic — the trap v1.23.0 deleted). ⚠️ `asOf` is a REQUIRED argument on the helper, deliberately: a default of `new Date()` is the bug.
  **Verified on the live client link:** `54.5h this period · 9 days left to period · 4 active tasks`. The 9 is the proof — today is the 26th, so a live count would read 5.
  Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 39 warnings (baseline), 311 tests, production build clean.
  ⚠️ **Self-inflicted mishap worth remembering: I ran `rm -rf .next` WHILE THE DEV SERVER WAS RUNNING**, which pulled Turbopack's SST cache files out from under it — the server then paniced repeatedly ("Unable to open static sorted file … No such file or directory") and served nothing, which looks exactly like a hung machine. **Stop the preview server before deleting `.next`**, then start it again.
- 2026-08-26 — **v1.33.0 — a report can be scoped to a day, and the app gets its first motion.** ⚠️ **MIGRATION `0034_report_link_through_date.sql` — pasted to Nitsan, NOT YET APPLIED when this shipped** (it degrades safely, see below). Two features in one release.
  **(1) HOURS THROUGH ‹date› — the cut-off, and the bug that made it necessary.** Nitsan published Anchor's weekly report on the Sunday AFTERNOON, by which time colleagues had already logged into the new week — so a summary of the week that ended carried **8h of the week that had just started**. His ask: *"got a solution for that? to set whats included or till what day the link will scope?"*
  ⚠️⚠️ **HIDING THE COLUMN IS NOT A SOLUTION, AND WE PROVED IT ON THE LIVE LINK BEFORE BUILDING THIS.** He first chose "hide the column only, no republish" (`hidden_columns = ["w:2"]`, which is applied at REQUEST time so it needs no republish and leaves the stamp alone). It removed the column — and left the 8h inside every total, because `totalMinutes` deliberately spans hidden columns (v1.0.2: hiding is a focus tool, not confidentiality). **Measured on the client's own page: the header read `62.5h this period` while the table's PERIOD column read `54.5h` and TOTAL read `62.5h`.** A page that contradicts itself by 8h, on the document the studio invoices against. He then chose the real fix. **Hiding is a focus tool; scoping is arithmetic, and only arithmetic can do it.**
  ⚠️ **THE CUT-OFF IS APPLIED IN EXACTLY ONE PLACE — the `clientEntries` filter in `buildReportSnapshot` — AND THAT IS THE WHOLE DESIGN.** Every figure in a report descends from that list: the week columns (`buildWeeks` reads it), each row's Total, the per-period totals, and the `periodTotals` the client's header figure is built from. Filter once and they all agree; filter anywhere downstream and you rebuild the bug above. Stored `custom_weeks` columns beginning after the cut-off are dropped too, or a hand-edited list leaves an empty trailing column.
  ⚠️ **IT DEFAULTS TO THE END OF THE LAST COMPLETE WEEK** (`shiftDays(startOfWeek(now), -1)` — a Saturday), so a normal Sunday-morning publish is unchanged and a late one is right by default. A plain DATE rather than a "skip the current week" switch, so a client billed to the 20th can be cut there too. Blank means everything, i.e. the old behaviour. ⚠️ **The PREVIEW is built with it**, so what the studio checks is what publishes — the cut-off can never be a publish-time surprise. ⚠️ It sits beside **Publish**, not among the filter pills: those change what the STUDIO sees, this changes the hours a client is billed against.
  ⚠️ **`through_date` IS A RECORD, NOT A FILTER.** The scoping is baked into the frozen `snapshot`, so nothing at read time consults the column and the public page is unaffected by whether 0034 has run. The publish WRITE puts it in `updateWithOptional`'s optional bucket beside `view_flags` — a missing column on a write is **PGRST204** and fails the whole update, which would have broken the one button that page exists for. **Verified in that exact state: Anchor was republished correctly with 0034 unapplied** — scoped, simply not recorded. ⚠️ And the read proved the sibling trap the hard way: naming `through_date` in a `select` while the column was absent **failed the entire query** (the app itself reads `report_links` with `*`, so it is safe).
  **306 tests** (was 300). Six for the cut-off, and each asserts a DIFFERENT figure moves — the row total, the week COLUMN disappearing, the period total and week cells agreeing, inclusivity of the cut-off day itself, a stored column past it being dropped, and no cut-off changing nothing. ⚠️ **Proven not vacuous: reverting the one-line filter fails 4 of them.**
  **ANCHOR REPUBLISHED, verified on the real client URL:** columns `9 Aug – 15 Aug` 35.5h + `16 Aug – 22 Aug` 19h, TOTAL **54.5h**, PERIOD **54.5h**, header **54.5h this period** — all four agree, and the 23–29 Aug column is absent. `hidden_columns` was cleared first: ⚠️ **it is POSITIONAL and survives publishing**, so `w:2` would have gone on hiding the 23–29 Aug week in NEXT week's link too. Stamp moved to **26 Aug** — the accepted cost of a correct republish. ⚠️ **Incidental find: there are TWO clients named "Anchor"** — the live one (127 tasks) and an archived duplicate (27 tasks, no links). Untouched, but worth a merge decision like In-reach/Quadream.
  **(2) MOTION — four behaviours, each a variant Nitsan chose from a throwaway `/motion-preview` route.** Built as a real route rather than a mockup (the `/chip-preview` device from v1.5.1) because motion cannot be judged from prose; he picked **1C, 2B, 3A, 4 yes, 5C** and the route was **deleted** in this release, as it was public by construction (outside the auth group).
  **1C — the task pane slides**, 260ms `cubic-bezier(0.4,0,0.2,1)`, backdrop fading on the same curve. ⚠️⚠️ **THE EXIT IS OWNED BY `closePane`, NOT BY A HOOK, and my first attempt was wrong in a way worth recording.** To animate out, the pane must keep drawing the task it was showing — `openTaskId` goes null immediately. I held the task in a REF and read it during render: **eslint went 38 → 111 warnings, 72 of them cascading from that one ref**, and the React Compiler is right to refuse it. The fix is to delay clearing the STORE instead (`closing` state + a timer), so the data stays real and nothing is duplicated. `useEnterTransition` now owns only the enter, which must cross a paint (`requestAnimationFrame`, not `setTimeout(0)`). ⚠️ Switching straight from one task to another does NOT animate — that is the common case. ⚠️ `PANE_MS` is one constant: a JS timer shorter than the CSS yanks the pane mid-slide, longer leaves it mounted and invisible, swallowing clicks. **Measured on close: 849 → 1200 → 1400px with the backdrop at 1 → 0.39 → 0.04, then unmounted.**
  **2B — popovers fade with a 2px rise**, 110ms, on **11 click-opened panels** (period selector, Show and Columns menus, context menus, autocomplete, add-time, the pane's own popover, the timeline's date popout). ⚠️ Enter-only by design: they are mounted conditionally, and an exit would mean a state machine at eleven call sites. ⚠️ **Pointer-following tooltips are deliberately excluded** (HoverTip, InfoDot, the chart and cell-selection bubbles) — animating something that tracks the cursor reads as lag.
  **3A — the row you just created flashes** brand-soft → transparent over 400ms. ⚠️ **Not decoration:** a time entry lands in a DATE-SORTED list, so this answers "which one is mine, and where did it go?". Implemented as **`freshEntryId` in the store**, cleared after 600ms, rather than callbacks threaded through six `LogTimeForm` hosts — so any list that renders entries can flash without being wired individually. Read by the pane's time list and `logged-day.tsx`.
  **4 — notices and the write-error banner** fade and rise 6px over 160ms: further and slower than a popover, because they arrive unbidden rather than in answer to a click.
  **5C — a timeline bar settles** into place over 200ms after a drag commits. ⚠️⚠️ **ARMED ONLY ON A REAL COMMIT, AND THE NARROWNESS IS THE DESIGN.** The bars are positioned with `left`/`width`, which ARE layout properties, so a standing transition would animate every bar whenever anything moved them — including a zoom switch, i.e. 108 elements re-flowing per frame on Anchor. It is armed after `setDrag(null)` and excludes the dragged bar: while the pointer is down the bar must track the cursor exactly, which was the one real risk flagged in the preview.
  ⚠️⚠️ **A GLOBAL `prefers-reduced-motion` GUARD, in one block, so a future animation cannot forget it** — and it uses `!important` deliberately, because the pane's slide and the bar's settle are inline `transition` values that a media query cannot otherwise reach. It matters here beyond politeness: the client report and the shared Gantt are opened by strangers on their own devices. **Verified the three `@keyframes` survived the build** (`pop-in` 0.11s, `notice-in` 0.16s, `row-flash` 0.4s) — the `mask:` shorthand precedent means CSS reaching the browser is checked here, not assumed.
  **eslint baseline moves 38 → 39**, and the one new warning was TRACED: `set-state-in-effect` in `useEnterTransition`, which is the hook's entire job. Gates: tsc clean, 0 errors, 306 tests, production build clean.
  ⚠️ **NOT verified live, and each for a stated reason:** the **row-flash** (exercising it writes a real time entry to the studio's hours, so it rests on the store field, the class and a measured 400ms `row-flash` animation) and the **bar settle** (driving a pointer-drag over a specific bar is unreliable enough that a pass would not mean much; the gate was confirmed absent at rest). **Mobile is unexamined for the motion round** — standing rule, desktop first.
- 2026-08-25 — **v1.32.4 — compact pass over the client-report rounds: two comments that asserted things which are no longer true, and two duplications I had left behind.** No migration, **no behaviour change — proven by measurement, not by reading the diff.**
  ⚠️ **TWO COMMENTS WERE LYING, which in this codebase is worse than having none** (the rule v1.19.10 recorded: a doc comment that describes something other than itself is read as true). (1) `report-table.tsx` opened its width block with *"the four lead columns are pinned"* — there are **three** (the Section column went in v1.20.0 when each group gained a heading row) and **a phone now pins none of them** (v1.32.1). (2) `public-report-view.tsx` justified `periodTotals` by saying *"the tiles below and the Billing periods pane both understated"* — **both of those surfaces were deleted in v1.31.0.** The RULE is still right and still load-bearing, so the fix is to re-point it at the three header figures rather than delete it. Sweep also checked the other references to removed things (the tile row, the aside, `delivered`/`remaining`): those all read in the past tense and are correct.
  **Two duplications folded into local components**, both of them mine from this batch: **`AmpMark`** and **`UpdatedStamp`**, each rendered twice on the page (phone instance + desktop instance). ⚠️ **The duplication itself is KEPT deliberately** — one DOM order cannot serve both shapes, since the figures and the period selector wrap between the two positions — but the **⚠️ notes that guard it are now stated ONCE** instead of drifting in two places. The one that matters most is on `AmpMark`: **the responsive `hidden`/`sm:*` must arrive via `className`, which lands on the WRAPPER, never on the mask span**, because `.brand-ampmore` sets `display: inline-block` unlayered in globals.css and beats any layered utility. Written on the component, so the next caller cannot miss it.

Its files showed as modified in `git status` while I was committing v1.32.1 — I staged only my own four and **deployed from a `git archive HEAD` extract rather than the working tree**, because `vercel deploy` uploads local files and would have shipped their unfinished work to production. Do that whenever `git status` shows changes you did not make: check the log for a version collision before bumping, and never deploy the working tree.
- 2026-08-25 — **v1.32.2 — a strict security review, and the two findings it produced: the one URL field that skipped the allowlist, and an email link taken from the Host header.** No migration. One data fix, run by Nitsan in the SQL editor (below). Nitsan asked for critical-severity findings only, so most of this release is what came back CLEAN — recorded because the next review should not re-derive it.
  ⚠️⚠️ **`tasks.figma_url` WAS THE ONE URL SURFACE IN THE APP WITH NO PROTOCOL ALLOWLIST — AND IT IS MEMBER-WRITABLE ON ANY TASK.** Every other one is guarded twice, deliberately: `links` runs `normalizeUrl` on write and `isSafeUrl` on render (`links-editor.tsx`), intake links run `normalizeUrl` in the route, the queue renders through `SafeLink`. The Figma field predates that convention and was never brought in — raw `href={task.figmaUrl}` in **both** `task-panel.tsx` and `task-list-row.tsx`, and 0011's trigger lists `figma_url` among the four columns a designer may write (on any task, since the row policy is `auth.uid() is not null`).
  ⚠️ **The impact is AVAILABILITY, not XSS, and getting that right changes what it is.** React 19's `sanitizeURL` **throws** on a `javascript:` href — confirmed in the installed production bundle, `"React has blocked a javascript: URL as a security precaution."` — and this app has **no `error.tsx` or `global-error.tsx` anywhere**, so the throw unmounts the tree to a blank page. One designer, one field, one row: My Tasks and that client's table go white for **everyone**, persistently, until somebody edits the row in SQL. Not a script running in an admin's session — the page simply ceases to exist. `data:`, `vbscript:`, `file:` don't throw and rendered as live clickable anchors.
  **Fixed at both ends, because rows predate the guard and can be written straight in SQL:** `normalizeUrl` on commit in both editors, `isSafeUrl` around both anchors. A refused value renders **struck through with a tooltip**, copying `links-editor`'s refusal rather than hiding the row — an admin needs to see that something unusable is stored.
  ⚠️ **THE ALLOWLIST THE WHOLE APP LEANS ON HAD NO TESTS. That is how the one field that skipped it went unnoticed.** New `src/lib/links.test.ts` — 8 tests over `normalizeUrl` / `isSafeUrl` / `hostLabel`, including that the two agree with each other, that a bare host is normalised but is NOT safe to render un-normalised, and the six dangerous schemes.
  **The real data, checked rather than assumed:** exactly one row studio-wide, `figma_url = "33"` on a task called "Research". A typo, not an attack — so the field was never abused. Worth naming what it did do: `href="33"` is a **relative** URL, so clicking "Figma" navigated the user to a 404 instead of opening anything. Nulled by id.
  ⚠️ **HOST-HEADER INJECTION INTO THE ADMIN NOTIFICATION EMAIL, FROM THE APP'S ONLY UNAUTHENTICATED WRITE PATH.** `/api/intake/[token]` built its "open the queue" link from `req.nextUrl.origin`, which derives from the **Host header**. Intake links get pasted into client emails by design, so anyone holding one could post a submission with a forged Host and the studio would receive a genuine notification — from `notifications@studionmore.com`, a verified sender, about a real brief — whose only link pointed at someone else's host. Vercel's host validation is the sole thing in front of that and is not a guarantee this app controls.
  **New `src/lib/app-origin.ts`** — fixed origin, falling back to `https://tracker.studionmore.com` when `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL` is unset, so **no Vercel env change is needed** and a missing var can never take the intake form down. Also wired into `/api/admin/invite`, which is admin-gated and far less reachable but builds the **password-set link** — the last URL that should be host-controlled. `notify()` lost its now-unused `req` parameter.
  **CLEARED, NOT FINDINGS — the areas checked, so the next review starts here rather than at the beginning.** Service-role confinement (the key appears only under `src/app/api/` and the two public server pages; no client component touches it). All 16 API routes gated — every one calls `auth.getUser()` except the four intake routes that are token-gated by design, and the admin ones re-check `profiles.role` server-side. Privilege escalation closed by 0021 (`role`/`active`) and the 0011/0022/0023/0027 task-column trigger, amended on each new column. `member_hr` admin-only in RLS with `/api/me/hr` whitelisting columns to the caller's own row. Upload safety: server-forced Content-Type from an extension allowlist, mirrored on the bucket. Intake IDOR: the `${link.id}/` path-prefix check and the `edit_key` + `intake_link_id` double check on both read routes, with one indistinguishable 404 for every failure. `sanitizeSnapshot` an allow-list, not a spread. Share tokens 16 bytes of `gen_random_bytes`. No SQL-injection surface, no unfiltered `.delete()`, one `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` and it consumes `escapeHtml`ed output.
  **NOT done, and named rather than left implicit.** (1) **No error boundary anywhere in `src/app`** — the multiplier that turned a bad field into a white screen. A route-level `error.tsx` would have made this a broken link instead of an outage, and it is worth its own release. (2) `tasks.type_id` is member-writable — the 0022 comment warns that a new column is member-writable by default, and 0024 added `type_id` without amending the trigger, so 0027's list omits it. Cosmetic (a colour axis), which is why it is not in this release. (3) `time_entries.minutes` has no bound in code or schema, so a member can write a negative or absurd figure into their own row and it flows into billing reports; internal-trust surface, UI-only entry point. Suggested `check (minutes is null or (minutes between 0 and 1440))`. (4) No CSP or security headers — `next.config.ts` sets only `devIndicators`.

Below `md` nothing is pinned — `pinCls` returns the background alone, `pinStyle` drops its `left`, the two hand-written sticky cells (the period band and the section heading) follow the same flag, and the frozen-edge shadow goes with them since there is no frozen edge. The hook keeps owning the desktop widths; a narrow render substitutes its own and leaves the key untouched. The drag handles are also suppressed on a phone — they would write to a key the narrow render ignores, i.e. a control that silently does nothing.
  **Task column sized by MEASUREMENT, not by feel.** He asked for "less wide to support 15 characters"; 15 characters of the row font (14px/380 Saans) measure **115px** on the canvas, and at the 128px I first used the name box was 92px — about twelve characters. It is **152px** (115 + 16px padding + a 21px indent), with the section indent trimmed `pl-7 → pl-5` below `sm` because 28px of a 152px column restates what the heading directly above already says. `Estimate` reads **"Est."** on a phone — at 64px the full word clipped mid-letter as "ESTIMAT", which reads as a broken cell rather than an abbreviation.
  **34px of padding given back to the table:** the page and the card each spent 16px a side, leaving a 309px scroller on a 375px screen. `p-2 sm:p-4` on the card and `p-2 sm:p-6` on the page → 341px.
  ⚠️⚠️ **THE MARK IS RENDERED TWICE AND THE `sm:hidden` LIVES ON A WRAPPER — THIS TRAP HAS NOW COST TIME THREE TIMES IN THIS REPO.** He asked for it "up inline with client name just aligne to right"; on a phone the figures and the period selector wrap between the name and the header's right-hand cluster, so a single instance landed three rows down as a stranded left-aligned banner.   **Two NEW figures beside `this period`, set identically:** **`days left`** and **`active tasks`**. ⚠️ `daysLeftIn` uses **`daysBetween` from `period-math`**, never `(end − now) / 86_400_000` — that module's version floors both dates to local midnight and rounds, which is what survives a clocks-change, and ms date arithmetic is the thing v1.23.0 deleted from this app. 0 means the period ends today; a period already past also reads 0, the honest answer to "how much is left".
  ⚠️⚠️ **`active tasks` IS COUNTED SERVER-SIDE OVER EVERY TASK, HIDDEN ONES INCLUDED** — new `periodActiveTasks` beside `periodTotals` in `sanitizeSnapshot`. Nitsan overruled — *"percentage is green on 100% red above it. below 100% its black"* — and he is right: a task landing ON its estimate is a job done as planned, worth a green, whereas a period at 90% of its cap is a warning by definition. New `estimateTone`, 3 tests.
  ⚠️ **THE MARK IS THE OFFICIAL ARTWORK, CROPPED — and it took two goes.** First as TEXT (`&more` in the brand font, which is what `app-shell.tsx` does when folded); he asked for *"the official logo - just the &more part"*, and text is the FONT approximating the mark, not the mark. New **`.brand-ampmore`** is `logo-1.svg` with a measured viewBox window: the glyph boxes were read with `getBBox()` in the browser — **"studio" ends at x=303, the "&" starts at 318, the final "e" ends at 693.24** — so the crop is the artwork windowed rather than a guess. ⚠️ Mask LONGHANDS only; the `mask:` shorthand is silently dropped by Turbopack/lightningcss (the same trap `.brand-wordmark` carries).

Nitsan rejected the placement outright — *"this shouldnt be on table - cap is just semantic… cap is general for client - not different each month - so maybe its a number next to 'current period' that can be editable like invoice day?"* — and he is right: a per-month column invites a per-month number the studio does not have. That column was reverted before it shipped. `clients.hour_cap` now holds it, edited exactly like Invoice day in the summary grid (3 → 4 columns).
  ⚠️ **THE POINT OF THE CAP IS THE COLOUR, NOT THE GATE.** Nitsan: *"its only semantic for us and the client to see that he doesn't exceed the cap without noticing, without permission."* So nothing is blocked and no warning interrupts anybody; the Current period figure simply renders as **`136h/150h`** and changes colour — **amber from 70%, red from 90%** (his thresholds). `capTone` is exported and has **7 tests** covering both boundaries exactly, past the cap, and ⚠️ **a 0 or negative cap treated as NO cap** — dividing by it would paint every client red for ever.
  ⚠️ **`Number("")` IS 0, WHICH IS WHY BLANK IS HANDLED FIRST.** `commitClientCap` clears the cap on an empty value and REFUSES anything that is not a non-negative number. A naive parse would turn a cleared cap into a **0h cap** and tell the client their whole period is overspent. Verified live: clearing gives `none`, not `0`.
  **Internal notes**, under Hours by month, per client (`clients.report_notes`). ⚠️⚠️ **NEVER REACHES A CLIENT, AND IS SAFE BY CONSTRUCTION RATHER THAN BY CARE:** it lives on `clients`, and the public report page selects nothing from that table — it reads `report_links.snapshot` alone, and `sanitizeSnapshot` builds its output field by field since v1.27.1, so a new client column cannot leak into a published report even by accident. ⚠️ **NOT `invoice_note`**, which looks free and is not: it renders as "Invoice day" ("20th").
  ⚠️ **0033's two columns go through `updateWithOptional`, not into `updateClient`'s row.** A missing column on a WRITE is PGRST204 and fails the WHOLE update — folding them in would have meant that, before the SQL was run, renaming a client or marking it internal silently failed too. And if the optional half IS dropped, a notice says so, rather than leaving local state showing a value the database does not have until the next refresh takes it away.
  **Report cells can be copied** — right-click a selected cell for "Copy N cells", or ⌘C. ⚠️ **TAB-SEPARATED DECIMAL HOURS**, not the "7h 30m" the cells show: the point of copying is to total them elsewhere and Sheets cannot sum "7h 30m". An empty cell copies as EMPTY, not 0 — a blank means nothing was logged, and a column of zeroes averages differently. ⚠️ ⌘C **defers to the browser** when a text selection exists or focus is in a field, or it would steal a copy the user meant. ⚠️ Right-clicking a cell OUTSIDE the selection selects that one first — copying figures the user was not pointing at is the wrong surprise for numbers headed into an invoice. ⚠️ The key handler is registered once and reads `copySelection` through a ref written in an EFFECT, never during render (the v1.19.7 rule); ⌘C only arrives from a real keypress, always after paint.
  **280 tests** (was 273). Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / **38 warnings — new baseline, was 37**; the extra is the notes pane's adopt-incoming-value effect, the same `set-state-in-effect` advisory `client-notes.tsx` already carries for the identical pattern. Traced, not banked.
  **Verified in the browser on Visitt, signed in:** the Cap column is gone from the periods table; the grid reads `Current period · Hour cap · Invoice day · Active tasks`; cap set to 150 and notes typed, **both surviving a full reload**; then both cleared and confirmed back to `none` / empty. ⚠️ **The 136/150 COLOURING was not seen live** — it needs a period containing today, and Visitt's periods all ended; the 7 unit tests cover the thresholds instead. ⚠️ **The copy was not exercised end to end either**: the browser tool's document focus makes clipboard reads unreliable (it refused with "Document is not focused" earlier in the session), so the TSV builder is verified by construction.
  ⚠️ **METHOD NOTE, cost several attempts: `dispatchEvent(new Event("blur"))` DOES NOT COMMIT a React `onBlur`** — React delegates to `focusout`, so the synthetic event never reaches the handler and the textarea looked saved while nothing was written. A programmatic value set (native setter + an `input` event) DOES drive React's `onChange`. So the working combination is **synthetic value + a REAL click elsewhere**. Also `cmd+a` in the browser tool selects the PAGE, not an input's text — use `triple_click`, and to clear a field type a SPACE where the handler trims.
  **NOT done — the other half of the batch, all client-link facing:** remove the "Delivered to date" pane; move "this period" up beside the client name rather than a boxed tile, with a "current billing" element beside it (`August (1/8-31/8) 62.5h`) whose chevron opens the recent periods in the same form; make the whole page expand responsively (titles, wordmark and table) to any screen; and a percentage chip beside each estimate showing how much is done. The cap must also flow into the published snapshot from `clients.hour_cap` for the client's own cap/remaining tiles to read from one number.
- 2026-08-24 — **v1.29.0 — hiding a task stops changing the client's money figures. The summary tiles show real totals.** No migration. **Nitsan's decision, 2026-08-24**, on the one finding audit slice 4 deliberately left to him — this closes the last item that had been "left alone pending Nitsan's call" since **v1.0.2**.
  ⚠️⚠️ **THE TILES WERE SUMMED FROM THE ROWS THAT SURVIVED SANITIZATION, SO HIDING A TASK MOVED THEM — IN THE EXPENSIVE DIRECTION.** `periodSummary` reduced over `snapshot.sections`, from which `sanitizeSnapshot` had already removed every hidden task. So "Delivered to date" and the current-period tile UNDERSTATED, and because `remaining` is `cap − charged`, the **Remaining tile OVERSTATED the budget left**: a 40h cap with 36h logged and one finished 12h task hidden for focus read **"this period 24h · Remaining 16h" when 4h of cap remained** — on the page the studio invoices against, to the person paying.
  **It contradicted the rule the sanitizer is built on**, which is why this is a fix and not a preference: *hiding is a focus tool, not confidentiality*. That is exactly why `totalMinutes` still spans hidden PERIODS (v1.0.2 reverted an earlier "fix" that broke it, and the file carries a ⚠️ telling nobody to reinstate it). The tiles were the one surface still behaving as though hiding meant subtracting.
  **`sanitizeSnapshot` now returns `periodTotals`** — the true hours per surviving period, summed over EVERY task. ⚠️ **Computed BEFORE the rows are filtered**, because afterwards those hours are simply gone and cannot be recovered. ⚠️ **Returned ALONGSIDE the snapshot, not inside it**: it is a summary, not a row anybody can unfold, and putting it in the payload's task list is how a "hidden" row comes back. ⚠️ **Indexed like the periods that SURVIVE**, not the originals — pinned by a test, since an off-by-one here would silently pair a cap with another period's hours.
  ⚠️ **AND THE TRADE, STATED PLAINLY BECAUSE IT IS REAL:** a hidden task's hours are now recoverable in aggregate — period total minus the rows shown. That is the accepted position recorded in v1.0.2 and re-affirmed by this change. **Anything that must not be SEEN belongs in the hidden lists, which are stripped server-side and are genuinely absent from the payload; a focus filter is not concealment and must not be used as one.**
  **273 tests** (was 268). Five new: the totals unchanged by hiding a task while the row itself stays absent from the payload; every task counted, not just visible ones; a cap reading staying honest; the indexing following the surviving periods; and the totals still true when EVERY task is hidden. ⚠️ **Proven not vacuous** — restoring the old row-summed behaviour fails 4 of the 5.
  Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 37 warnings (baseline), 273 tests, production build clean. **VERIFIED END TO END ON THE REAL VISITT LINK, with a task actually hidden and the report republished** (Nitsan asked for it after the release).
  ⚠️ **Run as THREE republishes, and the shape of the test is the point:** a single one would have mixed "changed because a task is hidden" with "changed because hours were logged since the last publish" and proved nothing. So — (1) republish UNCHANGED to get a baseline at today's data, (2) hide a task and republish, (3) unhide and republish to restore.
  | | Delivered | Aug (current period) | `blog fixes dev` |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | baseline, nothing hidden | 1053h | 12h | present |
  | **task hidden** | **1053h** | **12h** | **absent from the payload** |
  | restored | 1053h | 12h | present |
  The task hidden was **`blog fixes dev` — 4h total, 4h of it in the current period**, so under the old behaviour the tiles would have read **Delivered 1049h and Aug 8h**. Both held still, while the ROW was physically gone from the payload (not hidden with CSS) and the control row `Demo Page` stayed. Exactly the intended split: the figures are real, the row is genuinely absent.
  ⚠️ **Visitt was restored** — same four rows as before, same totals, view flags untouched ("Latest period only" and "Only rows with hours" both still on, 0 rows hidden). **The link is now stamped "last updated 25 Aug 2026" rather than 21 Aug**, which is the unavoidable cost of three republishes; the CONTENT is identical at every step, so today's hours match the 21 Aug snapshot exactly.
  ⚠️ **STILL NOT EXERCISED LIVE: the "Remaining" tile** — the one that actually misled, since it is `cap − charged`. Visitt's latest period has **no `hourCap`**, so that tile does not render at all on this client. It is covered by unit test only; seeing it needs a client whose current period has a cap set.
- 2026-08-24 — **v1.28.3 — the client page's billable share stops being dragged down by hours it can never count, and the helper every hours figure flows through stops lying about its unit.** No migration. Audit slice 6b: `/code-review` over `client-view.tsx` (2,937) + `dashboard.tsx` (1,965). Two findings. **This completes the six-slice audit begun at v1.23.1.**
  ⚠️ **THE BILLABLE SHARE DIVIDED BY A TOTAL ITS NUMERATOR COULD NEVER REACH.** `ClientStats` returns `total: total + unattributed` — the recovered pre-Everhour hours sitting on `tasks.legacy_hours`, which as its own comment says "are NOT in entrySumsAll (they never became entries)" — while `billable` was accumulated only inside the `entrySumsAll` loop. The tile renders `billable / total`, so a client whose work is **entirely billable**, with 100h tracked and 100h recovered, displayed **50%**. Now the billable part of the recovered hours is counted too (`unattributedBillable`, from the same `tasks` walk), so both sides share one basis and the percentage answers "how much of this client's work is billable" rather than "how much of the ITEMISED part is". The tooltip beside the total already reasons about this split for the CHARTS — the share was simply missed.
  ⚠️ **CORRECTION TO MY OWN FINDING, MEASURED RATHER THAN ASSUMED, because the first number I put on it was wrong.** I reported the exposure as the **3,953.75h** of remainder the recovery created. That figure is stale: **v0.99.34's spread pass moved 3,758.75h of it into dated entries**, and its own entry records that only "**4 tasks / 195h with no date signal**" were left alone. Checked live: **Anchor and Blazepod both report `unattributed = 0`** and their tiles render unchanged (91.5h / 68%, 6,535.5h / 97%). So the fix is correct and the bug was real, but **today's blast radius is ~195h on 4 tasks, not ~3,954h** — worth knowing before anyone goes looking for a big number to move.
  **`taskHoursDone` → `taskMinutesDone`, across all 8 call sites.** It returns MINUTES (`taskMinutes(id) + legacyHours * 60`) and always did; `client-view.tsx` even bound it to `const hoursDone` and was saved only by the `/ 60` on the next line. ⚠️ **No caller was wrong — every one was checked**: two divide by 60 before comparing to `estimateHours`, four pass it to `formatHoursShort`/`formatHoursDecimal` (which take minutes), and six already called it `doneMinutes`. This is a trap removed, not a bug fixed: a name promising hours, used as hours, multiplies every comparison by 60 with no type to catch it — `total > task.estimateHours` would mark a task over budget at a sixtieth of its estimate. The sibling `taskLegacyMinutes` directly below was already named correctly, which is what made this one stand out.
  Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 37 warnings (the baseline v1.28.2 set), 268 tests, production build clean. ⚠️ **NO NEW TEST, and the reason is stated rather than skipped:** the share is computed inside a `useMemo` in a 2,937-line component, and extracting it to make one assertion testable is a bigger and riskier change than the fix. It is verified by construction plus two live no-op cases; **I could not find a client currently carrying unattributed hours to see the number move.** The rename is covered by the compiler — 8 sites, tsc clean, and `taskHoursDone` appears nowhere in `src`.
  **Cleared, not findings.** `dashboard.tsx`: `StatTiles` reads `entrySumsAll` but renders only inside the admin branch, so no member sees backfill in their own figures; `useHoursScope` splits admin/member sources correctly; `MemberWelcome.todayLine` reads `timeEntries` not `entrySums` (the v1.19.1 staleness rule) and excludes legacy; `dailyTargetMinutes` falls back to 8h on a null capacity; the previous-period accumulation cannot double-count. `client-view.tsx`: `sectionIsEmpty` measures against ALL tasks and groups; `DeleteGroupModal` blocks deletion once hours exist.
  **THE AUDIT IS DONE — all six slices, v1.23.1 → v1.28.3.** In order: (1) `report-snapshot`/`aggregate` → the duplicate-link race and a DB constraint; (2) `period-math`/`date-ranges` → the unbounded chart projection; (3a) `db`/`snapshot` → unordered paging and a focus-frozen refresh; (3b) `store` undo/redo → undo expiring itself in under a minute; (4) the public report path → the sanitizer became an allow-list; (5) the intake route → two rate limits that bounded nothing; (6a) `client-timeline` → milestones outside the window; (6b) here. **Not done, and each is recorded where it belongs:** the `custom_weeks` per-column delta, `buildReportSnapshot`'s per-entry × per-week loop, the hidden-task summary tiles awaiting Nitsan's call, a durable (cross-instance) intake mint cap, and private storage buckets with signed URLs.
- 2026-08-24 — **v1.28.2 — a milestone can no longer be drawn where nobody can scroll to it, and the marquee hits the diamond it can see.** No migration. Audit slice 6a: `/code-review` over `client-timeline.tsx` (3,573 lines). Two findings; the file is carefully written and that is the honest result.
  ⚠️⚠️ **MILESTONE DATES DID NOT WIDEN THE STUDIO CHART'S WINDOW, ON THE CHART WHERE MILESTONES ARE CREATED.** `from`/`totalDays` was built from task dates plus today; `MarkLayer` then positions each mark at `daysBetween(from, onDate) * pxPerDay` with **no clamp**, while the scroller's width IS `totalDays * pxPerDay` — so a launch dated beyond the last task was drawn past the edge of the scrollable area and **unreachable at any zoom**, while still showing correctly on the CLIENT's shared chart. **v1.12.1 fixed exactly this for `public-gantt-view.tsx` and the fix was never applied here** — its log entry even names the failure ("a launch is routinely set AFTER the last task's due date"). ⚠️ The local `const marks: Date[]` **shadowed the milestone list** declared 170 lines above, which is the likeliest reason they were never included; it is `extremes` now.
  **Fixed at the right depth: one `chartWindow()` in `gantt.ts`, used by BOTH charts.** The bug was two files computing the same window and only one being corrected — which is precisely what that module's own header warns about ("copying them would have been a silent invitation for the two to drift a day apart"). The duplicate is gone rather than patched twice.
  ⚠️ **THE MARQUEE MISSED THE DIAMONDS IT WAS DRAWN AROUND.** `rowBands` gave a deadline-only task a hit box starting at its day column's LEFT EDGE, while the diamond is RENDERED centred in that column (`left + max(0, pxPerDay/2 − DIAMOND/2)`). At day zoom that is 26px per day against an 11px diamond — a **7.5px offset, so box and diamond overlapped by 3.5px of 11**: a rectangle drawn tightly around a visible diamond selected nothing, and one drawn through the empty cell to its left selected the task. Day zoom has been the default since v1.11.0, so it was the case people met first. ⚠️ It clamps to zero at week (9px) and month (3px), which is why it never showed up there. Spanning bars were always correct — checked: their band and their render share one formula.
  ⚠️ **AND FIXING THIS SURFACED TWO REAL LINT ERRORS THAT HAD BEEN INVISIBLE.** Replacing the inline window memo with a call made the component ANALYSABLE to the React Compiler, which then reported `draggedRowId`/`draggedFromContainer` — module `let`s reassigned from inside the component — as `react-hooks/globals` **errors**, breaking the 0-errors gate. They are one `const dragRow` object now, written through `beginRowDrag`/`endRowDrag` declared outside the component. ⚠️ **They cannot become a ref**: `TimelineRow`, a SEPARATE component, reads the dragged id to decide whether to accept a drop, so a ref in `ClientTimeline` could not reach it without threading a prop through every row.
  ⚠️ **eslint baseline moves 36 → 37, and the extra one was TRACED rather than banked.** It is a `react-hooks/immutability` advisory on `commit(d)` inside the drag effect — same family as the other 36, and newly VISIBLE for the same reason the errors were: the component is now analysable. Nothing about `commit` changed. **New baseline: 37.**
  **268 tests** (was 261) — seven for `chartWindow`: the week of padding, widening for a mark after the last task AND before the first, containing today with no dates at all, the Sunday snap at week/month but not day, and the ~13-week / 1-year floors. ⚠️ **Proven not vacuous** — dropping the dates from the window fails 4 of them.
  **Verified in the browser on Anchor's real chart**, signed in: 108 rows, **3 milestone lines at 182 / 1092 / 1950px inside a 4,229px window — none off either edge**, no crash. Also checked the empty case on Studio (290 undated tasks): the correct "no open tasks with a due date yet" state, which is `chartWindow([], today, zoom)` exercising the today-only path.
  Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 37 warnings, 268 tests, production build clean. ⚠️ **NOT verified by an actual marquee drag** — the diamond fix is arithmetic (the hit box now applies the same `max(0, pxPerDay/2 − DIAMOND/2)` the renderer uses) and driving a pointer-drag over a specific 11px target through the browser tool is unreliable enough that a pass would not have meant much. **NOT done:** slice 6b — `client-view.tsx` (2,937) and `dashboard.tsx` (1,965).
- 2026-08-24 — **v1.28.1 — the intake form's two rate limits start bounding what they claim to.** No migration. Audit slice 5 — the unauthenticated intake surface, reviewed at max effort: the submission route (502 lines), the upload route, `uploads.ts`. 3 findings, all fixed.
  **First, what held, because most of it does.** Path traversal is properly blocked (`startsWith(link.id + "/")` plus a `..` check); every claimed attachment is re-verified against what STORAGE holds — real size, real stored mimetype, checked against the type the claimed filename implies — so a `.txt` uploaded and then claimed as `photo.png` is dropped; the bucket's `allowed_mime_types` is the real enforcement and the route is honest about being belt-and-braces behind it; every rejection is RECORDED rather than silently dropped; the `edit_key` path checks key, link ownership and status; every parser is capped (`MAX_INTAKE_FILES + 3`, deliberately, so overflow can be reported).
  ⚠️⚠️ **BRIEF EDITS BYPASSED THE RATE LIMIT ENTIRELY, AND EACH ONE EMAILS EVERY ADMIN.** The limiter counts rows **created** in `task_requests`; an edit UPDATEs an existing row and returns before any insert, so it was never counted and nothing else bounded it. Anyone holding their own `editId` + `editKey` — which the client's browser keeps, by design, and which is the whole basis of editing without an account — could loop the request: a fresh "Updated brief" email rendering the entire brief to every address in `intake_notify_emails` on every pass, Resend quota burned, and `seen_at`/`seen_by` cleared each time so the queue re-flagged it as needing review. Both actions now share one 8-per-10-minute window, via `.or(created_at.gte.…,edited_at.gte.…)`.
  ⚠️⚠️ **THE UPLOAD FLOOD CAP COUNTED LANDINGS WHILE GUARDING THE ENDPOINT THAT MINTS.** It lists objects already in the bucket — but that endpoint writes nothing, it returns a signed URL, and the object appears only when the client PUTs to it. So a script calling it in a burst and uploading nothing saw an empty folder every time, was never refused, and came away holding **one single-use 30MB write URL per call**. Its own doc listed this as guard 4 of 4 on "the only [endpoint] that hands out write access to a bucket", and it did not bound the thing it was attached to — on a **1GB tier that a year of real briefs used 29MB of**. New `src/lib/mint-cap.ts` counts what the endpoint issues. ⚠️ **BEST-EFFORT AND SAID SO RATHER THAN GLOSSED:** the state is a module variable, so on serverless it is per-instance and resets on a cold start — several instances mean several allowances. It bounds the common case and raises the bar; **it is not a guarantee, and a durable version needs a counter the instances share, i.e. a migration. NOT done.**
  ⚠️ **BOTH LIMITERS FAILED OPEN, AND THE FIX IS DELIBERATELY ASYMMETRIC — read this before "making them consistent".** Neither read the `error` from the query it depended on, so a failed count became `0 >= 8` (submission) or `[]` (upload) and the limiter was simply off, silently — including under the **402 the project returns over quota**, i.e. exactly when it can least absorb traffic. Now: the **upload** cap **fails CLOSED** with a 503, because a refused upload is retried in seconds and loses nothing. The **submission** limiter still **fails OPEN, and now logs** — refusing there costs a real client their brief, which is the failure this whole area was rewritten to end (v1.19.2: a client lost one and the studio never learned it existed), and "a form that refuses to submit is a client who phones instead". A spam window is recoverable; a lost brief is not.
  **261 tests** (was 255). Six for `makeMintCap`: the allowance, per-key isolation, the window sliding rather than resetting wholesale, idle keys being pruned instead of growing for ever, and — the one worth having — **a refused mint not consuming further allowance**, so a flood cannot push its own window forward.
  ⚠️ **VERIFIED AGAINST THE REAL ENDPOINT WITHOUT CREATING A BRIEF OR SENDING MAIL**, which mattered because a malformed filter here breaks submission for every client: captured a live intake token from the queue's copy button (by patching `clipboard.writeText`, since the pane's own copy throws when the document is unfocused), then POSTed a submission with empty required fields — rejected at the required-fields check **after** the limiter had run, and **no "intake rate limit check failed" line in the server log**, so PostgREST accepts the new `.or()` filter and the limiter ran normally.
  Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 36 warnings (baseline), 261 tests, production build clean. **Cleared, not findings:** the notification email escapes only `&`/`<`/`>` and puts URLs in quoted `href`s, but `normalizeUrl` runs every client URL through `new URL().toString()`, which percent-encodes every quote — checked, unreachable. Public storage buckets remain the known open item from v1.0.1. **NOT done:** slice (6), the big UI files.
- 2026-08-24 — **v1.28.0 — the default team portrait follows the member, instead of being one colleague's photograph used for everybody.** No migration. Nitsan supplied two illustrated cut-outs (`man_avatar.png`, `woman_avatar.png`) and asked for them as gender-dependent defaults in place of `cutout.png`.
  ⚠️ **THE SOURCE IS `member_hr.gender`, WHICH IS ADMIN-ONLY AT THE RLS LEVEL — so this cannot be a client query.** A designer's browser selecting from `member_hr` gets nothing back, and team cards are visible to every signed-in member (studio-wide read visibility is intentional). New **`/api/member-avatars`** does it with the service role behind a session check, exactly as `/api/celebrations` does for birth dates and for the same reason. ⚠️ **It returns `{profileId: "man" | "woman"}` and NOTHING ELSE** — not national ids, not addresses, not birth dates, and **not the recorded gender string itself**. A profile whose value is missing or unrecognised is simply ABSENT from the map. Never widen it to return the row. ⚠️ Nitsan chose this over a new `profiles` column when offered both; the alternative would have kept HR out of it entirely, and is the thing to revisit if publishing an HR-derived attribute to every colleague's browser ever becomes a concern.
  ⚠️ **`avatarVariantFor` MATCHES EXACT TOKENS, NEVER SUBSTRINGS — "female" CONTAINS "male"**, so an `includes` check would have answered every woman in the studio with the man's portrait. That is the one bug `src/lib/avatar-variant.ts` exists to make impossible, and it is pinned by test. The column is free text seeded from a spreadsheet, so English and Hebrew are both handled (`m/male/man/זכר/גבר`, `f/female/woman/נקבה/אישה`) plus trailing punctuation; anything else returns null. ⚠️ **Gender is NEVER inferred from a name** — a name is not a statement about anyone's gender, and a wrong portrait is worse than a neutral one. There is a test asserting the studio's own first names all fall through.
  ⚠️ **BLANK NOW MEANS THE MALE AVATAR — Nitsan's explicit call**, in one constant (`DEFAULT_PORTRAIT`). Recorded because the obvious choice is the wrong one: `cutout.png` is a cut-out of a **real colleague's photograph**, so using it for everybody presents one person's likeness as everybody else's — the defect v0.99.17 describes. An androgynous illustrated figure is the less wrong default. ⚠️ It is still a default that can be wrong about somebody: a woman with no recorded value is drawn as a man until it is filled in, and the fix for an individual is her HR field, not this constant.
  ⚠️ **THE TWO ALPHA CONSTANTS WERE WRONG FOR THE NEW ASSETS AND ARE NOW PER-ASSET.** `HEAD_TOP_MARGIN 0.039` and `SILHOUETTE_RIGHT_MARGIN 0.128` were measured from `cutout.png` — right while it was the only portrait, wrong the moment a second silhouette arrived. Measured from the alpha channels: cutout **0.0375 / 0.1281**, man **0.0688 / 0.1797**, woman **0.0688 / 0.2094**. Using cutout's numbers would have cropped the new heads in the wrong place and left the hero portrait floating off the panel edge. They live in a `PORTRAITS` table now; **re-measure if an asset is replaced** (`sharp(...).raw()`, scan for the first and last pixel with alpha > 8). ⚠️ An UPLOADED portrait falls back to the neutral geometry — its silhouette is unknown, so cropping it by another figure's measurements would be a guess.
  **Compressed, as asked:** 2032²/2048² PNGs at **3.1MB and 3.4MB → 308KB and 327KB** at 640×640, matching `cutout.png`'s 371KB. An avatar that costs 3MB is a page-weight bug in waiting.
  **Verified in the running app, signed in.** ⚠️ **The first check found nothing changed, and the reason was a STALE DEV BUNDLE** — the assets served 200 while `man_avatar` appeared nowhere in `/team`; a dev-server restart fixed it (the trap the Stack section already warns about). After that: the endpoint went **9 → 11 entries** once Nitsan filled the two blanks, and the cards read `Itay Biran: man → man_avatar.png`, `Michal Ner-David: woman → woman_avatar.png`, every other member on their own upload, **0 broken images**. ⚠️ **Worth knowing before someone reports a bug: most cards look like the same blue mannequin, and that is a coincidence of style, not a fault** — those are genuine Supabase storage uploads that happen to resemble these figures. Confirmed by reading each `img` src. And the two new assets are genuinely distinct: **42.7% of visible pixels differ at 64px**.
  **255 tests** (was 249) — six for `avatarVariantFor`, including the female/male substring case and the never-guess-from-a-name rule.
  Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 36 warnings (baseline), 255 tests, production build clean. ⚠️ **`cutout.png` is now reachable only as the `neutral` variant, which nothing selects** — kept deliberately, since it is the honest fallback if the illustrated defaults are ever withdrawn. **NOT done:** mobile is unexamined (standing rule — desktop first, then ask), though nothing here renders differently on a phone; and the audit's remaining slices, (5) the intake route and (6) the big UI files.
- 2026-08-24 — **v1.27.1 — the client report's sanitizer becomes a list you opt into, and two filters that only pretended to enforce anything are gone.** No migration. Audit slice 4 — the public `/report/[token]` path, the highest-stakes surface in the app. 3 findings; **two fixed, one deliberately left with Nitsan** (below).
  **First, what held.** The token is 128-bit (`encode(gen_random_bytes(16),'hex')`), an inactive link and an unknown token both `notFound()` identically so nothing can be enumerated, the page reads only the frozen `snapshot` for that link's own client with no live query, and hidden tasks and hidden period/week columns are **physically absent** from the payload with every row's arrays reindexed alongside the headers. That is the guarantee v0.99.3 built and it is intact.
  ⚠️ **THE SANITIZER COULD NOT KEEP ITS OWN PROMISE, BECAUSE ITS TOP TWO LEVELS WERE A SPREAD.** `{ ...snap, periods: snap.periods.filter(…) }` copied every key of the stored jsonb and passed each period object through whole, while the doc above it promised the client "never receives hidden data in any form — not in the DOM, not in the JSON payload". `link.snapshot` is a **`jsonb` column CAST to `ReportSnapshot`** — a cast asserts a shape rather than checking one — so TypeScript could not see what was actually in there. **No live leak, and that was checked rather than assumed:** every version of `buildReportSnapshot` back to **v0.92** allow-listed its own output (`periods: sorted.map(p => ({label, from, to, hourCap, advanceHours}))`). The hazard was the future one — any field later added to a snapshot or a period (an internal note, a rate, an assignee, a period's `paid` flag) becomes client-visible the moment it is published, **with no code change on this path and no type error to catch it**. Now built field by field, matching the task mapping directly below it, which was already an allow-list. ⚠️ **`invoices` is deliberately NOT carried forward:** the type declares it, nothing writes it, nothing renders it, and one stale comment mentions an "invoices pane" that does not exist. If it is ever implemented it gets added on purpose — which is the point of a list you have to opt into.
  ⚠️ **TWO FILTERS IN THE VIEW COULD NEVER MATCH, AND READ AS THE ENFORCEMENT.** `visiblePeriods` and the Billing-periods list both filtered on `hiddenColumns.includes(columnKey(i))` — but on this page `hiddenColumns` is `leadingHidden`, which only ever holds `"estimate"` and/or `"total"` (the two leading columns, whose values are nulled rather than dropped), while `columnKey(i)` returns `` `p:{i}` ``. So both kept every element, always. The OUTCOME was correct — hidden periods were already stripped server-side — but the code looked like the client was enforcing it, and someone trusting that could move period-hiding into the view, or drop the server-side filtering believing this covered it, and either ships hidden columns to the browser. Both filters deleted, `columnKey` no longer imported there, and a ⚠️ states where the enforcement actually lives.
  ⚠️⚠️ **LEFT FOR NITSAN, NOT FIXED — HIDING A TASK OVERSTATES THE CLIENT'S REMAINING BUDGET.** The summary tiles sum `t.periodMinutes[i]` over the tasks that SURVIVED sanitization, so hiding a task — documented since v1.0.2 as a **focus tool with no effect on totals**, which is exactly why `totalMinutes` still spans hidden periods — cuts the period's charged hours and inflates `remaining`. Concretely: a 40h cap with 36h logged, one finished 12h task hidden so the client reads the current work, shows **"this period 24h" and "Remaining 16h" when 4h of cap is left** — on the page the studio invoices against. The v1.0.2 entry already records this as open ("hiding DOES currently move those tiles, which contradicts the rule above. Left alone pending Nitsan's call") and it is **still undecided**. Fixing it means sending the true per-period totals alongside the sanitized rows, i.e. changing what the payload contains, so it is his call and not a unilateral change. **Raised again with the concrete number; awaiting his decision.**
  **249 tests** (was 245). Four new, pinning the allow-list at each level: an unknown top-level key, an unknown key on a period, on a week column, and on a task. ⚠️ **Proven not vacuous** — restoring the spread fails 3 of the 4. **The task-level one passes either way and is kept deliberately**: it documents that the task mapping was already correct, so a future refactor cannot quietly turn it into a spread.
  Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 36 warnings (baseline), 249 tests, production build clean. ⚠️ **NOT verified against a live published link:** the change is provably shape-preserving for every snapshot the app has ever written (all allow-listed, so the fields are identical), and the two deleted filters were no-ops — but no client report was opened to confirm it renders unchanged. Worth a glance at any published link. **NOT done:** slices (5) the unauthenticated intake route, (6) the big UI files.
- 2026-08-24 — **v1.27.0 — undo works for longer than a minute, and stops blaming a colleague for your own edit.** No migration. Audit slice 3b: `/code-review` over `store.tsx`'s undo/redo core and mutation plumbing, 3 findings, all fixed.
  ⚠️⚠️ **YOUR OWN EDIT EXPIRED YOUR OWN UNDO HISTORY, AND THE MESSAGE BLAMED SOMEBODY ELSE.** The epoch that retires undo steps was bumped whenever the refresh fingerprint changed — but **the print is computed from the SERVER response**, so a local write alters it the moment it comes back, and `fingerprintRef` was re-baselined nowhere else (boot and the refresh apply, and nothing after a write). So: log an hour, wait one tick, press ⌘Z → the whole history is wiped with *"Someone else changed the studio data since then, so that undo is no longer available."* **Nobody had.** Every mutation reaches the print one way or another — a rename or a due date via the tasks hash on the ~3-minute tier, a logged or deleted hour via the 400-row feed on the 60-second tier — **so undo was usable only until the first tick that observed your own change, i.e. under a minute, for the whole life of the feature (v1.1.0).** Now `printWriteSeqRef` records `writeSeq` alongside the print, and the rule is one pure function beside `refreshVerdict`: `historyEpochShouldMove({printChanged, wroteSincePrint})`. ⚠️ **RESIDUAL, deliberate and stated rather than glossed:** a colleague writing in the SAME tick as us is indistinguishable, so the history survives and an undo could still revert them. One tick wide — and strictly better than a guard that fired on every edit and therefore protected nothing while breaking the feature it was guarding.
  ⚠️ **UNDOING A DELETE COULD RESTORE THE ENTRY WITH ITS DESCRIPTION ERASED.** `deleteTimeEntry` fell back to the slim `entrySumsAll` row with **`description: ""` and `movedFromTaskId: null` hardcoded** when the full row wasn't in the store, and `restoreTimeEntry` faithfully writes back whatever it is given — **in an app where a description is mandatory on every entry.** The typed text was gone from the database with nothing said. Reachable on all three day surfaces (`dashboard.tsx` DayLog, `logged-day.tsx`, `user-day-details.tsx`): they render from `loadDayEntries`, which returns rows to the CALLER **without merging them into store state**, so any entry older than the 400-row feed window took that path every time. `deleteTimeEntry(entryId, known?)` now takes the row, all three callers pass the one they already hold, and when neither is available **the delete happens with no undo recorded** — no undo beats one that silently rewrites the entry.
  **`withGroupInvariant` was a guard that did not guard.** It compared the group's section against the section the task was **LEAVING** (`before.sectionId`), so a patch naming a group AND a conflicting section passed through untouched whenever the group already sat in the task's current section — storing a task in a group that lives elsewhere, the exact state the function exists to make impossible, while its own comment claimed a patch's `sectionId` was "overruled rather than trusted". Now compared against the section the patch is ASKING for. `updateTasksBulk` had the mirror gap: its `groupId: null` guard was skipped whenever the caller happened to mention `groupId`, so a cross-section move could keep a group from the section it left — it now checks that the named group actually fits the target section. ⚠️ **Neither was reachable from today's four call sites** (client-view.tsx:1278 and :1686, task-panel.tsx:740, `groupTasksIntoNew`), so this closes a hole rather than fixing a live bug — worth doing because every reader tolerates the broken state by rendering the task loose, so a future caller would break it silently and the symptom would be a task quietly falling out of its group.
  **245 tests** (was 234). New `group-invariant.test.ts` (8 cases, including the exact regression) plus 4 for `historyEpochShouldMove`. ⚠️ **Proven not vacuous** — reverting the two logic fixes fails exactly 2 of them. ⚠️ **`withGroupInvariant` is now EXPORTED purely so it can be tested**; it has no other caller outside `store.tsx`.
  ⚠️ **The delete-undo fix is covered by construction only:** it lives inside the provider behind a live Supabase client, so exercising it means deleting a real time entry older than the feed window and pressing ⌘Z. Not done — that writes to real logged hours.
  Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 36 warnings (baseline), 245 tests, production build clean. ⚠️ **A warning appeared and was chased rather than accepted:** the count went to 37 because dropping the `entrySumsAll` read from `deleteTimeEntry` left it an unused dependency. Removed from the array, back to 36 — **check WHICH warning moved before treating a new count as the new baseline** (v1.23.1 recorded the opposite mistake, a count that fell for a reason that was not an improvement).
  **Cleared, not findings:** `record`'s 10-step cap and future-clearing; `asOneStep` saving and RESTORING the suppress flag (correct — a bare `false` would release `redo`'s own hold half-way); `restoreTimeEntry` carrying every column (v1.0.1's fix still holds); `inversePatch`; `updateTimeEntry`'s explicit row build. **NOT done:** slices (4) `report-sanitize.ts` + the public `/report/[token]` path, (5) the intake route, (6) the big UI files.
- 2026-08-24 — **v1.26.0 — every paged fetch gets a stable order, and a cursor left in a text box can no longer freeze the studio's data.** No migration. Audit slice 3a: `/code-review` over `db.ts` + `snapshot.ts` (the fetch/boot path), 3 findings, all fixed.
  ⚠️⚠️ **`fetchAll` PAGED WITH NO `ORDER BY`, SO A ROW COULD BE RETURNED TWICE OR NOT AT ALL.** PostgREST adds no implicit ordering and Postgres gives no stable row order for LIMIT/OFFSET without one — so page 2, being a **separate query**, could repeat a row from page 1 or skip one whenever the heap moved in between: a concurrent insert or update, a HOT update relocating a tuple, autovacuum. **The worst exposure is the query that matters most: `time_entries` is ~24,000 rows over 25 pages** (measured in v1.18.2), refetched on every cold tick **while designers are logging hours into that same table**. A skipped row is hours silently missing from `entrySums` — client report totals, the KPI tiles, every per-person figure — and a duplicated row is hours counted twice, with nothing surfacing either. `fetchTasks` (5 pages) could likewise drop or double a task. **Every call site was checked: not one passed an `order`.** Now `.order("id")` inside `fetchAll` — the primary key, so the sort is total and the paging is stable — applied BEFORE `modify` so a caller can still add its own primary ordering. ⚠️ Nothing downstream may rely on id order: every caller that cares sorts its own result, which is why this changes no rendered order.
  ⚠️⚠️ **FOCUS IN ANY FIELD SUSPENDED BACKGROUND REFRESH INDEFINITELY.** `refresh` returned early on `focusInEditor()` and set `refreshQueued` — but that flag is drained **only when a WRITE settles** (`wrote`/`counting`), and a focus-only deferral has no write to settle. So a cursor parked in a box suppressed every 60-second tick for as long as it stayed there, with **no upper bound and nothing on screen saying so**: clicking the header search box and walking away was enough to freeze tasks, the plan, the feed and the hours at that moment, with a colleague's edits never arriving. The only trace was the sync dot's "Updated …" quietly ceasing to advance — **the same invisible staleness v1.19.12 built a banner for.** Focus may now hold a refresh off only while the data is still fresh; past **`FOCUS_MAX_STALE_MS` (5 min)** it proceeds. ⚠️ The cap had to be applied in **BOTH** places — the pre-fetch guard and `refreshVerdict`'s `focused` argument — or the response of the very refresh the cap just allowed would be dropped on arrival.
