The race the focus test was guarding against is covered either way: `refreshVerdict` compares `writeSeq` from before the fetch against now, so an edit made while the fetch was out still defers the response.
  **`dev_items` still had a bare `.catch(() => [])`** — the exact defect `optionalTable` was written to remove, applied to the six tables in `fetchCold` by the v1.21.1 sweep and **missed on this one**, three lines away from a doc comment ending "If you add a fifth optional table, use this". Swallowing every failure renders as a client with nothing in development, indistinguishable from the truth — **including the 402 the project returns over quota**, which is the one failure `isServiceBlocked` exists to surface. Now wrapped, so only a missing table or column may read as no rows.
  **234 tests** (was 227). New: `fetchAll` orders by id on every page, returns every row exactly once across pages, stops after a short page, and needs no extra request on an exact multiple; `dev_items` reads a missing table as no rows but **rejects** a quota refusal and a permission error. ⚠️ **Proven not vacuous** — reverting the two code fixes fails exactly 3 of them.
  ⚠️ **The focus cap is covered by construction only, and that is stated rather than glossed:** it lives in `store.tsx` behind refs and a live clock, so there is no unit test for it — exercising it means holding focus in a field for five minutes against a running app. **Read the ⚠️ in `refresh` before touching it.**
  Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 36 warnings (baseline), 234 tests, production build clean. ⚠️ **NOT verified in the browser:** the ordering fix is invisible when it works (its whole point is that pages stop being able to disagree), and the focus cap needs a five-minute wait. **Cleared, not findings:** the `time_entries` and `tasks` column ladders step down one rung at a time and only on `isMissingSchema`; `mergeTasks`'s brief preservation; `mergeTimeEntries` erring toward keeping a row; `canonicalReportLink`; `updateWithOptional`'s PGRST204 handling. **NOT done:** slices (3b) `store.tsx` mutations + undo/redo, (4) `report-sanitize.ts` + the public `/report/[token]` path, (5) the intake route, (6) the big UI files.
- 2026-08-24 — **v1.25.0 — the admin charts stop inventing a bar on the 1st of the month, and a month label says which year it means.** No migration. Audit slice 2: `/code-review` over `period-math.ts` + `date-ranges.ts`, 4 findings, all fixed.
  ⚠️⚠️ **`bucketProjection` HAD NO MINIMUM-ELAPSED GUARD, so the running bucket was scaled by the whole period's length on its first day.** The only test was `elapsed > 0`. On the 1st of a month the factor was the month's length: a normal 27h day drew August at **837h against a real ~600h month**, and a heavy 40h day drew **1240h** — and because `MultiLineChart` scales to its largest value, that one invented bar **squashed every completed bar beside it**. On 1 January the year factor was **365**. Neither call site clamped it (`dashboard.tsx:533`, `:744`, `team/[profileId]/page.tsx:318` all use the factor raw), and it recurred **every 1st of the month**. Now `scaleFrom` refuses below `MIN_ELAPSED_FRACTION` (0.2), capping the factor at 5. ⚠️ **The cost is deliberate and is the honest trade:** for the first ~6 days of a month, and until mid-March for a year, there is no projection and the bucket is drawn as the partial thing it is. A visibly short bar beats a confident wrong one — but it IS the "fell off a cliff" reading the feature was built to avoid, so if that grates, `MIN_ELAPSED_FRACTION` is the one number to change.
  ⚠️ **A month label carried no year while up to 24 months are allowed — i.e. up to three calendar years on one axis.** An 18-month range covering two Januaries drew two bars both reading `Jan`, and `MultiLineChart` is handed only `labels` (dashboard.tsx:559) with **no per-point tooltip**, so hovering did not disambiguate either. Now `Aug 25` / `Aug 26` when the span crosses a year, plain `Aug` when it does not. **This is the convention the codebase already documents** at `client-reports/page.tsx:95` — "one month name can appear twice and the label alone is ambiguous" — so it was an inconsistency, not an open question. ⚠️ Note the client-report WEEK columns still drop the year and are left alone: each `<th>` carries `title={from → to}` with full ISO dates, which is the same convention satisfied a different way (v1.23.1 recorded that decision).
  ⚠️ **`bucketize`'s day/month decision counted the days that HAVE hours, not the range.** Its own doc comment said "≤31d range" while the code read `new Set(dates).size <= 31`, so a 90-day range worked on 12 scattered days took the DAY branch: 12 evenly spaced bars across three months, adjacent bars a day apart or five weeks apart with nothing showing the gap — **an axis that is not linear in time, read as a trend.** Now the SPAN between the first and last date, so that case buckets by month. The 31-day boundary is unchanged and pinned by test (31 → day, 32 → month).
  **`presetRange` now calls the shared `startOfWeek`** instead of re-deriving `now.getDate() - day` inline. Nothing was broken — both are calendar-based and both start on Sunday — and it is fixed because of what this exact duplication has already cost here: `report-snapshot.ts` carried byte-identical private copies of `parseISO`/`shiftDays` until v1.23.0, and the ms-based `addDays` survived for months because its call sites were scattered. If the week start ever changes, one definition changes with it.
  **227 tests** (was 216). ⚠️ **Proven not vacuous** — reverting all three code fixes fails exactly 4 of the new ones. `bucketize` and `presetRange` had no tests at all before this; the new `presetRange` cases include a week straddling the clocks-back Sunday.
  Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 36 warnings (baseline), 227 tests, production build clean. ⚠️ **NOT verified in the browser:** all four are pure functions and the projection change is only observable on the first days of a month or year, which cannot be staged without lying to the clock — the unit tests take `now` explicitly and are the real check. **NOT done:** the remaining audit slices — (3) `db.ts` + `store.tsx` (3,473 lines, needs splitting), (4) `report-sanitize.ts` + the public `/report/[token]` path, (5) the unauthenticated intake route, (6) the big UI files.
- 2026-08-24 — **v1.24.0 — one active report link per client, enforced by the DATABASE, and hours that can no longer be counted twice or vanish.** ⚠️ **MIGRATION `0032_one_active_report_link.sql` — APPLIED by Nitsan 2026-08-24**, after deactivating two stray links (the index cannot be created while they exist). From the first deep audit slice: `/code-review` over `report-snapshot.ts` + `aggregate.ts`, then a read-only sweep of the real data.
  ⚠️⚠️ **THE `ensureLink` DUPLICATE-LINK GUARD DOES NOT WORK, AND THE DATA PROVED IT.** v1.20.0 added a "ask the DATABASE before minting one" check naming Blazepod and No Traffic as victims. **Visitt got two active links 89ms apart on 2026-08-21 — two days AFTER that check shipped.** It awaits between the read and the insert, so two concurrent callers both see no rows and both insert; no app-level check can be atomic. There was **no unique constraint**. Now there is a partial unique index, and `ensureLink` treats **23505 as success**: it re-fetches and adopts the link the winner created.
  ⚠️ **WHY A DUPLICATE IS NOT COSMETIC.** `canonicalReportLink` prefers a published link, then the earliest — so the *other* row becomes invisible to the app while staying writable. Visitt's stray carried a hand-edited column **2 Jul – 25 Jul (24 days, 57.00h)** that swallowed columns 25/26/27 whole. Dormant only because the published link won selection: **delete or unpublish the good link and the stray becomes canonical, putting 57 phantom hours into the preview and the next publish.** Blazepod's pair (13h apart, 10–11 Jul, pre-guard) had no such divergence. Measured, not assumed: **no client ever saw wrong numbers — the published snapshot held the correct `20 Jul – 25 Jul`.**
  **Data findings from the sweep, all read-only:** **no overlapping and no inverted billing periods anywhere** (so the pre-v1.23.1 DST bug left no damage — it was only ever a way to *create* one), and duplicates on exactly two clients. Both strays deactivated, not deleted: `active=false` keeps the row and its token, and reverses with one UPDATE. ⚠️ The public page does `if (!link || !link.active) notFound()`, so those two tokens now 404 — safe here because **neither had a snapshot**, so neither served a report. ⚠️ For the ~2 min before Visitt's link was published the stray was canonical, so a Visitt URL in the wild could point at it; flipping `active` back is the fix. ⚠️ **Incidental: Blazepod has NO published report at all** — both its links were unpublished with no snapshot.
  **Three code fixes for hours that stop adding up.** (1) `handleEditColumnDates` now **normalises an inverted pair and clamps to its neighbours**: `buildReportSnapshot` adds an entry's minutes to EVERY column containing its date, so an overlap double-counts and the week cells stop summing to the row's Total — on a report a client reads. (2) The period date cells committed anything the picker returned; `commitPeriodDate` now **refuses an overlap or an inversion**, because the same double-count hits the per-period totals that `hourCap`/`advanceHours` are read against. (3) `buildReportSnapshot` routes a task whose `sectionId` matches **no** section of the client into "Other" — it was in no block at all, so its minutes were counted into `totalByTask` and then never rendered: **silent UNDER-reporting of billable work**. Both routes there are closed today (`on delete set null`; a cross-client move carries a target section), so it is a guard against bad data.
  **`aggregate.ts`: `sumInRange` and `minutesByUserInRange` DELETED — both had zero callers**, two of five exports. `sumInRange` was also a trap: `taskClient` was optional, so `sumInRange(sums, {clientId})` compared `undefined !== clientId`, skipped every entry and returned a plausible **0 hours** with no error, in a module whose numbers are billed from.
  **216 tests** (was 213). `buildReportSnapshot` had **none**; it now has three, including one asserting reported hours equal logged hours. ⚠️ **Proven not vacuous** — reverting the "Other" fallback fails 2 of them.
  Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 36 warnings (baseline), 216 tests, production build clean. ⚠️ **NOT verified in the browser:** the two validation guards are refusals in edit handlers, and exercising them means dragging real billing-period and column dates on live client data. **NOT done:** the audit's remaining slices (2) `period-math.ts`/`date-ranges.ts`, (3) `db.ts`/`store.tsx`, (4) `report-sanitize.ts` + the public report path, (5) the intake route, (6) the big UI files.
- 2026-08-24 — **v1.23.1 — the last ms-based date in the app, found by reviewing v1.23.0 rather than by anyone noticing.** No migration. First `/code-review` ever run on this project; it read the v1.23.0 diff and returned **one** finding.
  ⚠️ **v1.23.0 DELETED `addDays` AND ITS COMMENT CLAIMED "there is no reason for the ms-based version to exist" — WHILE `client-reports/page.tsx` STILL COMPUTED A DATE THAT WAY, IN A FILE THAT RELEASE EDITED.** The candidate-client-tabs cutoff was `toISODate(new Date(Date.now() - 90 * 86400000))`: 90×24h of wall-clock milliseconds, which is an hour short across a clocks-back transition and so resolves to the **wrong calendar day**. A client whose only recent activity sat on that boundary appeared in, or vanished from, the tab strip a day early. Now `toISODate(shiftDays(new Date(), -90))`. Tiny by itself — **the reason it is a release is the comment**: it asserted the pattern was gone, so the next reader would not have looked, and the assertion is now true.
  **Swept the whole codebase to make the claim honest.** Everything else that touches `86400000` is safe and was left alone, deliberately: `jewish-holidays.ts` steps days with `t += DAY` but is **UTC-anchored at NOON** (`T12:00:00Z`, `getUTCDay()`, `toISOString()`) and UTC has no DST; `period-math.ts`, `gantt.ts`, `about.ts` and `about-modal.tsx` divide by a day and `Math.round`, which absorbs the ±1h; the intake routes and `store.tsx` use ms for genuine **durations** (rate-limit windows, write-settle), where ms is correct; `team/[profileId]` divides by 365.25 for an age in years. **There is now no date-semantic millisecond arithmetic left in the app.**
  ⚠️ **THREE LEADS THE REVIEW RAISED AND KILLED, recorded because the eliminations are the useful part.** (1) `jewish-holidays.ts` looked like the same bug in the worst possible place — Rosh Hashanah, Yom Kippur, Sukkot and Simchat Torah all cluster within weeks of Israel's clocks change — until the noon-UTC anchoring showed it cannot drift. (2) The week column labels drop the year, and DualBird's report spans May 2025 → Aug 2026 with **two "August" period headings**; but each `<th>` carries `title={from → to}` with full ISO dates, which is exactly the convention `client-reports/page.tsx:95` already documents for the month chart ("one month name can appear twice and the label alone is ambiguous"). Consistent, so left as is. (3) The `throw` v1.23.0 added inside `buildWeeks` sits in a render path — but `buildReportSnapshot` is called **only** from the admin page (clients read the frozen snapshot), and it is unreachable anyway: a malformed date yields `"NaN-NaN-NaN"`, which fails the `while` condition and returns `[]` instead of throwing.
  Gates: tsc clean, **eslint 0 errors / 36 warnings — the baseline moved from 37**, 213 tests, production build clean. ⚠️ **The lost warning is not an improvement and should not be read as one:** `react-hooks` flags `Date.now` **by name** as impure, and `new Date()` is exactly as impure but is not matched by that rule. The call is still there and still impure; only the lint hit went away. **New baseline for future releases: 36.** ⚠️ **No new test:** the change is a one-line swap to a helper with 5 dedicated DST tests as of v1.23.0, and the cutoff itself is a 90-day recency filter with no assertable boundary that is not already covered by `shiftDays`.
  **The audit plan agreed with Nitsan, riskiest-per-line first, one slice at a time:** (1) `report-snapshot.ts` + `aggregate.ts` — the hours clients are billed from; (2) `period-math.ts` + `date-ranges.ts`; (3) `db.ts` + `store.tsx` (3,473 lines, needs splitting); (4) `report-sanitize.ts` + the public `/report/[token]` path — a leak there is the worst outcome in the app; (5) the unauthenticated intake route; (6) the big UI files last, since bugs there are visible and low-blast-radius.
- 2026-08-24 — **v1.23.0 — the app's date arithmetic stops being an hour short, and the helper that made it so is deleted.** No migration. Started as a `/simplify` pass over v1.22.2 and turned into a correctness release when the review counted the call sites.
  ⚠️⚠️ **`format.ts`'s `addDays` IS GONE — `getTime() + days * DAY_MS`, ALONG WITH `DAY_MS` ITSELF. DO NOT REINTRODUCE IT.** v1.22.2 worked around it with a module-local `shiftDays` in `report-snapshot.ts` and a comment claiming it was left alone because it is "used app-wide, including for pixel maths where ms-based is correct". **That claim was false, and checking it is what made this a release: all 24 call sites across 8 files were date-semantic** — every one fed `toISODate`, `getDate()` or a day-keyed loop — and **zero** were pixel maths (the Gantt's geometry uses `daysBetween`/`shiftDays`, never `addDays`). So v1.22.2 fixed 1 of 25 call sites and documented the other 24 as fine.
  ⚠️ **FIVE OF THEM WERE LIVE, USER-VISIBLE BUGS, all on the clocks-back Sunday (Israel, late October), all of which shipped for months:** (1) `periodBounds("This week")` returned Sun–**Fri**, so every KPI, chart and comparison on the admin home **silently dropped Saturday's hours**; (2) `addDaysIso` on the client-reports page wrote a billing period whose `dateFrom` equalled the previous period's `dateTo` — **overlapping periods and double-counted hours**, in the same feature v1.22.2 was repairing; (3) the weekly plan's `for (d = rangeStart; d <= rangeEnd; d = addDays(d, 1))` emitted **26 Oct twice** — a duplicate day row and a duplicate React key — and ended a day short; (4) the Time Feed's week paging drifted to 23:00 and then named a **Saturday**, leaving every subsequent week a day off; (5) the dashboard's day stepper did nothing on that date.
  **The fix is a deletion, not a workaround.** All 24 sites now call the calendar-based `shiftDays` (`new Date(y, m, d + n)`), which lives in `format.ts` with `gantt.ts` re-exporting it for its own callers. Each site was read individually first: the swap is only safe because `shiftDays` zeroes the time of day, and **none** of the 24 relied on `addDays` preserving it — every one flows from `startOfWeek` (already midnight) or is consumed via `toISODate`. `addDaysIso` → `shiftDaysIso`, now using the shared `parseISO` instead of open-coding the parse.
  ⚠️ **THE TESTS WERE PROVEN NOT INERT, WHICH IS THE LESSON v1.22.2 PAID FOR.** `vitest.config.ts` now pins **`TZ=Asia/Jerusalem`**: it set nothing before, and **under `TZ=UTC` v1.22.2's whole "across a DST change" block passes with the bug present** — there is no transition to cross, so the guard against a two-hour outage was inert on CI and on any non-Israel machine. Then `shiftDays` was temporarily reverted to the ms arithmetic to confirm the new tests catch it: **the suite HUNG**, reproducing the original infinite loop. So the day-loop test now carries an iteration cap (a regression fails in a second instead of wedging CI), and `buildWeeks` throws a named error if its cursor ever fails to advance — a freeze has no console error and an idle CPU, which is exactly why it cost two hours.
  **213 tests** (was 204): `shiftDays` across the boundary in both directions, week-stepping that stays on Sunday, month/year/leap overflow, a bounded day walk, `periodBounds("This week")` keeping Saturday, and `shortRangeLabel`. `format.ts` had **no** test for its date helpers before this.
  **Also from the same review, quality only:** `report-snapshot.ts` had re-declared `parseISO` and `shiftDays` byte-identically from `gantt.ts` — one shared copy now. `buildWeeks` holds a **`Date` cursor** instead of re-deriving it from a string each turn (the shape the loop froze in) and answers "does this week have hours?" with **one forward pointer** over the already-sorted dates instead of rescanning every entry per week — it was O(238 columns × ~6,000 entries). The `customWeeks` ternary collapsed to one concatenation. `shortDate`/`periodLabel` were duplicated between `report-snapshot.ts` and the client-reports page, labelling the same screen — now one `shortRangeLabel` in `format.ts`, formatting directly rather than slicing tokens off `formatFeedDate`. ⚠️ Named `shortRangeLabel`, **not** `rangeLabel`: `period-math.ts` already exports that for naming a period selector ("Last week"), and `gantt.ts` has `dateRangeLabel`. And `report-table.tsx`'s ResizeObserver skips a write when the width is unchanged (observing the table's content-box delivers every height-only change, so folding a section re-wrote an identical width across ~57,000 cells) and observes the table **by ref** — `el.firstElementChild` silently un-observes it the moment anything else is added inside the scroller, with no type error.
  **Verified in the running app, signed in.** Time Feed paged 44 weeks back to `SUN 19` Oct 2025, then **onto** the transition: `SUN 26 … SAT 1`, a full Sun–Sat week, still Sunday-anchored six weeks later (`SUN 7` Dec). Weekly plan extended to **378 consecutive days, 7 Sep 2025 → 19 Sep 2026** — crossing both the October clocks-back and the March clocks-forward — with **zero duplicate days, zero gaps, `2025-10-26` appearing exactly once**, no React key warnings. Client reports: DualBird 58 columns ending `2 Aug – 8 Aug · 9 Aug – 15 Aug · 16 Aug – 22 Aug` under an **August** heading totalling 33.25h, proxy bar **8140/8140**; Blazepod 242 columns, 240 rows, 58,322 cells, **31,791/31,791**, no hang. Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 37 warnings (baseline), 213 tests, production build clean.
  ⚠️ **NOT verified live, deliberately:** the billing-period boundary bug (2 above). Reproducing it end-to-end means dragging a divider onto a DST date, which **writes real billing periods to the database** — the arithmetic is covered by unit test instead. **NOT done:** `custom_weeks` is still a whole-list snapshot, so `handleEditColumnDates` stores a LONGER list on every save (55 → 58 → …) and a gap *inside* the stored span still cannot gain a column; the deeper fix is a per-column delta and needs a jsonb shape change with back-compat. `buildReportSnapshot`'s per-entry × per-week loop (~1.45M iterations on Blazepod) is untouched.
- 2026-08-21 — **v1.22.2 — a client report's week columns follow the hours again, and the frozen-column list stops being a one-way door.** No migration.
  **The report:** *"im on client reports page on dualbird tab, but no columns of augost at all… i did created a period of august but still weeks columns end on July."*
  ⚠️ **A PERIOD AND A COLUMN ARE INDEPENDENT — creating the August period could never have produced a column.** Columns come from the HOURS (`buildWeeks`), and a period only groups columns that already exist. DualBird had **33.3h logged in August** with entries to the 20th, so the hours were there; the columns were not.
  ⚠️⚠️ **THE REAL FAULT: `handleEditColumnDates` SNAPSHOTS THE WHOLE RENDERED COLUMN LIST INTO `report_links.custom_weeks`, AND THAT LIST THEN *REPLACED* THE AUTOMATIC WEEKS FOR GOOD.** So the first time anyone nudged ONE column's dates, that client's report timeline froze on that day, silently and permanently — nobody chose to store a list, and nothing said it had happened. DualBird carried 55 stored columns ending 31 Jul. A hand-edited list is now an **override, not a freeze**: the edits are kept and the weeks after the last stored column are appended, which also means the next date edit persists them. Verified on the real data through the real `buildReportSnapshot`: **55 → 58 columns, with 33.25h landing in the three new ones** (independently matching the 33.3h measured in the DB).
  The appended run starts the day AFTER the stored list ends, so it can never overlap the column before it; only that first bucket can be partial, and it obeys the same "only weeks with hours" rule as every other (a partial bucket with no hours is correctly absent). `buildWeeks` is exported and has **9 tests** — it had none, which is how this survived.
  ⚠️⚠️⚠️ **AND THEN I FROZE THE WHOLE PAGE WITH AN INFINITE LOOP, FOR TWO HOURS, ON A BUG OF MY OWN MAKING.** Selecting DualBird — later Blazepod too — wedged the tab: no console error, dev server at 0% CPU. **`format.ts`'s `addDays` is MILLISECOND-BASED (`getTime() + days * DAY_MS`), so it is an hour short across a clocks-back transition.** My rewritten walk re-derived its cursor from a date STRING each turn: `to = toISODate(addDays(startOfWeek(from), 6))` lands at 23:00 on the **Friday** in late October, `from = to + 1` gives Saturday, and the next turn recomputes the same Friday — so `from` never advances. Fixed with calendar arithmetic (`new Date(y, m, d + n)`) local to this module. ⚠️ `addDays` itself is deliberately NOT changed: it is used app-wide, including for pixel maths where ms-based is correct.
  ⚠️ **WHY IT LOOKED CLIENT-SPECIFIC, which is what cost the time:** Anchor's hours are August-only and never cross a DST change, so it worked. DualBird and Blazepod span October, so they hung. **And my own unit tests used August dates, so they passed while the page froze.** Three tests now straddle 26 Oct 2025 and one spans a full year.
  ⚠️ **Two false eliminations of mine, recorded because the procedure is the lesson.** (1) I "reverted my change" by restoring the call site but LEFT the rewritten `buildWeeks` in place. (2) I cleared `custom_weeks` to null to isolate data from code — which sends the code down the AUTO path, i.e. straight through the rewrite. That run was evidence *for* my code being at fault and I recorded it as clearing it. **Check which code path a test actually takes before believing what it eliminates.** What finally cracked it was Nitsan's observation that **live v1.22.1 worked on every tab** — prod runs the committed code, so the fault could only be in the working tree.
  **Read from the Performance capture, and worth knowing how to read:** `Run microtasks → processRootScheduleInMicrotask → flushSyncWorkAcrossRoots_impl → renderRootSync → ReportTable`, plus `renderWithHooksAgain`, all inside ONE long task. **Microtasks drain within a task, so an infinite microtask/render loop appears as a single enormous task, not as thousands of repeats** — which is why the first capture read like "one huge render" and I sized the table (73 rows × 58 columns = 4,234 cells) to disprove that.
  **Kept from the debugging, because they were real defects either way:** the pinned scrollbar's measurement no longer lives in React state at all — it writes straight to the DOM. As state it minted a new object every observation (re-rendering even when the numbers were identical) and it decided whether the bar EXISTED, so the measurement could change the width it was measuring. ⚠️ And the observer now watches **the TABLE as well as the scroller**: a scroller's own box does not change when its CONTENT widens, so switching from a 58-column client to a 238-column one left the proxy sized for the old one (measured 7,750px against a 31,791px table). Now 8,140/8,140 and 31,791/31,791.
  Verified signed in at 1440×900: **DualBird selects in 3ms** with 58 week columns ending `2 Aug – 8 Aug · 9 Aug – 15 Aug · 16 Aug – 22 Aug` under period headings May·June·July·**August**; Blazepod 238 columns and 240 rows, no hang. ⚠️ DualBird's `custom_weeks` was cleared during the investigation and **restored from a backup, verified byte-identical (55 columns)**. Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 37 warnings (baseline), **204 tests** (was 195), production build clean.
  **NOT done:** there is still no way to ADD a column by hand, so a period with no hours logged yet has no columns — an empty column would show nothing, and this fix removes the need in practice.
- 2026-08-21 — **v1.22.1 — the shared timeline on a phone, one wording for one control, and chips that behave like controls.** No migration. All three from Nitsan reading the shipped v1.22.0.
  ⚠️ **MEASURED FIRST, AND THE NUMBERS ARE WHY THIS IS A RELEASE:** at 375×812 the page spent **144px on the header (three rows) plus 144px on the type chips (three wrapped rows)** — 288px of chrome before the chart, on the page a client is most likely to open on a phone. Header is now **102px in two rows** (name + mark, then zoom + the colour switch) and the chips are **one 44px row that scrolls**, which gives the chart 560px. The three controls cannot share one line: zoom 154 + switch 112 + wordmark 96 does not fit 343px.
  **The chip row bleeds to the screen edges** (`-mx-4 px-4`) so a swipe has somewhere to go and the row does not read as clipped mid-chip; from `sm` it wraps exactly as before. Each chip keeps `shrink-0`, or a nowrap row squeezes them all instead of scrolling.
  ⚠️⚠️ **`hidden sm:block` ON `.brand-wordmark` DOES NOTHING, AND THIS IS THE SECOND TIME THIS PRECEDENCE TRAP HAS COST TIME HERE.** Both wordmarks stayed visible and the header stayed three rows. `.brand-wordmark` sets `display: inline-block` in **`globals.css`, which is UNLAYERED**, while Tailwind emits display utilities inside `@layer utilities` — so the unlayered rule wins whatever the specificity, exactly as with the 16px form-field rule (v1.12.0). **Put the responsive display on a WRAPPER, never on that class.** Verified after: phone mark 80px visible, desktop mark `display: none`.
  ⚠️ **A second wordmark instance is deliberate**, and would otherwise read as duplication worth removing: one DOM order cannot serve both a 2-column mobile grid and the desktop 3-track row that CENTRES the zoom — moving the single mark costs the zoom its centring. It is a CSS-mask span: no image, no request.
  Also confirmed the page still opens on **week** zoom below 640px, so a phone does not land on the five-day view that reads as an empty plan.
  **One wording for one control.** Nitsan: *"'Color by type' is the one."* The switch here said "Color types" and the studio's Show menu said "Colour by type"; **both now read `Color by type`**, so the product is no longer mixed on that word. (`whats-new.ts` still carries the old spelling inside a published alt-text describing a shipped screenshot — left alone, because that text describes what that image actually showed.)
  **Hover states on the type chips, in BOTH states** — a chip with none reads as a label rather than a control. On: `bg-background` with the stronger border (measured `rgb(240,241,250)` / `rgb(209,213,219)` against a resting white chip). Off: the dashed border goes solid and the text lifts faint → muted (`rgb(92,100,120)`).
  ⚠️ **Method note: my first off-state reading came back as `--foreground` and looked like a bug.** It was taken one call after the click, mid-`transition-colors`. **A computed colour read immediately after a state change is the animation, not the result** — read it again.
  Verified at 375×812 and 1440×900 on Anchor's real shared link. Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 37 warnings (baseline), 195 tests, production build clean.
- 2026-08-21 — **v1.22.0 — the shared client timeline gets controls of its own, loses the budget, and stops looking like a poorer relation of the studio's chart.** No migration.
  ⚠️ **THE BUDGET IS GONE FROM THE QUERY, NOT JUST THE VIEW.** Nitsan: *"remove its budget column."* `estimate_hours` is no longer selected in `gantt/[token]/page.tsx`, `budgetHours` is off `PublicGanttTask` entirely, and the column, the section/group roll-ups (`sumBudget`, deleted) and the tooltip band went with it. v1.8.0 had argued the other way — the budget is the scope the client already agreed to — and that argument loses to a simpler one: **a schedule answers WHEN, and a number of hours beside every row invites the one question the chart cannot answer, which is how many of them are gone.** Verified against the served HTML on Anchor's real link: `Budget`, `budgetHours` and `estimate_hours` appear **nowhere**. The page's comment block now says hours never leave the database in either direction, which is finally true rather than nearly true.
  **A type filter that IS the colour key.** Seven chips on Anchor — a colour dot plus the type's name, toggling that type out of the chart. Nitsan's brief was explicit that they be **spread, not folded into a menu**: the studio's chart hides this behind "Show" because that page has a dozen controls competing, whereas here there are two, the reader is a client looking once a week, and a filter they have to discover inside a menu is one they will not use. Switching QA off dropped **19 rows** and the section counts followed.
  ⚠️ **Untyped tasks get a REAL chip, labelled "Other".** Without one they would be unfilterable, and switching every named type off would leave rows on screen with nothing explaining why they survived. ⚠️ And switching *everything* off is **allowed** — the footnote changes to "Every type is switched off — turn one back on to see the plan". Refusing the last one would be a control that silently stops working, and a blank chart with no explanation reads as "there is no work" rather than "you hid it".
  **Placement, after two rounds of his corrections:** first above the card, then **below the table, right-aligned**, sharing one row with the footnote (which keeps the left). Below rather than above because the chart is what the client came for and a row of controls between the header and the plan pushes the plan down for a filter most readers never touch — and a colour key makes more sense *after* the thing it explains. `Show all` was removed at his word, then restored at his word as **the leftmost item**, appearing only once something is hidden: a reset belongs before the things it resets.
  **`🎨 Color types`, left of the wordmark.** ⚠️ **The studio's chart already has this exact control** — `plainBars`, labelled "Colour by type" in its Show menu — which is what settled an ambiguous brief without a round trip: same default, same plain rendering (`--color-surface` fill with a 1px `border-strong` inset ring), applied to bars **and** diamonds so a plain chart has no coloured marks left in it. ⚠️ With it off the chips keep filtering but **lose their swatches**: a coloured key beside plain bars claims a mapping the chart is not making. It sits in the header's right-hand track rather than beside the chips, because it governs how the chart is DRAWN — the same kind of setting as the zoom — while the chips are about which work is on screen. **Spelling note:** he asked for "Color types" and the studio menu says "Colour by type", so the product is now mixed on that word; his to settle.
  **Then: "compare ui looks of shared timeline to internal timeline."** He named one difference and there were three.
  ⚠️ **(1) No scroll shadows.** Both ported with the studio's own technique: the horizontal one is **ONE full-height gradient stuck at the pinned column's edge, NOT a box-shadow per row** — the per-row version came out broken by every row border it crossed — and the header's is a bottom shadow whose negative spread stops it smearing sideways across the column titles. Each edge stays silent until there is something behind it, so the shadow is information rather than decoration. Verified the gradient renders at **left: 293**, exactly the pinned column's right edge.
  **(2) Today was a 2px BRAND-BLUE line with no date chip.** It is now the studio's single object — a black tag holding the date, a tail pointing down out of it, and the line continuing from the tail into the chart. ⚠️ Black rather than blue for the reason v1.11.0 recorded, which applies here identically: **with a type colour on every bar, a blue vertical reads as one more category rather than as today.** The tail is why the chip lives in the RULER and not the chart — the date and the pointer have to be one piece.
  **(3) No past wash.** Everything before today is now shaded at 5%, which is what makes the today line read as *the edge of the past* rather than one more vertical in a chart full of them. The weekend shading beneath it is 4.5%, so a past weekend comes out a little darker still, which is true.
  ⚠️ **Method note worth keeping: I measured "no scroll shadow" three times and nearly went hunting for a bug in code that was correct.** The selector was wrong — **browsers normalise `rgba(0,0,0,0.075)` to `rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.075)` in the serialised `style` attribute**, so a `[style*=…]` substring match on the authored form finds nothing. Match on `linear-gradient` and read the rect instead. Also: the browser tool's wheel `scroll` did not drive this inner scroller at all, while setting `scrollLeft` did and fired React's `onScroll` correctly — check the handler is attached (`__reactProps$`) before doubting the state.
  **Verified on Anchor's real shared link** (7 types, 108 dated open tasks) and on No Traffic's (**1 type → no filter row rendered at all**, since a single-type plan needs neither a key nor a filter). Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 37 warnings (baseline), 195 tests, production build clean.
  **NOT done, deliberately:** mobile is unexamined for this round (standing rule — desktop first, then ask), and this page is the one a client is most likely to open on a phone; the chips and the colour toggle are 44px targets already, but the pinned column and the new chip row want measuring at 375px.
- 2026-08-21 — **v1.21.1 — four ways the app could silently lose work you had just typed. Nitsan hit the first one for real.** No migration.
  **The report, in his words: "i edited a task - adding brief text to it and created a link. but after few minutes my coleuge siad its not there - and when i entered i did not see it there and had to re-eneter that link"** — on two Anchor tasks, `85695175` (💻 Platform overview - wireframe) and `2a7caa78` (⚙️ How it works - wireframe).
  ⚠️ **HE WAS NOT IMAGINING IT, AND THE DATABASE SAID SO BEFORE ANY CODE WAS READ.** Both briefs held ONLY the original intake text from Einav Maya (266 / 259 chars, ending `Submitted by Einav Maya`), and each task had exactly ONE `links` row, created 12:09:45 and 12:10:21 local — the re-entry. Every one of the 30 rows in `links` and every row created since 16 Aug was checked: **no trace of a first attempt anywhere, and no stray text on any neighbouring Anchor task.** Since he entered it twice and only one round exists, the first round never reached Postgres. **That single fact is what ruled out "it saved but didn't display" and pointed at the cause below** — worth remembering as the first move next time: ask the DB whether the row exists before reading a line of UI code.
  **(1) THE CAUSE: A FILLED-IN LINK FORM WAS THROWN AWAY WHEN ITS HOST CLOSED.** `BriefModal`'s footer says **"Saves when you close · ⌘↵"** and `close()` honoured that — for the textarea. The `LinksEditor` sitting in the same modal did not participate at all: a title and a URL were committed ONLY by clicking "Add link" or pressing Enter in one of the two fields, and Escape, a backdrop click, ⌘↵ and the **Done button** each unmounted the form and discarded the contents — silently, no row, nothing in the console, no failed request to find. ⚠️ **Escape was the worst of them**: `Modal` listens on `window`, so the natural "dismiss this little form" key destroyed the whole modal. The same trap sat in the task pane, where "+ Add link" opens the form inline and closing the pane discarded it. ⚠️ **The giveaway that this was the mechanism rather than a failed write: the two surviving link titles are literally `"brief"` and `"Brief"`** — "adding brief text to it and created a link" is the title field and the URL field of that one form.
  **Fixed by making every exit path commit.** `LinksEditorHandle` gained `commitPending()`; `BriefModal.close()` and a new `closePane()` in the task pane call it. Escape inside the form now closes only the form (and saves), leaving the modal open. ⚠️ **`done` is a ref, not state, and it is load-bearing**: two paths can ask to commit — this form's Escape handler AND the host's close handler — and without a once-only guard they insert the same link twice. ⚠️ Add and edit are now mutually exclusive so `commitPending` can reach a SINGLE `formRef` rather than guessing which form is on screen. **Cancel is the only discard path left**, deliberately, and the only thing still dropped on the way out is a form whose URL won't normalise — there is no storable link there (`url` is NOT NULL) and no form left to show a message in.
  ⚠️ **`useImperativeHandle` on the form has NO deps array on purpose** — `submit` closes over `title`/`url`, so a `[]` handle would commit whatever was typed on the FIRST render, i.e. nothing. That would have been an invisible failure of the fix itself.
  **(2) A NEW ROW COULD BE WIPED OFF THE SCREEN BY A REFRESH THAT WAS ALREADY IN FLIGHT.** `wrote()` exempted inserts, on the reasoning that "a new row can't be clobbered — if the refresh lands first the row isn't in the snapshot yet and the callback appends it; if it lands after, the row is already there." **That covers two orderings out of three.** A cold refresh ISSUED BEFORE the insert and LANDING AFTER its callback carries a list that predates the row, and every apply path replaces wholesale (`setLinks(c.links)`, `setPlanEntries`, `setClients`, …) — so the row vanishes from the screen while sitting safely in the DB, until the next cold refresh happens to bring it back. It is the exact race `refreshVerdict`'s own ⚠️ note describes for updates ("a background refresh is a READ THAT WAS ISSUED IN THE PAST"); inserts were simply excused from it. Most likely trigger in real use: **returning to a backgrounded tab, which fires a cold refresh, and immediately doing the thing you came back to do.** New **`counting()`** in `store.tsx` does the same bookkeeping as `wrote` for a query rather than a callback, applied to **all 18 insert sites** (links, tasks, sections, groups, clients, tags, types, plan entries + columns, comments, time entries, billing periods, day states, dev items, timeline marks, both inserts in `approveRequest`). The 6 undo-restore inserts already went through `wrote()` and were left alone. ⚠️ It is added to the 17 `useCallback` dep arrays rather than suppressed — it is a stable `[]` callback, so including it is simply correct, and the warning count stays at the 37 baseline.
  **(3) ONE FAILED QUERY BLANKED EVERY LINK IN THE APP, WITH NO BANNER.** `fetchCold` fetched six tables through a bare **`.catch(() => [])`** so an unapplied migration degrades gracefully — and an empty list there is **indistinguishable from the truth**: it renders as "no links anywhere". A timeout, a 500, a 401 during a token refresh, or the 402 Supabase returns over quota each took every link off the screen until a later cold refresh happened to succeed. ⚠️ **The `time_entries` ladder twenty lines below has warned against exactly this since 0016** — *"Any other failure must NOT be read as 'the column is gone', or a network blip drops the `legacy` flag"* — the reasoning just never reached `links`, `timeline_marks`, `task_types`, `task_groups`, `client_billing_periods`, `plan_day_states`. New `optionalTable()` tolerates ONLY `isMissingSchema` (42703 / 42P01 / PGRST204); everything else propagates, so boot shows its error screen and a refresh keeps what is on screen. **This is the one that explains a colleague not seeing a link that IS in the database** — it was not what bit Nitsan this time, but it produces the identical symptom.
  **(4) THE BRIEF EDITOR COULD OPEN EMPTY OVER A TASK THAT HAS A BRIEF, AND THEN SAVE THAT.** `const [draft, setDraft] = useState(task.brief)` seeded once, and `task.brief` is `""` for every task until `loadTaskExtras` lands, because the snapshot query doesn't select the column. Open the modal in the beat before that returns and `draft` was `""` forever — the `loaded` gate hid the textarea until the brief arrived and then revealed it **empty**, and closing wrote that empty string over real text. Now adopted once, on the false→true edge only: after that `task.brief` can change again from a background refetch of a colleague's edit, and adopting THAT would wipe what the person here is typing. Nothing can have been typed before the edge, since the textarea wasn't rendered yet.
  **VERIFIED IN THE BROWSER, signed in as Nitsan, against the live DB on both incident tasks.** All three exit paths save — Escape (form closes, modal stays open, row in the DB), Done, and the task pane's ✕ — each confirmed as a real row rather than local state, all four surviving a full reload with positions densified 1–4, and Escape producing exactly ONE row. **Cancel still discards**: a form filled with both fields left nothing behind, and closing the pane afterwards did not resurrect it. ⚠️ **The Escape shielding was measured, not assumed** — React's root container here is `document` (App Router hydrates the whole document), which sits BELOW `window` on the bubble path, so a synthetic `stopPropagation` really does shield `Modal`'s listener; confirmed by probe on the real page and then by the modal staying open in use.
  ⚠️ **And (4) was reproduced rather than argued.** `fetch` was patched to delay ONLY the `select=id,brief` query, a task opened, and Edit clicked 184ms later — caught exactly: "Loading the brief…", no textarea, query still in the air. When it landed the box held the **real 259-character brief**; before the fix it would have been empty. Closed untouched, both briefs are byte-identical to the baseline taken first (`d35a670ecd89` / `cb5cb8b2d993`). **(2) and (3) cannot be driven from a browser** — one needs a refresh mid-flight, the other a failing query — so they rest on the tests and the code, and that is stated rather than glossed.
  **Test rows removed and the DB re-read**: three test links deleted gated on their exact URLs so nothing adjacent could be caught, both tasks back to their single real "Brief" link, briefs unchanged, `links` back to exactly **30 rows** — the count from before the session. Zero `example.com` rows anywhere. No console errors.
  **195 tests** (was 191). The four new ones pin the thing most likely to be silently undone: a missing table reads as no rows, while a code-less failure, a 402-shaped failure and a **permission error (42501)** all reject rather than blanking the table — every one of which passed before the fix. Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 37 warnings (baseline), production build clean.
  **NOT done, deliberately:** mobile is unexamined (standing rule — desktop first, then ask), though the brief editor is read-only on a phone so only the pane's link form is reachable there. `addLink` still appends nothing optimistically, so a failed insert shows the write-error banner and no row — correct, and untouched.
- 2026-08-19 — **v1.21.0 — every publish opens the link it just published, and the v1.20.0 round gets its cleanup pass.** No migration.
  **Publish now opens the report in a new tab**, Nitsan's ask, in his words: *"this way it will enforce me to check and see what im sending."* ⚠️ **The tab is opened BLANK, synchronously, before any `await`, and pointed at the report once the write lands.** It cannot simply be opened at the end: by then the `ensureLink` + snapshot write have spent the click's transient activation, so `window.open` counts as an unsolicited popup and Safari blocks it outright. Blank-then-navigate is the one shape that survives a popup blocker. The tab is closed again on every path that does not publish, so a failure never leaves a stray tab. ⚠️ **And the tab is pointed at the URL BEFORE the clipboard write, not after** — opening a tab moves focus, and `clipboard.writeText` rejects on an unfocused document, so awaiting the copy first would strand the tab on `about:blank` and swallow the toast. The copy is now non-fatal and the toast says whether it happened.
  ⚠️ **One real correctness gap closed: v1.20.0's own link-migration fix only covered one of the two paths.** That round added `preferLink` (published wins, else oldest) and used it on the Publish page — but `ensureClientReportLink` in `src/lib/report-links.ts`, which is what the **"Client report" and copy-link buttons on the client view** actually hand a client, still ordered `created_at desc` and still inserted without asking the database first. So the exact bug that round called its correctness find was fully reachable through the other button. The rule now lives once as **`canonicalReportLink` in `src/lib/db.ts`**, both paths resolve through it, and four cases in `db.test.ts` pin published-beats-newer and oldest-otherwise.
  **~60 lines of unreachable table layout deleted.** `showSection` was `!showSectionTotals` and both call sites pass `showSectionTotals`, so it was a compile-time `false`: the Section `<th>` (with a resize handle for a column that could never render), two `<td>`s, `secW` and five interlocking width/offset expressions were dead the moment (3) above shipped. The grouped layout is the only layout now, and the frozen-column offsets no longer have a second configuration nobody can reach to verify.
  **Wasted work removed from the report table.** `gridRows.indexOf(t)` per row made rendering quadratic (~45k reference comparisons at 300 rows) → a `Map` keyed on `t.id`, which also drops a silent dependency on object identity that any stray `.map()` in the pipeline would have turned into `-1` with no type error. `latestMinutes` rescanned every week the client ever logged, three times per task (row cell, section subtotal, grand total) → the in-period week indices are computed once and cached per task. `periodTotals` summed all ~100 columns when the footer reads only the ~4 visible ones → keyed to the visible set, which matters most with `Latest period only` on. The `rowVisible` filter ran twice over every task → once.
  **Duplication folded up:** one shared `ViewToggle` (`dim` + `touch`, both appearances preserved exactly) instead of a copy per toolbar that had already diverged on the 44px touch target; `toggleIn` replacing four inline copies of the membership ternary; `ReportViewFlags` named once in `types.ts` instead of the shape spelled out in four places including an `as` cast that would have kept claiming two booleans after the column grew a third; one `fmtEst` for the estimate rounding rule; the month name computed once per bucket. `MiniColumns` now keys on the `YYYY-MM` bucket id rather than the month label — (4) above deliberately made that label ambiguous, and it was the React key.
  ⚠️ **Deliberately NOT done, and why.** Wrapping the derivation block in `useMemo`: the fixes above remove the asymptotic and large constant-factor costs, a memo would not stop the resize-mousemove re-render (that re-renders regardless), and keying it correctly fights `foldedSections`' unstable identity — low remaining benefit, real staleness risk. Event delegation for cell selection: mooted by the above, and a behaviour-risky rewrite of working drag logic. **The one worth picking up next: stamp `periodIndex` / `latestPeriodIndex` onto the snapshot in `buildReportSnapshot`** — "which period does this column belong to" is currently answered by a start-date rule in one place and a week-overlap rule in another, and they can disagree invisibly; it was left alone because it changes the published snapshot shape.
  Publish still records the **stored** filters, not the effective ones, so publishing while `Show all` is lit ships the filters you are not looking at. Left as-is on purpose — `Show all` is documented as temporary with the filters restored after, so the stored value is the studio's real intent — but flagged here because it is a genuine judgement call and a one-line change if Nitsan wants the other reading.
  ⚠️ **Gates only, no browser check.** tsc clean, 191 tests (4 new), eslint 0 errors at the same 53-warning baseline, build clean. The cleanup is provably render-identical — `leadingCols`, `colSpan`, the widths and the footer label all collapse to exactly what `showSectionTotals: true` already produced — but **the new tab was never actually clicked**: it needs an admin session on a real client, and Nitsan said "no need to check it for me thanks". Popup-blocker behaviour and the focus/clipboard interaction are exactly the kind of thing that only shows up in a real browser, so if a publish ever lands on a blank tab, that is where to look. Same posture as v1.1.0/v1.1.1.
- 2026-08-19 — **v1.20.0 — the client report becomes a tool you can read: filters, folding, cell totals, frozen first columns, and the view the studio publishes is the view the client opens.** Migration **0031 APPLIED by Nitsan** (`report_links.view_flags` jsonb).
  **Nitsan's ask, in his words: "lets help clear the noise for a user."** Everything below came from him reading the shipped page across several rounds, so the shape is his and the reasoning is recorded per item.
  ⚠️ **THE VIEW FILTERS ARE DELIBERATELY *NOT* `hiddenColumns`/`hiddenTaskIds`, AND THAT SEPARATION IS THE WHOLE DESIGN.** Those two lists are censorship: `sanitizeSnapshot` physically removes those rows and columns server-side before a client's browser sees them (v0.99.3). Had the new filters reused them, narrowing your own view to one period would have silently shipped that to the client on the next publish. They are a THIRD thing — `view_flags`, DEFAULTS the client can switch off — and the toolbar says so out loud: "These only change your view — the eye icons are what the client sees."
  **Three filter pills, buttons rather than a dropdown** (his open question). Three options, flicked constantly while reading, and their state is the thing you need to see; a dropdown hides exactly that. **`Latest period only`** cuts the columns to the latest payment period's weeks. **`Only rows with hours`** drops tasks with nothing logged — ⚠️ measured against **the columns currently on screen**, per his correction, so it narrows further when the period filter is on and a column hidden from the client can never keep a row alive. **`Show all`** does not overwrite the other two, it SUSPENDS them: they render dashed-outline while overridden so the state you will return to stays legible. Verified the round trip — 8 columns / 102 rows → 7 / 4 → Show all → 8 / 102 → off → back to exactly 7 / 4 with both pills still set.
  ⚠️ **"Latest", never "active".** An earlier round used the period containing TODAY, which is a different thing the moment a period exists ahead of time — and fatal on a published report, since a frozen link would total a different period next month with nobody republishing it. One rule everywhere now: latest by end date.
  **Sections fold, and carry a subtotal row** — estimate, total, every week and the period column. A folded section keeps showing its numbers and an `Unfold N sections` shortcut appears. The grand total deliberately still counts folded rows: folding is collapsing for reading, not filtering.
  **Cell selection like a spreadsheet.** Click, drag a rectangle, shift-click to extend, Escape to clear, with a big bold bubble showing the total plus how many cells carried hours. Verified against hand arithmetic: a 12-cell drag over `2h, 28h, 22.5h, 5.5h` and eight blanks read **58h · 4 of 12 cells · avg 14.5h**. ⚠️ **The selection carries the SIGNATURE of the layout it was made against** (folded set + both filters); a stale signature reads as no selection, rather than being cleared from an effect — folding renumbers the grid, and there must be no render in between where old coordinates address new cells. Also ⚠️ the bubble is `fixed` and a SIBLING of the scroll box: inside it, it would be clipped, which is the same trap as everything else in this file.
  **The first four columns are frozen and resizable** (`colw.client-report-lead`, `useColWidths`/`ResizeHandle`). Verified scrolled 9,000px sideways on Blazepod's **31,851px-wide, 243-column** table: the lead headers hold at x = 245/385/645/721, exactly the scroller's edge plus the cumulative widths. ⚠️ **The separator on the last frozen column is an inset SHADOW, not a border** — `border-collapse: collapse` does not paint borders on sticky cells reliably. ⚠️ And a sticky offset can only be computed from widths we control, which is why those four are definite and the rest are auto.
  **The horizontal scrollbar is pinned to the bottom of the screen** — on a long report the table's own scrollbar is thousands of pixels below the fold. A sticky proxy strip whose only content is a spacer as wide as the table, with the two scroll positions mirrored; the real scrollbar is hidden so there is only ever one. ⚠️ Measured via `ResizeObserver`, which delivers an initial callback on observe — so the measuring lives entirely in the callback rather than being called straight from the effect, which is what `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` is warning about.
  **Publishing now carries the filters, and the client gets controls of their own** — `Show all periods`, `Only rows with hours`, and section folding. Verified on the real Baseline link: 85 columns → 7 when the client narrows to the latest period, fold works with the section keeping its subtotal, and drag-select is correctly ABSENT for clients. ⚠️ **The consequence, stated because it is a real trade:** for a client to be able to REVEAL a period, its numbers have to be in the page. So a filter default is not concealment. That is consistent with v1.0.2's rule that hiding is a focus tool — but anything that must not be seen belongs in the eye icons, which are stripped server-side, and that was re-verified.
  ⚠️ **TWO PRE-MIGRATION HAZARDS, one of which would have taken down every client link.** The public report page had `view_flags` in its column list, and **a missing column NAMED in a select fails the whole query** — which there means `notFound()` on every client's report URL until the SQL ran. It reads `*` now. And the publish WRITE hits `PGRST204` (v1.19.4's lesson: a missing column on a write is not `42703`), which would have broken the one button this page exists for — it goes through `updateWithOptional`, so the report publishes and the toast says the filters were not saved. Both were verified with 0031 deliberately unapplied.
  **Six bugs Nitsan hit while using it, all fixed in the same round:**
  ⚠️ **(1) The `…` client menu never appeared.** It was rendering and being CLIPPED: it lived inside the tab strip's `overflow-x-auto`, and **a box with `overflow-x: auto` computes `overflow-y` to auto as well**, so a panel dropping below the strip is cut off rather than overlaying the page. Moved out of the scroller, which also stops it scrolling away with the tabs. It was doubly invisible — even open it holds only one client, since every other client is already a tab.
  ⚠️ **(2) A long task name rendered as three dots and nothing else.** Not a width bug: the cell had `truncate` and the name was an inline-block `<button>`, and `text-overflow: ellipsis` cannot break an atomic inline box, so the browser replaced the whole button with the ellipsis. The truncation now lives on the NAME inside a flex row, with `title` for the hover reveal he asked for. Verified: `Migration & onboarding - prep for dev` renders 169px of a 233px string as text. ⚠️ Latent shape, not new — the definite widths just made it hit constantly.
  **(3) The Section column was empty on every task row** once each group had a heading line, so it is gone and the task names indent under the heading (`pl-7`). The freed 140px went to the Task column (260 → **340**), which took Baseline from truncating to **0 of 77 names truncated**.
  **(4) `Hours by month`: labels above the bars, always.** They were outside on short bars and INSIDE the top in white on tall ones — the same value styled two ways by height, which reads as two kinds of number. Verified across Blazepod's 12 months: tallest 66px, shortest 5px, all 12 above, none white. `MiniColumns` has exactly one caller, so nothing else could move. Month names are three letters with **no year**, and the year moved into the hover (`Aug 2026: 66h`) rather than being dropped — with 12 buckets a month name can repeat.
  ⚠️ **(5) The folder tab was not attached to its own pane** — 12px of gap and a fully-rounded card, so it hung above a separate object. The strip is now the panel's top rail: `-mb-3` cancels the page gap, the rail carries the left/right/top border and NO bottom one, the panel carries no TOP one, and the active tab is the same `bg-surface` as the panel — so the only thing dividing them is the background change, which the active tab interrupts. Measured tab bottom **194.0** = panel top **194.0**, identical fill, no border between. ⚠️ **Deliberately NOT the usual `-mb-px` overlap on the active tab** — inside that scroller it would be clipped. Letting the background change do the work sidesteps the whole family of bug.
  ⚠️ **(6) The black ✕ on a tab was trimmed** — it hangs 4px above its tab and the scroller had no padding to hang into (same overflow-y-auto cause as (1)). `pt-1.5` gives it 6px: the 4px overhang plus **2px of slack**, because `pt-1` measured headroom of exactly 0 and would silently re-clip on any later nudge. Verified on a real browser hover, full 16×16 circle inside the box.
  **A cell hover state**, his ask: a weak wash of the SELECTED colour (`brand-soft/40`), so the hint reads as a preview of selecting rather than as its own state.
  **Widths:** the pane column and the periods table are flex siblings, so one number moved both — pane column 360 → **504px (+40%)**, periods table 1111 → **967px**.
  ⚠️ **AND A CORRECTNESS FIND: A CLIENT'S REPORT LINK COULD MIGRATE.** The links map kept the **most recently created** active link per client. Deterministic, and wrong: a report URL is permanent and gets pasted into a client's email, so a duplicate created later meant the app silently published to the NEW token while the client kept opening the old one and never saw another update. The rule is now **published wins, otherwise oldest** (only a published link can be in a client's hands; the oldest has had the most chance to be shared). ⚠️ **And the cause of the duplicates was found:** `ensureLink` checked only the in-memory map, which is filled by an effect — so Copy-link or Publish pressed before it resolved INSERTED a second link. It asks the database first now. Verified on real data: Copy link on Blazepod hands out `7eea3d03…` (10 Jul) where it previously used `492b35cd…` (11 Jul). **Blazepod and No Traffic each still carry a redundant inactive-able duplicate** — SQL to retire them was given to Nitsan and is optional, since the code picks correctly either way. Nothing was ever shared with a client, confirmed by him.
  Verified signed in at 1440–1743px against real data, and on the real Baseline client link. Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 37 warnings (baseline), 187 tests, production build clean.
  ⚠️ **Method notes worth keeping.** (a) **A PostgREST filter on an EMBEDDED resource is silently ignored without `!inner`** — `.ilike("clients.name", …).limit(1)` returned an arbitrary row, so a verification write landed on a different client's link than the one asked for (harmless here, both unpublished, and reverted). Dangerous shape for any script that writes. (b) **Dev-server hot reload preserves component state across an edit**, so a filter pill read `true` for a client whose flags were null and looked exactly like a broken restore; a hard reload read correctly. (c) Two of my own automated drags wrote column widths into Nitsan's `localStorage`; both were cleared.

- 2026-08-18 — **v1.19.12 — a 402 from Supabase now says so, instead of the studio silently going stale.** No migration. Asked for after a forced-402 experiment showed what the restriction due **12 Sep** would actually look like: **an already-open tab keeps working and quietly serves frozen figures indefinitely.** Background refresh failures are deliberately silent — normally a dropped connection, nothing anyone can act on — so the only trace was `[refresh] failed … Payment Required` in the console, and the only visible clue was the sync dot's "Updated 14:32" tooltip failing to advance. The next person to notice would have been **a client opening a broken report link**, since `report/[token]`, `gantt/[token]` and the intake API all read through the service role.
  **`isServiceBlocked(e)` in `db.ts`, and a banner.** ⚠️ **Deliberately NARROW, and that narrowness is the feature** — promoting every failed tick to a banner is how people learn to ignore banners. Only Supabase's quota refusal qualifies: persistent, actionable (billing), and otherwise invisible. Ordinary network failures, 401/403/500, and a missing column (which must keep degrading quietly down the ladder) all stay silent, pinned by test.
  ⚠️ **A 402 carries NO Postgres `code`** — it never reaches Postgres — so `DbError` gained a **`status`** field and `fetchAll` now passes the response status through. ⚠️ The two queries in `fetchHot` built directly rather than through `fetchAll` (the 400-row feed and `task_requests`) were throwing a plain `Error`, which **drops the status**; since any member of that `Promise.all` can be the one to reject first, they now go through a new **`assertOk`** so all paths carry it. The message is a secondary fallback for anything that still loses it.
  **The banner is NOT dismissible and NOT a reload prompt** — reloading cannot fix it, and a dismissed banner would restore exactly the invisibility this exists to remove. It **clears itself on the first refresh that succeeds**, so it cannot linger after the bill is paid. ⚠️ Placed bottom-centre and lifted clear of the phone's bottom nav (`bottom-20 md:bottom-4`) rather than as a bar at the top: the app header is sticky at `top-0` and the client page pins its own at `top-14`, so anything that pushed the layout down would knock that alignment out — see the z-index/sticky table in `client-view.tsx`.
  ⚠️ **The boot screen had to be fixed separately, because it returns BEFORE the banner ever renders.** Its copy blamed a dropped connection, which for a 402 would send someone to check their wifi for an hour; it now says the true thing when `serviceBlocked`. Role-gated copy: admins get "Usage has to be raised on the Supabase plan" plus a link to the org's usage page; designers get "Let an admin know" and no link.
  **Verified by forcing a 402 in the running app** (client-side `fetch` shim, nothing sent to the server): banner appears with the billing link as admin, appears with "Let an admin know" and NO link under `?viewAs=Nadav`, and **retracted itself** once service was restored, with the sync tooltip resuming and the data intact. **187 tests** (was 181). Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 37 warnings (baseline), production build clean.
  ⚠️ **Mistake worth recording: I overwrote `db.test.ts` with a heredoc** — the `ls` in the same command had already printed that it existed, and I didn't read the output before the write ran. Four `isMissingSchema` tests were destroyed, including the PGRST204 write-path case that v1.19.4 exists because of. Caught by the arithmetic — 181 − 4 + 6 = 183, and the file count stayed at 11 when a new file should have made it 12 — then restored from `git show HEAD:` and the new block appended, leaving a pure `+43/−1` diff. **Check whether a test file exists before `cat >` writes to it, and treat an unexpectedly small test-count increase as a missing file rather than a miscount.**
- 2026-08-18 — **v1.19.11 — `tasks` gets its own tier: the second half of the egress fix.** No migration. **The first fix worked and was not enough.** Measured on the Supabase dashboard 5 days after v1.18.2: workday egress **1,112 MB → 440 MB (−60%**, against the −57% predicted from the wire), and idle days — Fri/Sat, when nobody touches the app, so almost pure polling cost — **447 MB → 5.3 MB (−99%)**, which is the clean proof because no human activity confounds it. But the org sat at **9.99 GB against the 5 GB free allowance (200%, overage 4.99 GB)**, in a grace period with **projects restricted from 12 Sep 2026 and requests returning 402**. 99%+ of it is PostgREST — storage peaked at 14 MB, so attachments were never the problem.
  ⚠️ **The written plan's step 3 was wrong, and only measurement showed it.** It guessed the remaining candidates were "far smaller tables and unlikely to matter much". After the entries moved to cold, **`tasks` was 2,517 KB of a 2,861 KB tick — 88%, and 151 MB of the remaining 202 MB/hr.** `time_entries` was down to 15%, so the plan's own Fix 2 (splitting entries by client `archived`) had a ceiling of ~12% and was **dropped in favour of this**. Fitting the tier needs ~233 MB per working day.
  **`fetchTasks` is now its own function on `TASKS_EVERY_N_TICKS` (3 → ~3 min)**, and a cold refresh (boot, manual, back after 5+ min away) always takes it too. ⚠️ **The rows are UNCHANGED — only the cadence — and that was the whole decision.** 82% of tasks are `done` and 47% belong to archived clients, so fetching only the live subset would cut far more; but `tasks` is read by ~30 files and the store would have to UNION two sets, and **a union that is subtly wrong makes rows vanish from the UI, while a slower fetch cannot.** `mergeTasks` still receives the complete list every time it runs.
  ⚠️ **`fingerprint` took the same wound as in v1.18.2 and needed the same treatment.** It hashes task fields to answer "did somebody ELSE change something an undo step is sitting on?" — left reading `h.tasks`, two ticks in three would have compared a full list against an absent one and expired the undo history of anyone with a tab open, every minute. It now takes `{tasks, entrySums}` as a NAMED object rather than growing a third positional argument. ⚠️ **Both must come from the last SERVER response, never from local state** — hence `serverTasksRef`, written only by `applyTasks`, deliberately NOT mirrored from `tasks` state: your own optimistic edit is not a colleague's change.
  ⚠️ **The empty-studio auth canary was `tasks.length === 0`, which no longer arrives on every tick.** On hot-only ticks it is now "the 400-row feed AND the plan are both empty" — an AND, because either alone is legitimate (a quiet planning week) while both together cannot happen in a studio with ten years of history.
  **Two traps, both caught by tsc rather than by eye:** the row→`Task` mapping lived inside `fetchHot`'s return statement and would have been silently dropped by the extraction (leaving `DbRow[]` typed as `Task[]`), and **`refreshRef`'s own type annotation** had to learn the new flag or the interval could never ask for tasks.
  **MEASURED in the running app** over four consecutive ticks, `fetch` instrumented for response bytes: **370 KB · 370 KB · 2,892 KB (tasks, 5 pages) · 370 KB**. The fourth tick is the one worth waiting for — it proves the cycle RESETS rather than fetching every other tick. **1,211 KB/tick against 2,861 KB, −58%**; projected **440 → ~186 MB per workday**, under the 233 MB budget, and **1,112 → ~186 MB (−83%) across both fixes.** Also verified: search still finds tasks (so the tiered boot populates state), admin tiles still compute (41 tasks worked / 495.5h), the 2019 Quadream task opens with all 23 out-of-window entries and its Brief, no error banner, and **zero `[refresh]` "changed" lines across four ticks including a tasks tick** — with debug-level capture confirmed working first, so that negative means something.
  **Cost to the team:** somebody else's task edit takes up to 3 minutes to appear instead of 1. Own edits stay instant (optimistic), and the weekly plan stays on the 60-second tier. **`TASKS_EVERY_N_TICKS` in `store.tsx` is the single number to change** if 3 minutes reads wrong in use.
  **181 tests** (was 152), pinning what a future edit would break invisibly: a hot tick touches `tasks` zero times and fetches exactly four tables; `fetchTasks` keeps every migration's column and never selects `brief`; the print holds still on ticks 1–2 yet still catches a colleague's rename on tick 3. Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 37 warnings (baseline), production build clean.
  ⚠️ **STILL OVER QUOTA for the CURRENT cycle (to 5 Sep) — this cannot be un-spent, so the 12 Sep restriction risk stands regardless of the fix.** Supabase Pro is $25/mo for 250 GB and removes the cliff; recommended to Nitsan and his call. Watch the daily egress chart for the step down from 18 Aug.
