  ⚠️ **A measurement that lied, worth remembering:** I first tested for clipped selects with `scrollWidth > clientWidth` and got `false` for all five. **A native `<select>` truncates its selected option without ever growing `scrollWidth`** — that check only works on inputs and overflowing blocks. The screenshot was the evidence; the numbers agreed only about the widths.
  ⚠️ Also: a `//` line comment inside a JSX opening tag PARSES (between attributes you are in ordinary JS token space, so tsc and the build both accept it) but is not this file's style and reads as dead code — moved to `{/* */}` in child position twice.
  **Left alone, and stated rather than quietly skipped:** a long title still scrolls inside its own single-line `<input>` on a phone (307px shows ~38 characters) — inherent to an editable one-line field, and the alternative is a textarea that changes what the control IS. The submission pane below the list was already checked in v1.19.8 and fits.
  **Verified with a representative seeded pending brief** (files, a link, a 60-character title, a budget and a client date — the real queue was empty) at 375×812 and 1321px, then deleted: **0 pending briefs and no test rows left.** Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 37 warnings (baseline), 177 tests, production build clean.
- 2026-08-18 — **v1.19.8 — the mobile pass on v1.19.7. One of the two bugs never reached a phone; the other's fix arrived with a 16px tap target.** No migration.
  ⚠️ **THE MODAL FOCUS BUG COULD NOT HAPPEN ON A PHONE, and it is worth writing down WHY rather than "checked, fine".** It needed a caller that declares its close handler in its own component body, so a keystroke re-renders and re-runs `Modal`'s focus effect — and `onClose={close}` appears in exactly ONE file in the app (`brief-modal.tsx`). That editor is `isNarrow ? <div> : <button>` in the task pane: **on a phone the brief renders as plain text with no way to open the modal at all** (v1.12.0 made the pane read-only there). So no phone surface was affected, and the v1.19.7 fix is a pure improvement there rather than a repair.
  ⚠️ **The due-date hint DID have a mobile defect, and it was mine.** "The client asked for 27/8/26" puts the date in a `<button>` that resets the field, and inline text made that a **16px-tall tap target** — against the 44px floor the rest of this page has followed since v1.15.0. Fixed with **`inline-block px-1 py-3.5 -my-3.5 … sm:p-0 sm:m-0`**: the padding grows the hit area, the negative margin takes it straight back out of the layout, so the line still reads as one sentence rather than a button embedded in a paragraph. **Measured: 44px × 52px on a phone with the line still 16px tall; 16px and untouched at 1321px.**
  **Everything else measured clean at 375×812**: the hint is one line at 307px inside the card with no overflow, it stays hidden while the field still shows the client's date (so it is a signal, not a permanent row), and the submission pane's new `Wanted by` row fits at 96px label + 205px value with `document.scrollWidth` still 375 — the pane stacks under the list below `lg`, so it had room.
  ⚠️ **Noted, NOT fixed — pre-existing and outside this change:** on the pending card at 375px the three `<select>`s sit in a `grid-cols-2` and clip their own labels ("Section (optional)" and "Assignee (optiona…"), and the Budget/date row is tight. That predates today (v1.18.0's log already says the intake pane's mobile pass is outstanding), and the standing rule is desktop first then ask, so it is Nitsan's call whether the intake queue gets its own phone round.
  **Verified with a seeded pending brief** (the real queue was empty — ⚠️ Nitsan approved all four Partner Event briefs into tasks mid-session, confirmed against the DB rather than assumed) at 375×812 and 1321px, then deleted; **0 pending briefs left and no test rows.** Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 37 warnings (baseline), 177 tests, production build clean.
- 2026-08-18 — **v1.19.7 — two bugs Nitsan hit: every modal with a text field lost focus after ONE keystroke, and the client's requested due date was invisible on the submission record.** No migration.
  ⚠️ **THE FOCUS BUG WAS IN THE SHARED `Modal`, NOT IN THE BRIEF EDITOR, AND IT AFFECTED EVERY MODAL IN THE APP.** Reported as "when editing a brief in the popup, after typing 1 letter the focus is going out of the text field — so i can edit only 1 letter each time and get the focus back using my mouse". `Modal`'s focus effect had deps `[onClose]`, and `BriefModal` declares its `close` handler in its component body (it has to — it saves the draft on the way out), so **every keystroke re-rendered, minted a new function identity, and re-ran the effect, whose first line is `card.current.focus()`** — pulling focus out of the textarea and onto the dialog. Any caller whose own state changes while a field is focused had the same bug; the brief editor is simply the one with a 45vh textarea, so it was unusable rather than merely annoying.
  **Fixed at the shared level, in two parts.** (1) The focus effect is now **mount-only (`[]`)**. (2) It **yields to a child that already holds focus** (`if (!el.contains(document.activeElement))`) — which is what makes `autoFocus` on an inner input work at all: React applies `autoFocus` during commit and this effect runs after it, so focusing unconditionally had ALSO been defeating it, and every modal in the app was opening with focus on the card instead of its field. Measured after the fix: the brief modal opens with `document.activeElement === textarea`, and 13 typed characters all landed with focus never leaving.
  ⚠️ **The Escape listener is now its own effect, registered once, reading the latest handler through a ref.** Re-registering a window listener on every render is harmless in itself — the harm was that tying it to `onClose` dragged the focus call into the same re-running effect. Keeping them apart means neither can break the other again. ⚠️ The ref is written in an EFFECT, not during render: a ref written while rendering is a React violation and the linter flags it; the gap cannot matter because Escape only arrives from a real keypress, always after paint.
  **The due date.** ⚠️ **v1.16.0 deliberately removed the `due …` line from the brief TEXT** — Nitsan deleted it by hand in all three sample briefs, because the task it becomes has a Dates field of its own — but the intake queue's submission pane has no such field, so **the one date the client asked for appeared nowhere on the only screen that records what they actually sent** ("was hard to check if the date is correct and compare it to original client input"). Added as a **`Wanted by` field**, not put back into the prose, which respects that earlier decision. Source is `requested_due_date`, which the studio never writes — approving sets the TASK's `due_date` instead — so it stays the client's input for good.
  **And on the pending card, where it actually gets compared:** the editable due-date input is pre-filled from the client's date and then changed freely, so the moment it is touched the original is off the screen. A line under it now reads "The client asked for 23/8/26" — ⚠️ **only when the field no longer says it**, so it is a signal rather than another row on every card — and the date is a BUTTON that puts it back, since noticing the difference and wanting the original are usually the same thought.
  Verified in the browser signed in: the modal focus test above (nothing was saved — the test text was removed and the task's brief re-read at its original 1219 characters), and `Wanted by 23/8/26` on the Flags submission. ⚠️ Note the queue emptied mid-session because **Nitsan approved all four Partner Event briefs into tasks while this was being built** — checked against the DB rather than assumed, since a vanished queue is exactly what a bug would look like. Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 37 warnings (baseline), 177 tests, production build clean.
  **NOT done:** mobile is unexamined for both (standing rule — desktop first, then ask), though neither change adds anything a phone renders differently.
- 2026-08-18 — **v1.19.6 — cleanup pass over the revisions round. No behaviour change, and one latent failure removed.** No migration.
  ⚠️ **`db.ts` NOW OWNS THE WRITE-SIDE SCHEMA LADDER — `updateWithOptional()` — and the reason is that the pattern had been hand-rolled FOUR TIMES IN TWO RELEASES and the fourth forgot the guard entirely.** `markRevisionReviewed` wrote both of 0030's columns with no fallback, so in the window before Nitsan ran that SQL every "I've read it" click would have raised the red write-error banner. Found by the altitude review, not by me. The helper takes `required` and `optional` fields, drops the optional set and retries **only** on a missing-schema error, and is documented not to be used for a value the app then relies on (PGRST204 is also what PostgREST returns while its schema cache is stale after a DDL change). Three sites — the seen route, `approveRequest` and `markRevisionReviewed` — are now one call each; `db.ts` already owned the read-side `fetchAll`/`isMissingSchema` ladder, so this is the symmetric half it was missing.
  **The seen receipt does ONE round trip, not two.** The stamp and the 0030 baseline went as separate updates; they travel together now, with the FALLBACK being the thing that is separate rather than the write.
  ⚠️ **The what-changed panel's four blocks became `AddedItem`/`RemovedItem` — and they had ALREADY DRIFTED.** A file checked `isSafeUrl` before rendering an anchor while a client-supplied LINK never rendered as one at all, safe or not; the "Add to the task" button was seven lines of JSX duplicated verbatim. One component makes the inconsistency structurally impossible instead of something a reader has to notice, which is the same argument the parameterised email editor is built on.
  **`started` folded into the `editing` object** on the intake form. It was a second `useState` only ever read inside `{editing && …}`, so three call sites had to remember to clear both by hand — the exact shape that produces a stale-flag bug later.
  **The store derives `updatedRequests` once.** The shell badge, the home banner and the queue each ran their own `taskRequests.filter(needsReview)`; three copies of one question is how a badge comes to disagree with the list it points at (same reason `task-rollup.ts` and `taskHoursDone` were extracted in earlier releases).
  **`CONTACT_FIELDS` moved to `intake-fields.ts`.** `brief-diff.ts` was re-asserting "name/email/company describe the person, not the work" that the form's own first step already encodes. Also narrowed the `diffBriefs` memo key to the two fields it reads.
  **Skipped, with reasons:** routing `brief-diff` through `readSubmission` — different contracts, that one validates a COMPLETE submission and returns null on junk, while a diff must tolerate two partial blobs and always produce a comparison, so reusing it would turn "half a diff" into "no diff"; extracting a shared clipboard hook — the pattern is hand-rolled ~6 times app-wide and the two in this file have genuinely different shapes, so it is its own cleanup; and making the two email templates a registry keyed by kind — two kinds is not a table, and the parameterised editor already prevents the drift that mattered.
  **Verified in the browser against a seeded revision of an approved brief**: the out-of-fold prompt, the panel (`old goal` → `new goal`), **Add to the task** working then disabling to "On the task", and "I've read it" re-baselining — with the task's own brief still reading `REFINED` and the added file present in `links`. Test rows and task deleted, queue re-read clean. Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 37 warnings (baseline), 177 tests, production build clean.
- 2026-08-18 — **v1.19.5 — a client's later change to a brief is a REVISION the studio reviews, and it can never overwrite work already done.** Migration **0030 APPLIED by Nitsan** (`task_requests.answers_ack` + `acked_at`, plus a partial index on `edited_at`).
  **The two asks, both Nitsan's, and the second is the one that shaped the design.** (1) *"if an intake is marked handled and is updated by client you need to mark it as Updated, mark it as unhandled and notify me on the dashboard."* (2) *"what happens if i get an update to a brief i already turned into a task, edited its text and refined it as i wish — no maybe i get new files or updated file to the one i already got, maybe its an update to text i already refined and rewritten? i want to see what changed and deal with changes with carefulness not erasing edits i already made."*
  ⚠️ **THE WHOLE ANSWER IS A BASELINE: `answers_ack`.** Without a record of what the studio last READ, the app can only say "something changed"; with one it can say exactly what. It is snapshotted when a brief is marked seen or approved, and **never** by a client edit. **One rule for every status** — `needsReview` is `edited_at > acked_at` — rather than a flag per case, which is how a pending brief and an approved one come to disagree. Presented to Nitsan as three options for how much to automate and two for whether post-approval edits are allowed at all; he chose "show the diff, you apply" and "yes, as a revision".
  **What a revision does NOT touch, which is the point:** the task, its brief, its status, `created_at`, `client_notified_at`, or `answers_ack`. ⚠️ **Verified against a real approved brief with a task whose brief read `MY REFINED TEXT — do not lose this`**: after the client's edit the task was byte-identical, the baseline still held the OLD goal, the status was still `approved`, and `seen_at` was null.
  ⚠️ **`seen_at`/`seen_by` are CLEARED on an edit** (ask 1). A brief marked read is not read once the client rewrites it, and leaving a ✓ on it is how a change goes unnoticed. Surfaced in four places, all driven by the same `needsReview`: the **header bell** as its own line ("*N briefs changed by the client*" — counted SEPARATELY from pending, because a revision of an approved brief is not pending and the old count could never see it); the **sidebar Intake badge** and its aqua highlight; a **banner above the KPI tiles on the admin home**, naming the briefs, because a badge in the header is easy to walk past; and in the queue. **All of it disappears once the changes are read** — the badge means "unread", not "ever edited".
  ⚠️ **A revised brief that is already a task hides inside the collapsed "Show handled" list** — the one place an update must not be able to hide, since that is where the studio has already drawn something. So the count is stated OUTSIDE the fold as a button that opens the list ("✎ 1 handled brief has been updated by the client — show it"), and the row carries an `updated` pill beside its status.
  **The "what changed" panel** shows each altered answer with the old struck through above and the new below, plus **Copy new** — so folding a change into a rewritten task is a decision, never an overwrite. ⚠️ **The only automated action is adding a NEW FILE (or link) to the task**, because attaching one is additive and cannot lose anything anybody wrote; the button disables itself once that URL is already on the task. ⚠️ A file the client REMOVED is shown struck through with no action at all — them withdrawing it says nothing about whether the studio should drop what it has. **"I've read it"** re-baselines and writes nothing else: reviewing a revision is not approving the brief and not telling the client anything, and folding those together is how an admin approves something they only glanced at.
  **`src/lib/brief-diff.ts` is a pure module with 10 tests**, because its answer decides whether an admin's rewriting survives; the UI renders a decision rather than making one. ⚠️ **Files are matched by URL, not by NAME** — a client sending a second `logo.png` from another folder is ordinary, and a duplicated brief deliberately shares the same object. ⚠️ **Name, email and company are never reported as changes**: identity is answered on the details step by whoever is filling the form in now, so a colleague sending a revision would otherwise flag them every time. ⚠️ Whitespace-only differences are not changes. ⚠️ **A brief with no baseline says so** ("No earlier version was recorded — read the submission itself to be sure") rather than rendering an empty diff, which reads as "nothing changed" — the one wrong conclusion here, since it invites approving a revision unread. That applies to every brief predating 0030, including Dor's four current ones, until their next acknowledgement.
  **The receipt email now says the right thing** — Nitsan: *"the email for 'tell a client weve seen it' should say we seen the update in that case."* A second template (`app_settings.intake_seen_update_email`, its own default, its own editor in Settings) reading "your changes to X reached us". ⚠️ **A separate template, not one message with a word swapped**: the first tells someone their brief arrived, this one tells someone a change to a job the studio may already be working on was picked up. ⚠️ **The fallback follows the KIND**, so an unset update template does not quietly tell a client their brief "reached us" a second time. ⚠️ **AND THE SEND-ONCE GUARD HAD TO BE SCOPED.** It was `client_notified_at` alone — right for stopping a double click or two admins mailing the same acknowledgement twice, and it would have refused the update receipt **forever**. It now refuses only when there is no unacknowledged edit. The Settings editor was PARAMETERISED rather than copied, so the placeholder list, the escaping and the save behaviour cannot drift between the two.
  ⚠️ **The PGRST204 trap again, and it would have broken APPROVING.** Both the seen route and `approveRequest` now write the 0030 columns, and a missing column on a WRITE reports `PGRST204`, not the `42703` a SELECT raises. Without a fallback an unapplied 0030 would have made the receipt button and the approve button fail — the queue's whole purpose. Both step down one rung, via a shared `ackUpdate` in the route and an inline retry in the store. **Verified with 0030 deliberately unapplied before it was run.**
  ⚠️ **One lint warning was a real bug**: parameterising the Settings editor left the load effect reading props that were not in its dependency array, so a change of key would have kept showing the other template's saved text. Fixed rather than suppressed.
  Verified in the browser signed in as admin: the home banner naming the brief, the bell and both badges counting it, the out-of-fold button opening the handled list, the panel showing `flags for the stage` → `flags for the stage AND the entrance` with the removed file struck through, and **"I've read it" clearing the panel, both badges and the count in one click** with the baseline re-snapshotted in the DB. **All test rows and the test task were deleted and the queue re-read — only Dor's real briefs remain.** Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 37 warnings (baseline), **177 tests**, production build clean.
  **NOT done, deliberately:** a revision does not re-open a brief the studio REJECTED; there is no per-field "apply to the task" for text (copy is the deliberate friction — the task's words are the studio's); and the studio is not told WHICH files a client removed from a task that already links them, since nothing here deletes a link.
- 2026-08-18 — **v1.19.4 — a returning client can duplicate or edit a brief they've already sent, and orphaned uploads get swept.** Migration **0029 APPLIED by Nitsan** (`task_requests.edit_key` + `edited_at`, plus an index on the key — two plain columns; `task_requests` is admin-only for ALL operations under 0001 and every public path is a service-role route, so no policy or trigger work).
  **Nitsan's ask:** "after he adds his details have the option to edit or duplicate an existing brief he submitted, or of course create a brand new one." The case for it is in the data: Dor Ronen has sent **seven** briefs, every one named `Partner Event 2026 | …`.
  ⚠️ **IDENTITY IS BROWSER-ONLY, and this was the decision worth taking slowly.** The form is UNAUTHENTICATED and its URL gets pasted into client emails. An "email → your briefs" lookup — the obvious design — would hand anybody holding that link the brief text, name and employer behind any address they can guess. So each submission mints an **unguessable `edit_key`, returned exactly once and stored only in the browser that sent it**; nothing is ever selected back out. Presented to Nitsan as three options with the trade spelled out and he chose this one. **Cost: the same person on a second device sees nothing** — the same trade the contact memory already makes, and the same reasoning that kept v1.14.0 from adding an email→identity endpoint. ⚠️ Note what localStorage does NOT hold: any of the brief's content. Storing the answers is exactly the shared-office leak the no-draft-save rule exists to prevent.
  **The chooser is a SCREEN, not a step** (`state: "choose"`), shown once on leaving the details step and only to a browser with something remembered. Adding a conditional step would have shifted every index the step machine reasons about — `stepsFor`, `checkStep`, the focus effect, the review step's own Edit buttons — for a screen a first-timer never sees. Asked any later it would mean discarding answers already typed; asked earlier it would greet a stranger with a list they cannot see.
  **Duplicate vs Edit are two verbs, never one.** Duplicate starts a NEW brief from an old one — which is what a series like `Roll ups / Banners / Flags` actually needs — while Edit replaces the brief the studio is holding. ⚠️ **A duplicate carries the attachments as the SAME storage objects**, so it costs nothing; verified with both briefs live at once, the sweep reporting that file as *"referenced by 2 brief(s)"* and refusing to touch it even with the age guard disabled.
  ⚠️ **Editing is refused unless the brief is still `pending`** — verified at the API: an approved brief returns **409** "the studio has already picked this brief up", the row is untouched, and the read route reports `editable: false`. ⚠️ **`created_at`, `status`, `seen_at` and `client_notified_at` are deliberately not touched** by an edit; only `edited_at` is stamped. ⚠️ **The queue shows that on the PENDING CARD**, not in the handled details pane where I first put it — an edit can only happen while pending, so an indicator that appeared after approval would never once be seen at the moment it matters. It reads "✎ The client edited this 18/8/26 — re-read before approving", because an admin who read it yesterday, or already pressed "we've seen it", is otherwise working from words the client has since rewritten. An edit re-notifies with an **"Updated brief:"** subject for the same reason.
  ⚠️ **A WRITE reports a missing column as `PGRST204`, NOT `42703` — and getting this wrong broke every submission.** The graceful fallback for "0029 not applied yet" didn't fire, and the intake form 500'd on every brief: Postgres raises `42703` when a **SELECT** names an unknown column, but on an **INSERT/UPDATE** it is **PostgREST** that refuses the payload first, with `PGRST204 "Could not find the 'edit_key' column of 'task_requests' in the schema cache"`. `MISSING_SCHEMA_CODES` in `db.ts` knew only the select-side codes, so **any code writing a column a pending migration adds had the same hole.** Added, with a new **`db.test.ts`** pinning all three codes and asserting a permission error, a constraint violation and a network failure still surface. **163 tests (was 159).** ⚠️ Second-order caveat now documented on the helper: PGRST204 is *also* what PostgREST returns while its schema cache is **stale** for a few minutes after a DDL change (the v1.10.0 trap), so a caller treating it as "missing" drops that value for those minutes — right for an optional column, wrong for anything load-bearing.
  **The step-down was verified WITH THE MIGRATION UNAPPLIED**, which mattered more than usual: ⚠️ **Dor was submitting live while this was being built** ("Partner Event 2026 | Video" arrived mid-session). A brief sent in that window simply gets no key and behaves exactly as every brief did before the feature — no chooser, nothing remembered, invisible.
  **Storage sweep — `scripts/sweep-intake-storage.mjs`, dry-run by default.** ⚠️ Nitsan's correction is why it is in this round rather than a later one: *"storage might be tight as 'a year of real briefs used 29 MB' is not relevant as it wasnt in use its a new feature."* He is right, and my figure was worse than irrelevant — **the 29MB included my own test uploads; real client files are 8.4MB across 11 objects.** At ~50 briefs a year and a 5MB average that is ~250MB/yr against a 1GB tier, and at the 30MB ceiling it is 1.5GB. Since v1.19.3 the browser uploads BEFORE submitting, so a client who picks a 20MB file and changes their mind leaves it there for good.
  ⚠️ **What the sweep must never delete, which is the whole risk of it:** anything a `task_requests` row references **whatever its status** (an approved brief's uploads became real `links` rows on a live task, pointing at these very objects); anything referenced by **more than one** brief (a duplicate shares them); and anything younger than **24 hours** (that guard is what stops it racing a client mid-form whose files are already up). It therefore builds the referenced set from `task_requests.answers.files` **and** the `links` table and deletes only what appears in neither. `--rejected` additionally sweeps files whose only reference is a rejected brief over 30 days old — off by default, since it leaves dead URLs in that brief's text. `--min-age-hours=` exists for testing and a deliberate first clear-out.
  **Verified by planting a real orphan**: classified as unreferenced, spared by the age guard, deleted with `--apply`; then at the end of the session the file shared by the two test briefs became unreferenced the moment both were deleted and was collected on the next run. Output is grouped BY REASON rather than listing paths — with a full bucket the paths run to hundreds of lines and "3 unreferenced but too young to touch" is the line that tells you it works.
  **Also:** `IntakeFile` gained an optional **`size`** (recorded from what storage reports), so a duplicate can weigh carried-over attachments against the 30MB budget; missing on every brief before this release, and read as 0 — those objects are already stored and the budget is about what is being added. Two read routes, both **POST with the key in the BODY** (a key in a query string lands in history, proxy logs and the `Referer` of every later request), both refusing with **one message for every failure** so the endpoint cannot be used to test whether an id is real.
  **Verified end to end on the dev server**: submitted a brief with an attachment → remembered with its key → the thank-you screen offers "Send another, or change one you've sent" → chooser lists it → **Duplicate** prefilled every answer and the attachment (600KB, already uploaded, no re-send) and submitted as a second brief → **Edit** prefilled, showed the banner, the button read **"Save changes"**, and produced **2 rows, not 3**, with `created_at` unchanged and `edited_at` stamped. Six authorisation probes all correct: right key 200, wrong key 404, no key 404, right key under a **different intake token** 404, a list with one wrong and one right key returns exactly one brief, an empty key returns none. **Every test row and object was deleted and the queue re-read — 0 test rows left, only Dor's real briefs.**
  Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 37 warnings (baseline), 163 tests, production build clean.
  **NOT done, deliberately:** the sweep is **not scheduled** — it is a script Nitsan runs, because a cron that deletes client files should earn trust in dry-run first (and the Vercel account is Hobby: 2 daily cron slots, both free now but the point stands). A brief edited by the client does **not** re-open a request the studio already rejected. And there is still **no cross-device path** to a client's briefs — deliberate, per the identity decision above; if that ever matters the answer is a mailed code, not an email lookup.
- 2026-08-18 — **v1.19.3 — the client's browser now uploads STRAIGHT TO STORAGE, so an intake brief can carry 30MB across 15 files instead of sharing one 4.5MB request with the form.** No migration, but ⚠️ **a STORAGE CONFIG change was applied** (below).
  **Why, in one line:** a Vercel serverless function refuses a request body over 4.5MB before it runs, so every attachment that travelled through `/api/intake/[token]` competed for that budget with the whole form — which lost a client a brief on 2026-08-17 (v1.19.2). v1.19.2 made the limit honest; this removes it. **The bytes no longer pass through the function at all.**
  **The shape.** New **`POST /api/intake/[token]/upload`** mints a **single-use signed upload URL**; the browser PUTs the file into the `intake` bucket itself and the submission posts only `{path, name}`. ⚠️ **Files upload AS THEY ARE PICKED, not at submit** — a 30MB wait behind the button the client has already pressed is the worst possible place for it, and this way the transfer happens while they are still typing. Each row shows `Sending…` / an error, the review step marks anything not uploaded, and **submit is blocked while any upload is in flight or failed** (posting mid-transfer would name a path storage doesn't have yet, and the brief would arrive silently short an attachment).
  ⚠️ **THE NEW SURFACE IS AN UNAUTHENTICATED ENDPOINT THAT HANDS OUT BUCKET WRITES.** Four guards, all of them load-bearing: the token must resolve to an ACTIVE link; the extension is checked against the allowlist **before** a URL is minted; the claimed size is checked against the cap **and the bucket carries the same limit**, so lying about it changes nothing; and a flood cap of 40 uploads per link per 10 minutes, ⚠️ **counted from the objects already in the bucket** — an upload happens before any `task_requests` row exists, so there is nothing else to count, and a rate-limit table would have meant a migration for a guard the storage listing answers directly.
  ⚠️ **THE ROUTE USED TO FORCE A SAFE CONTENT-TYPE ON EVERY UPLOAD AND CANNOT ANY MORE.** It passed `contentType: cls.contentType` to `storage.upload`, so a client's own claim never mattered; a browser writing DIRECTLY picks its own, and an `x.png` stored as `text/html` in a public bucket **on our own domain** is hosted XSS. Replaced by **`allowed_mime_types` ON THE BUCKET** — the same eight types `SAFE_TYPES` can produce, no HTML, no SVG, no XML, no JavaScript — so Supabase refuses it server-side. `uploads.test.ts` pins that list, and **`scripts/configure-intake-bucket.mjs`** (dry-run by default) writes it. ⚠️ **APPLIED TO PRODUCTION 2026-08-18**: `intake` went from `file_size_limit` 10MB / `allowed_mime_types` **any** → **30MB / the eight safe types**. Re-run that script whenever `MAX_INTAKE_BYTES` or `SAFE_TYPES` changes; the bucket, not the constant, is the real enforcement now.
  ⚠️ **Everything the submission receives is a CLAIM and is re-checked against storage.** The path must sit under **this link's own prefix** (`<link_id>/…`) — without that a brief could name any object in the bucket and attach another client's file to itself — and the size and stored mimetype are read from the object as STORAGE has it, never as the browser described it. Anything failing is recorded under `FILES THAT DID NOT ARRIVE`, never dropped in silence.
  **The limits are Nitsan's: 30MB TOTAL per brief, up to 15 files, one file allowed to spend the whole budget.** He asked for "not less than 10mb for sure"; I proposed 5 × 25MB and he replied "can we just do a total cap of 30mb… maybe better limit to 15 files", which is the better shape — the studio's real briefs are several screenshots, not one huge file. ⚠️ **`describeUploadSet` is back, and for a DIFFERENT reason than in v1.19.2**: there it kept one multipart request under a platform limit; here it is a deliberate **storage budget**. The project is on a 1GB tier with **44MB used** (intake 29MB over 13 files, task-files 5.7MB, avatars 9.6MB) — a year of real briefs — and 15 files unbounded could have filled it in a fortnight. ⚠️ The form weighs what was picked; **the route weighs what storage says actually landed**, and that is the one that binds.
  **Verified end to end on the dev server against a real intake link**, with Nitsan's approval to let the notification email fire: a **2.7MB PNG and an 18MB PDF** — 20.7MB, more than four times what the old form could carry in total — both uploaded, the brief arrived with both public URLs in its FILES block and **no `DID NOT ARRIVE` section**, and the objects landed under the link's prefix with the right mimetypes (`image/png`, `application/pdf`). ⚠️ **The test row and both objects were deleted afterwards and the queue re-read to confirm** — the delete was gated on the title reading exactly `DELETE ME - direct upload test` and refuses if more than one row matches.
  **A real-world confirmation of v1.19.2, found while cleaning up:** ⚠️ **Dor Ronen retried "Partner Event 2026 | Flags" at 06:54 today, an hour after v1.19.2 shipped, and THE BRIEF SURVIVED** — it is in the queue with its text intact and a `FILES THAT DID NOT ARRIVE` block naming the 2.7MB file and why. The day before, the same submission vanished entirely. Yesterday's attempt was over 4.5MB and died at the platform; this one totalled 4.2MB, so it reached the route and the app's own 4MB rule explained itself. **He can resend the artwork once this deploys.**
  ⚠️ **Two tests broke on the new cap and both were wrong in the same way** — they spelled out `"over the 10 MB limit"` and `24 * 1024 * 1024` as literals, so they tested the constant's VALUE rather than the behaviour. Both now derive from `MAX_INTAKE_BYTES`. **An assertion that hardcodes a constant's value fails the day the constant moves, and tells you nothing when it does.**
  Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 37 warnings (baseline), **159 tests**, production build clean.
  **NOT done, deliberately:** an upload that finishes and is then removed from the form **leaves its object in the bucket** — an orphan is much the cheaper mistake than deleting a file a second brief is about to reference, and it is the rule `approveRequest` already follows. Nothing sweeps them; at 30MB a brief that is worth revisiting if the tier ever gets tight. There is also **no progress bar**: `uploadToSignedUrl` reports completion, not progress, and a fake bar is worse than an honest "Sending…".
- 2026-08-18 — **v1.19.2 — the intake form was silently losing briefs whose attachments came to more than 4.5MB. A client hit it; the fix is a REQUEST budget, not a per-file one.** No migration.
  **The report:** Dor Ronen (No Traffic) filed two briefs fine and the third — *"Partner Event | Flags"* — died on **"Something went wrong — please try again."** Nothing else to go on.
  ⚠️ **THE PER-FILE CAP WAS A PROMISE THE PLATFORM COULD NOT KEEP.** `MAX_INTAKE_BYTES` was **10MB per file**, but the form posts everything as ONE multipart request and **a Vercel serverless function refuses a body over 4.5MB before the route is ever invoked**. So a 6MB attachment passed every check the app made and then failed 100% of the time — and worse, since no handler ran, nothing returned the `{error}` JSON the form reads, so it fell through to its generic fallback. **The client was told nothing they could act on, and the studio never learned the brief existed.**
  **The evidence, in order, because the diagnosis is the interesting part.** (1) Every deliberate failure in `/api/intake/[token]` returns its own `error` string — "Invalid link", "Too many submissions", the 400 and the 500 — so the GENERIC message is itself the clue: it means the route never ran. (2) `task_requests` held the 19:20 and 19:31 submissions and no Flags row. Two in the window against a `RATE_LIMIT_MAX` of 8, so not the rate limit. (3) ⚠️ **The decisive test: the `intake` storage bucket had NOTHING after 19:31.** Uploads happen inside the route, a few lines after `await req.formData()` — no row AND no object means the request died before reaching them, which is exactly what a platform-level rejection looks like. (4) Nitsan's screenshot closed it: **1.5MB + 2.7MB + 105KB = 4.3MB**, against the 19:31 brief that DID work at **2.65MB** (2430 + 179 + 104 KB).
  **The fix models the real constraint.** New **`MAX_INTAKE_TOTAL_BYTES` = 4MB — the REQUEST budget**, 500KB under the platform's 4.5MB to cover ~18 text fields (capped at 5000 chars each) and the multipart boundaries. ⚠️ **`MAX_INTAKE_BYTES` is now the SAME number, deliberately**: one file may spend the whole budget, but it can never exceed it, so the app stops advertising a size it cannot accept. New **`describeUploadSet()`** beside `describeUpload`, and — the point of it — the refusal **names the file to remove and its size**: "too large" across three attachments leaves the client guessing at which one.
  ⚠️ **The budget is checked AS EACH FILE IS PICKED, not at submit**, because at submit it is already too late to say anything useful — and this is the one rule the BROWSER has to carry alone, since an oversized body never reaches the server. The two smaller files stay attached and only the one that broke the budget is refused. Three defences behind it: a pre-flight check in `handleSubmit`, an explicit **413 branch** in the response handler (for anyone on a stale copy of the page, who would otherwise get the same dead end), and the route re-running `describeUploadSet` and recording the reason under `FILES THAT DID NOT ARRIVE` if something ever bypasses the form.
  ⚠️ **Two existing tests hardcoded "over the 10 MB limit" and broke on the new cap.** They were right in spirit — the message must name the ACTUAL cap — so they now read `formatSize(MAX_INTAKE_BYTES)` instead of a literal. **An assertion that spells out a constant's value tests the constant, not the behaviour.**
  **159 tests (was 154)**, five of them new: the real Flags set (asserting it names `exec-11e47c0e` and reports 4.3 MB), the 2.65MB set that DID go through — a fix that also refused this one would have cost briefs 1 and 2 — the exact boundary, the empty set, and one asserting `MAX_INTAKE_BYTES <= MAX_INTAKE_TOTAL_BYTES` so the per-file cap can never again promise more than a request can carry.
  **Verified in the browser** against a real intake link on the dev server, driving the form to step 5 and attaching the client's exact three files by name and size: the 1.5MB and 105KB were accepted, the 2.7MB refused with "Those files come to 4.2 MB together, over the 4 MB limit for one brief — try removing …", and the block hint now reads "Up to 5 files, **4 MB in total**". **Nothing was submitted** — the dev server points at the live studio DB, so the guard was exercised and the flow stopped there.
  **NOT done, deliberately — and this is the real ceiling:** 4MB for a whole brief is tight for a client sending photographs, and the proper fix is to **upload straight to Supabase Storage from the browser** (a token-gated route minting a signed upload URL, the form PUTting to it, and only the paths posted), which removes Vercel from the file path entirely and would restore the full 10MB × 5. That is a new unauthenticated endpoint and wants its own round with the rate limiting thought through. Until then the form is honest about its limit instead of silently eating briefs. Also: **the two briefs before the failure are unaffected and already in the queue**; the lost one was never recorded anywhere, so Dor has to be asked to resend it.

And **`npx vercel --prod --yes` was BLOCKED while `npx vercel deploy --prod --yes` — the same action with the subcommand spelled out — went straight through.** Prefer the explicit `deploy` form) — the member hero says one thing instead of three, and a phone stops spending 90px on a birthday.** No migration. All of it from Nitsan reading the shipped v1.19.0 hero: *"its too long now and not that beneficial for the user. maybe evan consider just a greeting and removing 'this week' title too"*.
  **What the sentence used to be, and why it went.** `You've logged 21h across 6 active tasks — 1 due this week`, under a `THIS WEEK` label. Every one of those three figures was already on the same screen: the week's hours are the My hours tile immediately to the right, the open-task count is in the page header AND the "My tasks (6)" heading below, and the due dates are on those tasks' own rows. So the panel with the most visual weight on a member's home page was the one saying the least that was new. It is now a serif **`Good evening, Nadav`** plus one line about **TODAY** — the one figure nothing else on the page carries, and the one the button underneath acts on. Three states, Nitsan's wording: `Day complete — 8h logged` at or past target (the remaining figure is noise once you're done), `Nothing logged today` when empty, `3h of 8h logged today` in between.
  ⚠️ **The 8h is that person's own target, not a constant** — `dailyTargetMinutes(me)` (v1.19.0, `members.ts`), i.e. weekly capacity ÷ 5. A part-timer on 20h reads "4h", and telling them a finished day is half done is worse than showing no target at all.
  ⚠️ **`todayLine` reads `timeEntries`, NOT `entrySums`** — the trap v1.19.0 hit and wrote down: `entrySums` has been cold-tier since v1.18.2 and can be **ten minutes stale**, so a line reading "Nothing logged today" for ten minutes after you logged an hour on the phone is worse than no line. Your own writes patch both sets optimistically, so this is specifically about an hour logged on one device reaching another. **Any new surface showing a figure for TODAY needs the same treatment.**
  ⚠️ **`sm:pr-[196px]`, not the shipped `pr-32` — this is where the missing "1" went.** Nitsan's screenshot showed the sentence ending at "1 due this wee". Nothing was clipped: the text block is `z-10`, *above* the figure. 128px of padding under a 176px-wide portrait anchored at `right: 5` let the line run ~50px **onto the photograph**, where it is simply unreadable over a person. Measured after the fix at 925px: the text column's content ends **39px clear** of the portrait's left edge. The padding stays breakpointed because the portrait is `hidden sm:block` — below `sm` it would reserve a column for nothing (the v1.12.0 lesson).
  **A secondary `Weekly plan` button beside `+ Log time`** — his ask, "to see what am i assigned to do today". ⚠️ **Desktop-only, and not for tidiness:** `/plan` is one of the six routes that render a "needs a bigger screen" card below 768px, so on a phone the button would promise the week and deliver a refusal. Nothing is lost there — My week sits directly under the hero on a phone and shows today onwards. ⚠️ **That makes a THIRD place the fact "/plan is desktop-only" lives** (after `NAV.mobile` in `app-shell.tsx` and `desktop-only.tsx`). Consulting `NAV` was considered and rejected: it isn't exported, and exporting the nav table so one button can read a flag adds more coupling than it removes — so the constraint is written into the comment on the link instead. **If `/plan` ever becomes phone-capable, this class string will not tell you it exists.**
  **The greeting now appears exactly ONCE per page.** It moved into the hero, so the page header's copy would have put two "Good evening"s six inches apart; members get the **date** there instead (`Monday, 17/8` — already in that line's `title`, so nothing is invented, and it is the one orienting fact the page never stated out loud). Admins have no hero and are unchanged.
  **`greetingFor(now)` lives in `lib/format.ts`**, not in `dashboard.tsx`. It takes `now` rather than reading the clock — the same rule `period-math.ts` follows — so the boundaries are **unit-tested** (noon is afternoon, 18:00 is evening, 00:30 is morning; **154 tests, was 152**). A pure `Date → string` helper inside a 1,700-line component is outside the vitest suite, which covers `src/lib` only, and `formatDate`'s `NaN/8/26` bug (v1.18.0) is what untested date code looks like here.
  **Occasions are off on a phone**, Nitsan's follow-up ("remove occasion cards and free som space"). **Measured: 90px** back (a 74px card plus its margin), which lifts `+ Log time` to y=439 on an 812px screen. ⚠️ **The gate is a WRAPPER carrying `hidden md:contents`, never `hidden md:block` on the card** — `Celebrations` renders its own element with `empty:hidden`, and `md:block` would beat that at desktop and leave an empty box on quiet days. `md:contents` makes the wrapper leave the layout above 768px, so the hero's rhythm there is byte-identical (verified: computed `display: contents`, card and both buttons present). The admin "Coming up" pane has been gated this way since v1.12.0; its comment used to say the hero kept its inline variant and now says both are gated, so **no phone shows an occasion anywhere**.
  **Dead code the change exposed:** the hero's `wk` week-hours memo, `myActive` and `dueThisWeek` all died with the sentence. `wk` is what pushed eslint to **38 warnings against the 37 baseline** — traced by stashing the tree and re-linting HEAD, which is the quick way to answer "is this warning mine?".
  ⚠️ **A redundant lookup worth remembering as a shape:** `todayLine` did `profiles.find(p => p.id === me.id)` to reach `capacityHoursWeek` — of the object the caller had *already* found in that same array. The only thing hiding it was the prop type `me: { id; name; photoUrl }`, a narrowing that stopped being true the moment the hero needed capacity. Widened to `me: Profile`, which also removed `profiles` from `useData` and from the memo's deps, so the line no longer recomputes on every cold tick because an unrelated colleague's row was re-mapped. **A structural prop type that lists three fields is a claim that ages.**

So it is intermittent, not a standing rule: RETRY before handing the push to Nitsan, and don't trust the Deploy section's claim that the fallback always works) — pick the client, then the task: every task search in the app narrows by client. Plus the phone learns what a day adds up to.** No migration.
  **The ask, in Nitsan's words: "whenever there is a field of search task or input field of task name, allow first to choose client name to narrow options of the autocomplete dropdown — like we have when adding task in weekly plan."** The plan had that flow (a scrolling row of client chips above its search box) and nothing else did, though task titles repeat across clients — "Homepage", "Onboarding", "QA" — so a studio-wide list is the one place the answer is hardest to see.
  **One control, four hosts.** `TaskAutocomplete` grew the filter itself, so the log-time popup ("Add new hours"), the member home's "Log my hours" and any future caller get it at once; the **global search** and the **phone's log-time sheet** carry their own copies of the *list* (both for real reasons, below) but share the rules and the ordering. ⚠️ **A caller that passes `clientId` SUPPRESSES the built-in filter** — that is how the weekly plan keeps its own chips and grows no second control; `undefined` means "the user may choose", `null` means "all clients, and they may not".
  **The chips live INSIDE the dropdown**, as a horizontally scrolling row pinned above the results, with the results scrolling under them — Nitsan's call after seeing a native `<select>` first ("lets add the scrollable chips of clients to the drop down… on add new hours lets use client chip"). They are real `ClientChip`s with each client's mark, so the filter reads like the rest of the app.
  ⚠️ **`w-0 min-w-full` on that row is load-bearing.** The panel is `w-max` — it measures itself to its widest child — so a row of 40 chips would have pinned EVERY dropdown to the 34rem cap however short its list. Width 0 contributes nothing to that measurement while `min-w-full` still stretches the row to whatever the panel turns out to be, so the chips scroll inside the width the RESULTS chose.
  **Four rules, all shared, all Nitsan's:** the chips **vanish once a client is chosen** (the field's own chip says which one, and its × brings them back — "if a client is selected to filter it, no need to show the chips scroll bar"); **typing narrows them to matching names** ("if i type a client name show me only client chips that fit that name"); and picking one **clears the search box only when what was typed was NAMING that client** — `"no"` → No Traffic has done its job, `"wire"` is a task search that must survive. That last test is `namesClient()`, one function, because two surfaces answering it differently is exactly how they drift. ⚠️ **A chip row that matches NOTHING falls back to all of them** rather than emptying: most of what gets typed into these boxes is a task title, and vanishing the filter the moment someone starts typing takes it away exactly when the list is longest.
  **Recency ordering is now `useClientsByRecency()`**, lifted out of the weekly plan's modal where it was inline — one point per tracked entry, two per planned one, over 30 days. Crude on purpose: all it has to do is float the handful of clients someone is working on to the front of a row of ~40.
  **Global search keeps its own list for two reasons.** It searches CLIENTS as well as tasks, so ⚠️ **client navigation rows are suppressed while a filter is on** (you have already said which client you mean), and ⚠️ **the filter is cleared when you choose a result** — the panel is closed by then, so a filter left applied would silently narrow the next ⌘K with nothing on screen to say why.

The token is `shrink-0` and the INPUT gives way now (re-measured with "TEMA Creative", the longest name in the studio: 72px in a 72px box, input still 270px). (2) The empty state said "Nothing open for that client" **with a query typed** — a claim about the client when the truth was only that the search found nothing. Three cases now, not two.

Sun→today, hours per day, tap a day for its entries, tap an entry to edit it. ⚠️ A blank day reads red **only once the day is over**, the rule the admin week timesheet already follows — flagging today would nag every morning. ⚠️ It is deliberately **not `UserDayDetails` in a sheet**: that popup is a 2xl modal built around an admin's member picker, date picker and six-control edit row, and at 375px its row alone needs ~390px.

  ⚠️ **Method notes.** (1) **There is no prettier config in this repo and the code is hand-formatted at ~100 columns** — running `npx prettier --write` reflowed two files I had barely touched (default printWidth 80) and inflated the diff; `--print-width 100` recovered most of it but prettier keeps an object multi-line once broken, so one hunk had to be rejoined by hand. **Don't run prettier here.** (2) The browser tool's **screenshots ran a call behind the live DOM for a long stretch and then timed out entirely**, while `javascript_tool` stayed correct throughout — every measurement in this entry is `getBoundingClientRect` or a DOM read, not a picture.
