  **The team was told, in the app — the first `whats-new.ts` entry since v1.13.0** (Nitsan's call; the four releases between shipped silently). Three items: *Narrow a task search to one client* (`all`), *Your day adds up as you log it* (`all`), *See the week you've logged* (`member`).
  **The third item's visual is a REAL SCREENSHOT Nitsan took of the shipped pane** — the first raster in `public/whats-new/` and the standard to aim for: a capture cannot drift from the app, which is every drawing's weakness. Its margin is `#f0f1fa` at full opacity — the app's own page colour rather than transparency — so it reads as a piece of the product instead of a card floating on brand blue. ⚠️ **It also forced a real fix: `WhatsNewItem.image` now takes optional `width`/`height`, and an `element` that is not 3:2 MUST give them.** The panel handed `next/image` a hardcoded 360×240 and the browser derives the aspect ratio from those attributes rather than from the file, so this 1206×927 capture would have been **squashed 14% vertically with nothing erroring**. Measured after the override: rendered 317×244, ratio 1.301 against the file's 1.301, and 0.85 of the pane's real on-screen size (its 14px rows land at ~12px).
  ⚠️ **I set the middle item to `member` first and it was WRONG.** I had concluded from a DOM sweep that an admin's bottom bar has no log-time action, because no button's TEXT read "+ Log time" — but that control is icon-only with `aria-label="Log time"` and `app-shell.tsx` renders it **unconditionally**. **Read the gate in the code, not the labels in a screenshot.** An admin has the same sheet, so the item is `all`.
  Two generated visuals, both at life size, both with their crop chosen for the scale the panel shows: **`build-whatsnew-clientfilter.mjs`** (360×179 → 0.88, so a 14px title lands at 12.4px) and **`build-whatsnew-dayhours.mjs`** (403×840 → 0.74). ⚠️ **The phone one is drawn with its sheet high up the screen, and that is a constraint of the PANEL:** a `phone` image renders `h-[620px]` inside a 440px box anchored top, so only the top ~553px of the drawing is ever seen and a sheet at the true bottom of an 812px screen would be cropped away entirely. The script asserts its content ends inside that band (12px of headroom) rather than leaving it to eye. The filter element **shows the gesture, not the outcome** — a cursor on the No Traffic chip with the list still un-narrowed, because the chips and a chosen client cannot share a frame; the three results carry the argument instead, all called some form of "Homepage", one each for DualBird, Anchor and No Traffic.
  ⚠️ **`naturalWidth` is NOT the reliable check for these SVGs that this log has claimed it is.** On the admin load it read 360 (fine); on the very next load it read **0 with `complete: false` across two separate probes** while the picture was plainly painted in the screenshot and the network showed **200 OK**. Verify a what's-new visual by the wire and the pixels; `naturalWidth: 0` is evidence of nothing on its own.
  ⚠️ **Dismissing the panel WRITES `whatsnew.seen` in localStorage, so verifying it consumes the real announcement.** Read the key first and put it back — it was `v1.18.2` in this browser and was restored to `v1.18.2` after each check, so the studio still gets the v1.19.0 panel on next sign-in.
  Verified in the browser at 1440×900: as admin, the client-filter step renders its element at 317×158 (0.88); as `?viewAs=Nadav` the panel **auto-opens** off the real upgrade path (`seen` v1.18.2 < v1.19.0) with exactly three steps in order, the third on the wordmark, ending in "Got it".
- 2026-08-13 — **v1.18.2 — the whole `time_entries` table left the 60-second tick. Egress fix, part 1 of 2.** No migration. Supabase egress hit **8.08 GB against the 5 GB free tier** in the last billing period, and one query was almost all of it: `fetchHot` paged the ENTIRE `time_entries` table (`id, task_id, user_id, date, minutes, legacy, date_estimated`) — ~10 years, tens of thousands of rows — **every 60 seconds, for every open tab**. That fetch moved to `fetchCold` (~every 10 minutes, and already triggered by boot, manual refresh, and a tab regaining focus after 5+ minutes away), so `entrySums` moved from `HotSnapshot` to `ColdSnapshot`. Roughly a **10× cut in how often the biggest payload in the app is pulled**; `fetchFull` spreads `{...cold, ...hot}` so boot is unchanged.
  ⚠️ **`entrySumsAll` was NOT replaced with a server-side aggregate**, which is the obvious-looking fix. It feeds real per-ROW work across client totals, task-list rows, dashboards, weekly timesheets, team member pages and client reports — an aggregate would mean reworking every one of those call sites. Frequency and (next round) payload size are the levers here.
  ⚠️ **The column-degradation ladder moved character-for-character**, `isMissingSchema` handling included. It is not simpler for living on the cold tier; degrading past `legacy` still leaks ~4,000h of pre-Everhour backfill into every personal figure on the site.
  ⚠️ **Two things read `entrySums` off the hot snapshot and had to be re-pointed, or the move would have been silently wrong.** (1) `mergeTimeEntries` uses the sums as the authority on which OUT-OF-WINDOW feed rows still exist (a task opened via `openTask` loads entries far older than the 400-row window, and they must survive a refresh). It now reads the last cold set from **`entrySumsRef`**, not from `h` — passing `[]` would evict every out-of-window row on every tick. Up-to-10-minute-old sums err toward KEEPING a row, which is the safe direction. The ref is written synchronously in `applyCold` (`applyHot` runs before that state commits) and mirrored by an effect, so local optimistic edits — logging, editing or deleting an entry — stay visible to it. (2) **`fingerprint` now takes the sums as an argument.** Left reading `h.entrySums` it would have compared a full set against an absent one every minute and reported "somebody else changed something" on every tick, which expires the undo history. A hot tick passes the sums already in hand, so that half of the print holds still. The **400-row feed window is now folded into the print instead** (count + minutes), which keeps recent time-entry activity — the only kind an undo step can target — detectable at the hot cadence rather than the 10-minute one.
  **Deliberately unchanged:** the 400-row activity feed stays hot, so recent activity still appears within a minute; a member's own logged hours still update instantly on their own screen (optimistic local state, unrelated to fetch cadence); a tab away 5+ minutes still does a full refresh on return. **The user-facing cost:** aggregates refresh every ~10 minutes rather than every 60 seconds *while a tab stays continuously open and focused*. Nothing else moves.
  **MEASURED in the running app** (Nitsan signed in, `fetch` instrumented to record per-request response bytes — this is a change with no visible effect in the UI, so the only honest verification is the wire). **Per tick, one open tab:**
  | | before | after |
  |---|---|---|
  | hot tick (60s) | 7,850 KB | **2,861 KB** |
  | cold tick (~10 min) | same | 7,912 KB |
  | per focused hour | **471 MB** | **202 MB** |
  The `entrySums` query itself is **4,989 KB over 25 pages** (~24k rows) and its frequency dropped 60×/hr → 6×/hr, so it went **299 MB/hr → 30 MB/hr — the 10× the plan predicted**. Total egress per open tab falls **57%**, not 90%, and the reason is the finding below.
  ⚠️ **`tasks` IS NOW THE PROBLEM, and the plan says the opposite.** The written plan's step 3 guessed the remaining candidates were "far smaller tables and unlikely to matter much by comparison." Measured, `tasks` is **2,517 KB over 5 pages on EVERY 60-second tick — 88% of what a hot tick now costs, and 151 MB/hr of the remaining 202.** `time_entries` is down to 30 MB/hr, i.e. **15%** — so Fix 2 (splitting entries by client `archived`) has a ceiling of roughly **12% further reduction**, while `tasks` is worth up to 75%. Two cheap levers on it, neither explored yet: it carries `legacy_hours`/`legacy_title`/`activity_from`/`activity_to` on all ~4,500 rows though they only mean anything on the ~150 recovered ones, and nothing about a task list needs 60-second freshness except the drag-and-drop surfaces. **Re-prioritise before building Fix 2.**
  Also verified live, both regression risks named above: the 2019 Quadream task "UI/UX December 19" was opened (23 entries, all far outside the 400-row window) and **still showed all 23 after two hot ticks** — the only `time_entries` reads in that window were the 400-row feed and the task-scoped 10 KB refetch, no full-table page-through; and **zero `[refresh]` "changed" lines were logged across four ticks** (console capture of debug level confirmed working first, so that's a real negative, not a silent tool), meaning the fingerprint holds still rather than expiring undo every minute. `snapshot.test.ts` pins both in both directions — a recording stub client asserting which tier asks for what, and four fingerprint cases including "a hot tick that changed nothing" and "a new entry with the same total". **152 tests (was 146).** Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 37 warnings (baseline, unchanged), production build clean.
  **Part 2 is written and deliberately NOT built** (`~/Downloads/egress-fix-plan.md`): splitting `time_entries` by client `archived` status. Ship this first, watch **Supabase → Project Settings → Usage → Egress for 3–5 days**, then decide — and on these numbers the next move is `tasks`, not Fix 2.
- 2026-08-12 — **v1.18.1 — the handled row reads like the rest of the app.** The client is a **`ClientChip` with its icon**, exactly as everywhere else; ⚠️ passed `link={false}`, because the chip sits INSIDE the row's select button and an anchor nested in a button is the same invalid nesting that silently broke selection in v1.18.0. The submitter gained a **"by"** prefix, and the client and date left the run-on line under the title to become their own **columns** (`sm:grid-cols-[minmax(0,1fr)_auto_auto]`) — they are what you scan a handled list by. They collapse back under the title below `sm`, where three columns would leave the title ~90px.
  **"Show handled" moved out of the page header to sit directly above the list**, with a count. A control that far from the thing it changes reads as a page-level setting rather than a filter on the list below it. That also simplified the header back to two items, retiring the `basis-full` wrap-arbitration the checkbox had needed there.
  The pane **widens with the screen** — `lg:w-[22rem] xl:w-[28rem] 2xl:w-[34rem]`, measured 352 / 448 / 544px with no horizontal overflow, and the page's own cap goes to `max-w-7xl` while a pane is open.
- 2026-08-12 — **v1.18.0 — the intake queue can show what the client actually sent.** Handled rows now carry the **client, the submitter and the date** (they showed a title and nothing else), and selecting one opens a **details pane beside the list** with the submission exactly as it arrived. ⚠️ This closes a real hole: `approveRequest` copies a cleaned brief onto the task and every hand-edit after that overwrites it, so `task_requests.brief` — the submission copy, FILES and LINKS blocks included — is the ONLY surviving record of the client's own words, and until now nothing in the UI could reach it.
  ⚠️ **`<button>` inside `<button>` is invalid HTML and broke selection outright.** The row was a button wrapping the delete control; the browser re-parents the inner one, and clicking the row did nothing at all — invisible to `tsc` and to eslint, caught only by driving it. The row is a div holding a select button and the delete button as siblings.
  ⚠️ **`formatDate` was printing `NaN/8/26` — a PRE-EXISTING bug** on the pending cards, not something this round introduced. It splits on "-" expecting `yyyy-mm-dd`, so a `created_at` timestamp made the day `11T17:48:52.724326+00:00`. Fixed at the source with `.slice(0, 10)` (harmless for date-only strings), and `format.test.ts` is new — that helper had no coverage at all.
  Attachments in the pane are rendered from `answers`, **not scraped out of the brief text**, and each URL goes through `isSafeUrl` before becoming an href — they are client-supplied. The pane is a flex SIBLING of the list (`lg:sticky lg:w-96`), so below `lg` it stacks underneath rather than becoming an unusable fixed rail; **mobile has not been done** and needs its own pass.
- 2026-08-12 — **v1.17.3 — intake form: one add button per block, and deliverables that confirm.** ⚠️ **v1.17.2 shipped TWO add buttons** on links and files — the new right-aligned one in `BlockHeader` plus the old one underneath, which survived because the replace that was meant to delete it no longer matched after an earlier class change. Both duplicates gone, and the header button keeps the **descriptive** label ("Add deliverable" / "Add link" / "Add files"), never a bare "Add".
  **A deliverable now confirms.** Filled in and pressed Done, a row collapses to a summary line — name, `79 × 47 in · Print`, details — with Edit and Remove. Left as live inputs, three deliverables were a wall of boxes that all looked focused with no sense of which were finished. ⚠️ Open/closed is tracked by a client-side **id, not an index**: removing the first of three would otherwise re-open whichever row slid up into its place. That `id` is stripped before submit, and a row counts as real if ANY of its four fields was filled — filtering on the name alone would silently drop a deliverable where someone typed only a size.
  ⚠️ **One deliverable is always present and open.** An empty block with only an "Add" button asks the client to opt in to describing the thing they came here to describe. Seeded in the parent's INITIAL state rather than in an effect (`react-hooks/set-state-in-effect`), and empty rows are dropped at submit, so a client who ignores it costs nothing.
  ⚠️ **The kind cards are `bg-surface` in BOTH states** — white, rather than letting the page's tinted background show through — with selection carried by `border-brand ring-1 ring-brand`. That is what actually fixed the hint legibility reported in v1.17.1: over the page tint it measured 4.5:1 (the AA floor) and read as muddy; on white it is **5.92:1**.
- 2026-08-12 — **v1.17.2 — the intake form, second pass on Nitsan's notes.** The mark is now **`&more` alone, right-aligned, as TEXT** — the same way `app-shell.tsx` draws it folded, so there is one definition of it rather than a second copy in SVG; it identifies the sender, and the form's own title is what the client came to read. The three repeatable blocks (deliverables, links, files) share a new `BlockHeader`: title and description left, **the add button right**, which also stops the "add" control sitting at a different height in each block depending on how long its copy runs.
  **"Several pieces" → "Several deliverables"**, and ⚠️ **each deliverable now carries its OWN dimensions and format**. A real set varies — the Partner Event brief had Banner 1 at 79 × 47 inches and Banner 2 at 75 × 47 — and a single shared dimensions field forces that difference into prose, where it reads as description rather than as a spec. Rendered under the name as `79 × 47 in · Print`.
  ⚠️ **`Anything to avoid?` moved into the brief, straight after the goal.** What to avoid is the other half of what to aim for, and the client who has just written the goal is the one holding the answer — at the end of the form it was answered 11 times in 49.
  **Steps rebalanced to six**, because step 4 had swollen to nine questions: About you · What you need · Technical details · The brief (5 questions) · Content & references (the copy, the deliverables and the files/links that go with them) · **Review & send**. ⚠️ The review reads from the SAME `values`/`kinds`/`deliverables` state the FormData is built from, never a parallel description — a summary assembled independently is one that can lie, and it exists precisely so the client can trust what leaves their hands. It shows **only answered things** (a screen padded with "—" teaches people to stop reading it) and every row has an Edit that jumps back to the step that owns it.
- 2026-08-12 — **v1.17.1 — the stepped form, after Nitsan used it.** ⚠️ **BUG: arriving at the final step auto-submitted after ~2s.** Not a timer — React reused the SAME DOM button when the step changed, so pressing Next into the last step turned that very element from `type="button"` into the submit button *mid-click*, and the browser then applied the click's default action to the button as it had just become. Fixed with distinct `key`s on Next and Submit, forcing a new element so the in-flight click belongs to the old, discarded one. ⚠️ Any conditional swap of a button's `type` inside one form has this hazard.
  **Kinds are now MULTI-select** (checkboxes, not radios): one task routinely holds pieces of different kinds — a roll-up and a social post for the same event — and forcing a single choice meant the client picked the nearest and was never asked about the rest. `fieldsAsked` takes the **union**, and `IntakeAnswers.kind` became `kinds: string[]`; `readSubmission` still reads the old single `kind` string. Each kind gained an emoji, `aria-hidden` and BESIDE the label, never instead of it.
  ⚠️ **The hint text measured exactly 4.5:1** against the selected card — the AA floor, passing on a technicality, and reported as hard to read. `text-faint` → `text-muted` and the fill `bg-brand/10` → `/5` (the brand border and the tick already carry the selected state) brings it to 4.86.
  Also: the studio **logo** now heads the form via the same `.brand-wordmark` mask the app shell uses (clients had no idea whose form they were filling in); "Details" → **"Technical details"**, which is now never skipped because ⚠️ **every kind asking `format` now also asks `dimensions`**, plus a `techNotes` catch-all on every kind — "always a comments field to fill what's not obvious where it goes", because every structured question is a guess and this is the box for what the guess missed. Links and files **moved to the brief step, under Copy or content**, where the person describing the content is the one holding the reference. ⚠️ Body text went `text-sm` → `text-base` throughout: below 16px iOS Safari zooms the page on focus, which throws the layout around mid-answer.
- 2026-08-12 — **v1.17.0 — the client form is stepped, and asks by kind of work.** No migration (`answers` is jsonb). Nitsan asked for steps or tabs; ⚠️ **steps only earn their keep because step 1 changes what the later steps ask** — splitting the same 21 questions across five screens would have been the same form with more clicks. Tabs were argued against and rejected: they signal "optional, pick what you like", so people submit having never opened two of them.
  ⚠️ **The form is no longer hardcoded JSX.** `src/lib/intake-fields.ts` holds `FIELDS` and `WORK_KINDS` as DATA, and the form, the per-kind question sets and `missingFields` all read the same table. That is what finally makes `MISSING INFORMATION` honest — it used to mean "every blank in a hardcoded list of 12" (so a copywriting job was reported as missing its dimensions and animation) and now means "what we asked you and you didn't answer". ⚠️ `fieldsAsked` falls back to asking EVERYTHING for a submission with no kind, because every archived submission predates kinds and was in fact asked everything.
  ⚠️ **The eight kinds are DERIVED from the 49-submission archive**, not invented: clustering real task names put 48 of 49 into social (15), website (9), event/booth (6), presentation (4), document (4), graphics (4), swag (3), logo (2). They are client-facing wording only and map to NOTHING internally — an earlier design had them setting the task's Type, dropped because Nitsan assigns Type himself, which also removed a mapping that would have needed syncing whenever a task type was renamed or deleted.
  **Deliverables are now structured.** A repeatable name + details block, for the kinds that arrive in multiples. ⚠️ This is the only honest answer to "make Roll-up 1 bold": across three real briefs the headings were `Roll-up 1`, `Visual direction`, `Front` while the lines beneath them included `Notraffic logo` and `Full event agenda` — same length, same punctuation, no rule separates them, and a wrong bold asserts a structure the client never wrote. Declared as data, a name can be emphasised with certainty. It renders plain until the `**bold**` renderer lands; that is the single line that changes.
  ⚠️ **Focus management must live in an effect keyed on `step`, not in the click handler.** Focusing inside `go()` — even wrapped in `requestAnimationFrame` — runs before React commits the new step and lands nowhere: verified in the browser, where `document.activeElement` was still `<body>` after pressing Next. Also: the fieldset is `aria-labelledby` the visible `<h2>` rather than carrying its own `sr-only` `<legend>`, which was announcing every step name twice. Submit errors gained `role="alert"` — a failed submit used to be completely silent to a screen reader. ⚠️ **No draft autosave, even though a wizard begs for one**: the existing rule against storing the brief in localStorage (a shared machine at a client's office leaking one client's request into the next person's form) outranks the convenience.
  ⚠️ `animated` was NOT cut despite earning one useful answer in 49 — 43 of those were static web and print pieces where it never applied. It is asked only of social, graphics and event work. Same reasoning kept `Things to avoid` (11 of 49 real, and useful when answered) on the final optional step rather than deleting it.
- 2026-08-12 — **v1.16.2 (DEPLOYED + committed `39aa6c9`, pushed to main; prod verified serving v1.16.2 on tracker.studionmore.com) — the global search dropdown stopped opening behind the client page's own header.** No migration. Nitsan hit it typing "one pager" on a client page: the results list was sliced through by the client's name, tabs and buttons.
  - ⚠️ **A self-inflicted regression from v1.10.0, and the two constraints behind it are worth stating together, because satisfying one alone breaks the other — both have now shipped as bugs.** (a) The client page's sticky header is a stacking context (sticky + z-index), so the Show and Columns menus *inside* it are capped at ITS depth however high their own z-index goes — it therefore has to beat the Timeline's ruler, or both dropdowns open under the dates. That is what `z-[31]` was for. (b) But the APP header is `sticky z-30` + **`backdrop-blur`** — also a stacking context — so the search dropdown is capped at **30** no matter that it asks for `z-50`. Anything on the page above 30 paints over the search results.
  - Fix: the whole page now lives **below 30**. The client header drops to `z-[25]` and the Timeline's internals come down into a 22–24 band keeping their relative order — ruler `z-[22]`, mark labels and the drag readout `z-[23]`, the horizontal-scroll shadow `z-[24]`, row sticky name column unchanged at `z-20`. `fixed` popups (context menus, the mark editor, HoverTip) stay at 40/50/70 and are unaffected.
  - **The scale is now written into [client-view.tsx](src/components/client-view.tsx)** above the header, as a table, with both constraints named. Rediscovering it cost two bugs; it should not cost a third.
  - Not a bug elsewhere, checked: the weekly plan's corner `<th>` is `z-30` but its `<thead>` is `sticky z-20` — a stacking context — so the 30 is clamped and never competed with the app header. The public Gantt's `sticky z-30` is on a page with no app chrome.
  - **Verified in the browser** on Anchor with Nitsan's session: the search list paints unbroken over the title, tabs and buttons; the Show menu still opens above the ruler and chart; and scrolled 300px the app header measures `z-30` at top 0 with the client header `z-25` pinned at top 56. ⚠️ Screenshots taken mid-scroll show a blank band at the top — a compositing artifact of the capture, not a layout break; measure `getBoundingClientRect()` rather than believing it.
- 2026-08-12 — **v1.16.1 — the derived format, checked against 49 real submissions.** Nitsan pointed out the three pairs v1.16.0 was built from were all one person, one client, one event, all **Print** — and supplied the archive export (49 submissions, May 2025 → Jul 2026, 8 clients, 15 submitters). ⚠️ **43 of the 49 are Digital**, so the sample really was unrepresentative; the rules held anyway, with three corrections. (1) The written-out-nothing list was **measured, not guessed**: 46 instances across 49 briefs, ~one per submission, `-` alone filling Dimensions 13×, Notes 7×, Things-to-avoid 4×. It now also strips trailing punctuation and smileys before matching, because the archive holds `none.`, `Nope.`, `None :)` and `--`, and covers `N/R` beside `N/A`. ⚠️ It must NOT over-reach — `THE WORLD.`, `Thank you!` and `1.5` are real answers and are pinned by test. (2) `animated` is answered **No 42×, Yes once** in 49 — the suppression generalises well beyond print, and the question itself is a candidate for deletion in the form round. (3) ⚠️ **Budgets are PROSE**: "As much as it needs to take", "need a pricing on each bundle", "2 hours". `parseBudgetHours` reduces all of those to nothing, which is exactly why the `Budget:` line stays in the brief while the due date goes — the Dates field survives a date intact, `estimate_hours` cannot survive a sentence. Measured effect across the archive: the old format printed ~478 lines of headings, `Not provided` and missing-lists against 763 lines of real content — **~38% boilerplate**.
- 2026-08-12 — **v1.16.0 — the brief stopped reading like a form dump.** No migration. ⚠️ **The format was DERIVED, not designed** — Nitsan supplied three real before/after pairs (No Traffic: Banners, Roll ups, Name Tags), each the generated brief beside the task he hand-edited it into, and **his deletions were the specification**. In all 3 he removed: the `SUMMARY` heading and the `notraffic requests "X"` opener (the client is the Client chip, X is the task title), `due …` (the Dates field), `animated: No` (all three are print — the absence of a property nobody asked about is not information), every `Not provided` line, the entire `MISSING INFORMATION` block, and the `FILES`/`LINKS` URL dumps. In all 3 he kept the answered labelled lines verbatim and in order, and the client's own free text untouched. So `briefParts` emits **only what was answered, never what the task already shows, and never reworded**. Result on his own three: 20/28/45% shorter, and the generated Name Tags brief now comes out **identical to the one he made by hand**.
  ⚠️ **`Format:` and `Dimensions:` are separate lines and never combined.** The old opener embedded the size in the format (`Print (85 × 200 cm)`) while `TASK DESCRIPTION` printed it again, and he resolved that duplicate three different ways across three briefs (deleted it, kept it, moved it to the end) — two plain lines remove the decision. ⚠️ **Budget stays but the due date goes**: a date survives its field intact, whereas `estimate_hours` is a number and "5-8" loses its range the moment `parseBudgetHours` touches it. ⚠️ A written-out nothing (`none`, `n/a`, `nothing`, `-`) counts as blank — a real submission's Notes read "none" and he deleted it — but **"no" does not**, since it can be a real answer.
  ⚠️ **`/^no/i` also matches "Not sure yet"**, which is one of the three options the form offers for "Would it be animated?" — so a client saying they don't know would have been read as "no" and dropped. Caught by a test, not by eye; `isNo` is anchored at both ends now. **`assembleTaskBrief` had NO test coverage at all** before this (the 12 existing tests covered only `renderSeenEmail` and `readSubmission`), which is exactly how the format drifted this far.
  The notification email now renders the **same `briefParts`**, so the two can't diverge — which retired the `🧠 AI Summary` heading as a side effect. There is no AI in this app and never was; that block was string concatenation. Its missing-field list survives as a quieter "Not answered", because a *notification* legitimately wants to say what to chase.
- 2026-08-12 — **v1.15.1 — the intake form was silently eating client attachments.** No migration. Reported as "it says 5 files, only 1 attaches" — and the plumbing was innocent: the input had `multiple` and the route already looped `form.getAll("files").slice(0, 5)`. ⚠️ **A bare `<input type="file" multiple>` REPLACES its selection on every pick**, so a client choosing files one at a time kept only the last, and nothing rendered the list to reveal it. `FileRows` (modelled on the existing `LinkRows`) now holds `File[]` in state with the input demoted to a hidden trigger; ⚠️ its `onChange` must reset `e.target.value = ""` or re-adding a file you just removed fires no `change` event at all. ⚠️ **`handleSubmit` builds `new FormData(e.currentTarget)`, so it must `delete("files")` and re-append from state** — exactly the override `links` already performs on the next line.
  ⚠️ **The deeper bug was that EVERY refusal was a bare `continue`.** Wrong type, over 10MB, storage error — the client was thanked and the studio never learned an attachment was meant to exist. `describeUpload` (new, in `uploads.ts`) is now called by **both** the browser and the route, so the form refuses exactly what the server would and says why *before* submitting; anything still dropped server-side is listed under a **`FILES THAT DID NOT ARRIVE`** block in the brief and in red in the notification email. The overflow past 5 is reported too rather than being eaten by `slice` — verified live with a 7-file submission (3 stored, 4 reported with reasons), then deleted with its storage objects and the queue confirmed back to the same 4 real rows.
  **The allowlist widened**: `heic`/`heif` above all — every iPhone photo, so a client attaching four photos and a PDF got exactly one file through — plus `tif`, `tiff`, `eps`, `indd`, `sketch`, `fig`, `mov`, `mp4`, `rar`, `7z`, all forced to `octet-stream`. ⚠️ **`svg` stays rejected** (active content in a public bucket on our own domain — the point of the allowlist), and ⚠️ **`IMAGE_TYPES` was deliberately NOT widened**: it backs avatars and client icons, which render inline, and browsers display neither HEIC nor TIFF. ⚠️ `extOf` returns the whole filename when there is no dot, so a dotless name refused with ".screenshot files aren't supported" — caught by a test, not by eye.
- 2026-08-12 — **v1.15.0 — the mobile pass: the intake round on a phone, and Clients arrives there.** No migration. Desktop was signed off first, per the standing rule; everything here is phone-only and nothing above `md` changed.
  **The intake surfaces first.** Only what the v1.14.0 batch touched, and only where a phone can reach it — `/settings` and `/intake-queue` are both in the phone drawer, and the public form is filled on a phone more often than not. ⚠️ **The client form's link row was genuinely broken**: title and URL side by side at 375px gave the title 235px and left `flex-1` a **26px** URL field. They stack below `sm` now. The form's `p-8` also spent 64px of a 375px screen on padding (`p-5 sm:p-8`), and the queue's action row was a `flex-wrap` that stranded Approve on its own line under a Reject it has nothing to do with — it is two explicit rows on a phone and one right-aligned row from `sm`. Delete, notify, Reject, Approve, "Show brief" and the link controls are all 44px targets below `sm` and revert to tight desktop sizing above it. ⚠️ The card's title block had `min-w-0` but **no `flex-1`** — so it could shrink but never claimed the leftover space, and "Show brief" + delete took what they liked. That clipped the title **on desktop too** and squeezed the submitter line to ~230px on a phone, where it broke after nearly every word ("No / Traffic" on two lines). `flex-1` plus dropping the controls to their own row below `sm` fixed both at once. ⚠️ **The phone drawer KEEPS its avatar/name/sign-out block** even though the desktop sidebar dropped it: the desktop reason was that the header already names you top-right, and a 375px header has room for the wordmark, the sync dot and the bell and nothing else — remove it there and a phone names the signed-in person nowhere and cannot sign out. It gained "Latest updates" as its own row (a third item on the `About · v1.14.0` line overflows 375px). The 6th Settings tab needed nothing — `Tabs` is already `overflow-x: auto` (525px of tabs scrolling inside 343px).
  **The About panel became a TABBED popup on a phone.** It was drawn for a 5xl card and stacked to 1,039px inside an 812px viewport; because `Modal` is `fixed` + `-translate-y-1/2`, that overflowed off BOTH ends, carrying the ✕ above the screen with nothing to scroll. Now `max-h-[92dvh]` + three `md:hidden` tabs (`The studio` / `Timeline` / `Who built it`), every pane `md:block` so a laptop renders exactly what it did before. All three fit with **no scrolling** (537/617/637px); the `overflow-y-auto` is a safety net for when the generated figures grow, not the plan. ⚠️ The release rail is a percentage layout with 128px captions — fine across 900px, hopeless across 375, where eight milestones printed on top of each other — so below `md` it becomes a vertical list. Two overflow bugs fell out of this: the ledger column is a GRID ITEM (`min-width:auto`, so it refused to shrink and pushed both bars off the card — fixed with `min-w-0`), and hidden CONTAINERS still hold their padding and row gaps, which left ~80px of dead white under one tab.
  **Clients and one client's task list now work on a phone** (`client-mobile.tsx`), and both `desktop-only.tsx` entries are gone. ⚠️ These are SEPARATE components chosen in JS via `useIsNarrow`, not the desktop ones made responsive: `client-view.tsx` is ~2,900 lines of drag-and-drop, marquee selection, context menus and three view tabs that assume a pointer and ~900px, and rendering it behind `md:hidden` would still mount all of it. The phone build is the **task list only** — Timeline and Board are simply not offered, since the Gantt pins a 238px column before the calendar starts. ⚠️ It does **not** reuse `TaskTable` — that was the first attempt, and it cost ~850,000 iterations per render: `TaskTable` walks EVERY time entry in the studio inside its own `useMemo`, which is fine for the one instance on My Tasks but not for the ~18 this page mounts (one per group and per section), each scanning ~24,000 entries, and again on every 60s background refresh. `client-mobile.tsx` has its own one-line `TaskRow` reading the store's `taskMinutes`, which is the same total computed once — and `task-list-row.tsx` keeps zero props it added. Section → group → task nests with the same defensive rule as every other reader — a task counts as grouped only when its group belongs to its own section. Creating/reordering sections, dragging between them and inline editing are deliberately absent: those are desk jobs, and tapping a task opens the normal pane, which IS phone-built. The admin bottom bar swaps **My Tasks → Clients** (a designer, who has no `/clients`, keeps Tasks); the loser is still one tap away in the drawer.
  ⚠️ **The client page's card is ONE line, no "+ Time".** There the card is a row in a list you scan — section, group, twenty tasks — so the two-line card with its own action button turned one section into a lot of scrolling; title left, hours and date right (`items-baseline`, so a wrapped title keeps its figures level with the first line). Seven tasks now sit where four did. **My Tasks keeps the full card**, and that asymmetry is the point: that page exists to log time against your own work, while the client page answers "what is the state of this client's work". Sections AND groups both fold (`g:` namespaced keys, as on desktop), and **only the headings indent — the cards run the full width.** Indenting cards spent ~24px of every title, and 24 more inside a group, restating what the heading directly above already said.
- 2026-08-11 — **v1.14.0 — the intake round: the client's side of the form, and the reply back.** Migration **0028 APPLIED by Nitsan** (`task_requests.seen_at` / `seen_by` / `client_notified_at` — three plain columns; `task_requests` is already admin-only for ALL operations via 0001's `admin all` policy, so no policy or trigger work was needed).
  **Files and client links become REAL `links` rows on approval.** `approveRequest` reads `task_requests.answers`, and every uploaded file and every "+ Add link" row the client added is inserted into `links` (0022) against the new task — the same rows the studio's own link editor writes, so they render and edit identically. The brief copied onto the task is then **re-assembled without the FILES/LINKS blocks**: `assembleTaskBrief(a, attach?)` takes its attachments optionally, and the submission keeps them as text (the queue card and the notification email are the only way to reach them before approval) while the task does not. Leaving both would put every 150-character Supabase storage URL in the brief twice, which is the noise 0022 exists to remove. The link insert is deliberately **best-effort**: the task exists and the request is approved by then, and failing the approval over one link row would leave the queue and the task list disagreeing.
  **Three required fields, not twelve** — Name, Email, Task Name, which is exactly the server's own floor. Everything else is optional; `assembleTaskBrief` already lists gaps under MISSING INFORMATION, so nothing is silently lost, and a form that refuses to submit is a client who phones instead.
  **Repeat submitters are remembered in `localStorage`, NOT server-side.** The obvious design — an endpoint taking an email and returning the name/company behind it — was rejected: this form is unauthenticated and its URL is pasted into client emails, so that endpoint hands anyone who guesses an address the name and employer attached to it. The browser copy covers the case that actually recurs (same person, same laptop, brief after brief) and one click clears it. ⚠️ Never store the brief itself — a shared machine at the client's office would leak one client's request into the next person's form.
  **The client read-receipt is a BUTTON, never automatic.** Nitsan asked for mail "once a request is seen by an admin"; firing on render would mean opening the queue to triage one submission quietly emails every other client waiting in it. `/api/intake/seen` is admin-authed (cookie session → `profiles.role`, the `member-emails` pattern), and it is a route rather than a store write for two reasons the browser can't cover: `RESEND_API_KEY` is server-only, and the send-once guarantee needs the `client_notified_at` check and stamp inside one request. ⚠️ **`seen_at` is stamped even when the mail fails** — the admin did read it, and leaving the row untouched because a mail server was down would make the queue lie; `client_notified_at` stays null so the button remains.
  **The wording lives in Settings → Intake form**, in `app_settings.intake_seen_email` (key/jsonb, admin-write by RLS — no schema of its own). `renderSeenEmail` fills `{firstName}`/`{name}`/`{task}`/`{company}`/`{studio}` **before** escaping and escapes the whole result: escaping the template first and splicing raw values in is how `Smith & Sons` reaches a client as `Smith &amp; Sons`. The subject is not escaped (it is a header) but is stripped of newlines. Covered by `brief.test.ts` — 12 tests including the `javascript:`/`<script>` cases and the empty-task-name dangling dash.
  Also: approving now **opens the new task's pane** (`approveRequest` returns the id); every queue card has a delete, which leaves uploaded objects in the `intake` bucket on purpose — an approved request's files are already live links on a task, and orphaned objects are much the cheaper mistake; and the sidebar foot dropped the avatar/name/role block for textual **About · Latest updates · Logout** (the signed-in person is already named top-right), with "Latest updates" re-opening the what's-new panel via `whatsNewSince(null, …)` so it works for someone already up to date.
  ⚠️ **Untested end to end: the actual Resend call for the receipt.** Everything either side of it is verified, but firing it would have emailed a real No Traffic contact. The template renderer is unit-tested and the submission-notification mail already proves the account and `notifications@studionmore.com` work.
- 2026-08-11 — **v1.13.0 — task GROUPS: a subject inside a section.** Migration **0027 APPLIED by Nitsan.** Hierarchy is now **Client → Section → Group → Task**, one level only. A section is a phase ("Website"); a group is a subject — the several tasks that make up one webpage. **Nothing is ever logged against a group**, which is the whole reason it is its own table.
  ⚠️ **`task_groups`, NOT `tasks.parent_id` + an `is_group` flag.** The Asana model would have given the Timeline bar for free and been the wrong trade: ~30 files iterate `tasks` (My Tasks, the Board, autocomplete, Reports, KPI tiles, intake, `taskMinutes`) and every one would have silently started counting container rows as work. Keeping groups out of `tasks` means `tasks` still means what it meant yesterday. 0007 flattened the Projects layer for the same reason.
  ⚠️ **0027 re-declares the 0011 trigger to protect `group_id`** — the fourth migration to need that (0022 `start_date`, 0023 `timeline_position`). The trigger names its protected columns, so a new task column is member-writable by DEFAULT, and `section_id` has always been admin-only.
  **The one invariant: a task's group must belong to the task's section.** It is NOT a DB constraint — a composite FK would need `task_groups(section_id, id)` unique, which forbids the null-section case — so it lives in **`withGroupInvariant`** in `store.tsx`, applied to the PATCH before it is recorded, so `inversePatch` sees both keys and one ⌘Z restores both. Every reader is defensive to match: a task whose group sits in another section renders **loose**, never disappears. `updateTasksBulk` clears `groupId` whenever it sets `sectionId`/`clientId` — the only shape a uniform patch can carry the rule in, and correct rather than merely safe.
  **Store gets the section quartet one level down** — `addTaskGroup`/`updateTaskGroup`/`deleteTaskGroup`/`reorderTaskGroup`, same dense 1..n, one undo step, `wrote()` tails. Two deliberate differences: a group's position is scoped to its **section** (two sections' groups are independent runs), and **deleting one DISSOLVES it** — tasks move up to the section — rather than refusing while occupied like `deleteSection`. `deleteSection` now also refuses on groups, and `sectionIsEmpty` counts them, or the trash would be enabled and then fail. ⚠️ **`reorderTask` and `addTaskNear` are now scoped by `(clientId, sectionId, groupId)`** — a group's children densify among themselves, and renumbering the section's loose run from inside a group would shuffle rows nobody touched.
  **Order within a section: groups first, then loose tasks — NOT interleaved.** Interleaving needs one position space across two tables and a reorder that renumbers rows it doesn't own. Told Nitsan; his to overrule.
  **Timeline: a group draws a read-only STACKED BAR, not a section's bracket.** A bracket claims "everything under here falls between these dates"; a group is a thing in its own right, so it gets a bar across its children's span with two shims peeking out behind the top edge. ⚠️ **Read-only by Nitsan's choice** and `pointer-events-none`, so a marquee begun over it still reaches the rows beneath. Dragging it (moving every child by the same working days via the existing `updateTasksVaried`) is a noted follow-up. ⚠️ **Elements, not `box-shadow`** — CSS allows exactly ONE box-shadow declaration, which is why `barShadow` exists.
  ⚠️ **THREE places walk the rows by height** — the `bodyH` reduce, the marquee's `rowBands`, and the render loop — and all three had to learn the third row kind or the marquee selects the wrong tasks. Verified by measurement: a box over three rows *below* a folded group selected exactly design/visuals/mobile; an unaccounted `GROUP_H` would have shifted it by exactly one row.
  ⚠️ **`GroupHeaderRow` pins `STICKY_W`, not `leftW`** — same trap `SectionHeaderRow` hit, where an opaque 666px band sat on top of the calendar and swallowed the summary bar whole.
  **"🧮 Section totals" in the Show menu**, per tab, **on by default for the Timeline** (a folded group is a bar with no numbers otherwise) and off for the Tasks tab. ⚠️ **It must NOT light the Show trigger** — the trigger reports what you are NOT SEEING, and a total being off hides nothing; verified the button stays grey/muted with totals on. One helper, **`src/lib/task-rollup.ts`**, so four surfaces cannot disagree; it absorbs the Timeline's old inline group min/max. ⚠️ **`Section.estimateHours` (recovered from the old section name) BEATS the sum of its tasks**, and **`Section.legacyHours` must never be added** — `taskHoursDone` already folds each task's own `legacyHours` in, so adding the section's counts the same hours twice. Pinned by a test.
  **Timeline totals fill the real columns (Dates/Duration/Actual/Budget); the Tasks tab gets a compact strip instead** — that header has a `flex-1` name and no column grid, and forcing one would pin every section name to a fixed width to make room for numbers that read fine as a line. ⚠️ The Tasks strip counts plain Sun–Thu working days; the Timeline passes the studio's holidays, so a duration there can be a day shorter. **The Timeline's is the one to trust** — it is the one drawn against a calendar.
  **Public client Gantt shows groups too**, as folded summary bars in the client's own colour, and both container kinds now carry a rolled-up **budget** in the tasks' own column (a blank there beside rows that all have a number read as missing data). ⚠️ **Budget only, never logged hours** — verified in the served payload: the only fields present are id/title/sectionId/groupId/startDate/dueDate/typeName/typeColor/order/budgetHours, and the two `:26,` hits are `"order":26`, not the 26h logged on that task. Ordering is copied tiebreak-for-tiebreak from the studio chart, since a client comparing the two must not see a different order.
  **The task panel gained a Group row** under Section — dragging is fine on the client table and hopeless from a task opened via the plan or the feed. ⚠️ It offers **only the groups of the task's own section**: a group belongs to one section, so offering another's would move the task twice in one control. Hidden entirely when the section has no groups.
  ⚠️ **A `night`-only contrast failure, caught by measuring all four themes.** The group bar is `color-mix(--foreground 58%)`, so it composites DARK in the three light themes and **LIGHT under `night`** — where a white folded-group label measured **3.0:1**. `--background` fixed night and dropped `electric` to 4.09; **`--surface` clears both ends (4.61 / 4.61 / 4.97 / 6.2)** and is the principled choice anyway: the label is punched out of the bar, showing the row's own background through it. Same rule as v1.9.0 — a tint of a token and that token travel together; only a FIXED accent needs a fixed ink.
  ⚠️ **Method note, cost me two false conclusions: the browser tool's `key` action needs the literal `"Enter"`. `"Return"` dispatches `{key:"", code:"", which:0}`** — an empty event that matches nothing. I concluded from it that implicit form submission was broken and wrote that "verified against the DB" into a code comment; it was verified against a keystroke that never arrived. **Probe `e.key` before believing a keyboard test.** (The explicit Enter handler on `AddGroupRow` stayed anyway — it is the convention `InsertTaskRow` set in v1.9.1 and cannot be wrong.) Also: `read_console_messages` accumulates across reloads, so a pile of 400s can be entirely pre-migration — navigate, then read.
  Verified in the browser signed in as admin on Anchor, with 0027 applied: group created, renamed, folded, filled via the panel, dissolved; `group_id` and `section_id` both persisted across a hard reload; ⌘Z restored a cleared group (⚠️ **focus must leave the `<select>` first** — the undo binding deliberately defers to the browser's native undo inside a field); section/group/task figures agreed exactly (`26h / 100h`); marquee correct; public link correct. **All test data removed and the client verified clean** (0 groups, 0 grouped tasks, "Branding" back to its original section, dates and budget untouched). **No time entry was written.** Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 36 warnings (unchanged), **73 tests** (was 59), production build clean.

  - **Right-click gained "New group…" and "New section…"** on both task tabs. ⚠️ The group item is CONTEXT-AWARE: right-click a row that is part of a multi-selection and it becomes **"Group the N selected tasks…"**. Showing both every time would make the common case pick from four items.
  - **"Group…" in the selection bar**, put in **`TaskBulkControls`** rather than in each bar — the client table's `SelectionBar` and the Timeline's both render it, and the whole point of that component is that the two cannot drift. New store method **`groupTasksIntoNew`**: ⚠️ it REFUSES a selection spanning two sections (a group belongs to one) and says which axis disagrees, rather than silently re-sectioning tasks — which on a client page would move work between phases. Surfaced through the new **`showNotice`**, not `noteWriteError`: being told "that isn't possible" is not a failure to save. ⚠️ Creating the group is not undoable and the move is one `updateTasksBulk` step, so ⌘Z takes the tasks back out and leaves an empty group — visible, and one click to remove.
  - **Drag-into-a-group already worked**; verified both directions with synthetic `DragEvent`s (drop on the group sets `group_id`, drop on the section header clears it).
  - **Timeline group bars are BLUE and the folded stack is OPAQUE** — the neutral `--foreground` mix read as black, which put the chart's heaviest mark on the one row that isn't work. ⚠️ **`--brand` is `#0b43ed` in ALL FOUR themes**, and on `night`'s `#141519` surface that is **2.64:1** — survivable for a filled button with white text, not for a 3px rule and an 11px caption. Hence a new **`--group-bar`** token that `night` lifts to the palette's light blue `#6181e8`. Measured 6.91 / 6.91 / 6.91 / **5.05**, and the folded label is `--surface` (punched out of the bar) so ink and bar invert together.
  - **Two shapes for two states**, his direction: **open** = a thin blue rule with the name ABOVE it and **no end tips** (the rows are the caption, and repeating the section's tips one level down made two brackets of the same shape in different sizes); **folded** = the stacked bar, because the rows are gone and the group has to stand in for them.
  - **A group's task rows are indented on the Timeline** (`TL_INDENT`, padding on the pinned block so `STICKY_W` is unchanged). ⚠️ Only the CHILDREN move — a loose task and a group are really siblings, so aligning those exactly would be more correct, but it would re-indent every row on every client's chart for a feature most sections don't use.
  - **Removing a group asks WHICH removal you meant** — a real dialog, not a `confirm()`, because `confirm()` has two buttons and both would have to be "yes" while one reading CASCADES to time entries. "Remove the group only" (tasks move up, keeping every hour and date) vs "Delete the group and its N tasks", and ⚠️ **the destructive option is DISABLED when any task carries logged hours**, with the figure in the copy — the same rule `deleteTasksBulk` already follows. Verified: with 26h on Branding it came up disabled and explained.
  ⚠️ **Method, twice over.** (1) **The browser tool's `key` action needs the literal `"Enter"`; `"Return"` dispatches `{key:"", code:"", which:0}`** — an empty event matching nothing. I read a failed keystroke as a broken form submission and wrote "verified against the DB" into a comment; it was verified against a keypress that never arrived. **Probe `e.key` before believing a keyboard test.** (2) **The stale-CSS trap is real and silent**: `--group-bar` was correct in `globals.css` and simply absent from the served bundle. `curl`ing the CSS chunk and finding `brand-soft` 40× and `group-bar` 0× is what proved it; `rm -rf .next` + a dev-server restart fixed it. Also `read_console_messages` accumulates across reloads, so a pile of 400s can be entirely pre-migration.

Its visual is **generated at life size** by **`scripts/build-whatsnew-group.mjs`**: 372×206 drawn with the client table's own values, which the panel shows at ~318px — **0.85, so a real 13px label lands at 11.1px**. ⚠️ The crop is chosen FOR that scale: drawing the Timeline's pinned column plus a useful slice of calendar is ~570px and would have scaled to 0.55, putting those labels at 7px — the exact failure the phone mockup was rebuilt to avoid. The hierarchy is the news and the hierarchy is in the name column. Verified in the panel with `naturalWidth 372` (not the silent-failure 0). The second item gets **`build-whatsnew-totals.mjs`** — the Show menu at `show-menu.tsx`'s own values (372×422, so 0.85 and a 14px label at 12px, standing 360px inside the 440px panel). ⚠️ **The WHOLE menu, type list included**, not the switches with the rest sliced off: a card cut at its edge reads as a fault, which is what makes this one taller than its sibling. ⚠️ And the trigger is drawn **grey, not lit** — totals being on hides nothing, and a picture showing it brand-coloured would teach the opposite of what the control does.

Extracting it would have meant threading eight no-op callbacks through a public page to reuse a rectangle.
  - ⚠️ **Milestone dates widen the chart's date window.** `from`/`totalDays` was computed from task dates plus today, and a launch is routinely set AFTER the last task's due date — so the one date the client most wants to see would have been drawn off the right-hand edge with no scroll reaching it.

Below the pinned column's `z-20` it slides under instead. Verified by hit-test, not by eye: `elementFromPoint` at the flag's own position returns the pinned section header. The studio can live with the overlap; the page a client reads should not have it.

The **today line does exactly the same** — both measured inside the pinned column at the same moment — so the mark lines match what already ships rather than adding a new artefact. Fixing it means an opaque backdrop behind the pinned column on both charts.

- 2026-08-11 — **v1.12.0 (DEPLOYED + committed `d777223`, pushed to main; prod verified serving v1.12.0 on tracker.studionmore.com) — the tracker works on a phone.** No migration. Scope chosen with Nitsan and deliberately partial: **log time, see my tasks, approve/triage**. Six routes stay desktop-only. Every change is `md:`-prefixed or gated on a new `useIsNarrow()`, so **nothing at ≥768px moves** — verified by measurement at 1440 (sidebar 208px, content margin 208px, `main` padding 24px, header one row, all unchanged).
  ⚠️ **iOS Safari zooms the page whenever a focused field is under 16px, and every field in this app is `text-sm`.** That looked like it needed an `Input` primitive and 45 edits before any mobile work could start. It didn't: **`globals.css` is UNLAYERED and Tailwind v4 emits utilities inside `@layer utilities`, so an unlayered rule beats a layered one whatever the specificity** — six lines in a `max-width:767px` block override `text-sm` at all 146 field sites. Verified in the built bundle: the rule lands at byte 72096, `.text-sm` at 42201, no enclosing block. The `maximum-scale=1` viewport hack is deliberately NOT used (iOS honours it inconsistently and it breaks WCAG 1.4.4).
  **Shell = bottom bar + drawer** (`app-shell.tsx`). Bar: Home · Tasks · **+ Log time** (an action, not a route) · Inbox (admin) · Menu. The sidebar is `hidden md:flex`; the content margin is `md:`-prefixed on BOTH branches, or it indents by the width of a rail that isn't there.
  ⚠️ **`--sb-active-fg` IS NOT USABLE AS FLAT TEXT ON THE SIDEBAR BACKGROUND.** It is the ink for text on `--sb-active-bg` (the white pill), and under `electric` it is `#0b43ed` — the exact blue of `--sb-bg`. The bar's active item rendered at **1.00:1 in BOTH `electric` and `gallery`**, i.e. invisible, while looking fine in `night` and `default`. Caught in a screenshot, then confirmed arithmetically across all four. The pair that always works on a bar with no pill is **`--sb-fg` (active) vs `--sb-muted` (inactive)** — 6.9–20.0:1 and 4.2–5.7:1.
  **Six routes are desktop-only** — `/plan`, `/clients`, `/clients/[id]`, `/reports`, `/client-reports`, `/feed` — hidden from the drawer via `NAV`'s new `mobile` flag AND given an explaining card in `desktop-only.tsx`. **Not a redirect**: several are linked from elsewhere and a redirect destroys the URL somebody was sent. The card renders INSIDE the shell, so nav still works. **Keep the two lists in agreement** — hidden with no entry means a phone renders the real, broken page.
  **Log time, two shapes.** From a task (row or panel) the task is known, so it is one bottom sheet. From the bar's "+" it is two steps — full-screen search with recents, then the form — because cramming search + results + duration + description + date into one sheet leaves the search two rows. ⚠️ **The phone picker cannot reuse `TaskAutocomplete`**: its list is `absolute` and the sheet body is an `overflow-y-auto` scroller, which clips BOTH axes (fifth instance). The rule is shared as **`useTaskMatches`** and the phone renders it inline. Quick chips 30m/1h/2h, `md:hidden`.
  **Task panel: three tabs (Details/Time/Talk) and READ-ONLY fields on a phone**, Nitsan's call. ⚠️ The panes are wrapped in **`contents` / `hidden md:contents`**, not rendered conditionally — `display:contents` removes the wrapper from layout so at ≥768px the children stay direct flex items and the existing `gap-5` is untouched; conditional rendering would also discard the comment draft on every tab switch. Read-only reuses the **member** branches that already existed at all six `isAdmin ?` sites. ⚠️ **`QuietSelect` (Assignee/Type/Status) had NO read-only branch** — those are member-editable by design — and was the only editor to survive the gate; it now renders the chosen option's label as text.
  ⚠️ **`PeriodStepper` must own its own overflow.** Four pills + the stepper need ~500px, so it wrapped to three rows at 375px. The first fix made it `flex-nowrap` and leaned on the home page's scrolling filter strip — which pushed **/team's document to 637px wide**, because /team and /reports host the same control with no scroller. It now scrolls itself (`-mx-4 … overflow-x-auto … md:overflow-visible`), which fixes all three hosts and can't be broken by a future caller.
  **Admin home header: ~440px → 136px**, at Nitsan's request ("don't waste place, stack them better"). Greeting, tenure, billable switch, range pills, stepper and client select each took a row because one `flex-wrap` row simply wraps six items at 375px. Now two lines: identity with the tenure counter pulled up beside the name, then billable + client with the stepper `order-last basis-full` below it. **First KPI tile moved from y≈570 to y=224** — all four tiles and the week timesheet now fit the first screen. ⚠️ `order-last` rather than reordering the JSX: DOM order IS the desktop order.
  **Also:** `Tabs` (`ui.tsx`) gained `overflow-x-auto` + `shrink-0` — it had neither wrap nor scroll, so Settings' five tabs ran off the edge with no way to reach the last two (reaches all 8 call sites); member "My week" scrolls below `sm` (5 cards × 63px fits neither a weekday nor an hours figure); the hours-by-client **donut is `md:` only** (drawn at a literal 300×300 with a legend beside it — at 375px the client names become one character each) while the trend chart stays, since it already renders through a `viewBox` at `width="100%"`; `TaskTable` renders **client-grouped cards** below `md` and the `colw.*` widths are simply not consulted there, so a dragged desktop layout survives a phone visit.
  **New files:** `src/lib/use-is-narrow.ts` (⚠️ built on **`matchMedia` + its `change` event**, never a `resize` listener — same source of truth as the `md:` classes, and the browser tool does not dispatch `resize`), `mobile-sheet.tsx`, `mobile-log-time.tsx`, `desktop-only.tsx`, `primitives.tsx` (Button/Field/Card — every variant emits the class string that already ships, so a converted call site is provably a no-op; **no weight prop**, because globals.css collapses medium/semibold/bold onto one weight and that is how 23 button strings grew from ~5 real buttons).
  Verified in the browser signed in as admin at 375×812, at the 767/768 boundary (both flip together — `matchMedia` and the `md:` classes agree, no dead zone) and at 1440×900. Log-time verified end to end **without writing a row** (the dev server points at the live studio DB). Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 36 warnings (+3, all the pre-existing `set-state-in-effect` advisory shape), 48 tests, production build clean.
  **Follow-ups from Nitsan reading it on the phone, same release:** "Coming up" (Celebrations) is off on mobile — ⚠️ the gate goes on a WRAPPER with `md:contents`, because the pane's own div carries `empty:hidden` and adding `hidden md:block` beside it would let `md:block` win at desktop and leave an empty box on quiet days. The studio roster is a **list** below `sm` (two columns of portrait cards gave each ~150px for an avatar, a name, an hours figure and a bar stacked — four rows of chrome and two people per screen; on their side it is one row each). Team-card photos go **124 → 64px** on a phone, which more than halves the card — it's a `size` NUMBER, so it needs `useIsNarrow`, not an `sm:` prefix. Team and intake headers stack: both were one no-wrap `justify-between` row, so the subtitle and the checkbox squeezed each other and every label broke mid-phrase.
  ⚠️ **On the intake header, `flex-wrap` alone did nothing.** The title block is `flex-1`, so flex shrank IT to min-content rather than wrapping anything — leaving all three items on one line with "Intake Queue" broken across two rows. **`basis-full` on the checkbox** is what forces the break. The three are flex SIBLINGS reordered with `order` rather than nested in two groups, which is what lets the button ride beside the title on a phone **without a second instance** — it prefetches its URL on mount, so two would mean two fetches. Its label is "Form link" below `sm` and "Copy form link" above (two spans, not a JS branch, so there's no hydration flash), and the h1 drops to `text-xl` because at `2xl` the title plus the button need 7px more than a 375px screen has.
  **Also new: a "Version X is out" panel — an onboarding walk-through on a laptop, a plain list on a phone.** `src/lib/whats-new.ts` holds the notes; `whats-new-modal.tsx` renders them.
  - **It shows everything you MISSED, not just the newest release.** `whatsNewSince(seen, isAdmin)` returns every release newer than the version you last dismissed, so somebody back from leave is caught up in one panel. If it only ever announced `APP_VERSION`, two releases in a week would mean the first reached whoever happened to sign in that day and was silently lost for everyone else. ⚠️ **Compare with `compareVersions`, never as strings** — `"v1.9.0" > "v1.12.0"` alphabetically, which would hide every release between them. Pinned by a test.
  - ⚠️ **`seen === null` means BOTH a brand-new account and the whole team the first time this ships**, so it shows the entire list. That is deliberate: it is what catches the studio up on v1.10–v1.11, which predate the panel. **Trimming `RELEASES` is the only lever for stopping a new hire reading history** — a date cutoff would go stale silently.
  - **Each item carries its own `image` and becomes one STEP** with a Next button (`MAX_STEPS` 5, `MAX_RELEASES` 3; the remainder is summarised as "+ N more", and ⚠️ that count must cover items dropped by BOTH caps — a test pins it). An item with no picture falls back to the studio wordmark on the blue. **Draw images 200×400 (a phone) or 1:2** — the blue panel crops the bottom so the device bleeds off the edge.
  - **A phone gets the plain list instead** — no stepper, no pictures: five Next taps is a lot for something nobody asked to read, and the pictures are phone mockups being shown on a phone.
  - **The `audience` tag is the whole point** — an admin-only change shown to a designer is noise that teaches them to dismiss the panel unread, which costs you the release where it mattered. A release whose every item was for the other role is dropped entirely rather than shown empty.
  - ⚠️ **Dismissal is localStorage, not `profiles`** — the repo's convention for per-user UI preferences (v1.1.0), and 0021's trigger lets a member write only `name`/`avatar_url`/`photo_url`, so a `seen_version` column would need an amended trigger or a service-role route for "I've read this". Cost: laptop and phone each show it once. ⚠️ **Marked seen on DISMISS, not on open** — a reload mid-read would otherwise lose it permanently. Suppressed on `/welcome`, which IS somebody's first sign-in.
  - ⚠️ **Two layout traps this hit:** `className="p-0"` on `Modal` does nothing (needs **`p-0!`**), and **`ModalClose` is not absolutely positioned** — dropped into a flex row it becomes the first column and shoves the blue panel off the card's edge.
  - **To announce a release: add an entry whose `version` is newer than the last.** No entry, or no items for that role, means no panel. Verified: admin got 5 steps, `?viewAs=Nadav` got the member subset, dismiss persisted across a reload. 57 tests (was 48).
  **Second round of phone follow-ups (Nitsan, same release) — the member home fits two screens instead of four.** Every change is `md:`-prefixed or gated on `useIsNarrow`; measured unchanged at 1440 (header 52px, hero `mt-9`/`pr-32` intact, both hidden rows back at 278px and 366px, My week five columns).
  - **"Log my hours" and "My tasks" are `md:` only now** — on a phone they duplicate the bottom bar's "+" and Tasks, and the two of them WERE the whole second screen. ⚠️ **That orphans the hero's `#log` anchor**, which is the CTA the release was built around: the pane it scrolls to no longer exists there. The hero now renders **two buttons** — a `md:hidden` one opening `MobileLogTimeSheet`, the `hidden md:block` anchor above it — rather than a JS branch, so there is no hydration flash. The sheet is self-contained, so `Dashboard` owning a second instance beats threading a callback down through `AppShell`'s children.
  - **My week shows today onwards below 768px.** ⚠️ **The fallback is load-bearing**: the studio week is Sun–Thu, so on a Friday or Saturday every card is in the past and filtering leaves an EMPTY pane — it falls back to the whole week there. ⚠️ **Its layout breakpoint had to move `sm` → `md` to match `useIsNarrow`**: the card count now varies below 768, and `sm:grid-cols-5` would have laid two remaining days out in fifths of the width. Cards gained `grow` so a short week fills the row; with all five the basis already exceeds the screen, so nothing grows and it scrolls as before.
  - **Header: four rows → two.** The member's "Tasks assigned" counter and the client select are both `md:` only — the bottom bar already carries Tasks, and scoping your own hours to one client is desk work. Identity + the period stepper is what's left.
  - **The gaps around the blue hero were mostly two `mt-9`s that a phone never needed.** ⚠️ **`mt-9` is the portrait's headroom and the portrait is `hidden sm:block`** — so below `sm` it was 36px of nothing above the hero; the tiles' own `mt-9` only earns its place at `lg`, where they are the column BESIDE the hero and must start level with it. Same cause, same file: ⚠️ **`pr-32` is the portrait's COLUMN**, reserving 128px of a 295px line on a screen with no portrait, which is what wrapped the hero heading onto three lines. All three are now breakpoint-gated, and the page gap is `gap-2 md:gap-4`. Result: 8px between every pane, hero heading on two lines, the whole member home ending at 929px.
  - **The phone wordmark is `text-brand`.** It inherited the foreground and so rendered Studio Black — and below `md` it is the ONLY place the mark appears. **The DRAWER's wordmark is deliberately left white**: it sits on `--sb-bg`, which under `electric` IS brand blue, so brand-blue ink there is invisible — the same 1.00:1 trap as `--sb-active-fg` on the bottom bar.
  - ⚠️ **Method: `resize_window` dispatches NEITHER `resize` NOR a `matchMedia` `change` event.** `mq.matches` flips but nothing fires, so anything gated on `useIsNarrow` keeps its old shape while the `md:` classes around it have already switched — My week read three cards at 1440 until a reload, which looks exactly like a broken hook. Measured: an armed `change` listener took **0 hits** across 375 → 1440. **Reload after resizing.** The hook's own comment claimed the opposite and has been corrected.
  Verified in the browser signed in, at 375×812 (`?viewAs=Nadav` for the member view) and 1440×900. The hero's phone CTA was confirmed to open the log-time sheet; **no row was written** — the dev server points at the live studio DB. Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 36 warnings (unchanged — the new `useMemo` hoists `weekStartIso` out of its dep list rather than adding a suppression), 59 tests, production build clean.
  **NOT done, deliberately:** global search is desktop-only on a phone (it wants ~400px and the 375px header already holds the wordmark, sync dot and bell — the log-time sheet has its own task search); the intake-queue detail screen and the read-only member summary are still the desktop layouts, which already respond acceptably.
- 2026-08-10 — **v1.11.1 (DEPLOYED + committed `040ddfb`, pushed to main; prod verified serving v1.11.1 on tracker.studionmore.com) — an edit no longer jumps back when a background refresh was already in the air.** No migration. Nitsan: *"sometimes when I change something it jumps back"* — he guessed undo, other users, or the sync. It was the sync, and the diagnosis is worth keeping because the guard that failed looks correct.
  - Every store mutation is optimistic, and `refresh()` guards against clobbering one — but it tested `writesBusy() || focusInEditor()` **only before fetching, never again after the response landed**. A refresh already in flight when the user edited something returns rows read BEFORE that edit; by the time they land the write has usually settled, so every "is a write in flight?" test says no and `applyHot` paints the pre-edit row over the user's own change. `mergeTasks` preserves only `brief`, so everything else is wholesale. The next tick then fetches the real value and it jumps forward again — which is why it reads as a flicker rather than as lost work. **The server was never wrong.**
  - ⚠️ **A busy-counter cannot answer this question.** `writes` goes back down when the write settles, and the settle usually beats the response home. New **`writeSeq`** counts writes ISSUED and never decrements; sampled before the fetch and compared after, it's the only test that can tell the response is older than the screen. Rule extracted as **`refreshVerdict()`** in [snapshot.ts](src/lib/snapshot.ts) — `apply` / `stale` (superseded by a newer refresh; drop silently) / `deferred` (older than local state; drop AND refetch) — with a test file pinning the settled-write case.
  - The same race existed in **`loadTaskExtras`**, the open task pane's lazy re-fetch of comments, attachments and hours — the most-edited surface in the app. Guarded too, but **only when `refetch: true`**: the first open has nothing to fall back on, so bailing there would leave the pane permanently empty.
  - Known and accepted: if a deferred response's write had already settled, nothing drains `refreshQueued` until the next 60s tick. Staleness is capped at the normal cadence, so it isn't worth a retry timer that would poll every 1.5s during heavy editing.
  - **NOT verified at runtime** — reproducing the race needs a signed-in session and Claude can't enter credentials. Verified by construction, 48 tests (was 42), tsc, lint and a production build.
- 2026-08-10 — **v1.11.0 (DEPLOYED + committed `cebabf6`, pushed to main; prod verified serving v1.11.0 on tracker.studionmore.com) — the Timeline's marks get calibrated, and the Board becomes the studio's statuses.** No migration (0025 + 0026 already applied). Eighteen commits, nearly all from Nitsan reading the shipped v1.10.0 in front of him.
  - **The two verticals were the wrong way round.** The milestone lines were solid and the today line was half-strength, so a mark the studio invented outranked the one fact the chart can't be wrong about. Swapped, then today went **black** rather than brand-blue: with type colours on every bar, a blue line reads as one more category. Today is now a single marker — a black date chip in the ruler with a downward tail meeting the line, per Nitsan's reference — drawn **beneath** the bars, so it locates work rather than crossing it out. The past is shaded; the chip, the line and the shading all land on the same pixel (checked at 532px on a day-zoom chart).
  - **Section brackets: 3px, dropped low in a 36px row, dark not pink.** `SECTION_BAR_H` was `BAR_H * 0.3`, which at 6px still read as a bar of its own — a section is a bracket over its rows, so the tips talk and the line just joins them. `SECTION_H` went 30 → 36 to leave room for the name **above** the bracket, which is what a folded section shows in place of its rows.
  - **Task bars: 27px in a 34px row, with a soft two-layer shadow, and an optional plain (white) mode.** ⚠️ **CSS allows exactly ONE `box-shadow` declaration**, so the selection ring, the plain-mode outline and the drop shadow have to be composed into one string — see `barShadow` in [client-timeline.tsx](src/components/client-timeline.tsx). The multi-select ring is `inset 0 0 0 1px var(--brand)`; 2px was Nitsan's first complaint about it.
  - **The Show menu's three view settings became emoji + switch rows**, while the type filter stayed checkboxes — those are a list you tick items out of, these are settings you flip. Only the type filter lights the trigger, so the resting state of a client page can't look filtered.
  - **The Board's columns are now the studio's statuses (`tags`), and its cards drag between them.** They used to be three hardcoded pillars — To do / In progress / Done — that existed nowhere else in the app and could only be set by dragging, so the board was a second, private status system. The status chip came off the cards (the column already says it), a leading **"No status"** column holds the unassigned, and the rail scrolls sideways rather than wrapping when the statuses outgrow the width.
  - **Board became its own tab** — `Task list · Board · Timeline · Overview` — which retired the list/board segmented control. Both task arrangements **stay mounted** when hidden: column widths, selection and collapsed sections are local state a round trip would throw away. Each keeps its own Show settings, following the per-tab rule.
  - ⚠️ **The board's horizontal scrollbar was thousands of pixels down the page**, because the rail was as tall as its tallest column (117 cards on Anchor). The rail is now measured to the window (`window.innerHeight - top - 24`, floor 320) and each column scrolls internally, so the only scrollbar on the rail is the horizontal one and it's always on screen. `min-h-0` on both the column and its card list is load-bearing.
- 2026-08-09 — **v1.10.0 (DEPLOYED + committed `239d427`, pushed to main; prod verified serving v1.10.0) — the Timeline gets multi-drag, marquee selection and milestones; both task tabs get one Show menu and one layout.** Migrations **0025** (`gantt_links`) and **0026** (`timeline_marks`) APPLIED by Nitsan. ⚠️ **After a `create table`, PostgREST serves a STALE schema cache** — every insert came back *"Could not find the table in the schema cache"* with a 404 for several minutes while the table plainly existed. It self-heals, or `notify pgrst, 'reload schema';` forces it. Don't debug the code first. ⚠️ And a Turbopack **parse error can outlive the file that caused it**: the dev server replayed one for a file `npm run build` compiled cleanly. `rm -rf .next` and restart before believing it.
  **Multi-drag.** Grab a bar that is in the selection and the whole selection shifts by the same number of working days, each task keeping its own duration. MOVE only — a 2-day task and a 3-week task do not want the same edge nudge. New store method **`updateTasksVaried`**: per-task patches as ONE history entry, the varied sibling of `updateTasksBulk`. Looping `updateTask` would have written the same rows and left ten undo steps behind. Verified: 3 bars +2 days, committed, one ⌘Z restored all three.
  **Marquee.** Press empty calendar, drag a rectangle. Hit-tests the BAR, not the row, so a box over August takes the tasks that run in August rather than everything whose row it crossed. Starts only from `data-chart-bg`, so a press on a bar is left to the bar's own drag.
  **Milestones** (`timeline_marks`): click the ruler to place one on the exact day under the pointer at any zoom, type its name, drag it to move, trash to delete. ⚠️ **Deliberately NOT snapped to a working day** — task dates are, because work happens Sun–Thu, but a launch can be a Friday. ⚠️ **The line and its label need OPPOSITE depths**, and that needs TWO layers: the line under the bars (a milestone marks the work, it doesn't cut through it) and the label above everything. One wrapper with a `z-index` creates a stacking context the label can never escape, which is how the name field ended up covered by the section bar it sat on. ⚠️ **The drag must NOT `preventDefault` on pointerdown** — that suppresses the click that follows, which is why the trash did nothing; the drag records whether it moved and the rename click bails on that instead. ~~Always visible on `/gantt/[token]`, so a mark is client-visible the moment it is saved.~~ **← WRONG, corrected 2026-08-11: the public page never fetched or drew `timeline_marks` at all. Shipped in v1.12.1.**
  **One "Show" menu** for completed / undated / type, on both task tabs, **per tab**: Undated wants opposite defaults (the Timeline hides dateless rows, the table shows them) and one value cannot have two. ⚠️ **The trigger reports state** — folding filters into a menu is how a filtered chart comes to look like a quiet month. Only the TYPE filter lights it; completed/undated being off is these views' resting state, and a badge on open is noise everyone learns to ignore. **The legend is gone entirely** — the Type column shows each row's colour beside its name, which is the same information per row rather than a key you have to hold in your head. The Show menu is the only filter control, and ~130 lines went with it.
  **Both tabs now share one toolbar**: identity and Share in the header, tabs left, the view-mode switch centred (day/week/month, or list/board), Show + Columns right. Client-report buttons removed from the client page; the edit pencil moved beside the client name, unboxed.
  ⚠️ **`bg-background/40` over a `bg-background` canvas is the same colour** — the row hover tint was invisible the moment it reached the calendar. And a translucent tint REPLACES `bg-surface` rather than layering over it, so the pinned column went 40% transparent on hover and the calendar showed through the task's own name.
