Pinned blocks now mix an opaque tint; the chart area keeps a translucent one, because it paints over the grid and an opaque tint blanks that row's weekend shading.
  Also: week rules 7%→10% and month rules 15%→18%; month markers 12px uppercase tracked (AUG); today line 1px→2px.
- 2026-08-09 — **v1.9.5 — the app's Timeline card takes the height the page leaves it.** No migration. Same complaint as v1.9.2, now on the studio's own Timeline rather than the client's: `max-h-[min(70vh,640px)]` put a 1679px-tall plan in a 640px box with 175px of empty window underneath at 1800×1000, and far more on a 27".
  **MEASURED, not `h-dvh` + `flex-1` as on the public page.** That page owns its whole layout; this card is one tab of three inside the app shell's `main`, and giving the shell a definite height would change how Tasks and Overview scroll. The card reads its own top offset on mount and on resize instead, which costs one layout read and leaves every other page alone.
  ⚠️ **Subtract what is BELOW the card as well.** Taking only `innerHeight − top − padding` left the document 29px too tall — a scrollbar on a layout whose whole point is not to have one — because the "N tasks with no due date aren't shown" note sits under the card inside the same panel. It now measures the panel root's bottom minus the card's bottom. Result at 1440×900: card 762 → the footnote sits exactly on the fold, `scrollHeight === innerHeight`. At 1600×1400: 1162px, 33 rows visible instead of 18. Floor of 320px so a very short window scrolls the page rather than showing a sliver of chart.
  ⚠️ Method, again: **`resize_window` does not dispatch a `resize` event** — hand-fire `new Event("resize")` or the listener looks dead. And a measurement read in the same tick as the state change reports the OLD value; read it in a later call.
- 2026-08-09 — **v1.9.4 — "Add time" on a task row opens a real popover again.** No migration. Nitsan pressed it on My Tasks and got a collapsed strip with a clipped form in it.
  ⚠️ **`AddTimePopover` was `absolute` inside the table's `overflow-x-auto` wrapper — and a scroll container clips BOTH axes.** So the panel was cut off at the wrapper's edge and took the row's height with it. It is now `fixed`, PORTALLED to `document.body`, positioned from the button's rect, flipping above when the window runs out below (verified at a 300px-tall window: opens upward, fully on screen). **This is the FOURTH time this wrapper has caught something** — the timeline Dates popout (v1.5.0), the assignee list (v1.6.0), the task-search dropdown (v1.1.2) and now this. **Anchored panel in this app ⇒ `fixed` + measured + flipped. There is no case where `absolute` is right.**
  ⚠️ **`e.currentTarget` read INSIDE a `setState` updater is null.** React clears it when the handler returns and the updater runs later, so `addPopoverPos(e.currentTarget)` threw on every click. Capture the element in the handler body first. Same family as the v1.2.0 drag bug that committed from inside a `setDrag` updater — **updaters are not handlers; nothing about the event survives into them.**
  **Second, independent bug the fix exposed:** the stacked `LogTimeForm`'s control row needs ~388px and the panel gave it 262, so the DATE was cut mid-value (visible in Nitsan's screenshot as "09/08/20"). ⚠️ **That row's width is not a constant — the member `<select>` is sized by the LONGEST NAME IN THE STUDIO**, so hiring can break it. The row is now `flex-wrap` (it degrades instead of overflowing, and this helps every one of the six surfaces sharing that form), and the popover went **w-72 → w-[27rem] (288 → 432px)** at Nitsan's request, which is wide enough that the row fits on one line and the description gets the full 406px.
  Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 33 warnings, 42 tests, production build clean.
- 2026-08-09 — **v1.9.3 — the About panel becomes legible: the (i) tooltip stops spilling out of its own card, the greys pass contrast, and the (i) glyph is drawn rather than typeset.** No migration. All from Nitsan reading the shipped panel.
  ⚠️ **The tooltip's text ran 1,277px out of the side of its 240px card.** Not a width or a position bug — **inherited `white-space`**. The two dots by the donut sit inside legend captions carrying `whitespace-nowrap` (added in v1.9.0 to stop the captions wrapping), so the card kept its box while the sentence refused to wrap. `InfoDot` was already re-stating `normal-case`/`tracking-normal`/`leading-relaxed` for exactly this reason — **a card of prose has to re-state every text property its host may have set for a LABEL** — and `whitespace-normal` was simply missing from that list.
  **`text-faint` is 2.62:1 on white and the panel used it at 10–11px.** Every instance became `text-muted` (**5.92:1**) — there is now no `text-faint` left in the panel. Same pass: the blue hero's secondary text was `white/70` (**4.11:1**) → `white/85` (5.86:1), and the (i) dots were `opacity-60`, compositing to ~2.5:1 on a 14px target, under the 3:1 floor for a control → `opacity-80`. **Checked under `night` too**, where `faint` and `muted` INVERT (faint is the darker one there): the change helps rather than hurts, 3.06:1 → 7.12:1.
  ⚠️ **The circled "i" is now a lucide `Info`, not a bordered circle with a letter in it.** `leading-none` makes the line box shorter than the font's own content box, so the ascent overflowed upward and the glyph sat **1px high**, touching the ring (measured: 1.5px clearance above, 2.5px below). Any pixel nudge fixing that would be **a fact about Rubik**, and this app falls back to Rubik only until the real Saans arrives — at which point the nudge would be wrong again. **An icon centres by geometry**; verified 0px vertical asymmetry across all 18 dots. The Timeline's hint (i) in `client-view.tsx` was already an `Info` icon and only needed the `text-faint` → `text-muted` fix; those two are the ONLY (i) affordances in the app.
  **Also:** the rail's milestone dates went 11px/380 → 13px/570. Note that `font-semibold` here resolves to the SAME 570 as `font-medium`/`font-bold` (globals.css collapses all three), so **size is the other half of the hierarchy, not decoration** — the same lesson as v1.7.0. Tightest same-side gap between rendered captions is 21px, nothing runs off the card, and the panel still fits with no scroller at 1440×900 and 1280×720 (612px, unchanged). The hero's "years covered · 2016–2026" dropped its range — "11 years" already says it.
  Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 33 warnings, 42 tests, production build clean.
- 2026-08-09 — **v1.9.2 — the public client Gantt (`/gantt/[token]`) uses the screen it is given.** No migration. Nitsan reported it "not responsive" with a 27" screenshot: the card sat capped in the middle of an empty window while the chart scrolled inside it.
  ⚠️ **`min-h-screen` is not a height, and that is why the first fix didn't take.** Removing the card's `max-h-[min(78vh,760px)]` and giving it `flex-1` made it grow to its own CONTENT (1700px of Anchor rows) and the PAGE scrolled — exactly what the card's own scroller exists to prevent. A flex child only caps against a DEFINITE height, so `main` is **`h-dvh`** (`dvh`, so a phone's collapsing URL bar doesn't leave a dead strip) and the card is `flex min-h-0 flex-1`. `min-h-0` is the half that lets it shrink below its content.
  **The width cap is deliberately 2200px, not the app's 1500px.** Every other page is reading-width text and tables; this one is a single chart that is ALWAYS wider than the window (91 days × 26px at day zoom = 2.4k), so every pixel is another day the client sees without scrolling — capped at all only so it doesn't stretch on an ultrawide. Measured at 2560×1400: **69 days visible, up from 43**, card 1242px instead of 760, no document scroll on either axis.
  **It was also genuinely broken on a phone**, which matters because this is the link CLIENTS open. At 375px the header's three grid tracks collided — the client's own name squeezed to nothing behind the zoom control with the wordmark on top of both — so below `sm` it wraps to two rows (name, then zoom and wordmark at either end). The pinned column narrows 316 → 206px (`useStickyWidth`, server-rendered wide and corrected on mount) and a narrow screen **opens on week zoom**, because day zoom left ~40px of calendar, about a day and a half, which reads as an empty plan. On MOUNT only — rotating the device must not overrule a zoom the reader picked. **That pinned width is the ONLY responsive dimension**: the bars stay at a fixed px-per-day so the chart reads the same on every screen and scrolls instead.
  ⚠️ **Method note: the browser tool's `resize_window` does NOT dispatch a `resize` event**, so the pinned column appeared not to respond until a hand-fired `new Event("resize")` proved the listener works. Don't conclude a resize listener is broken from that tool alone.
  Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 33 warnings (+1: the mount effect reading `innerWidth`, same advisory shape as the existing ones), 42 tests, production build clean.
- 2026-08-07 — **v1.9.1 — right-click a task to insert one above or below it, a "Show undated" toggle on the Timeline, and the Timeline hint tooltip stops painting under the table.** No migration.
  **New store method `addTaskNear(anchorId, where, title, opts)`.** `addTask` always appends, which is the whole reason this exists. It slots the new row into **both** orderings — `position` (client table, per section) and `timeline_position` (Timeline, per client) — so it lands beside its anchor whichever view created it, rather than adjacent in one and at the bottom of the other. ⚠️ It **densifies** the section rather than opening one gap: every task the imports created has `position = 0`, so a section can be entirely zeros and there is no gap to open — renumbering 1..n is what makes the placement mean anything (same reason `reorderTask` does it). Like `addTask` it is deliberately **not** undoable; nothing that creates a task is.
  ⚠️ **The Timeline insert passes `copyDates`, seeding start/due from the anchor.** That view only draws tasks that HAVE a due date, so a dateless insert would vanish the instant it was created and read as a failed command. The client table doesn't need it — it lists undated tasks fine.
  **Both views open an inline composer rather than creating a row named "New task".** Changing your mind costs an Escape instead of a delete, and delete here cascades to time entries. ⚠️ **Enter is handled explicitly, not by implicit form submission** — the input sits inside a wrapper div and the form has no submit button, and Enter silently did nothing until the `onKeyDown` was added. The pre-existing `AddTaskRow` has the same shape; if it ever misbehaves, this is why.
  **Gating follows each view's own rules, which differ:** the client table's menu is open to everyone, matching its "Add task…" row, which was never admin-gated; the Timeline's is admin-only, matching every other editable thing in that view.
  **"Show undated" toggle (Timeline only).** Lists the tasks with no dates at all as rows with **no bar**, so they can be scheduled from the Dates cell instead of being invisible with a footnote. `Row` gained `undated`; an undated row borrows today's date so the row shape stays valid and **every drawing branch checks `row.undated`, never the dates**. Four places needed that guard and one was missed first: a task with a start but no due still printed a real duration ("1 day") because the read-only fallback tested `row.hasStart` alone. The Tasks tab doesn't get the toggle — it already lists undated work.
  ⚠️ **The Timeline's (i) hint card was painting UNDER the table.** It was `absolute z-40` inside the client page's header bar, which is `sticky top-14 z-10` — **a positioned ancestor with a z-index is a stacking context, so nothing inside it can rise above the Timeline's own `z-20` sticky header**, no matter how high its z-index. Fixed by portalling to `document.body` and positioning `fixed`, exactly as `HoverTip` and the weekly plan's search dropdown (v1.1.0) already do. **This is the third time this bug has appeared in this codebase — check for a positioned ancestor before raising a z-index.**
  Verified in the browser with Nitsan signed in, on Anchor: menu opens in both views; a Timeline insert landed directly after "Branding" with its 9–18 Aug dates copied, and appeared right after Branding in the client table too, proving both axes slotted; a client-table insert above "Home page - design" landed first in the section. **Both test tasks were deleted afterwards and the client verified clean (0 remaining).** Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 32 warnings, 42 tests, production build clean.
- 2026-08-07 — **v1.9.0 — an About panel behind the sidebar version number: what the tracker holds, and what it cost to build.** No migration.
  **The panel is a POSTER, not a dashboard.** It fits on one screen with **no scroller** — that is a design constraint, not an accident, and it is what the 13 (i) tooltips are for: prose that would sit under a section lives in the dot instead. If you add a figure, take one out; don't drop the type below 11px to make room (the first draft did, and 10px is smaller than anything else in the app).
  **Every number is GENERATED — `node scripts/build-about-stats.mjs --apply`** rewrites `src/lib/about-data.json` from three places at once: the studio figures from Supabase, the build figures from `git`, and the effort figures from the local Claude Code transcripts. Nothing is typed in by hand, so nothing silently rots. Opening the panel costs one import and **no queries** — the JSON is a build artefact. ⚠️ The transcripts are machine-local: on any other machine the script keeps the existing `effort` block rather than zeroing it.
  **Three bands:** a blue hero (87,577 hours · 11 years · 33 days, plus the 7 retired tools as pills), a **full-bleed tinted timeline**, then the white body. The timeline is a rail with **one tick per release at its true date** — 49 of them, and 27 July's 13 show as a visible cluster, which is the honest picture of how this was built (bursts, not a drip). 8 editorial milestones alternate above/below so neighbours can't collide; the first and last anchor to their edge, because a centred caption at 0% hangs half its width off the card.
  **Two split bars beside a donut.** The bars are the two facts that are both divisions — 42,953h recovered vs 44,624h logged, and 36h Claude vs 21h Nitsan. The models are a **donut, not a third bar**: three shares of ONE whole, where three stacked bars each totalling 100% would invite comparing rows that measure different things. `$5,868` sits in the hole.
  ⚠️ **The cost figures are two different claims and the panel says so.** `effort.costUsd` is a **list-price valuation** of the compute (per-model tokens × current API rates, with the **2× 1-hour cache-write multiplier read from the `ephemeral_1h` usage field rather than assumed**). `ACTUAL_SPEND` in `src/lib/about.ts` is the **bill actually paid** — one $25 seat, apportioned to the build's real span, so it stays true as the project runs. ~$27 against $5,868 is ~214×, and the gap is prompt caching: cached reads bill at a tenth of normal.
  ⚠️ **`className="p-0"` on the shared `Modal` SILENTLY DOES NOTHING.** Tailwind orders `p-4` after `p-0` in the stylesheet, so class order in the string never decides it — the card kept a 17px white frame for several rounds. It needs **`p-0!`**. Worth knowing for any modal that wants to bleed to its own edges.
  **Three fixes to shared components, all additive:** `Modal` gained `3xl`/`4xl`/`5xl` widths and now focuses with **`preventScroll: true`** (a card taller than the viewport made the browser scroll to reveal it, and on a COLD open — web fonts still loading — the content is briefly taller, so the panel opened part-way down and stayed there). `InfoDot` gained **`side="up"`**, because a tooltip on the bottom row opening downward is clipped, and an explanation you can't finish reading is worse than none.
  ⚠️ **Two colour pairs that passed in `electric` failed in `night`**: `bg-brand-soft` + `text-brand-dark` landed at **1.5:1** (brand-soft flips to a dark navy), and `bg-aqua` + `text-foreground` at **1.2:1** (foreground goes near-white while aqua stays bright). The rule both fixes follow: **a TINT of brand + the foreground token move together and work in both themes; a FIXED accent like aqua needs a FIXED ink** (`text-[#06112f]`). Lowest ratio is now 6.9:1. The same reasoning is why the timeline band is `bg-brand/[0.055]` rather than `bg-brand-soft`.
  Verified in the browser with Nitsan signed in at 1440×900, 1280×800 and 1280×720: 612px card, fits every one, no tooltip clips off-screen, all 8 stat labels unclipped, and the donut's amount clears its hole by 10.6px (it cleared by **0.2px** at the first size — fine on the Rubik fallback, an overflow the moment the real Saans lands). Gates: tsc clean, eslint 0 errors / 30 warnings, 42 tests, production build clean.
- 2026-08-07 — **v1.8.0 (DEPLOYED + committed `baad3e9`, pushed to main; prod verified serving v1.8.0, `/gantt/<token>` 200 and a bad token 404) — a shareable, LIVE Gantt link for clients.** Migration 0025 (`gantt_links`) APPLIED by Nitsan before the deploy. (`gantt_links`) Before it was applied `/gantt/[token]` 404'd cleanly (the query errors, `data` is null, `notFound()` fires) rather than 500ing — worth knowing for any future table-backed public route.
  **Also in this release:** "Show completed" became a toggle button, Share sits right of the pencil in the header (the cluster swaps per tab — report actions on Tasks/Overview, the schedule link on Timeline), and the public page got a black client mark (`filter: brightness(0)` — these marks are drawn WHITE for the coloured tile they sit on in the app, so on a page with no tile they were white on white), foldable sections, no Dates column, a centred zoom, the studio wordmark, and a Budget column. ⚠️ **`main` on a public page needs `w-full min-w-0`**: `body` is `display: flex`, so it is a flex item whose automatic minimum size is its MIN-CONTENT — the 4000px chart. Without it the page grew to 1500px on a 1180px screen and scrolled as a document.
  **Why its own table and not a second path on `report_links`:** the two must be revocable independently, and sharing a schedule must not hand out the hours. A report is a PUBLISHED, FROZEN statement of money — `report_links.snapshot` exists precisely so a client never sees live numbers. A Gantt is the opposite: a plan is only worth reading if it is the current one, so `/gantt/[token]` is `force-dynamic` and re-reads every request.
  ⚠️ **The narrowing happens in the `select`, not in the view.** LOGGED hours, assignees and status never leave the database; `status` is read ONLY to exclude completed work. The BUDGET is shown (column + tooltip) — it is the scope the client agreed to and says nothing about how much of it has been spent, which is the thing they must not see. Nothing to leak through a prop, a serialised payload, or a later edit to the component. Same service-role, no-anon-policy pattern as `/report/[token]`.
  **`src/lib/gantt.ts` is new**: every pure date/geometry helper (`parseISO`, `shiftDays`, `daysBetween`, `toISO`, `dateRangeLabel`, the working-day calendar, `ticksFor`, and the bar/row constants) moved out of `client-timeline.tsx`, which now imports them. There are two charts drawing the same picture and a client reading a published plan has to see the same bars on the same days — copying the helpers would have been an invitation for the two to drift a day apart.
  **The Timeline tab drops the Client-report buttons** (the report is about hours; that tab is about dates) and gains **Share**, which copies the link. The URL is prefetched on mount so `clipboard.writeText` stays synchronous — after an `await`, Safari has already lost the user-gesture that permits it.
- 2026-08-07 — **v1.7.0 (DEPLOYED + committed `975985e`, pushed to main; prod verified serving v1.7.0 on tracker.studionmore.com) — a second round on the same batch: the Timeline loses a row of chrome, gains instant tooltips and named bars, and the type hierarchy is fixed system-wide.** No migration.
  ⚠️ **globals.css collapses `.font-medium`, `.font-semibold` AND `.font-bold` onto ONE weight (570).** Saans is used at 380 and 570, nothing else. So a "make this heavier than that" request cannot be answered by changing the class — both the Timeline's and the client table's section headers were rendering at exactly the same size AND weight as their own task rows. The fix in both places is to move the ROW down to the body weight (380) and leave the heading at 570, plus one step of size (12→14 in the Timeline, 14→16 in the table). **Check the computed weight before trusting a font class in this app.**
  **Chrome removed.** The how-to line above the chart became an (i) beside the client name (a real `<button>`, opening on hover AND focus, no `title` delay). The legend and Columns button moved onto the tab strip — by `createPortal` into a slot ClientView hands down, so `hiddenCols` and the used-type set stay in ClientTimeline. The tab strip became a **grid** in the process: the zoom control was `absolute left-1/2`, out of flow, so the legend slid straight under it at every width. Three tracks with `auto` in the middle keeps it centred and gives the legend a box of its own. Legend labels are cut to 10 characters with the full name in `title`.
  **Bars carry their task's name**, truncated by the bar, dark on the tint and white once the fill passes 55%; `BAR_H` 16 → 20 to fit the line, track alpha 0x3d → 0x52 so the bar doesn't vanish under the text.
  **The bar tooltip has three bands**, not five identical lines: a heading washed in a weak tint of the task's own type colour (name + type, no dot — the wash says it), then label/value fact rows, then the drag hint smaller and fainter. No dividers: the tint already ends where the facts begin. `HoverTip` takes NODES rather than a `\n`-joined string, which is what made the flat version inevitable.
  ⚠️ **`EditableTextCell` starts in its RESTING state, so a pencil used to hand you a second "Click to edit" target** for the intent you had just declared — true in the Timeline's two pencils as well as the client table's new one. It now takes `startEditing` (mount into the input) and `onExit` (fired on commit AND on Escape — without it, Escape left the caller's `renaming` flag stuck true and the editor undismissable). All three call sites pass both.
  **Section renaming is the pencil's job in the client table too**, next to the title rather than flung to the far right: the name truncates inside a `flex-1` box that also holds the count and the pencil, instead of being `flex-1` itself and eating the row's spare width.
  **Tooltips are instant.** The bars' `title` attributes are gone — the browser sits on one for about a second, long enough that you click the bar instead, which is a write. `HoverTip` is `fixed` and portalled to `document.body`, positioned from the pointer and flipped near an edge, for the usual reason: the chart's scroller clips both axes.
  **Sticky shadows.** One full-height gradient sticking at the name column's right edge, NOT a box-shadow per row — per-row, every row's bottom border cut across it and it came out as a column of smudges. The header keeps a box-shadow because it is a single element. Both appear only when something is actually behind them, and both are deliberately faint.
  **The month band is gone from the Timeline header** — 25px on every visit to print six words. The first tick INSIDE a month prints the month's name in place of its date, in the emphasis weight, with a rule down its left edge that GridLayer continues through the rows. The year is stated once, where it changes, and only where it fits (a day-zoom tick is 26px). Three grid weights now: month `foreground/15`, week `foreground/7%` (the Sat→Sun boundary at day zoom), day `border/40`. ⚠️ **`border-strong` is not usable for a divider** — it computes to oklab lightness 0.87, indistinguishable from the weekly rules at 0.93/40%. Default zoom is now **day**.
  **The legend folds to a stack** of overlapping swatches and unfolds on hover (`max-w` transition, the usual trick for animating from `auto`). Because it can't grow at rest it can never reach the centred zoom control, which is what the `lg:` breakpoint had been papering over. Its rings are `ring-background`, NOT `ring-surface`: this toolbar sits on the page, so #fff read as a bright stroke against #f0f1fa.
  **The (i) sits on the Timeline tab itself**, via a new `after` slot on `TabItem` — it can't go in the label, because a button cannot contain a button.
  **Client Overview** is now `lg:grid-cols-[7fr_3fr]` — notes and links take the width, the figures are a 30% sidebar.
  ⚠️ **Column resize: never make one column of a resizable table `flex-1`.** The client table's Name cell was `flex-1` with its stored width as `minWidth`, and that produced BOTH of Nitsan's complaints at once. Total row width was fixed, so dragging any other column's handle right took the width out of Name — the handle stayed under the cursor and the column grew to the LEFT, which reads as backwards. And dragging Name's own handle did nothing, because it moved a minimum that sat far below the width flex had already given it. Name is now a fixed 320px like every other column (clamped to a 160px floor, because a drag that "did nothing" still WROTE a width to `colw.client-tasks` and it would otherwise arrive crushed), and the table scrolls.
- 2026-08-07 — **v1.6.1 — the Timeline gets a Type column, a hover affordance for opening a task, and section bars that look like summary brackets instead of work.** No migration. Browser-checked on Anchor with Nitsan signed in. (Superseded by v1.7.0 before it shipped; the changes below are in production as part of it.)
  **Type column.** `TL_COLS` gains `type` (96px, between Who and Dates), so the thing that decides a bar's colour finally has a per-row answer — until now the only key was the legend on the right, and you counted swatches. `EditableSelectCell` with all eight studio types; 91px of usable control inside the 96px cell, unlike the 44px assignee `<select>` that had to become a real dropdown. Admin-gated like every other editor in this table, even though 0024 leaves `type_id` member-writable — members still set a type from the client table.
  **Open-details icon.** A hover-only `Maximize2` in the name block, beside the pencil. The name already opened the task, but the only signal was a hover underline; on a Gantt where every other click drags something, that isn't enough. Verified: opacity 0 across all 28 rows at rest, 1 on the hovered row, and clicking it opens the pane.
  **Section bars are brackets now.** `SECTION_BAR_H = round(BAR_H * 0.3)` = 5px, radius 1, with a 6×9px downward tip at each end (the CSS border-triangle: a 0×0 box whose coloured top border mitres into a transparent side border). Tips are suppressed under `TIP_MIN_W` or the two would meet and the span would read as a chevron. Built from Nitsan's description — the reference image wasn't in context — so the shape is the standard Gantt summary bracket.
  ⚠️ **The bug this uncovered: `SectionHeaderRow` was pinning `leftW`, not `STICKY_W`.** v1.6.0 changed the task rows to stick only grip+checkbox+name (238px) but left the section label sticky across the whole 666px left table. Scroll the chart sideways and that opaque band sat on top of the first 666px of calendar and swallowed the summary bar — present in the DOM, correctly positioned, invisible for most of the scroll range. The label now pins `STICKY_W` and the rest follows as a plain filler that scrolls away with the columns it belongs to. **When the left table's sticky rule changes, change it in BOTH row types.**
- 2026-08-06 — **v1.6.0 (DEPLOYED + committed `3a5fb01`, pushed to main; prod verified serving v1.6.0 on tracker.studionmore.com) — the Timeline becomes editable end to end, the tables get columns you can choose, and the sidebar folds.** No migration. Two rounds of Nitsan's feedback in one session, all browser-checked with him signed in.
  **⚠️ The date-cell bug and its whole family.** `EditableDateCell` closed the editor `onBlur`, and the browser's calendar is SEPARATE CHROME: clicking its month name or ‹ › arrows blurs the input, and unmounting the input took the picker down with it. There is now **no blur handler** — it closes on Escape, on a committed change, or on a pointerdown elsewhere in the DOCUMENT, and clicks inside the native picker never reach the document, which is exactly why that test works where blur did not. Clicking a cell no longer auto-opens the picker either (you land in typing); the calendar is a button beside the field. The editor is also a POPOUT rather than the cell: a date input needs ~120px and these cells are 72px, which is what made pressing one look broken.
  ⚠️ **Anchored panels here must be `fixed` and must flip.** Three of them (timeline Dates, the assignee list, the client-table date popout) live inside `overflow` scrollers that clip BOTH axes, so `absolute` is cut off on the last rows; and `fixed` alone still runs off the bottom of the window, which is how the Dates panel's Done button became unreachable. Each measures its anchor and flips above it when the window runs out. The pane's Dates popover is anchored `right-0` instead, because it sits in the right column of the two-column grid.
  **Timeline table:** only the NAME block sticks (grip + checkbox + name, 238px) — pinning the whole 566px table spent half a laptop's width on columns you weren't reading. Admins edit name (pencil; the name itself still opens the task), assignee (a real list with faces, not a 44px `<select>`), budget, section name, and **Duration — which moves the DUE date only, and only where the task has a start**; a deadline has no duration to change and inventing a start is a different decision. The completion circle became a **selection checkbox** with shift-ranges and a batch bar. Zoom moved to the tab strip (centred), Show completed to the left of Client report, hint left / legend right, and a **Columns** menu per table (persisted, validated on read).
  **Bars redrawn to Nitsan's reference:** one 16px shape, `borderRadius: 4` (~1:4 of the height — a full pill stopped short bars reading as spans), the colour at 24% as the track, hours-against-budget filling it solid, `overflow-hidden` squaring the fill's inner edge. The old 2px completion rule is GONE — it read as a hairline floating above the bar and said what the fill already says. Hours moved outside the bar, to its right. **A live date chip** appears the instant a drag starts and updates per day, beside the edge being dragged (verified: 18 Aug → 21 Aug at +3 → back, nothing written when the delta returns to 0).
  **Elsewhere:** Start/Type columns on the client table + a resizable name column; batch set of status/type/start/due; table dates lose the year (`formatDayMonth` — `formatDate` keeps it for anything historical); number cells are typed fields, no spinner arrows; the pane's Due row became **Dates** showing "9/8 – 18/8"; an EMPTY date seeds from the other end so the picker opens on the right month (a starting point, never a saved value); links get a trash icon and a **+ Add link** on the Brief heading line; the sidebar folds to a 64px icon rail with no-delay tooltips, the ampersand centred on the rail and `«`/`»` pinned right.
- 2026-08-06 — **v1.5.2 (DEPLOYED + committed `4476e15`, pushed to main; prod verified serving v1.5.2 on tracker.studionmore.com) — the mark becomes editable from Settings, and any colour is allowed.** No migration. Three asks from Nitsan in one turn: a bigger mark on the client page (28 → **40px** against the 24px title — on their own page the mark leads rather than annotates; the sticky bar still pins flush, checked on Studio at 287 tasks), logo edit/remove from Settings, and a hex field in the client-record popup.
  **`ClientMarkPicker` moved out of `client-info-modal.tsx` into its own [client-mark-picker.tsx](src/components/client-mark-picker.tsx)**, because two surfaces now need the whole thing. A second copy would drift on the three rules that have to agree: the palette, the `/api/client-icon` route (where the admin check and the Content-Type allowlist live — a browser-side upload with the anon key would have neither), and "an uploaded image wins over the glyph". The file also exports `ClientMarkModal` for callers that aren't already a client form.
  **Settings → Clients: the mark IS the button.** Clicking it opens the picker over the list — a 25-glyph grid plus an upload has no place inline in a list row. ⚠️ **The native colour swatch was REMOVED from that row on purpose**: it sat immediately beside the new mark and two coloured squares in a row read as two different things. The inline hex field stays for quick edits; the colour wheel moved into the popup. Don't "restore" the swatch.
  **Any hex, not just the twelve** — a colour wheel + a text field in the picker, so it reaches the pencil modal and the Settings popup at once. ⚠️ **The field commits only a valid `#rrggbb` and snaps back otherwise**: a half-typed `#0b4` written to the row paints that client's chips transparent everywhere. Both paths tested on Anchor (`#0b4` → snapped back, no write; `#123456` → committed and repainted the row, mark and preview live) and **reverted to `#ef0bd0`**.
- 2026-08-06 — **v1.5.1 (DEPLOYED + committed `1c6203d`, pushed to main; prod verified serving v1.5.1 on tracker.studionmore.com) — the client mark actually shows up.** No migration. Nitsan reported that the icons and uploads added in v1.3.0 still rendered as letters on the clients list. Cause: [clients/page.tsx](src/app/(app)/clients/page.tsx) drew its own 32px square from `client.name[0]` on `client.color` — it predates `ClientAvatar` and was never switched over, so `icon`/`icon_url` had nowhere to render. Replaced with `<ClientAvatar size={32}>`.
  **Then the same mark replaced the colour dot inside `ClientChip`** (16px at `sm`, 18px at `md`, both round), which reaches all 12 chip sites at once — feed, reports, my-tasks, task pane, dashboard, autocomplete, member page, day details. `ClientAvatar` gained `radius: "lg" | "full"` for it: `lg` is the standalone tile, `full` the mark inside a pill. Two competing radii — an 18px squircle inside a fully-round capsule — was the variant that looked wrong in the preview, so the pill always gets a circle.
  ⚠️ **The mark sits FLUSH to the pill's left edge (`pl-0.5`), not inset like the old dot** — that is what keeps the capsule at its previous height, measured at 22px (`sm`) and 26px (`md`) both before and after, so no feed row, table row or header grew. If a later change re-adds symmetric padding, every chip gets taller and the rows follow. Width grows 4px at `md`; nothing lays out against chip width.
  **Left as dots, deliberately:** global search, the settings client list, client-reports tabs, the weekly-plan filter — all 8–10px, where a glyph is unreadable and the colour is doing the work. An uploaded *logo* is also weak at 16px (Studio's `&` survives because it's one bold glyph; a wordmark wouldn't) — the preset glyphs are what this size is for.
  *Method note: the variants were shown to Nitsan as a throwaway `/chip-preview` route rendering real clients in real styles rather than as a mockup — cheap, honest about the fallbacks, and deleted once the pick was made.*
- 2026-08-06 — **v1.5.0 (DEPLOYED + committed `fe5beef`, pushed to main; prod verified serving v1.5.0 on tracker.studionmore.com) — the Timeline becomes a real project table, and "Tags" are renamed "Status" in the UI.** Migrations 0023 + 0024 were applied by Nitsan before this. ✅ **This one commit carries v1.2.0 → v1.5.0** — all four were built in a single session and none shipped separately; every one was checked in the browser with Nitsan signed in.
  **Timeline rebuilt to the Asana Gantt reference**, in our own tokens: a left TABLE (grip · completion circle · name · assignee avatar · **Dates** · **Duration** · **Actual** · **Budget**) beside the chart, **grouped by the client's sections** with a collapsible header per group and a slim summary bar in the client's colour spanning that group's whole range. Month names sit above the tick row; the today line gained the reference's dot.
  **A task with no start date is now a DIAMOND, not a one-day bar.** That's the substantive change, not decoration: "we owe this on the 20th" and "this takes one day" are different claims, and the old single-day sliver made them identical. Duration reads "—" for those, and dragging the diamond's edge is still how it becomes a scheduled span. The legend says so.
  **Planned vs spent in one bar.** A **thin 2px rule** along the top spans the scheduled dates and its strength tracks completion (opacity 0.28 → 1 across the budget); the band beneath fills with logged hours against that budget. So a column of rules reads as "which of these is actually moving" without comparing fill widths. Over budget overrides both to red. *(First built with a diagonal hatch for the plan — Nitsan found it too loud, and he was right: a texture that busy competes with the bars it's describing.)*
  **The Dates cell is click-to-edit.** Dragging is good for "a bit later, a bit longer" and hopeless for "the 14th", so the cell opens a popover with both ends and a "Clear start" (back to a deadline), committed as ONE `updateTask` so it stays one undo step alongside the drags. ⚠️ It renders `fixed`, positioned from the cell's rect: the chart sits in an `overflow-x-auto` scroller and **a scroll container clips BOTH axes**, so an absolutely-positioned popover would be cut off at the row's bottom edge — the same trap as the task-search dropdown in v1.1.2. A typed date is deliberately **not** snapped to a working day (naming the 14th is explicit in a way nudging a bar isn't); a non-working choice is accepted and labelled instead.
  ⚠️ **Drag-reorder is now confined to one section.** Dropping across a boundary is refused rather than re-sectioning the task — `timeline_position` exists precisely so the Timeline's order can't reach into the Tasks tab's grouping (see 0023), and allowing cross-section drops would have undone that. Move a task between sections from the task pane.
  **Verified live on Anchor** (0023/0024 applied): bar 144px = 16 calendar days at 9px/day for 18 Aug→2 Sep; the deadline diamond sits exactly 6 days right of it, matching 18 Aug → 24 Aug; collapsing a section takes its bars from 2 → 0 → 2; setting a Type drove it into the tooltip, the bar colour and the legend; the top rule measures 2px at opacity 0.28 with zero `repeating-linear-gradient` left anywhere; and the Dates popover opens `fixed`, unclipped, with Start empty and Due 2026-08-23.
  ⚠️ **Method note — scripted `<select>` changes are not reliable against React.** Setting `.value` via the native setter plus a synthetic `change` event reported the right option locally but left the WRONG type persisted twice (a stray "Wireframe", then "Content" on a different task), each found only by reading the row tooltips back after a full reload. Verify any scripted write by reloading and re-reading, or drive the real control. All of this session's test content on Anchor — start dates, brief, client link, stray types — was reverted and confirmed clean after a hard reload.
  **"Tags" → "Status" in the UI.** They always were statuses — "in design", "Client approval", "Approved". Renamed on the task pane, the client table header + sort hint, the My Tasks/home column, and Settings ("Task statuses", now with a line distinguishing it from Task types). ⚠️ **The code still says `tag`/`tags`/`tag_id`** — renaming the table, the column and ~40 references is a large, risky diff for a label change, so the mapping is documented at the top of the `Tag` interface instead. Two collisions worth knowing: `Task.status` (todo/in_progress/done) is a DIFFERENT thing that the UI never calls "status" (it's the completion tick and the board columns), and the client-record modal's "Status" row was renamed "Archive" to free the word.
  Gates: `tsc` clean, `eslint src` 0 errors / 24 warnings, 42 tests, production build clean.
- 2026-08-06 — **v1.4.0 — task types colour the Timeline, the client edit button shrinks to a pencil, notes move where members can read them, and the pane gets a real text hierarchy. MIGRATIONS 0023 + 0024 REQUIRED.**
  **Task types (0024).** Nitsan asked to colour Timeline bars "by type" and named seven: development, QA, design, wireframe, content, image making, client side. Those are KINDS OF WORK; the studio's existing tags are workflow STATES ("in design", "Client approval", "Development", "Approved"). Presented as three options, he chose **a separate Type field** — so a task can be a QA job AND awaiting approval, which one shared list makes impossible. New `task_types` (seeded in the migration, re-runnable via `where not exists`) + `tasks.type_id`, a Type row on the task pane above Tag, an admin editor in **Settings → Studio setup** beside Task tags (colour swatch, inline rename, in-use count, delete that says how many tasks lose the label), and bars drawn in the type's colour: base at ~18% alpha, fill at full strength. **Over budget still overrides to red** — the money reading has to win over the category. A legend lists only the types actually on that client's board; naming seven when two are in use is a key to a map you aren't looking at. `type_id` is deliberately NOT in the 0011 protected list: describing the work is collaborative like `tag_id`, unlike scheduling it.
  **Client info → an icon-only pencil, record only.** The button now opens name / billing note / mark / archive and nothing else, and it's **admin-only** — there was nothing in it for a member to press. **Notes and links moved to the Overview tab** (`client-notes.tsx`), which is where they belonged: they're studio-wide reading, and hiding them behind an admin button hid the one part of that panel that wasn't admin work. Admins edit in place; notes commit on blur (no modal close to hang a save on), and an effect adopts an incoming value on refresh without clobbering half-typed text.
  **Text hierarchy, per the Asana reference.** Every section label was `text-xs uppercase tracking-wide text-faint` — 12px micro-caps at the LOWEST contrast on the page — so "Brief", "Attachments" and "Discussion" (the pane's structure) sat in the same register as a file size, and nothing announced where one part of the task ended and the next began. Now three levels and only three, named as constants (`SECTION_HEADING`, `FIELD_LABEL`): **title 22px/570**, **section heading 14px/570 at full `#06112f`** in sentence case, **field label 13px/380 muted `rgb(92,100,120)`** with its value at foreground. Measured in the browser, not eyeballed. "Time — 0m total" split so the heading is "Time" and the total rides beside it at incidental weight.
  Gates: `tsc` clean, `eslint src` 0 errors / 24 warnings (+1: ClientNotes' adopt-incoming-value effect, same advisory shape as the existing ones), 42 tests, production build clean.
- 2026-08-06 — **v1.3.0 — the pane stops shouting, the discussion becomes a conversation, the Timeline learns the studio's working week, and a client gets a face. MIGRATION 0023 REQUIRED.**
  **Corrections to v1.2.0, at Nitsan's direction:** (1) **a task always opens in the side pane; maximising is an action, not a mode.** Full screen persisted to localStorage, so one click of maximise meant every task afterwards took over the screen — including tasks opened from the plan or the feed, where the whole point is keeping the thing behind it in view. Now reset on each open. (2) **Admins can delete a message** in the discussion. Undo re-inserts the row with its ORIGINAL id AND `created_at`, so a restored comment lands back in its place in a time-ordered thread instead of jumping to the bottom; `author_name` rides along too, since 2,175 of the 2,397 imported comments have no profile and it's the only record of who wrote them.
  **Quiet-until-touched.** Every value in the pane is editable and the UI said so with a permanent box around each one — fourteen outlined controls the eye has to discount before it can find the assignee. `QUIET_FIELD`/`QUIET_SELECT`/`META_ROW` + a `QuietSelect` wrapper: chrome appears on hover AND on `focus-within` (a control whose only affordance is hover is invisible to the keyboard). Native chrome that CSS can't reach is handled by hand — `appearance-none` for the select arrow, `[&::-webkit-calendar-picker-indicator]:opacity-0` for the date input's glyph. The Figma box (a full-width outline on every task that has no Figma file, i.e. most of them) and the attachments dropzone go quiet the same way. Measured on the Branding task: hovered row → chevron opacity 1, border `rgb(229,231,235)`; un-hovered rows → opacity 0, `rgba(0,0,0,0)`.
  **Discussion is its own surface** (the Asana reference). Everything above it is a form — label left, value right, on the pane's own white; the thread is the one part that's a conversation, so it sits in a recessed `bg-background` panel with a real `<h3>` + a count, and its bubbles flip to `bg-surface`. ⚠️ That flip is load-bearing: the old bubbles were `bg-background` ON a `bg-surface` pane, and once the panel behind them became `bg-background` too they would have vanished into it — only the border was doing any work. Timestamp and the (hover-only) delete control share one faint line, so the row costs nothing until you reach for it.
  **Timeline v2.** Actual and Budget hour columns sit between the task name and the chart; the row carries **only the assignee's avatar** (a name would halve the room for the title, and who it's on is the one person-fact a Gantt row needs); rows are **drag-reorderable** by a hover-only grip, persisted to `timeline_position`; a **weak** vertical grid (`border-border/40`, drawn once behind all rows rather than per row) lets you read a date off a bar without competing with it.
  **Working days are excluded from planning, not just shaded** (Nitsan's choice of the three options). Fri/Sat AND any day the weekly plan marks as a holiday (`plan_day_states`, expanded to a date set) are drawn darker and skipped by the arithmetic: `isWorkDay`/`snapToWorkDay`/`addWorkDays`/`workDaysBetween` mean a dragged date always lands on a working day, and a MOVE preserves the bar's length in WORKING days — a three-day job pushed onto a Thursday still gets three days of work and simply stretches across the weekend. Verified live: Branding read "16/8/26 → 31/8/26 · 12 working days", and a move produced "18/8/26 → 2/9/26 · **12 working days**". Shading verified by count: 13 Fridays + 13 Saturdays across the 91-day window. Per-day shading is dropped below 6px/day (at month zoom the stripes would be denser than the data on them).
  **Client identity.** `ClientAvatar` (new `client-avatar.tsx`) renders an uploaded mark, else one of 25 preset glyphs, else the initial — always on `clients.color`. Icons are stored **by name**, so the column survives an icon-library upgrade and an unknown name falls back to the initial rather than a blank square. It now sits before the `<h1>` on the client page and in the Client info header, and the modal gained a colour row + glyph grid + upload. The upload goes through a new **`/api/client-icon`** rather than straight to storage: that route holds the admin check and reuses `classifyImage`'s allowlist with a server-forced Content-Type. ⚠️ It writes `clients` with the SERVICE KEY, which bypasses RLS entirely — **the explicit `role === "admin"` check in `requireAdmin()` IS the access control there**, not a convenience. It reuses the existing `avatars` bucket under a `client/` prefix so no bucket has to be provisioned by hand. The store gained `patchClientLocal` for the same reason `patchProfileLocal` exists: the route already persisted the value, so calling `updateClient` would issue a redundant write and record an undo step for a change the store never made.
  Gates: `tsc` clean, `eslint src` 0 errors / 23 warnings, 42 tests, production build clean.
- 2026-08-06 — **v1.2.0 — the brief becomes editable, links get titles, a task gets a permalink and a full-screen pane, a client gets notes, and the client page gets a draggable Timeline. MIGRATION 0022 REQUIRED — Nitsan applied it before the deploy.** ✅ **Verified in the running app with Nitsan signed in** (he ran 0022 first, so every check below ran against the real schema).
  **The finding that started it:** Nitsan reported the brief field on an Anchor task was "closed to edit". It wasn't a permission bug and it wasn't Anchor — **the brief had never had an editor anywhere in the app.** `task-panel.tsx` rendered it as a static `<div>`, and the only thing that ever wrote `tasks.brief` was the public intake form (`assembleTaskBrief` → `api/intake/[token]`). A task created inside the app read "No brief yet." forever. The write path underneath was already complete (`taskPatchToRow` handles `brief`, and migration 0011 deliberately leaves it member-writable) — only the UI was missing.
  **Migration 0022** (`supabase/migrations/0022_links_notes_and_start_date.sql`): a `links` table (title + url, hung off EITHER a task or a client, `check ((task_id is null) <> (client_id is null))`), `clients.notes`, and `tasks.start_date`. ⚠️ **It also re-declares the 0011 trigger to add `start_date` to the protected list.** That trigger enumerates its columns by name, so a NEW task column is member-writable by DEFAULT — without the amendment any designer could re-schedule any task through the API and the Timeline's read-only gate would be UI-only. Link policies are split for the same reason briefs are member-writable: task links are collaborative material (`write task links`), client links sit beside the billing note and are `is_admin()`.
  **Degrades before the migration, on purpose.** The task select grew a new TOP rung (`client_id, start_date, legacyCols, cols`) rather than folding `start_date` into the existing one — collapsing rungs is how the `legacy` flag gets dropped, which is the mistake that ladder exists to prevent. `links` is fetched with `.catch(() => [])`. Observed live: pre-migration the browser logged 2×404 (no `links` table) and 4×400 (no `start_date`) and the app worked; post-migration a fetch-instrumented refresh logged **zero** failed requests.
  (1) **Brief editor in a modal, not inline.** A brief is paragraphs and the pane is a 576px column with the hours, the time list and the whole comment thread beneath it. `BriefModal` is `layer="raised"` (the pane is z-40/50). It **saves on close** — Escape and backdrop included — because losing a just-typed paragraph is worse than saving an edit someone meant to abandon, and Cmd-Z still undoes it. ⚠️ **It refuses to save until the brief has actually arrived.** The brief is lazily fetched by `loadTaskExtras`; `loadedTaskExtras` is a ref added BEFORE the fetch resolves, so it could not answer "has it landed". New `briefLoaded(taskId)` in the store, set after the detail query applies and deliberately monotonic (`mergeTasks` keeps the brief across refreshes). Without it, opening the editor a beat early and closing it would write `""` over real text.
  (2) **Titled links** (`links-editor.tsx`, shared by the task brief and the client panel). Only the TITLE renders — the studio's links are 200-character Google Doc URLs, and the Branding task's own comment thread has Michal pasting that exact URL twice as raw text, which is the whole argument. Because the title is all you see, `normalizeUrl` checks the scheme before storing (bare `docs.google.com/…` gets `https://`) and `isSafeUrl` checks again before rendering: a `javascript:` URL under a friendly title is a trap nobody could spot. An unsafe row renders struck-through and unclickable rather than being hidden, so an admin can see it.
  (3) **Copy task link + full screen** on the pane's top-right. The link is `/?task=<id>` — the home route, because `/clients` is admin-only and a permalink a designer can't open isn't one. Deep-linking reads `window.location` in a mount effect rather than `useSearchParams`, which would drag a Suspense boundary into the shell for a one-shot read. Clipboard failure (Safari, insecure origin) falls back to a prompt rather than flashing "Copied!" over a clipboard that didn't change. Full screen is the same panel widened (`inset-0`) with the content column capped at `max-w-3xl` — a comment thread set across 1900px is unreadable — and remembered in `localStorage["task.fullscreen"]`, read in an effect.
  (4) **Client info panel** (`Client info`, top-right of the client page): notes and links for everyone, and for admins the client record — name, billing note, archive. **The standalone Archive button moved in here**; two archive buttons on one screen is one too many. **No Delete, by Nitsan's decision** — deleting a client cascades to its tasks, sections, time entries and report links, so those hours would leave every total with no way back. The panel says so.
  (5) **Timeline tab** — a Gantt of the client's dated work, admin-draggable and member-read-only. Only tasks WITH a due date appear (a bar has to end somewhere) and the count of what's excluded is printed underneath, so the view is never quietly partial: Anchor shows 2 bars and "26 open tasks with no due date aren't shown". A bar runs `start_date → due_date`; **no start = a single-day bar, and dragging its left edge is how a start gets set in the first place**. The fill is logged/budget with BudgetBar's own over-budget red. Weeks-across-3-months by default (Nitsan's pick) with Day/Month zoom; every position is computed in DAYS and multiplied by a per-zoom `PX_PER_DAY`, so one drag maths serves all three. Date arithmetic is DST-safe by construction (`new Date(y, m, d + n)`, never `+ 86_400_000`). Move and resize commit in ONE `updateTask`, so re-scheduling is one undo step.
  **Two bugs found by testing, both mine:** (a) the drag committed from inside a `setDrag` updater — updaters run during render, so it called the store's `setTasks` mid-render and React threw *"Cannot update a component while rendering a different component"*. Fixed with a `dragRef` so the commit happens outside any updater. (b) The 8px resize handles covered narrow bars entirely: at week zoom a one-day bar is 9px, so it could be resized but never moved. Handles now only render at `barWidth >= 30px`; narrower bars are move-only.
  **Verified live** (Anchor, admin): left-edge drag set a start date that survived a hard reload; a move drag changed both dates and the Tasks table showed the new due date (31/8/26), proving the timeline writes the same field the table reads; a link added in Client info persisted across reload and renders as "Client branding questionnaire"; a brief typed in the modal saved and rendered; `?task=<id>` opened the pane cold on the home page; and `?viewAs=Nadav` showed "Read-only — only admins can re-schedule", no drag handles, and a Client info panel with no client-record section. A fetch-instrumented drag + refresh window recorded **0 failed requests and 0 JS errors**.
  ⚠️ **Method note:** the browser tools' `get_page_text` and console buffer both served STALE content repeatedly during this session while screenshots were fresh — a "Loading the studio…" text read and a list of long-dead 400s both sent me chasing nothing. Trust a screenshot or a live `javascript_tool` probe over either. Likewise, reloading immediately after an edit can execute the PREVIOUS Turbopack bundle: the deep link looked broken until a second reload, and the giveaway was a runtime probe returning null while the served chunk already contained the new code.
  Gates: `tsc` clean, `eslint src` **0 errors / 23 warnings** (baseline 22; +1 is the pane's localStorage effect, the same advisory shape as the existing ones), 42 tests, production build clean.
- 2026-08-04 — **v1.1.2 (DEPLOYED + committed `e071f71`, pushed to main; prod verified serving v1.1.2 on tracker.studionmore.com) — the task search dropdown floats, settings split into per-topic tabs, the weekly plan's columns are one width, and a plan entry that isn't a task can be edited.** ✅ **Checked in the app by Nitsan before deploying** ("all works") — the first batch in three releases that didn't ship blind. (1) **`TaskAutocomplete`'s result list is an overlay, not part of the layout.** In flow it grew whatever hosted it: the day-details "add hours" popup stretched as you typed and, because the form row is `flex-wrap` with stretch alignment, the duration/description/Add controls beside it stretched to match. The list is now `absolute top-full` with `w-max min-w-full` capped at `min(34rem, 100vw - 2rem)`, so it hangs OUTSIDE the popup and sizes to its own content — task titles were being trimmed to "Vo…" in a list whose whole job is telling two tasks apart. **Measured in a static harness against the real compiled CSS** (the only way to check this without a signed-in session): card height 205px identical open and closed, panel 522px wide against a 334px input, bottom edge 91px past the card, zero rows truncated; one absurd title caps at 544px and truncates instead of running off-screen. Two details that make a floating list behave: `onMouseDown → preventDefault` on the panel, or blur unmounts the row between mousedown and mouseup and swallows the click; and it only opens while the input has focus, so it can't hover over the rest of the card afterwards. Picking now **fills the input with the chosen title and closes the list** (the log-time form keeps that input on screen, so it was the only record of what you'd chosen), typing after a pick **clears the remembered task** via the new `onQueryEdited` — otherwise hours could be filed against a task the user had typed away from — and the picker is remounted by `key` after an add so it can't keep naming a task that is no longer selected. (2) **Settings' own-account surface splits into three tabs — My pictures / My details / Password — for everyone**, not just for members. Members had no strip at all (their whole page was one tab, which is also what made it the longest page in the app: two picture uploaders, fourteen HR fields and a password field in one scroll); admins keep Clients and Studio setup after those, so five tabs. The same three blocks are the same for both roles, so splitting for one and not the other would have been two page shapes for one page. `settings.tab` remaps the stored `"account"` to `"pictures"` on read, and the **details panel is lazily mounted then kept mounted** — `HrDetailsForm` is controlled state behind ONE explicit Save, so unmounting on a tab switch would silently discard fourteen fields of typed text. (3) **Every person column in the weekly plan is exactly as wide as every other — `w-[175px]`, Nitsan's floor** — and a long chip wraps instead of widening its column: under auto table layout a column was sized by its longest chip, so one designer's column was visibly wider and the width moved around as the week's work changed. `table-fixed` on the table, a width (not a min-width) on the header cells, since under fixed layout the header row's widths ARE the column widths. `w-full` stays and only ever adds: measured in Chrome, columns totalling MORE than the container keep their widths and the wrapper scrolls, and totalling less the spare is split evenly — so no min-width is needed. ⚠️ **A measurement I got wrong first and then re-ran, worth remembering as a method note:** I concluded from a static harness that a fixed table squeezes its columns (128 → 94px) and added a computed `minWidth` to the table to stop it. It doesn't squeeze — the harness was loading a **stale copy of the built CSS in which `.w-32` was absent**, so the cells had no width at all and fixed layout simply divided the table evenly, which reads exactly like a squeeze. Re-tested against a fresh bundle: `w-32` at a 900px container holds 128px and scrolls, with and without the min-width. When a harness measurement contradicts the spec, suspect the stylesheet before believing the browser. Chip labels moved from `truncate` to `break-words` (client names stay truncated — a second line of 9px uppercase is noise), and the column header name truncates with a `title`. **At ~10 columns the grid is now ~1846px wide, so it scrolls sideways on a laptop** — that is the cost of the 175px floor; hiding unused columns in the column manager is the lever. (4) **Clicking an absence or a free-text chip opens an editor** (admins only, `canEdit`), because there was no way to change either one: right-click offered Copy and Delete, so fixing a typo or turning a sick day into a vacation meant deleting and re-adding. `AddEntryModal` became **`EntryModal`**, one component for both jobs — editing IS choosing again, so a separate editor would have been the same three controls with a different verb — and it gained a Remove button, the current absence kind marked, and a prefilled search box when the entry is a note (with the free-text row reading "Save as text" rather than "Add"). **Task chips deliberately keep opening the task pane**, which is where a task is edited. New **`updatePlanEntry(id, patch)`** in the store: one undo step, only the named fields touched, and — the lesson from `updateTimeEntry` — the camelCase↔snake_case mapping stated **once** in `PLAN_ENTRY_FIELDS`, which builds the DB row, the local patch and the inverse from one list. (5) **The plan's search offers COMPLETED tasks, and picking one reopens the task** — Nitsan's rule: work comes back, and a task marked done too early is exactly what someone needs to schedule. `TaskAutocomplete` gained `includeDone` (off everywhere else — in a log-time form a finished task in the list is just a way to book hours against it by accident); done rows sort after the open ones, are struck through and carry a "done" pill, and the 12-row cap counts open matches first so finished work can't bury live tasks. The reopen lives in the **store**, not the modal (`plannedTaskToReopen` → `status: "todo"`, folded into the same undo step as the plan write), because every route a task takes into the plan — add, edit, paste — goes through `addPlanEntry`/`updatePlanEntry`, so a new caller can't bypass the rule.- 2026-08-04 — **v1.1.1 (DEPLOYED + committed `7049374`, pushed to main; prod verified serving v1.1.1 on tracker.studionmore.com) — seven follow-ups from Nitsan's first read of v1.1.0.** ⚠️ **Deployed WITHOUT browser verification** — no signed-in dev session was available in that session, so both v1.1.0 and these follow-ups reached production having passed only tsc / lint / 42 tests / a production build. If anything in this batch is wrong, the likely places are the geometry (absence fill in a tall row, the plan rail handle, the three-pane home row) and the store's rewritten boot path. (1) **"Hours by client" is one pane with a Split / Over-time tab** — the donut and the per-client trend answer the same question, so they were two panes competing for a slot rather than two facts. The scoping logic they share is now `useHoursScope`, so they and "Hours over time" can't disagree about which hours they describe; members see the donut with no tab strip. **"The studio" roster took the freed row and is full width again**, back to 5–7 cards across instead of wrapping at four. (2) **The weekly-plan resize handle sits in the MIDDLE of the gap** (`-left-2 -translate-x-1/2`; it was centred on the rail's own edge, i.e. the right end of the gap) and `ResizeHandle` gained `visibility="always"` — a short grey grip visible at rest, because a pane edge, unlike a table column edge, gives no hint it can be dragged. (3) **Absences really do fill the cell now.** `inset-0` was already reaching the cell's borders; the problem was that `PlanCell` only ever grew to its own content, so in a row made tall by a neighbouring cell the fill covered one chip's worth of height. Fixed with the `h-px` table-cell trick — a `<td>` gives a percentage-height child nothing to resolve against unless it has a height of its own, and the row overrides `h-px` anyway, so `PlanCell`'s new `h-full` finally stretches to the row. (4) **A section whose tasks are all done folds away with them**, and comes back with "Show completed" — measured against ALL tasks, and an EMPTY section stays visible (it has nothing finished to hide behind, and a section you just created must not vanish as you make it). Nitsan first read this as a per-section Hide *button* and asked where it was; he confirmed automatic is what he wants, so the only addition is that **it now says so** — a "N finished sections hidden — show completed" line at the foot of the table, which is also the control. Folding a section away silently reads as data loss. (5) Reports task popup: the task name is the **title** (`text-lg`), not a caption over the rows. (6) Member page: **open tasks moved up under the four tiles**, so the left column fills the height the chart sets beside it; "Most active clients" takes the full-width row below. (7) Team page: the layout toggle shows **"Cards" / "Table" beside its icons**.
- 2026-08-04 — **v1.1.0 — 23 changes from a fortnight of real use: the task pane becomes the single place a task is edited, and the app refreshes itself.** Planned as one batch with Nitsan; **no migrations** (his call, which dropped the "mark a section complete" ask — sections have no completed column). Per-user UI preferences go in **localStorage**, not a `profiles.prefs` column.
  **Shared primitives, extracted first** so nine of the items had something to sit on: **`Tabs`** (renders the strip only, never the panels — the member page needs its HR panel kept mounted; replaces the two copy-paste segmented controls), **`Modal`** + `ModalClose` (the overlay-40/card-50 recipe was in 9 places; adds Escape-to-close and focus-on-mount; `layer="raised"` = z-[60]/[70] for a popup opened from inside the task drawer, which is itself 40/50 and wins on DOM order), **`TaskNameLink`**, **`PeriodStepper`** (lifted from `dashboard.tsx`, generic over the range list — home 4, team 5, reports 4), **`LogTimeForm`** (one implementation of SIX hand-rolled add-time forms; only one of them had the admin member picker, so where an admin could backfill a colleague's hours depended on which screen they were on), **`TimeEntryModal`**, **`useMe`/`useIsAdmin`** (19 inlined `role === "admin"` checks; reading `currentUserId` through the store keeps `?viewAs` working for free), **`taskHoursDone`** in `src/lib/task-hours.ts`, and `period-math` gained **quarters** + `periodRange()` with tests asserting the new arithmetic is byte-identical to the team page's old inline expression.
  **Auto-refresh (no websockets).** `src/lib/snapshot.ts` splits the boot query into **hot** (tasks, plan entries, the 400-row feed window + entry sums, task requests, dev items — every 60s, never while `document.hidden`, focus debounced to 20s) and **cold** (people/clients/sections/tags/columns/periods/day-states — every 10th tick or a focus after 5 min away). **The two `isMissingSchema` column ladders were moved character-for-character** — degrading past `legacy` leaks ~4,000h of backfill into every personal figure. Three things a refresh must never do, each enforced: touch `loading`/`bootError` (would flash the splash over a working app), run `getUser()`/redirect (would navigate someone away mid-edit), or land mid-write. The last one needed a **pending-write registry** — a `wrote(label)` tail replacing `if (error) noteWriteError(...)` at 30 update/delete sites (inserts are left alone: a new row can't be clobbered). An **empty studio is treated as auth, not data**, and thrown away rather than applied. **Undo now expires**: history entries carry an epoch, a refresh whose `fingerprint` differs bumps it, and undoing a stale step clears the history with a neutral notice instead of pushing a pre-change value back over a colleague's edit. `loadedTaskExtras` is cleared and the open task re-fetched, and `openTask`'s three merges became **replace-by-task** so a deleted comment can actually disappear. A faint pulsing dot beside the search box is the only UI.
  **Weekly plan:** dropping a chip in someone else's column now **reassigns the task** as ONE undo step (new `movePlanEntryToCell` + an `asOneStep` helper that saves/restores the suppress flag rather than clearing it — `redo()` already holds it); it reassigns only for a task-linked chip landing in a real `member` column with a profileId, and dragging OUT to Studio/Waiting list deliberately does not clear the assignee. **Absences fill their cell**: the padding moved from the `td` into `PlanCell` so `inset-0` reaches the border (total unchanged, so chips are pixel-identical), and the drop outline gained `-outline-offset-2`. The right rail is **drag-resizable** (`colw.plan-rail`, 150–420, handle on the left edge via new `invert` option).
  **Bug — the search dropdown painted under the plan's sticky header.** Root cause: the app header is `sticky z-20` **and** `backdrop-blur`, so it is its own stacking context and its `z-50` dropdown could never rise above 20, while the plan's `thead` was also 20 and later in the DOM. Header raised to **z-30** with the whole z-scale written down beside it.
  **Client page:** name + actions on ONE line, `sticky top-14 z-10` under the header (must stay outside the `overflow-x-auto` wrapper or sticky dies), with **Tasks / Overview tabs** — Overview finally gives `ClientStats` a home, since it was `hidden xl:flex` and therefore invisible below 1280px. Section select-all is **hover-revealed** (the `group` goes on the header div, not the wrapper, or hovering a row would reveal it). **Sections drag to reorder** (`reorderSection`, dense 1..n, one undo step) with three guards keeping it apart from the task drags that share the table. **"Hrs/budget" split into Hours + Budget — in BOTH task tables**, sharing `taskHoursDone`; the My Tasks/home table keeps its utilisation % in the tooltip. Task name **opens the pane** and the inline rename is gone (with the redundant Maximize2 button) — so **the pane's title became editable**, or nothing could be renamed anywhere.
  **Task pane:** Budget → a prominent **`12 / 24 h`**, numerator computed, denominator inline-editable. Per Nitsan the numerator now **includes the pre-Everhour remainder** so the pane and the client table agree on the ~150 recovered tasks, and the time list carries an explicit "pre-Everhour history (not itemised)" line so the rows still sum to the headline. A square **`+` tile** (37px = a row's rendered height) opens the log-time popup, and clicking a row opens edit/delete — **only** for rows you may change; others keep today's markup exactly, no cursor, no hover, no handler. ⚠️ **Two latent bugs this made reachable, both fixed:** `restoreTimeEntry` dropped `legacy`/`date_estimated`/`legacy_author_name`/`moved_from_task_id`, so deleting a recovered entry and pressing ⌘Z would have resurrected it as an ORDINARY one and leaked its hours into every personal aggregate (delete is also blocked on legacy rows); and `updateTimeEntry` passed its patch straight to `.update()`, working only because minutes/description/date are spelled the same in both cases — it now builds the row explicitly, which is what let `userId` reassignment in (admins only; RLS refuses it for members anyway, since `own time update`'s USING clause constrains the very column being changed). Discussion restyled as a **conversation** — bubbles, own messages right, one avatar+byline per 10-minute run, timestamps under the bubble.
  **Reports:** period arrows + Now. The preset list simplifies to This week / This month / This year / Custom, because "Last week" was a preset that already encoded an offset and stepping back from it had no coherent meaning — ◀ still lands on a period labelled "Last month". **"All time" is deliberately absent**: the query is `.gte/.lte`, so unbounded would pull every entry ever into the browser. The task popup is fully editable via the shared `TimeEntryModal`, its title opens the pane, its client chip navigates, and the `id:""` sentinel is gone now that `addTimeEntry` returns the inserted row. ⚠️ A row **re-dated out of the period is removed from page state** — every total, the breakdown, the stats and the CSV derive from it.
  **Team:** cards ⇄ **table** layout (`team.layout`), columns Member/Role/Email/Started/Tenure/Left/Hours·period/Open/Billable, built from the page's own memos so the two layouts can't disagree; period **stepping** with quarters (and the "Hours · this month" tile label now follows the stepped period instead of lying). Range key persisted, **offset deliberately not** — reopening on "Q2 2025" would present stale hours as current. **Member page:** hours-per-month becomes a `MultiLineChart` (with 12 buckets `LineChart` labels nothing and degrades to a squiggle), tiles left / chart right, **open assigned tasks** in the chart's old slot, and **two tabs with HR details behind the second** — lazily mounted then kept mounted, which both protects `HrFields`' uncontrolled inputs and means `member_hr`/`member_notes` are **not queried at all** until an admin asks, so opening someone's page to check their hours no longer pulls national IDs into the browser. **Settings:** three tabs (My account / Clients / Studio setup); `ChangePassword` renders **once** — it was duplicated purely to balance a hand-split masonry, which is why the masonry went; a member sees no strip at all, and the stored tab is validated against the visible list. **Home:** one row of tiles (6) / week timesheet (4) / **Coming up** (2), the last with `lg:h-0 lg:min-h-full` so it fills the row without stretching it, its list rewritten from a 4-card carousel to a vertical scroller capped at 12 with a "+N further ahead" footer.
  Verified: `tsc` clean, lint **0 errors** and warnings **75 → 21** (all remaining are the pre-existing React-Compiler advisories), **42 tests**, production build clean. **NOT yet verified in the browser** — this session had no signed-in dev session, so every check in the plan's verification table is still outstanding.
- 2026-07-30 — **v1.0.5 — avatars on the week timesheet; an admin can log hours for anyone, on any date, from every entry point.** (1) The timesheet's name column carries an `Avatar` beside the first name (`w-20` → `w-28`); heights still line up with the tiles. (2) **`UserDayDetails` treats its `userId`/`date` props as a STARTING point, not a fixed target.** For an admin the header is now a member `<select>` + a date input that re-fetch on change, which is what finally makes the Time Feed's generic **"Add new hours"** button useful — it opens on today/self, and the studio's actual need is backfilling someone else's Tuesday. Members still get the plain static header (verified via `?viewAs`: no select, no date). (3) The task panel's add-time form gained the matching **date** field beside the member picker, and the date **stays put after an add** rather than snapping back to today, because backfilling is usually several entries on one past day. (4) **New `src/lib/members.ts` → `loggableMembers(profiles, meFirstId)`** — the "who may hold a time entry" rule was about to be copied into a third place. It excludes accountless profiles: since 0018 a profile is a *person*, and the ~24 former staff kept for attribution have no login and must never appear in a picker. (5) **A React subtlety worth keeping:** the popup stores its fetched rows **tagged with the person+day they belong to** (`{key, rows}`) instead of a separate `ready` flag. Switching person used to need `setReady(false)` inside the effect — a lint-flagged cascading render — and without it the previous person's entries flashed up under the new name. Deriving readiness from the key removes both problems. Verified live: switching to Shaked / 28 Jul re-reads 7h 30m with the right tasks, matching the timesheet cell.
- 2026-07-30 — **v1.0.4 — week timesheet trimmed to Time-Feed styling; admins can log hours for anyone from the task panel.** (1) The pane lost its title, its subtitle and the "Click any cell…" footer, and each cell lost its bordered box — cells are now plain `td`s with `hover:bg-brand-soft/60` and a faint `–`, exactly like the Time Feed timesheet. **The missed-day signal moved into the text colour** (`text-danger/60` on the `–`) rather than a tinted outline, so the grid stays flat but the pane still does its one job. (2) The card is `h-full` and the wrapping grid dropped `items-start`, so the two columns stretch together — verified identical at **276px**. (3) The "Time Feed →" link moved into the header row's otherwise-dead corner cell, since there's no title left to hang it off. (4) **`timeForUserId` in `task-panel.tsx`**: admins get a member `<select>` inside the add-time form, defaulting to **"Me"** and sorted with the current user first (alphabetical order buried "Me" between Nadav and Office). `addTimeEntry` already took an optional `userId` — this is the UI that was missing. Members never render the control and always attribute to themselves; verified via `?viewAs` that the select is absent for a designer. The list is active profiles with an account, since an accountless former-staff profile (0018) cannot hold a new entry. **Note the other admin path already worked** — clicking any cell in the week timesheet opens that person's day and logs to them, so this fills the gap for logging against a specific task.
- 2026-07-30 — **v1.0.3 — admin home: KPI tiles 2×2 at half width, week timesheet beside them.** Nitsan's ask, and it closes the studio-manager review's top gap ("nothing surfaces a designer who forgot to log hours") without a notification system. (1) `StatTiles` dropped `lg:grid-cols-4` for a plain `grid-cols-2`, and the admin branch wraps it with the new pane in `lg:grid-cols-2` — each tile keeps roughly the width it had when four spanned the page. (2) New **`week-timesheet.tsx`**: designers × the current week, first names only, cell = that day's hours. **Deliberately not the Time Feed timesheet in miniature** — no per-designer total, no per-day total, no expandable rows, because the question is only whether a cell is filled and a column of sums invites reading it as a performance table. Hover a cell for a native tooltip listing `Client · Task — Xh`; click opens the same day popup as Time Feed. (3) **Three judgement calls worth keeping:** a blank cell is tinted red only when the day is **over** (`iso < todayIso`) — flagging today would nag every morning about hours nobody has had time to log yet; rows are `role === "designer"` **plus anyone who actually logged that week**, since Itay/Michal/Nitsan are admins whose permanently-red rows would read as a problem, but `Office` is a shared *admin* account that does log (10.5h) and hours that exist must never be invisible on a pane whose job is spotting missing ones; and Fri/Sat columns appear only when somebody logged then, so weekend hours aren't hidden by the Sun–Thu default. (4) It reads `entrySums`, not `entrySumsAll` — a 2019 backfill row must never make a designer look like they logged. (5) **`UserDayDetails` + `EntryEditRow` extracted** from `feed/page.tsx` to `src/components/user-day-details.tsx` so both pages share one popup instead of drifting; it now uses the store's `loadDayEntries` rather than opening its own Supabase client, which removes the last direct-Supabase call outside the store. Verified in the browser: tooltip reads "Studio · Voyantis Case Study — 7.5h", the popup opens from the home pane *and* still from the Time Feed timesheet, tsc clean, lint 0 errors (77 → **75**, the extraction removed two), 37 tests, build clean.
