- 2026-07-30 — **v1.0.2 — hiding a task or a period is a FOCUS tool, not confidentiality.** Reverts the one behavioural change in v1.0.1 that acted on the opposite assumption. Nitsan: *"if i hide any task or period it doesnt hide it from summary — its just hiding it to focus on current period or current active tasks."* So the security review's "hidden report values are recomputable" finding is **not a finding**: an admin hides a finished period so the client reads the current one, and `total − Σ(visible periods)` giving the hidden number back is harmless because it was never secret. `sanitizeSnapshot` again ships each task's true `totalMinutes` spanning every period, hidden ones included; the test that asserted non-recoverability now asserts the opposite, so nobody re-"fixes" it. **The structural sanitization from v0.99.3 is untouched and still right** — hidden rows/columns are physically absent from the payload rather than hidden with CSS, which is what stops a client reading them at all. **Still open, a real mismatch this exposed:** the public report's summary tiles ("Delivered to date", the current-period tile, cap/remaining) are computed from the *visible* periods and *visible* tasks only, because the sanitizer removes hidden ones before the view sees them — so hiding DOES currently move those tiles, which contradicts the rule above. Left alone pending Nitsan's call, since making them span hidden rows means passing the true totals separately.
- 2026-07-30 — **v1.0.1 — review fixes: privilege escalation, attachment uploads, report leak, first tests.** Four review agents were run against v1.0.0 (ux / code / security / studio-manager). This carries the fixes; **migration `0021_harden_profile_writes.sql` MUST BE APPLIED.** (1) **CRITICAL, any member could make themselves an admin.** `own profile update` (0001) was `for update using (id = auth.uid())` with no `with check` and no column scoping — Postgres reuses USING as the check, and that expression only constrains `id`, so `update profiles set role='admin' where id = <me>` succeeded from the anon key and handed over `member_hr` (national IDs, addresses), `member_notes`, `finance_*`, `client_billing_periods` and every admin-only task column. Both real boundaries were bypassable by escalating *out* of them. 0021 adds `trg_profile_member_cols` in the shape of 0011 — members may change only `name`/`avatar_url`/`photo_url` — plus the explicit `with check`. **It exempts the service role, unlike 0011**, because /api/avatar, /api/member-image and /api/admin/users legitimately write profiles with the service key after verifying the caller's session; a null `auth.uid()` can only be the service key or the SQL editor, since an anonymous request matches neither policy and so never reaches the trigger. (2) **`/api/task-attachments` was the only upload route with no allowlist** — it stored `contentType: file.type`, attacker-controlled, into the public `task-files` bucket, so `brief.html` became a permanent script-hosting URL on the studio's own Supabase origin. Now goes through `classifyUpload` like /api/intake (the two image routes use the stricter `classifyImage`). (3) **A hidden report column was recoverable as `total − Σ(visible periods)`.** `sanitizeSnapshot` now re-derives each task's total from the columns that survived, on whichever axis had one removed (lower bound if both). **REVERTED in v1.0.2 — the premise was wrong, see below.** Moved to `src/lib/report-sanitize.ts` and unit-tested. (4) **Archived members could still use the app** — nothing anywhere gated on `active`. `(app)/layout.tsx` now renders `AccountInactive` (a UI gate; see the new **Offboarding** section — the real fix is banning the auth user, which 0018 made safe). (5) **Generic errors** on the member-facing routes (`me/hr`, `avatar`, `member-image`) instead of raw Postgres messages naming columns and constraints; `/api/me/hr` PATCH gained per-field length + format limits. `/api/admin/*` deliberately still returns the real message — admins are trusted and it's the difference between a debuggable failure and a shrug. (6) **First app tests: `npm test`** (Vitest). `period-math.ts` extracted from `dashboard.tsx` with `now` injectable — 28 cases over the period boundaries the KPI deltas read, including the `<=` end-of-period bug and "day buckets never project"; `report-sanitize.ts` 10 cases, one of which fails on the pre-fix code. **38 tests, and the app's number-crunching had none before this.** (7) **A failed boot no longer renders an empty studio.** The catch only logged; every pane then read zero, which is a claim about the data. New `bootError` in the store → a real error screen with a retry. (8) **The boot query's schema fallback no longer treats a network error as a missing column** — `fetchAll` throws `DbError` carrying the Postgres code and the fallbacks only step down on `42703`/`42P01` (`isMissingSchema`). This mattered: silently degrading past `legacy` leaks ~4,000h of backfill into days-worked, tenure and the feed. (9) `DayLog` no longer opens its own Supabase client and calls `mapTimeEntry` itself — new `loadDayEntries(userId, date)` in the store (the store only holds the recent 400 rows, so the query is real and has to live somewhere). (10) **UX:** `Avatar` gained `emptyTitle` — its own `title="Unassigned"` beat any wrapper's on hover, so a recovered entry's stored provenance was replaced by the word "Unassigned". **Note the review overstated this as Critical**: named ex-staff have had profiles since 0018 and render correctly (verified: "Edor Nisim"/EN), so only rows naming nobody were affected. (11) **`scripts/README.md`** — a status table (read-only / repeatable / done-once / break-glass) for all 26 scripts, plus the bulk-SQL warning and the pending items. The only record was this working log. Verified: tsc clean, lint **0 errors** (77 warnings, none in the new modules), 38/38 tests, and in-browser — KPI figures byte-identical after the period-math extraction (671.5h / 63% / 51 / 83.9h), DayLog steps 29→26 Jul fetching each day, the recovered row's avatar now reads "(date estimated)". **NOT fixed, deliberately:** the public storage buckets (`avatars`/`task-files`/`intake` are world-readable via `getPublicUrl`; needs private buckets + signed URLs and an intake-queue render change), and the `store.tsx`/`dashboard.tsx` monolith split the code review asked for — that is a large unreviewable diff and the lint warnings tied to it are already deferred to that pass.
- 2026-07-29 — **v1.0.0** — Out of pre-release. Nitsan: "you can make it version 1 — we are using it in the team." No code change beyond `APP_VERSION`; this release carries everything logged under v0.99.37–39 below, which were never deployed separately.
- 2026-07-29 — v0.99.39 — **Pre-2022 periods showed no people at all.** Nitsan: "why it doesn't show any members when I select a year before 2022?" Because **every** time entry before 2022 is recovered pre-Everhour history (verified against the DB: 2019 → 1495 entries, **0** non-legacy; 2021 → 1676 / 0; 2022 → 2333 / 509; 2023 → 4907 / 4433), and three surfaces gated on the legacy-free list. But **about a third of those old entries DO name a person** (2019: 518 of 1495), so the right gate is "has an author", not "isn't legacy". (1) `StudioTeamStrip` now reads `entrySumsAll` and buckets by `userId`; 2019 goes from an absent pane to **9 designers**, all archived. (2) `StatTiles`: **"Tasks worked" dropped the author gate entirely** — a task was worked whoever logged it, and it read 0 for every pre-2022 year beside thousands of hours (2016 now reads 31). **"Avg / designer" keeps only attributed hours, on BOTH sides of the fraction** — it was "0h · 0 designers" for those years and now reads 19.8h across 4 for 2016. (3) The honest part: most old hours name nobody, so the strip's cards can't add up to the period total. It now says so — **"· 6825.13h unattributed"** beside the designer count (2019, of 8223.38h), tooltipped. Without that the pane silently under-reports. (4) Also fixed the tile hover copy, which claimed the previous period was "clipped to today" even for a finished historical period — it now says "the whole of the previous period, since this one is already complete" unless the selection actually contains today.
- 2026-07-29 — v0.99.38 — **`profiles.end_date` (migration 0020, applied 2026-07-29), client chips are white capsules, billable toggle un-pilled.** (1) **Migration 0020 adds `profiles.end_date` and a `before insert or update` trigger that forces `active = false` whenever it is non-null** — in the DB rather than only the app, because Nitsan is filling these dates in by hand in the SQL editor later and no app code should be able to forget the implication. Clearing the date does **not** restore anybody; the Restore button in `/team/<id>` clears the date *and* flips `active`, and `updateProfile` in the store holds both halves of that invariant (setting `endDate` ⇒ `active: false`; `active: true` on someone with a date ⇒ `endDate: null`, or the trigger would immediately re-archive them and the button would look broken). Field lives in "HR details" beside Start date; the archived banner and the team-list row show the date when set; the studio strip's "past" tooltip reads "left <date>". `profiles` is fetched with `select("*")`, so no query needed updating. Applied and verified: the column is live and filterable, all rows `null` so far (the dates come later). (2) **`ClientChip` is now a white capsule** (`rounded-full border border-border bg-surface`, `px-1.5/px-2`) so the colour dot and the name read as one object; the link variant hovers to a brand-tinted border instead of underlining under the dot. The **donut legend rows got the same capsule** — the one other place a client name sits beside a colour dot with no container of its own. Left alone deliberately: the client-report tabs, weekly-plan filter buttons, settings list rows and global-search results, all of which already have their own frame. (3) "Billable only" lost its bordered pill — among the range pills it read as one more range button; it is now just the switch plus a label that tints when on.
- 2026-07-29 — v0.99.37 — **Studio strip includes past members; billable toggle; period nav never disappears.** (1) `StudioTeamStrip` no longer filters on `p.active` — the pane describes a *period*, and the `min > 0` gate was already the right one, so someone who left in March still shows for March. Past members get a **dashed border and a "· past" suffix** rather than a dimmed card: the hours are as real as anyone else's. This also **fixed a genuine disagreement on the page** — the strip read "7 designers" on This month while the "Avg / designer" tile divided by 8, because one archived person had logged July hours that counted in the tile and were invisible in the strip. Now 8 and 8; This year reads 14 with 6 past, matching its tile. (2) "Billable only" is a **switch** (sr-only checkbox + track/knob, so the whole pill stays one click target and keeps keyboard focus) and is the **first element in the filter row**. (3) The period arrows and **"Now" are always rendered** — the arrows used to vanish on "All time" and "Now" only appeared once you had already stepped away, so the row reflowed under the cursor. All three are now disabled + `opacity-30` instead, with a tooltip explaining why on "All time".
- 2026-07-29 — v0.99.37 — **Explained KPI tiles, projected last bucket, reports polish.** (1) New **`InfoDot`** in `ui.tsx` — a circled "i" with a real hover/focus card (a `title` attribute mangles three sentences into one delayed run). Each of the four admin KPI tiles now carries one via `StatTile`'s `info` prop: what the figure counts, what it deliberately excludes (recovered pre-Everhour rows in "Tasks worked"/"Avg / designer"), and **the exact compared window spelled out as dates**. The last tile passes `infoAlign="right"` so its card doesn't run off the page. (2) The "vs last" comparison already clipped the previous period to the same elapsed portion (`comparablePrevRange`); fixed its boundary — `today < sel.end` meant that **on the final day of a period the comparison silently reverted to the whole previous period**, reading as a collapse. Now `<=`. (3) **The running last bucket on both admin line charts is projected.** `bucketize` returns its `unit`; `bucketProjection(unit, lastKey)` gives the run-rate factor (month: `daysInMonth / today`; year: `daysInYear / dayOfYear`; **day buckets return null** — scaling "today" by the evening left in it is noise). `MultiLineChart` gained **`projection: {index, values}`**: it plots the estimate at that index, dashes the segment leading into it, italicises the label as `~Jul`, notes "dashed = Jul projected" under the headline, and shows "(X so far)" in the tooltip. The **headline total keeps reading real logged hours**; only the trend chip reads the projection, which is the point — estimated-full-July against a complete June. Verified on This year: 5453.75h headline unchanged, `~Jul` dashed on both panes. (4) Reports: **"Hours in period" is now the same `MultiLineChart`** as the home page (single "Hours" series, so no `totalSeries` needed); **"Hours by user" bars are split** billable-solid / non-billable-`bg-brand/30` — `SplitBar` gained `tone: "grey" | "blue"` (default grey keeps the client table as it was) and now titles both segments with their hours; `HBar` gained a `bar` slot to host it. **Chevrons** on every client row (display-only — the whole row already toggles) and on section sub-rows, plus a **collapse-all chevron in the table header** that reads "open" when ANY row is, so a half-expanded table closes on one click. (5) **"Coming up" lost its 30-day horizon and its title suffix** — it now shows the next 4 occasions however far off, `OCCASION_HORIZON_DAYS` is deleted, and `relativeDays()` switches to "in ~N months" past 60 days. `MEMBER_OCCASION_DAYS` (7) is untouched and still the only thing the dimming means.
- 2026-07-29 — v0.99.36 — **Donut doubled + up to 15 clients; "Hours over time" gained a billable line.** (1) `PieChart` `SIZE` 150 → **300px** and the legend is capped at the donut's height with `overflow-y-auto` — needed because `PIE_CLIENTS` went **6 → 15** (plus the "Other" tail). Verified on All time: 16 rows, scrollHeight 380 vs clientHeight 300, so it scrolls; This month has only 12 clients with hours, so no scrollbar and no "Other". (2) The admin "Hours over time" chart now draws **two lines when the page is showing everything** — All hours (brand blue) and Billable (`#16a34a`); with "Billable only" on the second line would retrace the first, so it's dropped. `perBucket` therefore carries `{minutes, billable}` per bucket. **`MultiLineChart` gained `totalSeries`**: its headline total summed EVERY series, which is right for disjoint per-client lines but double-counts here because billable ⊂ all hours (671.5h would have read 1,096h) — passing the series label makes the headline read that one series. Verified: 671.5h on This month, 87,250.8h on All time, both unchanged by the second line.
- 2026-07-29 — v0.99.35 — **Admin home restructured: no My-tasks pane, line chart, 4-card occasions carousel.** (1) The compact **My tasks pane is gone from the admin home** and **"The studio" roster took its grid slot** (admins triage from `/my-tasks`); the strip's inner grid drops `xl:grid-cols-8` → max 4 across, since it is now half-width. The member home keeps its My-tasks pane unchanged. (2) The header's **"Tasks assigned" counter is hidden for admins** (members keep it) — it belonged to the pane that left. (3) **"Hours over time" is a line chart for admins** — the same `MultiLineChart` as the pane beside it, one brand series, which also **thins the x labels** (`step = ceil(n/6)`) where the columns had rendered all 20+ daily ticks. Members keep `MiniColumnsLabeled`. (4) **"Coming up" is now a 4-card carousel**, soonest on the left, arrows stepping one card and only rendered past 4 occasions (`1–4 of N`). **Two horizons now exist, and that is the whole point of the dimming:** the admin pane looks `OCCASION_HORIZON_DAYS` (30) ahead, the member hero only `MEMBER_OCCASION_DAYS` (**7**) — so an occasion the team cannot see yet renders at `opacity-50` with a tooltip saying so. Both constants sit together at the top of the Celebrations block; change 7 to change what the studio sees. Verified live: Shaked's birthday (today) full-strength beside a dimmed "World Photography Day — 19/8 · in 21 days".
- 2026-07-29 — v0.99.35 — **Client chips wherever a bare task title was ambiguous.** Four spots, all because titles like "Homepage"/"Onboarding" repeat across clients: member home **My-tasks pane** and **"Log my hours"** rows swapped their colour dot for a real `ClientChip`; the **Time Feed timesheet** per-task sub-rows gained one (cell `max-w-64` → `max-w-80`, chip capped at `max-w-32`); and the **"Hours by client" donut legend** rows are now **links to the client page** (`PieChart` slices take an optional `href`; the aggregate "Other" slice has none and stays plain text). `link={false}` on the two home panes and the timesheet row on purpose — those rows are themselves a button/open a popup, so an `<a>` would either nest illegally or hijack the click; the donut legend is the one place a navigating name was actually asked for. Verified: clicking "No Traffic" in the legend lands on that client's page, and the timesheet sub-rows read "No Traffic · LinkedIn Banner_" / "TEMA Creative · Website Update july2026_".
- 2026-07-29 — v0.99.35 — **Admin home: "Billable only" toggle.** A pill+checkbox in the page-wide filter row (admin-only; `HomeFilter.billableOnly`, gated on `isAdmin` so a member preview can't hold it on) scopes **every** hour figure on the page to billable tasks: `StatTiles`, `MyGraphs` (hours over time + hours-by-client donut), `StudioClientTrend` and `StudioTeamStrip`. **The two billable-SHARE readouts deliberately keep all hours as their denominator** — the Billable tile and the per-designer bars — because a share of a billable-only total is 100% by construction and would say nothing; the tile's sub-label switches to "of all hours" and the first tile relabels to "Billable hours" so the scope is never ambiguous. `StatTiles` therefore tracks `curAll`/`prevAll` (unfiltered) alongside `cur`/`prev` (filtered). Verified live on This month: 671.5h → **424.5h** (63% of it), Tasks worked 51 → 32, Avg/designer 83.9 → 53.1h, the internal **Studio** client drops out of the donut entirely (No Traffic 31.5% → 48.4%), and the team strip reads billable hours against the unchanged true share (Nadav 156h·88% → 136.5h·88%).
- 2026-07-29 — v0.99.35 — **Client page task table has drag-resizable columns, like My Tasks.** `client-view.tsx` now uses the same `useColWidths`/`ResizeHandle` from `resizable.tsx` (localStorage key **`colw.client-tasks`**) for Assignee/Due/Tag/Hrs-budget; the "$" column is left fixed (one glyph, nothing to reveal). Widths reach `TaskRow` — two levels down through `SectionGroup` — via a new **`ColWidthsContext`**, for the same reason `SelectionContext` exists: threading them as props would bury the drag/drop signatures. Defaults are the px equivalents of the Tailwind widths the cells used to carry (160/64/112/144). **The resize immediately exposed the squeeze this table always had**: the name cell was `min-w-0 flex-1`, so widening a column crushed the task name to one character instead of scrolling. Fixed the same way My Tasks does it — name cells get a `min-w-32` floor and the table wrapper went `min-w-[720px]` → **`min-w-fit`**, so the table grows and the existing `overflow-x-auto` scrolls (verified: scrollWidth 1290 vs clientWidth 691). Verified by a real drag: 160 → 280px, persisted to localStorage, body rows followed the header, and task names that used to read "Car…"/"Ho…" now read in full.
- 2026-07-29 — v0.99.35 (**DEPLOYED + committed 356cea2, pushed to main**; prod verified serving v0.99.35 on tracker.studionmore.com, `vercel crons ls` reports none) — **Every live sync removed: the tracker is now the only system.** Nitsan's call, in two steps in one session — first the Google-Sheet weekly-plan bridge, then Everhour. **Deleted:** `vercel.json` (both crons — the file held nothing else, so there is no cron at all now), `src/app/api/plan-sync/`, `src/lib/weekly-plan-sync.ts` + the admin "Sync from sheet" button on the Weekly Plan header, `src/app/api/everhour-sync/`, `src/lib/everhour-sync.ts`, `src/components/everhour-sync-button.tsx` (the Time Feed "Sync Everhour" button), and — because the Everhour sync was its ONLY producer — the whole `sync_issues` surface: `/sync-issues` page, `/api/sync-issues`, the badged sidebar item, the bell's danger branch, the Client-Reports red banner, and `openSyncIssues` in `store.tsx` (incl. its boot query). **The `sync_issues` TABLE is left in the DB, untouched** — it was verified **empty (0 rows)** before removing the UI, so nothing was orphaned, and dropping it needs a migration nobody needs yet. `WEEKLY_PLAN_CSV_URL` removed from `.env.local`; `CRON_SECRET` and `EVERHOUR_API_KEY` are now unused by the app but kept locally because `scripts/sync-everhour.mjs` + `scripts/import-everhour.mjs` still use the key for a manual historical pull. **`scripts/import-weekly-plan.mjs` / `import-plan-sheet.mjs` / `sync-everhour.mjs` are deliberately KEPT** — one-off importers, run by hand, no schedule can fire them. **Owner actions DONE 2026-07-29:** the three env vars were deleted from Vercel and the Google-Sheet CSV link was unpublished — so there is no live path from either system into the tracker any more, only the hand-run scripts with the local key. Verified: tsc clean, lint **0 errors**, `npm run build` clean with none of the removed routes in the manifest, and in-browser — Weekly Plan header ends at "Columns", Time Feed header has only "Add new hours", sidebar has no "Sync issues".
- 2026-07-29 — v0.99.35 — **Time Feed day popup now names the client, not just the task.** `UserDayDetails` showed a bare colour dot + title, so "Onboarding" or "Homepage" — titles half the studio's clients share — gave no way to tell whose hours they were. The leading cell is now two lines in the same 192px: a real `ClientChip` above the task title (title now `text-foreground`, client stays muted), with a `Client · Task` hover tooltip. Verified in the browser: "Studio / Onboarding" and "No Traffic / LinkedIn Banner" on Nadav's 26 Jul cell, nothing truncated.
- 2026-07-29 — data — **2016 was missing from the finance DB entirely; imported (+1,180.50h of billing record, +847.53h into the tracker).** Nitsan spotted Quadream 2016 reading 345h in the tracker against **332h** in his sheet. Root cause: **the 0006 seed AND the granular feeder import both started at 2017**, because no 2016 workbook was on disk — so the "345h overage" was an artefact of having nothing to compare against. He supplied `PLAN_more_2016.xlsx` (now in `~/Documents/Claud cowork/financial sheets/`). **`scripts/parse-plan-2016.py`** (raw-XML, no openpyxl on this machine) → `data/finance-2016.json` → **`scripts/import-finance-2016.mjs`**: 20 clients / 117 client-month rows / **1,180.50h**, matching the sheet's own total row exactly and quadream to **332.00h** exactly, + 95 P&L rows on the existing `line_item` vocabulary. **INSERT ONLY works on the locked finance tables** — `finance_guard_locked()` is BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE, so rows go in already `state='final'` with no unlock dance (unlike a rename, which needs final→actual→final). **Two things specific to 2016:** it is a **PARTIAL year** (the studio began trading in **May**, so only months 5–12 exist; no Jan–Apr rows are written because absent ≠ zero billed), and **per-client revenue is not in the sheet** — only hours + rate — so `revenue_gross = hours × rate` as in 2017–2026. NOTE the sheet's own "before discount" row uses a **flat ₪250 for every client** (1180.5 × 250 = 295,125) while real per-client rates run ₪200–250, so the import totals 259,245; the same divergence exists in 2017 (826,585 vs 1,011,700), so this follows the existing convention rather than inventing one. Backfill re-run for the newly comparable year: 16 clients, 40 entries, 847.53h — and **Quadream 2016 correctly received nothing**, because the per-YEAR reconciliation saw the tracker already above the billed figure. Comparison now spans 2016→2026: **2016 reads 1,181 finance vs 1,194 tracker (+13h, 1%)**. **What the 13h is:** the annual totals agree but the MONTHLY shape does not — `"Ui system - 165hrs"` is one lump with no per-comment dates, so all 165h landed in Nov on Asana's completion date while the real work spread Aug–Nov (sheet: Aug 70 / Sep 93 / Oct 90 / Nov 75; tracker: 36.6 / 102.9 / 36.5 / 165). **Title-derived hours get the total right and the month approximately** — worth remembering before trusting any single pre-2023 month. Audit fix: the expected re-home mapping still sent project `as:455542718969443` to In-reach and so failed on the deliberate merge — now maps to Quadream. PASS.
- 2026-07-29 — **~~OPEN CORRECTNESS ISSUE~~ — RESOLVED 2026-08-26, see the entry at the top of this log. The 14,896h figure below was an UPPER BOUND on "shape unknown", not an error; measured against the real parent data the excess is ~197h across 6 tasks. Read the 2026-08-26 entry before acting on anything here.** Original note: **the roll-up rule was never applied outside the 23 legacy projects.** Nitsan doubted the size of the tracker-over-finance gaps and was right to. "Children win, parent zero" reads `data/asana/parents.json`, which was only ever populated for the tasks `fetch-asana-stories.mjs` ran over — the dissolved legacy projects. The much wider `recover-title-hours.mjs` pass (+14,668h across every other client) read **titles only, with NO parent data**, so a parent whose title is a roll-up of its children was counted alongside them. **`scripts/report-rollup-risk.mjs`** quantifies it: **14,896h of 23,792h asana-recovered hours (63%) sit on tasks whose subtask shape was never fetched.** Volta, which WAS covered, shows 50/50 and 0h at risk; Mobileye 7/300 and 4,837h; Autofleet 1,806h; Blazepod 1,764h — **the three biggest exposures are the three biggest overages against the finance sheets.** This is an UPPER BOUND, not an error estimate (most tasks have no subtasks) and it cannot be separated from genuine write-offs without the real parent data. **FIX, needs `ASANA_ACCESS_TOKEN`:** re-run `fetch-asana-stories.mjs` over the **965 gids in `data/rollup-refetch-gids.csv`** to extend `parents.json`, then re-run the reconciler so the rule applies studio-wide.
- 2026-07-29 — data — **Cross-checked the whole recovery against finance-admin, then closed the remaining gap from the billing record: +18,509h.** `finance_client_monthly` (imported from the PLAN_more_YYYY.xlsx workbooks) is an INDEPENDENT record of the same years, so disagreement locates missing data. **`scripts/compare-finance-hours.mjs`** (read-only) does the comparison; the headline first read −44% for 2018–2021 and looked like a failed recovery, but the split by cause showed almost all of it was clients with **no tracker row at all** — boards that predate Asana, nothing to recover from. On shared clients the Asana recovery was accurate to **−580h across 53,513h**. Three reporting errors of mine that the split exposed: the plan sheet carries **Studio as a revenue row** while the tracker holds it non-billable, which made 2024–2026 read −20% against a column that deliberately excludes it (now its own bucket); "not in the tracker at all" actually meant "no billable hours", conflating 88 clients that never had a row with 3 that have one; and per-client matching without prefix resolution was ~90% false gaps. **Then, per Nitsan ("for pre-Everhour years what matters is hours per month/year/client; per task and per member is less important — count on finance data"), `scripts/backfill-from-finance.mjs`** created **85 archived clients + 104 holder tasks + 647 monthly entries = 18,508.97h**, dropping the unmatched figure from 13,297h to **90h**. Studio all-time 68k→**86,364h**; 2017 4,989h · 2018 6,043h · 2019 8,223h · 2020 8,189h · 2021 8,905h. **TOP-UP, NOT REPLACE** — `written = max(0, finance − already)` per client per YEAR, so every hour already recovered from a real task keeps its task, date and author; a re-run now plans 0.02h. **Two bugs caught in the first dry run, both worth remembering:** (1) In-reach and Quadream alias to one key and the client map kept whichever row it saw first, so Quadream's shortfall was measured against In-reach's 27h → a **2,312h phantom**; the whole alias GROUP must be summed. (2) Reconciling per MONTH banks every shortfall and ignores every surplus — billing months lag work months — which invented **1,223h for Mobileye and 1,198h for Autofleet**, both already above their billed hours for the year. Reconcile per year, distribute on the finance monthly shape. Every row is `legacy = true, date_estimated = true, user_id = null` (verified 647/647) so days-worked/tenure/feed/personal stats are untouched; reversal is `delete from time_entries where legacy_author_name = '(from finance plan)'`. **The audit got a real new branch, not a loosened assertion:** a finance-derived month must exist in `finance_client_monthly` for that client — stronger than the Asana window check. Client-name matching extracted to **`scripts/lib/client-names.mjs`** because it had been copied into four scripts and an alias added to one silently didn't apply in the others.

The 6 task moves are in **`data/merge-clients-inreach-quadream.sql`**, still to run — `tasks.client_id` is trigger-protected). `Raven` / `Ravוn` / `Ravin` are all **Ravin** — three spellings across the yearly workbooks, the middle one with a Hebrew vav from a keyboard-layout slip. **CHECK client_rates BEFORE renaming a client:** finance-admin resolves a rate by NAME and every row has `client_id` null, so a rename moves which rate applies. Verified no revenue moved — Donsplus/Double overlap at the same ₪300 from 2023, and the In-reach-budget tasks all sit in 2019-07…2020-05 where both tracks read ₪225. **`finance_client_monthly` rows are `state = 'final'` and `finance_guard_locked()` rejects any UPDATE that is final both before and after** — the documented unlock path is to leave the final state first, so a rename is two steps (final → actual carrying the new name → final). `client_rates` has no such trigger. The Ravin monthly rename is **still pending** — the auto-mode classifier blocked the write; its 9 `client_rates` rows WERE renamed, so until it runs those monthly rows have no matching rate row (harmless: historical revenue is stored inline on the row, and Ravin has no current-year rows). Re-run `scripts/merge-finance-client-names.mjs --apply`.

New **`scripts/recover-title-hours.mjs`** (dry-run default) does it as a standalone pass, since the shape differs: titles only, no comments, and dates come from **Asana's `created_at` → `completed_at`** (all 969 had them) rather than a comment window. **966 tasks, 5,612 entries, 14,668.33h.** Scope is guarded to tasks with **zero** time entries, no existing `legacy_hours`, outside the 23 projects — so it cannot collide with real Everhour data. Years: 2020 768h→**4,018h**, 2019 1,517h→4,343h, 2021 805h→3,883h, 2018 1,825h→3,264h; total recovered 7,948h→**22,617h**, studio all-time 51,996h→66,665h. **A guard worth keeping:** a figure with NO `h`/`hrs` unit that is also >100h is more likely part of the name than an hour count — `"Storemaven - 404"` reads as a 404 page, and sibling tasks confirm that usage (`"404 page - 1.25h"`). 3 such tasks (632h) are SKIPPED and listed, never silently included: Storemaven 404h, `"אתר -124"`, `"Amnon's CES PPT -104"` — the latter two are plausible and can be added by hand. **Titles were NOT cleaned this pass** (renaming needs the SQL editor) and **316 budgets could not be set** for the same reason. **STILL UNRECOVERED: 1,255 tasks whose titles carry no figure** (1,223 have an `asana_gid`) — those need their comments fetched, i.e. a fresh Asana token. Review sheet at `data/title-hours-review.csv`. Audit assertion extended: an estimated date is valid if it sits inside EITHER the comment window (`activity_from/to`) OR the Asana `created_at`→`completed_at` window — the two passes used different sources.
- 2026-07-28 — v0.99.34 (deployed) — **Half the recovered history was invisible on every chart; now dated.** Migration **`0019_estimated_entry_dates.sql`** — **applied**. Of the 7,948.65h recovered, only **3,994.90h had dates**; the other **3,953.75h sat in `tasks.legacy_hours` with no day at all**, so no time-series could plot it — the early years read ~3,995h when the studio had logged nearly twice that. Those hours are undated because a task's TITLE recorded a bigger total than its comments accounted for (`"Ui system - 165hrs"`, comments totalling 8h → 157h with no date). Per Nitsan ("when it happened can be estimate"), `scripts/spread-legacy-remainder.mjs` (dry-run default) distributes the remainder across each task's **observed** window — proportionally to that task's real dated entries where they exist, evenly across the activity_from→activity_to months otherwise, else the section's `closed_on` month. **4 tasks / 195h with no date signal are left alone, never guessed.** Result: 237 entries, **3,758.75h onto the timeline**; 2016 79h→345h, 2017 81h→**1,441h**, 2018 741h→1,825h, 2019 1,203h→1,517h, 2020 440h→768h, 2021 524h→805h; studio total 48,222h→51,996h. Every such row is `date_estimated = true` with **`user_id = null`** (an estimated date must not be pinned on a named person) and shows with an asterisk + tooltip in the task panel. **The total is unchanged at 7,948.65h** — hours only moved out of `legacy_hours` into entries, so `total = Σ(entries) + legacy_hours` still holds. Reversal SQL is in the script header. **A near-miss worth remembering:** adding the new column to the store's boot query made its error fallback collapse to a column set with **no `legacy` flag**, which would have leaked ~4,000h of backfill into days-worked, tenure and the feed — it now degrades ONE column at a time (`legacy, date_estimated` → `legacy` → bare), verified against the live DB. **Audit assertions rewritten to be durable:** per-client task TOTALS were never an invariant (32 zero-hour Voyantis rows were tidied with bulk-select and turned the audit red), so the mapping is now asserted via **`project_id` → client**, which nothing modifies; Harmon.ie asserts its **TRACKED** 2103h exactly and reports recovered hours separately; the cutover rule applies only to comment-derived rows, while estimated dates must sit **inside their task's evidenced window**. Twice while writing those I forgot to SELECT the columns the new assertions read (`activity_from/to`, `closed_on`) and got false failures — check the select before believing an audit failure.

- 2026-07-28 — v0.99.32 (deployed) — **Former staff are now archived members you can open, with their pre-Everhour history.** Migration **`0018_people_without_accounts.sql`** — **applied**. **A profile is now a PERSON, not an account:** it drops `profiles.id references auth.users(id)`. Nitsan wanted the ~24 pre-Everhour authors on Team; the obvious route would have meant minting ~24 dormant logins on .com with invented addresses (a takeover risk if anyone later creates one of those mailboxes) purely to hold attribution. Dropping the FK is both the better model and the safer one — RLS compares `auth.uid() = id`, so a person with no auth row matches nobody and can never sign in. New `profiles.has_account` (false for these) + a CHECK that anyone accountless is also inactive, so they cannot leak into a picker or the weekly plan. `scripts/create-former-staff.mjs` (dry-run default) created 24 profiles — Edor Nisim 725h, Avishay Cohen 463h, dikla 459h, adi 336h, Itai Snapier 328h, Miri Kuntsman 294h … yam sasson 114.5h, Inbal Kim Doron **−83h** — and linked **1,346 time entries + 2,055 comments** to them; `legacy_author_name` is kept as provenance and makes it reversible. Names go in **verbatim** as Asana had them (`dikla`, `adi`, `ruth`) rather than guessing at capitalisation or surnames. **Team + member pages moved to `entrySumsAll`** so the history actually shows (the home page stays legacy-free, so no current member's days-worked or tenure moved). Three things this exposed: (1) `MemberPhoto` fell back to the shared studio cut-out, so an archived person displayed **a photo of an actual colleague** — new `fallback="initials"` prop, wired wherever `has_account === false`; (2) the member page's "Hours per month" was hardcoded to the last 12 CALENDAR months, so every former-staff chart was empty — now windowed on their last 12 months **of activity**, with year labels; (3) "Send password link" is replaced by "Kept for historical attribution — no account" (the API already refused with a 404, but offering the button was misleading). Note "Tasks" on a team card counts OPEN assigned tasks, so it correctly reads 0 for archived people. Verified: Edor Nisim shows EN initials, 725h, 3 clients (Quadream 350.5h / Yoco 305h / Anchor 69.5h), chart Mar 21→Feb 22.

- 2026-07-28 — **INCIDENT + RECOVERY: 620 tasks destroyed and rebuilt. READ THIS BEFORE WRITING ANY BULK SQL.** `data/0018_rehome_unsorted.sql` (2,326 statements, `begin;` … `alter table tasks disable trigger` … 620 `update tasks set client_id` … `delete from clients` … `commit;`) was pasted into the **Supabase SQL editor**. Outcome: the 14 client INSERTs committed, **every `update tasks` did NOT take effect** (no task ended up with `legacy_title` set), and the final `delete from clients` **ran anyway** — so 620 tasks still attached to "Imported / Unsorted" were wiped by `ON DELETE CASCADE`, taking 2,397 comments, 87 sections, 35 `projects` rows and Harmon.ie's **2,103.25h of time entries**. A `do $$ … raise exception if leftover > 0` guard sat immediately before that delete and did not fire. **CONCLUSION: do NOT assume the SQL editor runs a pasted multi-statement script as one atomic transaction, and never put a cascading DELETE in the same file as the UPDATEs it depends on.** Recovery was total, from the committed `data/` dumps: `scripts/restore-unsorted.mjs` (**inserts/upserts only, service role, dry-run by default, `--apply` to write**) rebuilt projects → sections → tasks → time entries → comments straight into the correct clients, restoring Harmon.ie to exactly 2103.25h and all 2,397 comments. **It restored 627 tasks, 7 more than were destroyed** — `everhour-tasks-all.json` holds 7 zero-hour placeholder rows ("1", "2", "123", Hero…) on the Studio podcast/website boards that the original 2026 import had skipped; keeping them was safer than trying to identify exactly which 620 rows had existed, and the audit baseline is now 4513. **The `data/` dumps are what made recovery possible — never remove them from the repo.**
- 2026-07-28 — data — **Pre-Everhour recovery APPLIED: 7,948.65h.** 3,994.90h as 1,533 dated `legacy` time entries (68 of them negative `מפתח` reductions, 1,346 carrying `legacy_author_name` instead of a profile) + 3,953.75h as `tasks.legacy_hours` remainder on 150 tasks, plus 24 sections given their recovered budget/hours/closing date and 352 task names cleaned. Applied by **`scripts/apply-legacy-hours.mjs`** (dry-run default, `--apply` to write) after `reconcile-legacy-hours.mjs`. **Which columns the service role may write was established EMPIRICALLY, not assumed** (throwaway task, probed, deleted): `tasks.legacy_hours` / `activity_from` / `activity_to` / `legacy_title` **allowed**; `tasks.title` and `tasks.estimate_hours` **BLOCKED** by 0011's trigger; `sections.*` entirely allowed (no trigger on sections). So only title+budget go through the SQL editor, as `data/task-titles-cleanup.sql` — 353 UPDATEs, **no DELETE and no DDL**, and instead of disabling the trigger it does `select set_config('request.jwt.claims','{"sub":"<admin uuid>"}',true)` so `auth.uid()` resolves and `is_admin()` passes. **Prefer that set_config trick over `disable trigger` for any future admin-only bulk write.** Verified after applying: `audit-rehome.mjs` PASS, and the admin home page reads **596h / 8y4m14d / 63% / 45 / 74.5h — byte-identical to before the import** despite 3,994h of dated 2020–2022 entries now present, which is the `entrySums` legacy filter doing its job. `reconcile-legacy-hours.mjs` now scopes on the **23 legacy `projects.everhour_id` values**, not the (deleted) client — `project_id` is the only identifier that survived both the re-home and the restore.
- 2026-07-28 — v0.99.29 — **Bulk-select tasks, and the groundwork for dissolving "Imported / Unsorted".** Migration **`0016_legacy_history.sql`** — **must be applied** (the app degrades gracefully without it; the boot query falls back). (1) **`taskPatchToRow` had no `clientId` branch** (`src/lib/db.ts`), so "move a task to another client" updated local state, wrote an **empty patch object** to Postgres and silently reverted on reload — moving a task between clients had never worked. Verified fixed end-to-end on a throwaway task: moved → reloaded → still moved → ⌘Z restored both client and section. (2) **Multi-select in the client view** (admin-only, the first anywhere in the app): row checkboxes + tri-state select-all on each section header and the table header, shift-click ranges, and a sticky action bar (move to client/section, billable, mark done, delete). `updateTasksBulk` / `restoreTasksBulk` / `deleteTasksBulk` in `store.tsx` do one `.in("id", ids)` write and record **one** undo step for the whole selection (the inverse is a list of per-task patches, grouped so undo is one round-trip per distinct prior value). **Move-to-client always sets `sectionId` too** — a section belongs to exactly one client, so keeping the old id strands the tasks. Bulk delete **refuses outright** when any selected task has logged time (cascade is unrecoverable), unlike single delete which only warns. **GOTCHA:** the checkbox had to fit inside the EXISTING 36px gutter (checkbox `left-1`, grip `left-[18px]`, grip width 28→18px, full height kept). Widening `pl-9`→`pl-14` cost the task-name cell 20 of its 89px — the fixed columns already overflow the table's 720px min-width, so any gutter growth comes straight off the name. (3) **`entrySums` now EXCLUDES pre-Everhour backfill by default**; `entrySumsAll` is the opt-in for client-facing and per-task totals (client stats, reports, `taskMinutes`, TaskTable's total column). Chosen this way round deliberately: a personal or time-series surface nobody remembered to audit degrades safely, rather than silently inventing 2021 working days for someone still employed.
- 2026-07-28 — data — **The comment thread decoded; 2,397 Asana comments imported.** Migration **`0017_legacy_entry_authors.sql`** — **must be applied** with 0016 before the data SQL. (1) **`מפתח` lives in the COMMENTS, with an explicit leading minus** — `"-11.5h מפתח https://…"`, `"-2.25h מפתח על שעות אור"`. Reading those as positive was inflating every task that had one. Netting one Volta QA task properly: work 35.25h − reductions 16.5h = 18.75h against a title of **17.5h** — the hand-maintained title was right all along. 68 reduction entries are imported with **negative minutes**, which is deliberate: they are real ledger lines and dropping them while keeping the positives is what made comment totals run 4× over the studio's own figures. (2) **An hours figure must LEAD the comment** (after an optional date prefix: `"23/11 5.75h שיחה עם טדי"`). A mid-sentence number is prose, not a log — the single worst false positive was `"כל עבודה שמושקעת מעל 17.5 שעות מבוטלת…"` ("work beyond 17.5 hours is written off"), a **cap being quoted**, counted as 17.5h of work. A bare number with no unit is also rejected now. Result: title and comments agree within 15% on **70%** of the 229 tasks that have both, and the residual bias flipped to comments being *lower* — the safe direction, since the title wins. (3) **The reconciler owns the entry list.** `build-rehome-sql.mjs` used to re-derive entries from the story cache, so it emitted hours for roll-up parents and for tasks the review had left at zero, and `Σ(entries) ≠ legacy_remainder` on the same task. It now reads `data/legacy-entries.json` verbatim; the reconciler **exits non-zero** if `total ≠ attributable + remainder`. (4) **Author identity without accounts:** `profiles.id` is a FK to `auth.users`, so "one inactive profile per ex-employee" would have meant minting ~27 dormant `@studionmore.com` logins for people who left years ago. Instead `time_entries.user_id` is nullable **for legacy rows only** (CHECK: `user_id is not null or (legacy and legacy_author_name is not null)`) and the raw Asana name rides in `legacy_author_name` — real per-person, per-date history, no accounts. Only 6 of 33 comment authors match the current roster (222 of 2,397 comments); the rest are yam sasson, Edor Nisim, Miri Kuntsman, adi, ruth, dikla… **Store typing does the safety work:** `entrySums` is `UserEntrySum[]` (non-null `userId`) because filtering out `legacy` is exactly what guarantees a real user — so no personal aggregation needs a null guard, and the compiler flags any that would. Final: **7,948.65h recovered = 3,994.90h dated entries (1,533 rows) + 3,953.75h remainder**, cross-checked between the reconciler, the SQL and `audit-rehome.mjs`.
- 2026-07-28 — data — **Three conventions Nitsan supplied after reviewing the CSV, all now encoded.** (1) **ROLL-UP PARENTS — the big one.** A parent task's title figure is the SUM of its subtasks: `"Q&A Movies - 128.25h"` has 7 children (`Storyboards - 34h`, `Design (70)- 50h`, `Animation - 18h`, …) that add up to the same 128.25h, and the import flattened parents and children into sibling tasks — so counting both **doubles every rolled-up figure**. Roughly two thirds of the unsorted set are subtasks. Decision: **children win, parent contributes 0**, with a guard — a parent is only zeroed when at least one of its children is present AND carries hours (`rollup-parent-kept-no-children` flags the rest, where the parent's figure is the only surviving record). Section names follow the same rule one level up: `Movies - 171.75h` ≈ the sum of its **top-level** tasks only, which is why `sections.legacy_hours` is display-only and never added to a client total. Parent shape comes from Asana (`parent.gid`, `num_subtasks`) and is cached to `data/asana/parents.json` by `fetch-asana-stories.mjs`; **without that file the reconciler warns loudly and does NOT zero anything** rather than silently double-count. (2) **`key` / `מפתח` rows are hours REDUCTIONS, not work** — `"מפתח שנעשה - 18.25h"` is a note recording how big a reduction was. Counted in **neither** direction per Nitsan: adding overstates delivery, negating would re-deduct something the studio's sheet had already netted off. Matched by `KEY_ROW` (guarded so "Keyboard" doesn't trip it) and flagged `key-reduction-not-counted`. (3) Budget **ranges** store the **high end** in `estimate_hours` (the ceiling quoted to the client); the low end rides along in the review CSV's `budget_low` and the full range survives in `legacy_title`. Note-like rows ("--- Keys ---", "Template Instructions", "Action Items") are **kept as tasks** — they carry no hours so they cannot distort a total. Client mapping confirmed by Nitsan against Asana: the `Website (232+61/134)…` and `Movies - 171.75h` sections and the `Storyboards - 34h` subtask are all Volta Solar; `Yoco new website - 67h` is Yoco.
- 2026-07-28 — gotcha — **`node --env-file` takes the LAST duplicate of a key.** A stray `ASANA_ACCESS_TOKEN="1/your-token-here"` placeholder line appended after the real token silently shadowed it and every Asana call returned 401 with no hint that the value was wrong. When an API 401s, check for a duplicate key in `.env.local` before assuming the credential is bad.
- 2026-07-28 — data (NOT YET APPLIED) — **"Imported / Unsorted" re-home + pre-Everhour hour recovery.** 620 tasks / 87 sections / 23 legacy projects. **Asana is not needed to place them**: `tasks.project_id → projects.everhour_id` still carries the mapping and the project name IS the client name in 21 of 23 cases. Mapping → 14 NEW archived clients (Volta, Cognigo (d.day labs), Quadream, Anchor, Siteaware, New Era, Yoco, One Zero, In-reach, Empathy, CSL, PDQ, PlayStudios, Mesh) + 4 existing (Collabria 53, Harmonie 33 **incl. 2103h**, Voyantis 25, Studio 29). **Second find: the hours were never imported.** Pre-Everhour the studio logged hours in Asana task COMMENTS and hand-copied the total into the title, budget in parens — `"Logo Update (8) - 5.25h"`. `task_comments` is **empty studio-wide**; 2,782 tasks with an `asana_gid` have zero time entries. Titles alone yield **6,142h across 221 of 594 tasks**; the other 373 need the comments. **Double-counting checked and dismissed**: only 51 tasks studio-wide have both a title hour and tracked time, and those are lump backfills logged at the **2022-12-04 cutover** with identical figures, or ongoing tasks where Everhour kept counting past the frozen snapshot — tracked always wins, so the rule is *never write recovered hours to a task that has any `time_entries`*. Scripts: `lib/legacy-hours.mjs` (parser, 35 unit tests, every case a real string that broke a simpler version) · `fetch-asana-stories.mjs` (**needs `ASANA_ACCESS_TOKEN` in .env.local** — a claude.ai connector can't help, this is a node script; caches per task to `data/asana/stories/` and is resumable because Asana rate-limits hard) · `reconcile-legacy-hours.mjs` (→ `data/legacy-review-*.csv`, **CSV not XLSX**: no xlsx lib is installed and Nitsan lives in Sheets; BOM + CRLF so Excel doesn't mangle the Hebrew) · `build-rehome-sql.mjs` (→ `data/0017_rehome_unsorted.sql`) · `audit-rehome.mjs`. **WHY THE SQL FILE:** 0011's `enforce_task_member_columns` trigger blocks `client_id`/`section_id`/`title` writes for anything without an admin `auth.uid()` — **including the service role AND the SQL editor** — so the generated file disables the trigger inside the transaction and re-enables it. **INVARIANT: `task total = Σ(legacy time_entries) + legacy_hours`**; `legacy_hours` is the *unattributed remainder*, never a duplicate of the entries. Order: apply 0016 → (optional) fetch comments → reconcile → **Nitsan reviews the CSV, which then beats the parser** → build SQL → run it → `audit-rehome.mjs`. Rollback: `delete from time_entries where legacy`; names restore from `legacy_title`/`legacy_name`; moves from `project_id`, which is never modified.
- 2026-07-27 — v0.99.28 — **Collapse/expand all sections from the table header, and empty sections are dimmed.** The header's leading spacer became a chevron button aligned with the section chevrons; it collapses everything when any group is open and expands everything when all are collapsed. Required lifting each `SectionGroup`'s `open` state into `ClientView` as a `collapsed: Set<string>` — **collapsed-by-exception**, so a newly created section appears expanded, with `""` standing for the null "No section" group. `onOpen` replaces the old local `setOpen(true)` when a task is dropped in. Only groups actually on screen count toward "all" (the empty No-section group exists only mid-drag). Empty section headers render at `opacity-50`, keyed off the same `sectionIsEmpty` (measured against ALL tasks) that enables the delete button — so **dimmed always means deletable**. Verified: 31 rows → 0 → 31 with the tooltip flipping, and a throwaway empty section rendered at opacity 0.5 vs 1 for a populated one (test section removed, no leftovers).
- 2026-07-27 — v0.99.27 — **Drag handle given a real hit area; tick moved next to the task name.** The grip was a **14×14** icon at the very left edge, and since only a mousedown *on it* arms the drag, missing it by a few pixels silently cancelled the gesture — which is why dragging still felt intermittent after v0.99.26. The hit area is now the full-height **28×39** left gutter (icon still fades in on hover, hit area always present). Row indent `pl-6`→`pl-9` to clear it, and the leading cell carries `LEAD_TIGHT` (`-mr-1.5`) so the complete-tick sits **6px** from the task name and **8px** clear of the handle instead of stranded between them; the same class is applied to the table-header spacer and the section-header chevron so all three stay aligned.
- 2026-07-27 — v0.99.26 — **Fixed drag ("worked once, then stopped"), added task delete, moved 32px from Tag to Hrs/budget.** (1) **Root cause of the drag failure: the whole ROW was `draggable`.** Any press-and-move started a drag — fighting click-to-open, making text selection in the title cell impossible, and letting a short drag be delivered as a *click*, which opened the task panel whose `fixed inset-0` overlay then blocked every later drag. Now **only a mousedown on the grip may start a drag**: `armedRef` (a **ref**, not state — toggling `draggable` from mousedown races React's batching, so the attribute could still be false when the browser decides the gesture) is set by the grip, and `onDragStart` calls `preventDefault()` when it isn't set. (2) **Second cause: acceptance was gated on `e.dataTransfer.types.includes(<custom MIME>)`**, which isn't reliably populated during `dragover` across browsers — so drop targets could silently never accept. Both the row and the section now decide from `draggedTaskId`, and `text/plain` is set alongside the custom type as a payload fallback. (3) **`deleteTask`** in the store + a trash button on each row (admin, hover) — **`time_entries`/comments/attachments are ON DELETE CASCADE**, so the confirm spells out how many hours will be destroyed, and it is deliberately **NOT in the undo history** (cascaded rows can't be restored, so an "undo" would bring the task back minus its hours — worse than none). Published client reports are unaffected: they ship frozen snapshots. This existed because a section can only be deleted when empty, and there was no way to empty one. (4) Tag column w-36→w-28, Hrs/budget w-28→w-36. Verified end-to-end on a throwaway section+task: delete disabled → task deleted → delete enabled → section removed, both rows confirmed gone from the DB.
- 2026-07-27 — v0.99.25 — **Reorder tasks, rename/delete sections, and hours visible on the client table.** (1) **`BudgetBar` returned null when `estimateHours` was null** — so a task with no budget showed NOTHING in that column and its logged hours were invisible on the client table unless you opened the task. Now shows the hours alone (bar only when there's an estimate); column header renamed **Budget → Hrs/budget**. (2) **`reorderTask(movedId, beforeId)`** in the store: places a task before another within its own section and rewrites that section's positions as a dense 1..n (stable, unlike fractional/midpoint schemes that drift toward collisions). Undo restores the exact prior positions. Drop on a sibling row shows an inset top line (not a border — a border would shift the row 2px); a drag from another section is deliberately left unhandled so it bubbles to the `SectionGroup` and becomes a move-in. **Reorder is disabled while a column sort is active** — positions would change invisibly and the drop would look like it did nothing. `draggedTaskId` is mirrored in a module-level variable because `getData()` is unreadable during `dragover`, and a row must decide mid-drag whether it owns the drop. (3) **Section rename** (inline `EditableTextCell`) + **delete, admin-only and only when empty** — emptiness is computed against ALL tasks, not the filtered list, since "Show completed" would otherwise make a section of done tasks look safe to delete; the store also refuses independently. The header became a div (an editable field and a delete button can't nest in a button). **NOTE — existing data:** every task's `position` was **0** (never set by the imports), so list order was incidental; the first reorder in a section assigns real positions to all of its tasks. Ordering logic unit-tested (9 cases incl. no-op and abort guards); the drag *interaction* for reorder was not exercised in-browser.
- 2026-07-27 — v0.99.24 — **Admins can move a task between sections**, two ways. (1) **Drag** — rows in the client list are `draggable` for admins with a `GripVertical` handle that fades in on hover, absolutely positioned in a widened `pl-6` gutter so appearing doesn't shift the row. The whole `SectionGroup` (header + rows + add-row) is the drop zone, ring-highlighted while hovered, so the target is generous rather than a thin line. Uses a custom MIME `application/x-studio-task-id` so the weekly plan and report table ignore these drags, and so `dragover` can decide whether to accept (`getData()` is only readable on `drop`). Dropping on the section a task is already in is a no-op — no pointless write, no junk undo step. The **"No section" group is normally hidden when empty but renders for admins mid-drag**, otherwise there'd be no way to drag a task OUT of a section; `dragstart`/`dragend` bubble to the table so it knows a drag is running without threading state through every row. (2) **Task panel** — new **Section** row with a dropdown of that client's sections (the Client row is now just the chip); members see it read-only. Both go through `updateTask({sectionId})`, which already mapped to `section_id` and carries undo/redo. **GOTCHA discovered:** migration 0011's `enforce_task_member_columns` trigger **also blocks the SERVICE ROLE** from protected task columns — `is_admin()` reads `auth.uid()`, which a service-key connection doesn't have, so it fails with "members cannot modify protected task fields". Server-side code can insert tasks but cannot update title/section/assignee/etc.; such edits must go through a user session.
- 2026-07-27 — v0.99.23 — **Fifth occasion group: "Studio & fun days"** (`src/lib/studio-days.ts`, 8 fixed-date entries — Women's Day, World Design Day, Hummus Day, Falafel Day, Emoji Day, Photography Day, Coffee Day, Artist's Day). Plain Gregorian month/day, so they ride the same recurring `MM-DD` path as birthdays — no calendar conversion, no re-seeding. Kept deliberately short and spread Mar–Oct (Sep/Dec are already covered by the Jewish holidays); days with competing claims (pizza day is 9 Feb in the US, 17 Jan internationally) were left out rather than guessed. **Sports/awards were considered and rejected as a group**: Oscars/Emmys/NBA Finals dates are announced annually and aren't computable, so a built-in list would need yearly updating and would go silently wrong — the exact failure mode the computed holidays avoid. Add those as *custom* occasions when announced. Carousel fix: the arrows shared the title's row and cost it ~60px of a column that only has ~200px (the portrait takes 216px of a ~477px hero), truncating "Shaked's bir…" — arrows now sit on their own row flanking the dots, and the title wraps to two lines instead of truncating.
- 2026-07-27 — v0.99.22 — **Occasions.** Migration **`0015_occasions.sql`** — needed only for CUSTOM entries; everything else works without it. Four groups feed the home "Coming up" pane: **birthdays** (from `member_hr.birth_date`), **work anniversaries** (from `profiles.start_date`), **Jewish holidays**, and **custom**. Which groups show is stored in `app_settings` under key `occasion_groups` (no schema — it's already key/jsonb), edited from **Settings → Occasions** (admin only) along with add/remove of custom entries (`recurring` repeats yearly on the same month/day). (1) **Jewish holidays are COMPUTED, never seeded** — `src/lib/jewish-holidays.ts` walks the range asking ICU (`Intl.DateTimeFormat` with `ca-hebrew`) for each day's Hebrew date and matches fixed Hebrew-calendar definitions. A seeded table would need re-seeding every year and would go stale the first year nobody remembered. Verified against ICU: 1 Tishri 5787 = 2026-09-12, 15 Nisan 5787 = 2027-04-22, Purim on 14 **Adar II** (5787 is a leap year) = 2027-03-23. The three days that MOVE to avoid Shabbat (Yom HaShoah 27 Nisan, Yom HaZikaron/HaAtzmaut 4–5 Iyar) implement the Knesset rules — validated against a real checkable date: 5 Iyar 5785 fell on a Saturday and the code shifts Yom HaAtzmaut to Thu **2025-05-01**, which is when it was actually observed. (2) `/api/celebrations` merges all four sources. It reads `member_hr` with the service role but returns **only `MM-DD`** — a birthday is studio-social, a birth year is not. Pre-migration a missing `occasions` table degrades to "no custom occasions" instead of breaking every home page; likewise `/api/occasions` GET still returns the group switches (they live in app_settings) rather than failing wholesale. (3) Writes in `/api/occasions` go through the **caller's session, not the service key**, so the DB's admin-only policy is the enforcement.
- 2026-07-27 — v0.99.21 — Member home: the duplicate lower "My hours" pane (`PeriodStat`) removed — its vs-last-period figure became a delta chip on the tile above, freeing the row so both graphs sit side by side; My tasks took the slot beside "Log my hours". `PeriodStat` **deleted** (no remaining caller) along with two now-unused chart imports. Occasions moved **inside the blue hero** (below the heading, above "+ Log time") as an inline variant with arrows + dots + "in N days"; it carries its own right margin because the portrait is scaled by the pane's HEIGHT (~216px wide against a 176px container) and a filled pill slid under it and hid the date. Hero "My week" button removed.
- 2026-07-27 — v0.99.20 — Member hero at half width with four filter-scoped tiles beside it; the portrait's head breaks out of the top (anchored `top:-34/bottom:0`, not a fixed height — `items-stretch` grows the panel to match the tile column so its height isn't known at author time) and the FIGURE's right edge sits 5px inside the pane (shifted by the silhouette's measured 12.8% transparent right margin, since anchoring the image's own edge leaves a gap that moves as the panel grows). **Celebrations bugs fixed:** birthdays never showed for members (member_hr is admin-only under RLS → client-side select returned nothing) — now via `/api/celebrations`; sorting compared the formatted "D/M" string so "10/8" sorted before "9/8"; anniversary year counts were off by one for dates rolled into next year.
- 2026-07-27 — v0.99.19 — Averages to 1 decimal (`formatHoursAvg`); logged time stays at 2 places since an entry of 0.75h must read back as 0.75. Michal Ner-David's name capitalised (profile + her weekly-plan column, which is generated from the first name at creation).
- 2026-07-27 — v0.99.18 — Portrait crown un-pinched: the head layer stopped exactly at the circle's top edge, where the circle is only ~20px wide against a ~32px head, so `border-radius` shaved the crown and it read as a flat cut. The head layer now runs 14% of the diameter past the edge. Team card name to `text-xl` with Billable moved into the stats row as a `PercentRing`. **Member archive**: the active/inactive control was a coloured pill reading "active" with no affordance — now an Archive/Restore button matching the client page, plus a banner on an archived member's page; team filter says "Show archived".
- 2026-07-27 — v0.99.17 — Portraits sit on a **brand-blue** circle with the head breaking the crop. The chequered background was not CSS: `placeholder.png` was a FLATTENED export with the Photoshop transparency checkerboard baked in at alpha 255, while the genuinely transparent master (`photo_transparent.png`) sat unused beside it. `DEFAULT_TEAM_PHOTO` now points at `cutout.png`, a 640px resize of that master (371KB, was 6.7MB). New `bleed` prop draws the image twice at identical geometry — once inside the clipping circle (body), once clipped to the band above it (head) — because one oversized image would also let the shoulders spill past the circle's curve. **`placeholder.png` is now unused** and could be deleted.
- 2026-07-27 — v0.99.16 — **Hours to 2 decimals.** A logged 0.75h rendered as "0.8": the stored minutes were always exact (0.75h = 45min), `formatHoursShort` was at `toFixed(1)`. New `formatHoursDecimal(minutes, digits=2)` trims trailing zeros. **Member emails** on Team + member page via admin-only `/api/admin/member-emails` (profiles has no email column; the address lives in auth.users, so reading it there avoids a denormalised copy that drifts). Team cards: portrait left, details stacked left-aligned. Member page: both pictures moved into the header as `PictureEditBadge`, "Pictures" pane deleted. Client **archive/unarchive on the client page** (`clients.archived` and the Settings control already existed).
- 2026-07-26 — v0.99.14 (deployed, LIVE) — **`/reset` no longer shows members a bare red `{}`.** The form passed `error.message` straight through, and Supabase's own auth mailer fails two ways that are both meaningless to a member: **429** when the built-in mailer's ~2/hour cap is hit, and **500 `unexpected_failure` / "Error sending recovery email"** when custom SMTP is misconfigured — supabase-js surfaces the latter as an `AuthRetryableFetchError` whose `message` is the **literal string `"{}"`**, which rendered as a red `{}`. Both now map to a plain sentence pointing at the working path (admin → "Send password link"), plus a `console.error` with name/status, plus a guard so any other empty-ish message can't reach a member. **DIAGNOSTIC NOTE for next time:** `curl -X POST "$SUPABASE_URL/auth/v1/recover" -H "apikey: $ANON_KEY" -d '{"email":"…"}'` distinguishes the two instantly (429 = capped, 500 = SMTP broken). When this was written the project's SMTP was returning 500 on every auth email right after being configured — Resend itself was healthy (key valid, `studionmore.com` verified, `eu-west-1`), so the fault was the values in Supabase's SMTP form. Resend SMTP wants **username = the literal string `resend`** (not an email — the usual mistake), password = a `re_…` API key with send access, `smtp.resend.com`, port 465/587, sender on the verified domain. **`/api/admin/invite` is unaffected by all of this** — it calls Resend's HTTP API directly and never touches Supabase's mailer, so admin-sent links keep working even while SMTP is broken. Also confirmed by end-to-end test that the v0.99.13 `token_hash` path works: `generateLink` → `GET /auth/confirm?token_hash=…&type=recovery` → **307 → `/reset/update`**. Members holding **pre-v0.99.13 emails must be re-sent a link** — those are PKCE `?code=` links and are permanently broken.
- 2026-07-26 — v0.99.13 (deployed, LIVE) — **Admin-sent password links never worked cross-device; fixed.** The team page's "Send password link" called `supabase.auth.resetPasswordForEmail()` **from the browser**, and `@supabase/ssr`'s `createBrowserClient` defaults to **PKCE** — so the `code_verifier` was stored in the **admin's** browser and the member got a `?code=` link. Opened on the member's own device, `/auth/confirm` had no verifier, `exchangeCodeForSession` failed, and they were redirected to `/reset` — the "very first screen". **The link was never going to work**; the only reason it ever seemed to take "two emails" was the member separately clicking "First time here?" themselves, which put the verifier in *their* browser. (1) New **`POST /api/admin/invite`** (admin-only, service role): `auth.admin.generateLink({type:'recovery'})` mints the token server-side **without sending**, and we mail a **`token_hash`** link via **Resend**. `/auth/confirm` already had the `verifyOtp()` branch for this — nothing had ever used it. Carries no browser state → opens on **any** device. Also **bypasses Supabase's built-in auth mailer (~2 emails/hour project-wide)**, which had made onboarding a team impossible ("mails exceeded"). (2) Route resolves the address from **`auth.users` by profile id** (`getUserById`) — `profiles` has no email column, which is why the button used to `window.prompt` the admin to retype it. (3) `/reset` now surfaces `?error=link` ("that link couldn't be used…") instead of a blank form — `/auth/confirm` had always passed it and nothing read it. **Member-initiated `/reset` still uses the PKCE path** (same browser, so it works) and still draws on the Supabase mailer **until custom SMTP is configured** — Resend SMTP is `smtp.resend.com:465`, username the literal `resend`, password a Resend API key, sender `notifications@studionmore.com` (send-only identity — no mailbox exists, and none is needed; Resend authorizes by verified *domain*). After enabling custom SMTP, **raise Authentication → Rate Limits → email** (locked at 2/hr until then) and the **email-template editor unlocks** (it's gated behind custom SMTP — that's why the template couldn't be found/edited). **Not verified end-to-end**: no real invite was sent (needs an admin prod session) — test on your own address before mailing the team. **Deploy gotcha re-confirmed:** `npx vercel --prod` **silently fails on the shell's default Node 18** (CLI 54 needs Node 20+) — always prepend the Node 24 PATH.
- 2026-07-25 — v0.99.12 (built, NOT yet deployed) — **Timers fully removed + Everhour gaps can no longer be ignored.** Migration **`0014_retire_timers_and_sync_issues.sql`** — **must be applied before deploying**. (1) **Timers gone for good.** A prior pass removed the Track buttons but left the feature wired: `timer-widget.tsx` was still mounted in the header and a "▶ Start timer" button still sat in the task panel above the manual add form. Deleted the component, the button, and `startTimer`/`stopTimer`/`completeTimerEntry` + `runningTimer` from `store.tsx`; the boot query that hydrated a running timer is gone. Migration deletes never-stopped rows (`minutes is null` — they hold no hours, only a start time, and nothing could complete them any more), drops the `timer_or_minutes` check, and makes `time_entries.minutes` NOT NULL. `started_at` is **kept** and commented as legacy provenance (completed timer entries still carry it); drop it later if you want. Members log time manually with a mandatory description — that is now the only path.
- **(2) `sync_issues` — the Everhour gap queue.** Unmapped entries used to be counted in a `console.warn` and forgotten (one real run: 43 imported, **21 skipped**), so a published client report could quietly understate billable hours. Now **one queue row per unimportable Everhour entry** (keyed on `everhour_id`, so a nightly re-sync over an overlapping window never double-counts and an old gap never falls out of the 14-day window). `everhour-sync.ts` gained `reconcileSyncIssues`: records new gaps, bumps `last_seen_at` on known ones, **reopens** anything marked imported that reappears, leaves `ignored` alone, and **auto-closes** issues whose entry has since landed. It also fetches `/team/users` (best-effort) so the queue says the person's name, not `user 1453915`. (3) **Admin surfaces**: new `/sync-issues` page (group by Everhour task or person; **Link to a task** via TaskAutocomplete, **Create the task** pre-mapped with client/section — inherits the client's billable flag like `store.addTask` — or **Ignore** on the record; each resolution re-syncs from the oldest affected date, capped at 90 days, so hours land immediately instead of waiting for the cron). New `notifications-bell.tsx` replaces the intake-only bell: one "Waiting on you" popover for intake + sync issues, badge = total. Sidebar gets a badged "Sync issues" item. **Client Reports shows a red banner when any gap is open** — publishing freezes the numbers, so that's the moment it matters. (4) A failed queue load **never** falls through to the reassuring "nothing outstanding" empty state — an all-clear we can't stand behind is the exact failure mode this replaces. Store carries a lightweight `openSyncIssues` count (RLS-scoped; returns 0 pre-migration so the app is safe to deploy in either order, though the page 500s until 0014 is applied). Verified locally: tsc clean, lint 0 errors (58→56 warnings), no console errors, panel/bell/nav checked in the browser.
- 2026-07-25 — v0.99.11 (deployed, LIVE) — **Removed the member salary field entirely.** The studio doesn't want salary held in this system (the finance-product P&L keeps studio-level salary *expense* totals — that's intentional and out of scope here). (1) Migration **`0013_drop_member_salary.sql`** drops `member_hr.salary` + its data — **applied by owner in Supabase**. (2) Code: removed the "Salary (₪)" input + its number-coercion/`type` special-cases from the admin team profile (`team/[profileId]/page.tsx`); updated `/api/me/hr` + `hr-details-form` + `0012` comments. (3) **Seed scrubbed**: `0008_member_hr.sql` had the salary column def + the historical salary values in its `values(...)` seed — removed via a PII-safe script (dropped the `salary` CREATE-TABLE column + the salary field from all 8 seed rows and the insert/select/alias lists; verified 16→15 cols consistent across insert/select/alias/every row without surfacing any PII). **No `salary` reference remains anywhere in `src/` or `supabase/` except `finance_salaries`.** **RESIDUAL:** the *git history* still contains the pre-scrub commits of `0008` with the old salary values — true erasure needs a `git filter-repo`/BFG history rewrite + force-push (destructive to the shared remote; owner's call, not done).
- 2026-07-25 — v0.99.9 — **Automatic Everhour sync** (the team is still logging in Everhour, so the tracker pulls from it on a schedule). `src/lib/everhour-sync.ts` is a TS port of `scripts/sync-everhour.mjs` (identical behaviour: insert-only, idempotent by `everhour_id`, skips unmapped task/user and now *reports* them). `src/app/api/everhour-sync/route.ts` — GET = cron (CRON_SECRET bearer), POST = admin button; `?days=N` sets the lookback (default 3, max 90). **`vercel.json` cron added**: `0 20 * * *` (23:00 IDT **daily**, `?days=14` so it also catches back-dated edits and weekend logging). ⚠️ **The Vercel account is HOBBY: daily-only crons, max 2 total** — an every-2h schedule (`0 5-15/2 * * 0-4`) was rejected at deploy with "Hobby accounts are limited to daily cron jobs". The 2 slots are now used (plan-sync + everhour-sync). For sub-daily syncing you'd need Vercel Pro, GitHub Actions, or Supabase pg_cron hitting the endpoint; user decided nightly + the manual button is enough (2026-07-25). Admin **"Sync Everhour"** button on the Time Feed header (`everhour-sync-button.tsx`, 14-day lookback, reloads on new rows). **`EVERHOUR_API_KEY` is now SET in Vercel production** (it used to be import-only/local — the cron needs it at runtime). Retire all of this when the team actually stops using Everhour: drop the two crons from vercel.json, the button, and the env var.

(5) **Intake/avatar hardening**: `src/lib/uploads.ts` allowlist forces safe server-chosen Content-Type + rejects HTML/SVG/JS; intake adds a DB rate limit (8/link/10min). **TODO before/at deploy:** apply migration 0011 in Supabase (before deploy); residual `npm audit` highs are transitive (Next's bundled `sharp` libvips CVEs + `postcss`) — no clean non-breaking fix yet, tracked.
